### Glossary

# INTRODUCTION

This is a book of spiritual stories – narratives about ordinary people’s devotional lives and their relationships with an extraordinary teacher. Shrii Shrii Anandamurti (1921-1990), more affectionately known as Baba, was a spiritual master of great power, compassion, and vision. He founded the socio-spiritual movement Ananda Marga, an organization that grew in size from a few devotees in India in the 1950s to a worldwide organization with tens of thousands of members today. This book deals with the experiences of Ananda Margiis – members of Ananda Marga – born or living in New York Sector, which is the organizational designation for the United States, Canada, Mexico, Central America, and the Caribbean countries.

Devotion – all-embracing love – is very important on the spiritual path, and in this book Baba’s devotees describe a wide range of experiences of this great love. For some, Baba is father; for others, friend; for still others, the beloved. Each individual feels that He is theirs alone. They feel great pain at being separated from Him. And the connection runs both ways. His disciples are His nearest and dearest. Hence the title of this book: “I Am Yours,” a phrase which Baba used when He departed from the Philippines in 1969.

Although Baba left His physical body in 1990, the spiritual relationships continue. As He reminded His followers, the true guru is the unchanging Supreme Consciousness permeating all things, a connection that is open to everyone.

He was born Prabhat Ranjan Sarkar in Jamalpur, in eastern India, on the full moon of May, 1921, an especially auspicious day (the Buddha was also born on this day). He gave His first initiation into meditation practices when He was still a young man, to a notorious criminal who approached Him in order to rob Him, but who instead experienced a conversion. This kind of dramatic transformation was typical of Baba’s effect on people. After coming into contact with Baba, devotees often experienced tremendous joy, peace, and inspiration, as if the true purpose and meaning of their lives was now clear.

Baba encouraged people to work for *dharma* (righteousness) and social justice. He consistently set an example for His devotees, showing them how to be more truly human by coming closer to God. And He inspired them to serve others.

In 1955, He founded Ananda Marga, an organization dedicated to these ideas and to reviving and propagating the ancient meditation practices of *Tantra*, a part of the Indian spiritual tradition with an emphasis on practice rather than theory. Although Ananda Marga was originally composed of family people, Sarkar soon created an order of renunciates – monks and nuns – who could work full-time as a complement to the family margiis to help implement these ideas in society. He soon also proposed an alternative socio-economic theory to capitalism and communism that He called the Progressive Utilization Theory or Prout. With its emphasis on strong ethical values and its extensive involvement in public relief work, Ananda Marga grew throughout the 60’s, attracting thousands of people, including many civil servants.

Both the Indian central government and the communist bloc of West Bengal began to perceive Sarkar and Ananda Marga as threats to their power bases. There followed a controversial period. In 1971, perhaps instigated by the government, several followers split from Baba and tried to implicate Him in conspiracy charges. He was arrested and jailed, and during the ensuing Emergency period in India (as Prime Minister Indira Gandhi declared martial law) thousands of margiis were also jailed. After seven years imprisonment, during five of which Baba fasted on liquids to protest a government poisoning attempt, all charges were declared to be unfounded, and He was released. Throughout, Baba had remained the eternal optimist, firmly committed to His principles.

He then traveled widely, including to Jamaica and Venezuela in the Western hemisphere, and later to Europe. In the 1980s, Baba continued to introduce and develop new spiritual and social theories. He also composed devotional music, a body of some 5000 beautifully poetic songs known as Prabhat Samgiit. He penned books explaining the contributions and personalities of Shiva and Krsna; developed a social outlook called Neohumanism which gave philosophical grounding to the ideas of universalism and social equality; wrote commentaries on linguistics; and offered a groundbreaking theory on the link between matter and consciousness which He called the theory of Microvita.

He did all this while continuing to meet with and inspire thousands seeking His spiritual guidance. The Indian scriptures say, “He alone is great who can make others great.” This is how many devotees feel about Baba, that His greatness was reflected in His commitment to uplifting the human condition. Baba always encouraged people to become responsible stewards of the planet. Unlike some gurus who have caught the public’s attention because of their luxurious lifestyles, His focus was not on Himself or His own comfort, but on serving others.”

This book follows a roughly chronological order, ranging from the reminiscences of a few early Indian devotees, to Ananda Marga’s arrival in the Americas, to the stream of devotees traveling to India to see this extraordinary master in person. Each remembrance is told in the words of the storyteller, expressing their own individual understanding of the philosophical ideals, and their own level of practice. Many of the stories are transcribed from videotape; (the editors have noted in brackets, for example, when the narrator laughed or cried while telling their stories). Several highlights are common to many of these stories. The experience of Personal Contact, or PC, offered disciples the chance to receive individual attention from Baba, to spend time with Him one on one, and receive His blessing. And there was *Dharma Samiksa*, an extraordinary period in 1981 during which Baba reviewed the spiritual life of each person who came to Him, noting their past, present and future, their health and karmic situation, and recommended physical and spiritual exercises for each. Thousands of margiis also attended the semi-annual *Dharma Maha Cakras* (DMCs) in India, three-day gatherings during which people enjoyed collective meditation, music, food, and Baba’s discourses. The highlight of these gatherings was Baba’s *varabhaya mudra*, a dispensation of spiritual energy.

An explanatory note needs to be made regarding the physical touch of the guru. Baba’s touch – whether a pat on the cheek, or a hug – is said to have had a deep and profound impact on the devotee, often removing karmic obstacles. Moreover, in both PC and *Dharma Samiksa*, Baba sometimes rectified the mistakes of His devotees through punishment. Often He would simply touch the disciple with His cane, transmitting energy to them; other times, He gave stronger hits. Although this kind of reprimand may be difficult to understand on its surface, it is common in the history of guru-disciple

relations, especially in *Tantra*. It was an expression of loving guidance, accepted gratefully by disciples, which served to remove an individual’s karmic obstacles. Indeed, following PC and *Dharma Samiksa*, many noted their lives had changed dramatically.

The editors have opted to capitalize pronouns referring to Baba, since this reflects the degree of respect and love Baba’s devotees feel for Him.

All quotations in italics at the beginning of chapters are by Shrii Shrii Anandamurti.

An abundance of Sanskrit or Bengali words appear in the text. Most of them are italicized and defined in the glossary, apart from several commonly used words such as “guru,” “sadhana” (meditation) or “darshan” (seeing the guru).

We hope the reader will come away from this book with a new understanding of Baba’s contribution to society. But more importantly, we hope that the depiction of the depth of love expressed for and by Baba and His devotees will inspire the reader in his or her own spiritual journey. For in the end, this was Baba’s goal: to propagate a spiritual vision and way of life; to teach us how to love.

# CHAPTER ONE:

## HOW COULD YOU LOVE ME SO MUCH?

> “*Brahma Himself is the Guru. There cannot be any second Guru. Generally people say that the form is the Guru, but the form is not the Guru; the Guru is expressing Himself through that form.*”

Ananda Marga was growing by leaps and bounds in India during the late 1960s, and margiis there experienced many extraordinary moments with Baba. Slowly the organization began to expand overseas. Baba began to send *acaryas* to New York Sector in 1969. These early *acaryas* discovered a great receptivity to the practices of meditation and yoga. People turned out in great numbers to learn, and many became devotees of Baba.

One of the first New York Sectorial offices was set up in Wichita, Kansas; soon, regular retreats furthering the teaching of meditation and yoga were held in many areas. Margiis busied themselves with social service activities, and many attended Local Full Timer (LFT) training sessions. LFTs dedicated themselves full-time for the mission, immersing themselves in service projects. Ananda Marga groups (units) sprang up all across the sector, holding weekly meditation and social gatherings. Stories of Baba’s omniscience and grace related at these gatherings inspired people to begin to travel to India to see this mysterious Master for themselves.

### Gayatrii Vashist

*(daughter of Ac. Candranath Kumar, India)*

I was initiated at age three, because my father was one of the early margiis. He used to go every Sunday to see Baba in Jamalpur, so Baba was the *acarya* of my brother and sister and my parents. I also wanted to do sadhana, but I was only three years old. My brother and sister used to tease me, “You are the only one Baba did not give sadhana to.”

I used to cry and ask my father, “I also want to learn.”

One Sunday he went to Jamalpur and asked Baba, “Baba, the youngest one wants to learn sadhana, but she’s only three years old.”

Baba said, “OK, you can teach her *nama mantra*.”

After that, Baba visited our place in Bhagalpur. I was there, feeling very proud that I was also initiated, that I could go and meet Baba. I was standing near the door, and Baba called me inside. I went and sat on Baba’s bed. Everyone was looking at me, with a “get down, get down” expression.

Then Baba said to me, “You do sadhana regularly?”

I told him, “Yes, Baba.”

“OK, fine, what do you feel, doing sadhana?” As a child I had heard some people say they hear flute music, so I thought that maybe if I said this, there would be great praise from Baba. I said, “Baba, sometimes I hear flute music,” and He said, “OHHH? Flute music?” I still can’t remember very clearly, but my parents told me this.

Another time Baba came to our place, and was sitting next to many flowers offered by devotees. As children we never bothered much about Baba, our eyes were on the flowers. Let Baba go and we will grab the flowers, we thought. Baba was leaving, and I went and took a big bunch of flowers. Baba shouted at me, “Gayatrii, it would better if you learn some discipline.” I was very scared, and put them back. As He was leaving, He turned back and smiled, and said to me, “Now you can take the flowers, I am going.” When I remember those incidents I feel we were really very lucky.

I was a naughty child. Once Baba was eating His food. My father, mother, brothers and sisters were all present. When Baba finished His food, He asked me if I wanted a *rasagulla*, an Indian sweet. He said, “Gayatrii, have a sweet.”

I was feeling shy, and I said, “No, I will not have one.”

My mother jokingly said, “Now you are offering and she is not taking, but when you leave, she will eat them all.”

I felt very bad, that my mother was complaining about me. I said, “Baba, she is always talking like this about me. But my father never says anything bad about me.” Baba started laughing and pointed at my father, and said, “He is a good boy.” As He was leaving, He told me, “Now I am not watching, you can have the sweet.”

Then Baba said to my mother, “You should not scold children much.” I felt very proud of myself, but after Baba left that room He said to me, “But as a child you should also behave in a nice way.”

My father was very close to Baba. Regarding family matters, Baba used to guide us, in little details. Once my father was transferred, we had to move, and all our luggage was placed on the truck. Baba came and said, in a sad mood, “It was a very nice house, but you all will have to leave it.” Afterwards we were transferred to Tatanagar, another city of Bihar. Baba visited our new place. It was the cold season and there was a fire in the room. All of us were sitting there. We did not want to leave Baba, and it was late. Baba told us, “Now you children should go and sleep; this is sleeping time. Tomorrow you can be with me again.” So what I gathered was that He was very particular about discipline, even for a child.

You may have heard about Makhan Dada, our cook. We had a big family, and he used to cook for everyone, including, on *dharmacakra* day, all the margiis who came. Once one margii came to see Baba at our place, and Baba asked him, “Do you do your sadhana regularly?”

The margii said, “Baba, I do it in the evening, but I can’t afford to do it in the morning, because morning time is too hectic and I have to rush to the office.”

Baba said to him, “If you want to learn how to do sadhana regularly, ask the cook Makhan. He’s working day and night, and if he can manage it, then why can’t you?”

My mother is a simple village lady, not very educated. She is the first lady *acarya* of Ananda Marga. Baba told her, in the beginning, “Ma, you have to prepare for the *acarya* exam.”

She said, “Baba, I am not very educated, I’m from a village, how can I do it?”

Baba said, “No, you have to do it.”

And there was another lady, an MA in English, very educated, who was also taking the exam with my mother. My mother was very nervous, and thought, “Why is Baba doing this to me, such an educated lady and me taking the exam together?” They both sat for the exam, and my mother cleared it, and that lady could not. So it’s all His Grace. He wanted her to become an *acarya*; it was His blessing.

My mother used to have a problem with sneezing. If she had something cold in the morning, or took a bath, for half an hour she would sneeze continuously. When my father learned sadhana, my mother was wondering, “What kind of sadhana is this, that he closes the door in the room? I think something is wrong with him; some day he will leave us and run away.”

So she asked him, “What kind of sadhana you are doing?” My father said, “If you want to learn, I will talk to my guru.” So he talked to Baba, and Baba said, “OK, you can bring her.” At that time, Baba was staying at the Railway Quarters in Jamalpur. They went, my father did *sastaung*, and my mother did also. And Baba said, “No, no, ma, not for you. You can do like this” [gives namaskar]. He always used to respect women. That day my mother had had for breakfast some cold thing like yoghurt, then she had her bath with cold water, and then she went to see Baba. And Baba initiated her. And from that day on, her sneezing problem disappeared.

Slowly, margiis were increasing in numbers. Baba started traveling by train from one place to another. Once He visited some city of Bihar, and we all went to see Him. A very large crowd was there to see Baba, and I was a little girl. The crowd pushed me on

to the track, and I fell down. No one was able to see me there. My father was speaking with Baba inside the train. All of a sudden the train started. At that moment my mother saw me. But because she was in a panic, she could not speak or shout. She was numb with shock.

All of a sudden, Baba, who was sitting in the compartment, asked my father, “How is Gayatrii, is she OK?”

My father was surprised. Why was Baba suddenly asking about her?

“Yes, Baba, she is OK,” he said. At that moment the signal came on and the train stopped. When the train stopped, my mother came to her senses and shouted, “Gayatrii is there!” Then someone pulled me out. I don’t remember it very clearly, because I was small. But something happened. My parents tell me that Baba saved my life. The train had already started, and exactly at that time He asked my father about me, and the train stopped for a few seconds, and I was rescued.

Once we had an employee named Gaya, a wrestler, very proud of his muscles. He was initiated by my father, and went to Jamalpur to see Baba. He went for field walk with Baba. They came across a small river, and were wondering how to cross it. He said, “Baba, you can sit on my back, I will cross it.”

As Baba got on his back, He asked, “Well, am I heavy?”

Feeling proud, Gaya said, “No, Baba, you are very light. I can carry anything.”

After going a few steps, he felt that something extremely heavy was on his back. He said, “Baba, you had better get down now.”

Baba started laughing. “Oh, am I heavy?” Wherever there is ego, Baba just finishes it.

### Giridhara

*Warren, Vermont*

In November of 1969, I received a postcard from a friend whom I had not seen in a year and a half and had lost track of. The postcard was mailed from India and it said:

> When the disciple is ready the Master will appear. Shrii Shrii Anandamurti, the possessor of the eight occult powers is the Master. I will be coming to Boston with a teacher of this meditation, please help me set up lectures.

This postcard was from the person who later became Didi Ananda Mitra. She was the roommate of an early girlfriend. In late March of 1970, Didi called me and said she wanted to bring Acarya Vimalananda Avadhuta to Boston and would I help set up lectures? I was happy to do so.

I had never met a real Indian yogi before, and he was an impressive sight in orange robes and turban. He had great charisma and a wonderful smile that radiated light and peace. You could see that there was something special in his face. Didi scheduled me to learn meditation so in the afternoon of April 3, 1970, I went to be initiated.

I was traveling to the West Coast. A big retreat was scheduled at Desolation State Park on Whidbey Island, north of Seattle. There were 96 people registered... most were young students or hippies, though fairly clean as a group. The setting was very beautiful, cabins on a small bay with tall Douglas fir trees climbing the side of a hill. We would sit under the trees with the beautiful smell and terrific view out over the water and he would give philosophy lectures. Once after about an hour and a half lecture in detailed philosophy, Dada said, “You know, philosophy is of no use whatsoever. Its purpose is only to make the intellect content enough to allow you to do your meditation.”

At the end of the retreat, Dada decided to do something that he had not done at all yet in the U.S., to lead a collective *Guru Puja*. I remember feeling very clearly as if a weight had been lifted from my shoulders. I felt light and I distinctly felt that I was walking about two inches above the grass. As we were getting into the car to leave, I

remember seeing the blades of grass with a great clarity and I knew that we were very high from this experience.

In the winter of 1971, we planned a retreat at a house outside of Boston. I came down and there must have been 25 new people that came by early evening. It was a very high and inspiring weekend. On Sunday night, I gave a ride back to New Haven to a friend of mine, Yamuna, and several new margiis from there. Even though I should have been tired, I felt very energized and so did my friend. This particular evening, Yamuna started meditation seven minutes before me. I noted on my watch when I set my timer and started my meditation. I remember hearing her timer go off, but I kept meditating. Suddenly, I felt my mind completely disappear... complete nothingness. Then I felt the universe coming back into creation and I felt tremendous waves of bliss. I was crying and experiencing waves of happiness beyond anything I had ever imagined. I cried and felt this for some time. Then I heard my timer. When Yamuna finished her meditation she had opened her eyes and she later said she saw Baba walk into the room and put His hand on my head.

I did not see Baba, I only experienced the *samadhi*. I believe this to have been *Nirvikalpa Samadhi*. From then on, I knew that Baba was my Guru and that He could take me all the way. That certainty has kept me free from any wavering or difficulty my whole margii career. I experienced this directly and I knew that it was related to the wholehearted and intense service that I had done these three days at the retreat. Yamuna was greatly vibrated and moved by the waves of bliss that I experienced. She got something of a contact high from this.

I think it was 1971 when Dada Yatiishvarananda came to the sector. There were three family *acaryas* here who had been keeping things going – Dada Raghawji, Lalji, and another. There were also enough margiis to keep a structure going.

Dada Y first flew into Montreal and I drove up to meet him at the airport. He was so thin. We were all very impressed with his energy. He was very nervous at first. I noticed something when we meditated with Dada that evening. I felt that as he did some lesson (probably 5<sup>th</sup>) I could feel energy in the room and my own energy go up.

The next day, we returned to Vermont. We had not had a *sannyasi* come to the dome before and we gave him the only bedroom, which was mostly occupied by a large waterbed. I expect that Dada had never seen a waterbed! He closed himself up in the room and did his meditation. We had prepared a dinner, but he never came out and so we eventually went to bed. He had been expecting us to call him for dinner and we had expected him to come out when he was ready. So Dada ended up with no food. He was very hungry and suffered mentally and physically that night, wondering how he was going to eat in this new country.

We arrived at Goddard College in Plainfield, Vermont in the early afternoon. It was a very hot day by Vermont standards and some students were walking around the campus, going to classes, completely naked. We walked onto the campus, and Dada saw this and I could see his mind reeling! So this is America! His mind was completely blown, but he didn’t say anything. We went into the hall where he was to lecture and introduced him. Dada asked us to make a rule that anyone getting initiated would have to have clothes on. Dada initiated many people for several hours that afternoon. One of the sisters really got into the meditation. She moved into the *jagrti* and later wrote beautiful devotional songs. Her name was Nayan. I used to sing one of these songs to my daughter to put her to sleep.

> In the hush of splendid skies our lord comes
> And like a flight of birds his eyes are dancing
> And in thy rhythms oh lord we are dancing, we are dancing
> And like the planets round the sun
> Oh my lord, in thy divine effulgence we are dancing, we are dancing.

### Vasant Vashista &emsp; India

I was initiated in 1964 by my *acarya* Shankarananda Dada, in Rajasthan, in a city called Udaipur, at the residence of Mangal Biharji. I must have been 21 or 22 at that time, and I was not inclined towards spirituality. I wanted to enjoy life.

But I was visiting my mother in Rajasthan, and it so happened that I got my initiation. And after two or three days Baba was coming to Delhi, and so in two or three

days time I got my Personal Contact (PC) in Delhi, for 45 minutes. And after several days, there was a *Dharma Maha Cakra* (DMC) in Jammu. So in a week’s time, I had my initiation, PC, and DMC. Things happened very fast for me, I was not ready. I just followed others around.

Before I entered the PC room, I heard that Baba used to punish people with a stick. I was standing outside the room before I could enter. I was not scared. I went in, and did *sastaung* to Baba. He asked me to remove my shirt, and then told me to get a stick from the corner. I understood now it was my turn. So I gave Him the stick and the moment I gave him the stick, I realized that now He would beat me. I started sobbing, crying like I have never cried before. I just gave Him the stick, and then started crying – I could not control my sobs. Baba just touched the stick to me, and said, “I feel like hitting you a lot but I cannot.” In Hindi He said, “Affection stops me from beating you.”

He could not beat me. And then He gave me a lot of love. There were certain promises He asked of me. And He also asked, “What do you want? Tell me what you want and I’ll give it to you.” He has kept His promises. What I wanted, He has given. Probably I have not come up to His expectations, what He wanted from me. This is what is in my mind.

PC with Baba for 45 minutes was something I can’t forget. After that, the biggest impact came in Jammu. There was not so much rush in those days. My mother said, “Why don’t you go and massage His legs?” It was after the DMC lecture, and Baba was taking rest. Anyone could go in. I went there, and I pressed his legs for 15 or 20 minutes. He was lying down. People said that if you massaged His legs, you could smell the smell of sandalwood, so I came back and told my mother that nothing was happening, there was no scent, nothing like that.

But the next day, the day for departure, all of a sudden something hit me. My *ista mantra* started automatically, and it didn’t stop for days together. Day and night, I made no efforts to do sadhana.

Now, because the *mantra* was going on nonstop – I was trying to stop, it was not stopping, I’m trying to get out of it, I can’t – I went to Delhi. I tried to go to the movies, here, there, but I was not enjoying any of it, because when the *mantra* is going on day and night, nonstop, there is only Baba that you see everywhere and nothing else. And

that’s what happened. Baba gives different experiences to different people, and this thing lasted 15-20 days. Probably it could have lasted forever, but when I came back to Bombay, things went back as they were. I can never forget it. I was always naughty, but still I understood that it was only Baba. Baba Nam Kevalam really meant Baba Nam Kevalam, nothing else.

One time I had the chance to go with Baba in Bombay on field walk. I was really free with Baba. Whatever came in my mind, I said.

“Baba, I have a little question.”

“What?”

“I attended DMC in Kolkata, and you gave darshan and DMC speech in Bengali. Everything was in Bengali, and I didn’t understand anything, because I don’t know Bengali. But still I enjoyed that DMC a lot. Also I attended DMC in Jaipur, and everything was in Hindi. I understood everything but never enjoyed it. Baba, why was it like that?”

Baba said that at the two annual DMCs in Kolkata in May or Anandanagar in December, “People don’t come to listen to my speech. There are many books you can read for that, but they come to enter that circumference, that area vibrated during annual DMCs only. People come and the moment they enter that perimeter they start getting peace, because the place is vibrated. In ordinary dharmacakras, the same vibration is not there. That’s why you enjoyed in Kolkata.”

There used to be annual DMCs in Kolkata. Tiljala was not built at that time. Baba stayed in a hotel, and from there He would go for His evening and morning walks, and His darshans. One senior margii came to know of the timing of Baba’s coming and going. At that time, there were so many people who would just rush to the *pandal* when He was coming out, and you couldn’t really be very close to Him, or see Him, or He see you.

So I got a chance to slip into His room, when He went for his walk, and the VSS (Volunteers in Social Service) didn’t catch me. Once inside, I was waiting for Baba to come back. He was gone for quite some time, and I didn’t know what to do, so I was just playing around with all His things, His glasses, and the jar from which He drank water,

and His seat, sometimes I was doing *sastaunga* there, or doing sadhana. It became dark. I put on the lights, and started doing sadhana in front of Baba’s seat.

All of a sudden, the door opened, and Baba entered. The VSS were behind Him. He said, “Vasant?” I said, “Yes, Baba.” That was just good enough for me. Then the guards put me out. But my work was over. Because at that time we just wanted Baba to look at us. Even if He would just give us a glance, it was good, and if He would talk to us, it was very good. We didn’t want anything more than that. Our visit was successful if He talked or looked at you. Even if the VSS people would say anything to me, I didn’t mind.

### Prakash

*Northhampton, Massachusetts*

I had a spiritual seeking in me, but I didn’t know I was looking for a guru. I met some friends teaching a yoga class at Dartmouth College, started taking the class, and decided to move in with all these other people who were meditating. Just after I moved in, I went to my first retreat, and that’s where I had my first encounter with Baba, in February of 1972. Four or five hundred people showed up at the retreat.

People were telling stories about *kapalik* sadhana – stories of meditating in the graveyard – and something magical happened. In the midst of a snowstorm, with winds howling outside, Madhuri told a story about how Anandamurti got His name.

Once when Baba was young, He attended the wedding of a relative. He got restless and went for a walk in the forest. It was late. A yogi was there, dancing and singing – ‘What is my name, I have no name, I’m on the path.’ Suddenly he sees Baba and asks Him what He is doing there. Baba replies, ‘What you are doing here?’ The yogi is charmed by Baba and answers, ‘I’m on my way to the graveyard to do the sadhana that my guru taught me.’

‘What kind of sadhana is it?’ Baba asks. And the yogi explained this sadhana to Baba.

Baba said, ‘Very interesting, but it isn’t quite right.’ So they sat down in the forest, and Baba taught him, initiating him into *kapalik*. He put the yogi into *samadhi*. Baba was sitting there waiting for this yogi to wake up, and finally He said, ‘I have to go home.’ The yogi, coming to, said, ‘You can’t leave me, I’ve been waiting lifetimes for you!’

Madhuri was telling this story, and she was speaking right from her heart, and suddenly a wave of bliss went through the room, and just burst in my heart. I started crying and crying and I didn’t know what it was, but I felt that I was home. I started to analyze the wave, and as I analyzed it, it stopped, and then I let go, and it would come again. I’d been involved a lot in the anti-war movement, in which you get really carried away and you get pulled out of yourself, but this was something different. It came from within, like an ocean wave comes up and through and bursts out, and I realized that was

my first realization coming from Baba. Something that Didi said about waiting lifetimes and lifetimes resonated with me. I wanted to know where I was going and what this was all about.

About a year after this I went to graduate school in dance at UCLA. I wanted dance to be about spirituality and service and activism, and there wasn’t scope for that at school, for meditation and yoga to be the basis of dance. So finally I dropped out, and went up to the Bay area, and went to LFT training. It was an incredible experience, in March of 1973. By this time I’d been given the name Prakash by Dada Jagadeva, a wonderful man who was very inspiring. My name means light, effulgent like the sun.

The LFT trainers were Madhuri and Giridhara. There were 19 or 20 of us in a very beautiful place in Los Altos Hills, with very intense meditation and stories. We were studying aikido, and psychosynthesis, and we would create dance. We studied the *cakras* and *kosas*, and Madhuri would relate it to Western religion. And she taught us that the way to understand Baba’s ideology is to understand how it relates to the society we live in, in a comparative way. She didn’t teach us things in isolation. It was not just about memorizing Ananda Sutram, but how to relate it to the practical world. We studied Buckminster Fuller, and psychology and the arts, and it gave us confidence that we could go out and teach people about Baba’s philosophy, and relate it to all that we knew, and we could use art and dance.

Eighteen people moving together, getting up early and running, it was incredible. There was a devotional vibration, and wonderful *satsaung*. And we just had this thirst for learning.

I became an LFT. My first posting was amazing, to start an ERAWS (Education, Relief and Welfare) LFT training center in Washington D. C. I went to D. C. for a big demonstration against the Indian government, because Baba was still in jail. There were hundreds of people camped out there. We all stayed in the *jagrti*, with people sleeping on the stairs, camping outside: it was a small property, but a large family. We had done rallies in San Francisco also, meditating in the street. We protested outside the Indian embassy, meditated in the streets, singing kiirtan.

After the demonstrations were over, the training center started. In the mornings I taught people spiritual philosophy, and in the afternoons, Madhuri and Bholanath, the trainers, helped set up internships throughout the D.C. area. We worked with the Black Panthers, in soup kitchens, in schools, doing all sorts of social service. The idea was to train people in the spiritual philosophy and to give them practical experience, and set up social service projects all around the country. I taught the way I had learned from Didi; I had all her notes. That’s when I really learned the ideology. I had to be established in my practices because I was teaching people.

### Manoranjan

*San Francisco, California*

My experiences with Baba go back to before I became involved with Ananda Marga. In this lifetime my spiritual awakening occurred fortunately or unfortunately through drugs. Any time I would use them I would have a spiritual experience. Sometimes my experience would become very confusing – I would have *siddhis*, in terms of telepathy and precognition. It was confusing to me. Sometimes I would become very disoriented.

I began to experience a voice inside. If I listened to the voice, things would become settled, and I would fight my way back again. The voice would say, “You shouldn’t take these things.” And I would say OK. But after some time it would become attractive and I would take it again. And the same disoriented feeling would come – reality would become confusing.

I was going deep in my mind, having profound experiences and not really understanding what was happening. I began to depend on the voice, though I didn’t know what to call it. At first I thought it was my grandfather, as it was a very loving thing. Your father wants you to do certain things, but your grandfather is older, and it’s more an unconditional type of relationship.

So I thought maybe it’s my grandfather, who was dead. Then I thought, “No, this is too heavy, too intense. I don’t know, maybe it’s Jesus.” The voice would always rescue me, and sort of chide me, “You should stop taking these things.”

But drugs were very stimulating to me. I had a spiritual hunger, but I had no direction. In 1970, I decided to travel; I was 19. I met someone in Luxembourg who gave me some LSD, and then I was in Valencia, Spain, and took some of it, and had a very intense experience, very difficult. A soldier was very unpleasant with me with his machine gun. It was quite scary. I was walking back to my hotel and suddenly it was like the world stopped. Everyone on the street – people, cars – stopped.

But inside my head the voice said, “I’ve told you for the last time! Stop taking this!” The voice was giving me a good spanking.

“Do you want me to test you? Should I throw you out in the street? I’ve told you for the last time.”

Then suddenly it all stopped, and cars started going again. I went back to my hotel room, and cried for hours, “I just want to be good boy.”

I was planning to go to Morocco and get into other mischief. But that was kind of a turning point for me. So I went back north and bought a book on yoga in Switzerland. It was mostly about hatha yoga, but it also talked about raja yoga and guru. So I decided to go to India, to see if I could find my guru. So many things happened. So-called coincidences occurred. It was like I was a little ant, and situations guided me this way and that way.

I thought when I got to India everything would be nice and peaceful. Ha! What a joke. Things were very difficult, there was so much cultural clash, and I said, “Well, I’ll go to Ranchi, and if things don’t work out there, I’ll wire for money to fly home.” I went for chai with one of the fellows at the hostel, totally in the bowels of Delhi, a place difficult to find. We’re drinking tea, and someone entered the tea shop, and he knew the fellow I’m with.

He said, “Oh, I just came from the Ananda Marga ashram in Ranchi.” In that moment I had a kind of déjà vu. It was like a stream of images, like a flip book, going through my mind. I couldn’t speak. It was a realization. It was obvious to me, that this was the voice. “OK, take me,” I said. So much time passed while I was in this state, a few minutes.

I took initiation in Delhi at R. Prasad’s house. I don’t remember the *acarya’s* name. I got *nama mantra*. I met Yatiishvarananda on his way to the U. S. Then we went to Ranchi. The week after my *diiksa* was a very intense time. I wanted to have Personal Contact, but I was a brand new margii. In the ashram, Baba’s darshan room was one room in the corner of the school. Mukul and I lived on the stage, behind the curtain. I couldn’t go in for darshan because I hadn’t had PC, but I would go out and sit outside the wall, with Baba on the other side, as close as I could get to Him, even closer than most of the people in the darshan hall. And I would think about Baba being on the other side of this wall.

Pranavananda was Office Secretary at the time. Every day I would go to him and say, “I’d like to get PC.”

He would give me these questions. “Why are you here?”

“Oh, I came to learn yoga.”

“Go back to the U.S., you can learn yoga there!”

So he would give me these tests. But I stayed. One day I came and said I want to have PC. And he said, “OK.” I lit up. And he said, “But first go meditate for 24 hours.”

And I was like, that’s pretty funny! He was smiling, but he’s serious. And I was like, “You’re out of your mind. I can’t even sit still for ten minutes!” [laughs]. Nonetheless I resolved to do the best I could. I went down by the lake and started meditating. I meditated for part of that day, and then the next day some more. At a certain point, I just couldn’t meditate any more. The first part of the meditation I was so angry with him, but by the end I was so grateful to him, because I’d pushed through to a different state of awareness.

I came back, he looked at me and said, “OK, come on.” I had heard from Mukul about his PC. Apparently he had done some unscrupulous things in his life, and Baba beat him with His stick pretty soundly. I was a little nervous, but went in anyway. Baba had me sit in front of Him. He was asking me questions that seemed a little silly. I thought, “Why are you asking? You know everything.”

“Where are you from? Are you a student?”

And I said, “Baba, I’m a student of life!”

And Baba got this look on his face like he’d bitten into the most sour fruit [laughs]. Then He just sort of went on.

“Have you been a good boy?”

Of course, I’m thinking of everything I’d ever done bad. “No, Baba.”

“Do you deserve punishment?”

“Yes, Baba.” He has this little bamboo stick.

“Take off your shirt. Raise your arms.”

Now I was really scared, standing there with my eyes closed, sweat just pouring off me, waiting. And I heard this whooosh, the stick swishing through the air, but it didn’t hit me. Still I had my eyes closed, I was shaking because I was tense. Again, whhooosh.

Still He didn’t hit me. Again. I was thinking, “Oh Jesus, He’s just been taking warm-up swings, He’s going to kill me.” I don’t know what He thought of that. He must have been really amused.

Then He said, “OK, put your shirt back on.” Oh, I must have been a good boy. So that was my punishment, just the suggestion of it. He had me come sit on His lap. He’s looking at me, and says, “Come on, smile!” [laughs]. Baba took His fingers and poked my mouth into a smile. I couldn’t keep looking in His eyes, it was too intense. I was just sitting there in awe. He was talking to me so sweetly.

“Lead a valuable life. Do something for humanity.”

Then He touched me at different points on my chest; I felt like I was a little machine and He was adjusting the dials on me for how I was supposed to go. That was it, a sweet experience.

One night in darshan He was talking about *Parama Purusa* and the permanent abode. He called on me, “Little boy, where are you from, where is your home?”

Immediately I formulated this whole thing to say where I’m from; I was kind of a young hippy kid. I was going to say, Portland, Oregon, United States, North America, Earth, Solar System, the Universe. And I had this whole thing in my mind, so I started out, “Portland, Oregon,” and He cut me off.

“He thinks his home is in Portland, Oregon?”

And then He shot me this look, and the exchange was, you think you’re clever, but don’t be so clever. He didn’t say it out loud, but that was the understanding I had. I knew He knew. It was obvious He was cutting me off.

I went back to India again in 1980. At that time Baba was in Kolkata. All these dadas were asking, “When did you arrive, have you had PC yet?” No, not since I arrived! Still being clever. So all these dadas were trying to get PC for me. I never lied, but thought maybe I could get PC again.

Baba was going for field walks, coming and going from the gardens. But PC wasn’t happening. I always bought flowers and went to Baba’s room in Jodhpur Park and put fresh flowers there every day. Because I knew He knew I was doing this. So every

day I was going to His house, going to darshans. After a week or two, I was getting a little discouraged. One night I was so tired, lying in my hotel bed. It was time to go, but I was so tired.

Then the voice in my head said, “Come on, why not try?” Alright, I got up, took a bath, put on clean clothes, bought a garland, and went to Baba’s house. I was waiting. There was no one there. No margiis, only one dada. I was the only one. Baba came for field walk. So, we went for field walk, Baba and I.

I kind of understood – you don’t get a second personal contact, but... We had kind of an understanding. So we drove out to the lake, and He went for His walk. And an interesting thing happened. We were walking, and a car pulled up. It was mysterious. Why is that car there? The thought came to my mind, that this could be a dangerous situation. I had some proficiency in martial arts. So, I was ready to give my life right then and there, for Baba. This whole drama happened and my response to it was in my mind. In retrospect my understanding was that it was Baba’s gift to me, that He structured this little event. And I connected with a place of courage within myself. There was no thought, just duty. He revealed something of myself to me.

We were really not talking too much. I was giving some thought to becoming an *acarya*, even though I was married. I didn’t want to come right out and ask Him. You ask Him advice, and He won’t give you the answer. But in a roundabout way I asked Him, since I knew there was a training center in Kathmandu.

I said, “Baba, I’m interested to know if Kathmandu is a good place for me?” He looked at me, stopped walking and turned, and said, “The whole world is a good place for you.”

Then we set off walking again. So I was no closer to the answer to my question. We didn’t talk much. I remember the experience with the car, the question, and He using a white towel to wipe His face of perspiration. I couldn’t formulate any questions. It was His gift to me; I didn’t need anything else. It just felt kind of complete. To me it was really special. No one else at Baba’s house? How often does that happen?

In a DMC, I was supposed to take Baba’s photo. It was known I was a photographer, so I was supposed to take Baba’s picture during the darshan. And it was

such a difficult thing. Because all these dadas, half of them were saying, “Come on, take it, take it!” and the other half were saying, “Sit down, sit down!”

Take it, take it! Sit down, sit down! So I was having maximum clash. I was supposed to be taking Baba’s photo, and there were 15,000 people in the tent. I was getting maximum clash, and no matter what, I couldn’t do the right thing. Arrghh! So finally, at the end, I took my photos, and then Baba stood up and did namaskar to the whole crowd. I took a photo, and then He turned around and did namaskar right to me. And my hands jumped. You know how in Zen archery, the whole idea is there’s no mind, no thought, and my hands were like that. The camera was in my hand, Baba did namaskar, and Baba took this photo. I had no thought involved in taking that photo. And the understanding came, Yes, yes, you had so much difficulty, and you persisted. You kept your mind in the right frame. It was like an acknowledgement. Good job, Manoranjan.

I find Baba’s presence in my life now in so many small ways. I think really it’s the small ways that are the most endearing, because I feel so taken care of. He looks after me in the tiniest ways – little things. He takes care of me. Perhaps you could say these things happen in every person’s life. And it’s true. God is in contact with each of His created entities. He has that personal relationship, and those experiences are there for everyone to become aware of. But Baba has helped me to become aware of these things. They may be there for everyone, but not everyone is aware of them. Just silly things, like leaving the house, and out of the blue, a voice comes, don’t forget such and such. Why did it come in my mind? It’s the same as the voice, “I told you to stop taking that.” Baba continues to be present, guiding me in little ways. Big ways too, but it’s actually the little ways that are sweeter. He takes care for even trivial things.

### Dharmaputra

*Ananda Kanan, Missouri*

I was initiated by Dada Vimalananda in a simple little house in San Francisco in 1969.

As I was growing up I remember a constant feeling of frustration. It was like I couldn’t find any truth or reason behind things. I had a lot of trouble with the authority of my parents, teachers and other adults. I’m sure all kids felt this but I was extreme about it and it gave me a lot of trouble. It was mostly through music and the writings of some great thinkers that I first caught some glimpses of universal truths that rang true to me. I realized there was truth in the world but I was having trouble finding it.

I didn’t believe in the God that was being shown to me through church, etc. In fact, I was incredibly angry with this so-called God. I used to curse Him in the privacy of my bathroom, shaking my fist at the ceiling and crying, “How could you create a world like this without any truth that makes sense to me?” I would blame Him for all the troubles I used to get in. I used to ask parents and teachers WHY a lot. And the answer, “Because I said so” or “God will punish you” only made things worse.

When I was 17, I had a drowning experience. I broke the first two rules of body surfing, which was going out alone and during a storm. The waves and swells were monsters, of 10, 20, even 30 feet. Before I realized what was happening the rip tides were carrying me out to sea. I had no choice but to ride the waves directly into the rocks and try to cling on for life. For 45 minutes I struggled with every ounce of my strength against the monster waves. Many times I smashed against the rocks and was flushed up and down in 20 to 30 feet of water like a giant toilet. Then for the hundredth time, as I was under 30 feet of water and was so weak I couldn’t even move my arms, I started to lose consciousness. That would have been the end.

Suddenly everything changed. All the tension and worry disappeared and I forgot who I was, where I was, or that I was in the ocean drowning. I felt a warm, friendly ‘Something’ out in front of me… like a friend or a father welcoming me. I started to merge into a beautiful, warm white light. As I was enjoying the wonderful safety of that light and at the very last possible second I had to remain alive, some invisible force

reached out and stopped the waves. Just as I was about to take my last breath and inhale water, the waves stopped and the water washed back out to sea. I looked down and saw that I was standing on the sand. I somehow crawled up the rocks. Then, the very second I made it to safety the waves came crashing back in full force

In shock and disbelief, I looked around half expecting to see WHO it was who had stopped the waves and saved my life. As if there was someone was up on the cliff watching... with his finger on a wave machine. It was completely obvious to me that there was some Great Force in the world watching at that moment. I felt that the personal hand of God had saved me. Why, I didn’t know. From that moment on I started to think more and more about God and felt that He wasn’t something far off in the sky but somehow, was VERY close to me. It became a very personal thing for me. For many years, I didn’t tell my family. I kept it to myself but used to remember it when I felt sad, alone or confused. It gave me great strength and hope then and still does.

One of the first places I heard the universal truths that I felt were missing from my life was in the words and music of Bob Dylan. To me they opened up a whole new way of thinking and understanding. They helped me to relate to some of the confusion I was feeling. He showed that a person could have deep, moralistic thoughts and feelings, and be cool at the same time. In fact he showed that true understanding and insight was REAL freedom and the coolest thing of all. He certainly wasn’t a goody-goody and his personal life and actions weren’t the best of examples, but in his honest, simple and straightforward style he accomplished a tremendous amount of work and his voice was heard around the world. I feel he helped advance collective consciousness in many ways.

Shortly after that I found some book written by Albert Einstein that really blew me away. Then I came across yoga books by Patanjali and Yogananda and read them and practiced those techniques for about two years. Then I came in contact with Ananda Marga. And I really felt I had gotten close to where I wanted to be. It was there that I first saw with my own eyes a spiritually vibrated person. That was Madhuri, who now is Didi Anandamitra. She had just returned from being with Baba and she was GLOWING with spiritual energy and bliss. It was undeniable. Right after initiation I felt different and in only three days I was able to walk away from and forget all the persistent bad habits that

had been smothering me all my youth. They just disappeared as if they had never happened.

But I still couldn’t accept the idea of a guru. I thought the spiritual path was something I could just read about, and practice and realize on my own. I accepted the fact that some great entity would have to tell me how to do it, but then I was on my own. The idea of another human being that I was supposed to surrender to and put all my faith in was totally against my instincts.

I’d been in Ananda Marga for two years, and a reason came up for me to go to India. Dada Yatiishvarananda needed someone to go for AMURT work in Bangladesh and I thought, “OK, I can go and do this AMURT work and while I am there I can see Baba.” This was late 1970 and things in India were already in chaos.

I attended my first darshan with Baba. I was around a corner in an L-shaped room but I couldn’t really see Him. I could hear him talking and there was all this excitement and people were going into *samadhi*. I felt comfortable and safe, out and away from all the drama around the corner; I wasn’t ready to be that close.

I went for Personal Contact on Christmas Eve Day. There were four or five of us. We had to wait outside this room. We were told to take a bath, and wear clean clothes. We were meditating on the verandah and I felt I had opened and cleared my mind and was trying not to have any preconceived ideas about guru, Baba etc. I was hesitant about the idea of guru but everyone around me was so into it and they were such beautiful and trustworthy people that I thought I should go along with it and see what was there.

As I was sitting there after my meditation some mysterious person walked by carrying a magazine and somehow it just floated down into my hands. I see now it was a cosmic set-up by Baba. It was an article from India Today, in their words “exposing” Shrii Shrii Anandamurti. They were claiming all these horrible things about Baba, saying He captured and kidnapped young people, brainwashed them and turned them into mindless orange zombies. All the fears and doubts I had about Guru completely came to the surface and took over my mind. It was like pouring gasoline onto smoldering coal. I was feeling all this darkness and rage and hate. My whole life I’d always hated the idea of falsehood, from politicians to preachers, always hated the abuse of power. How Baba knew this and brought it to the surface so forcefully is beyond comprehension.

So I was sitting there in mental chaos and I heard my name, “Dharmaputra, GO, it’s your turn.”

I thought, “Oh my God, NO, not NOW. Give me a moment to get my head together.” But they were pushing and pulling “Come on, come on, go, go, go now.” As I was getting up, one brother was coming out of the PC room having just seen Baba with a big smile and watery tears flowing down his face. And I was thinking… “You fool, you idiot, you sissy wimp, don’t you KNOW? How could you be so easily misled?”

At that moment I thought the LAST thing I EVER wanted to become was some blissed-out, spaced-out margii with big silly tears rolling down his cheeks.

I was being pushed into the PC room and my mind was so twisted with anger, hatred and fear that the room appeared as a long tunnel with Baba at the end and I could feel the walls closing in on me. Baba was sitting there. I went through the motions and did *sastaung pranam*. I sat up but couldn’t look at Him. I was thinking in my mind, “If you try ANYTHING, any sly little tricks to control or brainwash me, I’m going to know it and then I’m out of here. I’m not going to let it happen.”

Baba said nothing. He was so calm and composed. Then I felt His hand come over and gently, yet firmly, grab the top of my head. His strong fingers pressed against my skull. There was a vibration and for a second I felt weightless as if I was floating. Then He said some Sanskrit words and with a slight jerk and push He pulled His hand away.

I still had all this doubt in my mind but I finally looked up at Him. He had the most amazing peaceful and beautiful and unassuming smile on His face. And then, with His eyes and a slight movement of His head, He very clearly indicated to me, “Go now.”

I thought, “That’s it? That’s all there is to it? This is what I spent my WHOLE life preparing for? Where is the *samadhi*? Where are the visions of past lives on the wall?” Again, with this beautiful smile He gestured to go out, as if saying, “It’s OK, don’t worry, just go now and don’t be afraid… It’s not over yet”

I left and went by myself to think about what had just happened. And standing there, numb, in that smoky Indian afternoon light just looking out in the distance, slowly an incredible feeling of empowerment and conviction came over me. I realized that very soon I would find out who Baba was. If He really were a corrupt individual I would come

to know it and be able to accept that truth no matter what it meant. In fact I actually wanted to give up the idea of guru once and for all. So much responsibility goes along with having a guru, I thought. Who needs it? The conviction I felt made me feel liberated and free.

“I can do my meditation, my yoga asanas, I know those are good for me, and I will find out for sure just WHO this Baba really was. For SURE I will find out.” I now had a mission. And I thought how to do that was through reading His books. So I figured that I would just do my meditation and read Baba’s books with a calm, clear mind and like a great detective I would look for any signs of evil intentions. And over the next few days whenever I read a page or two of something Baba had written I thought, “That is so good, so wonderful and helpful.”

Then on Dec. 28, as I was traveling on a train to the *Dharma Maha Cakra* being held Varansi, I finished reading a couple of paragraphs in One Human Society and it just struck me. “Wow, this is not only the most far-reaching truth I have ever read, this is way beyond what I thought could ever be put into words… such magnificent, benevolent compassion. No ordinary human being could ever think that far and have such deep understanding of humans and their plight here in this life on this planet.”

I closed my eyes and right away it was as if it was night and I was floating in deep, deep space with stars all around me. I was thinking, “Wow, this is what I’ve ALWAYS wanted to do, to just jump and float into space with all the stars around me on all sides.” It was even more Real than the world as we see it every day and not like a dream at all. It was the most real experience I have ever felt. I could feel the vastness of the void and deep space. It felt so natural and wonderful and refreshing.

And then I noticed this bubble rising out of the void. As if you were looking into the vast night sky and a huge moon was rising. Then I saw the glasses, and the eyes, and a face, and it was Baba’s face. Although my only thought was, “It’s HIM! The greatest, most magnificent entity in the universe!” He was so huge out there. As He was rising up and out of the deep void, He was getting larger and larger. Then as He was about waist high, He started looking around as if He was looking at every individual in creation and

checking on their welfare and progress. I could feel His eyes coming my way and I could tell I was next. I almost felt the urge to hide. It was so powerful but there was nowhere to go. His eyes came upon me and looked straight at and into me. There was an incredible twinkle of Love and Recognition. HE SAW ME! HE KNOWS ME! It was so powerful. I knew I was His and He was mine. My Absolute Forever Father.

He continued to move up and up and grow larger and larger filling the entire universe. My eyes didn’t want to leave His face. It was so attractive and dear to me. Then, as He was rising all I could see was His shoulders, so broad and strong and covered in white loose cloth. Then only His chest was in view, then His waist, then His wonderful, strong hands and I wanted to jump and hold them but by then He was rising faster and faster and I couldn’t grab them. Then all there was, was His legs, then ankles, then His feet… his FEET. “The feet of GOD!” I thought, and wanted to hold on to them. They seemed so incredibly majestic and important to me. Then He popped through some kind of layer and all of a sudden I could hear and see all this noise and confusion. And then somehow, mixed in with all that, were all the rumors and the blah blah blah about Baba.

And the thought occurred to me that it was nothing more than the dust under His Holy feet. His mind and purpose and Mission were so far above and beyond anything anyone could say for or against Him. And He was as unconcerned about the scorn as He was about the praise that people were bestowing on Him.

Then the train jerked and I bumped my head against the rail car and I came back to this world and started laughing and laughing. Baba was so great and almighty that anything anyone could say about Him was just like the dust under His feet. And I had to laugh at the silliness of the thought, that “I” was going to determine WHO this Magnificent Baba really was, and “I” was going to do it with a little pea for a brain!

Then I had to laugh again as I remembered and realized that now here “I” was… a blissed-out, spaced-out margii with big silly tears rolling down my face!

But now I felt a powerful desire to touch Baba’s Holy feet. In the Personal Contact room I had been so close to Him but my mind had been poisoned with confusion. Now I understood that like a Cosmic Homeopathic Doctor, Baba had given me a good dose of the same poison that was already in my mind. So that when I went through the

sickness I could understand, by myself, in my own way, what Guru was to me. What patience and compassion He had, that He could forego looking good to me, for my own benefit. That was so amazing and beyond my human comprehension.

Then we found out that Baba had been arrested and I spent the next four months traveling in India waiting for Him to come out. The thought was constantly in my mind about Guru’s feet. I was being tormented by the thought that I would leave India without seeing Him again. The only thing I wanted to do before I left India was to be in His presence with a clear mind and touch His feet. It’s funny that before my experience the LAST thing I could EVER think of doing would be to touch someone’s feet. It was not even in my culture. It sounded so brainwashed and bad.

Then on my very last day as I was getting ready to leave we were told that Baba would sometimes come out and sit on the verandah at the hospital where He was staying under armed guard. We were allowed to quietly sit below and watch Him as He came out for an hour or so of fresh air.

I was thinking, “Baba, this is my LAST day and you are up there and I am down here, and I so much want to be in your presence, closer to you, and to touch my Guru’s feet before I leave.”

Then some unseen force opened my eyes and I was looking straight at Baba. He quickly turned His head and looked directly at me. And out of nowhere that same unseen force made me stand up and I started walking towards the stairs that lead to Him.

I’m not a brave person for this kind of thing but I somehow managed to walk right up to the guards and I handed them my new ballpoint pen. I was told at one time that American ballpoint pens were greatly valued in India by everyone as in 1971 they were quite rare. Before anyone could say anything I kept walking past the guards and ran up the stairs. While walking down that long hallway to Baba, there was so much hurry and anticipation in my mind that it felt like there were flames all around me. When I made it to Baba I just jumped and prostrated myself into the safety of His shelter and did *sastaung pranam*. Then I did *guru puja*. When I finished I opened my eyes and right there, six inches from my face was His right foot. And I just reached out and held it. Then I touched my head to it. No words could ever describe the incredible softness and velvety texture of His foot. Nothing on this planet could ever come close to that feeling. It was

truly heavenly – from another dimension. Baba had known and fulfilled my wish.

Dada Shivananda was there, and he suggested that I travel with him to South India while I was waiting. I had heard a little bit about him. Another *avadhuta* said, “Do you know whom you’re traveling with? This is Shivananda, who used to meditate for 24 hours a day.” Dada told me a story, about how he was meditating ten hours a day, twelve, fourteen, and each day he’d do more and more. At one point he went into *samadhi* 24 hours a day. Baba knew this, and sent some *acaryas*, and told them how to bring him back to this world, to lay him down and pour cold water on his navel. And they sent him back to Baba, who said, “You’ve done enough meditation, now you have to do service.”

What he was doing at first was just going around and touching people, showing them God. Apparently he did a lot of that in South India, because wherever we went, people were laying down in *sastaung pranam* to him. But he would say, “No, no, Baba is your guru.” Baba told him, “You are confusing people, they need to work for their *samadhi*, so stop that.”

The first time I saw him, he was talking to someone, and he stopped and turned his head and said to me, “Do you remember the time you were…” and he started bringing up these things in my mind. It was like he was going in my mind and pulling up something and bringing it out, and with his intuitive power of observation, he was helping me see it for what it was. He did it for my heavy *samskara* of being a bad boy. I’d been getting into trouble my whole life. Plus, I always felt self-conscious about my height. And those are two things that he brought up and after that they no longer bothered me. Another dada told me later, “he’s not supposed to do that.”

In one town, there was a professor who had been with Baba since the beginning. One room in his house where Baba stayed was now a meditation room. We went in to do sadhana there, and 45 minutes went by, then an hour. I had never really meditated that long up till then. Two hours went by, then two and a half hours and no one was moving and I was thinking, “How am I going to get out of this?”

Right around three hours I slipped into a place out of this world. And it was like I was trying to climb onto the lap of some Great Entity. There was so much love coming

from this entity but at the same time I was feeling all of the things that I had ever done wrong. I could see ALL of them in front of me like one flowing action. This entity was pulling me up but I was feeling so guilty about all the bad things I had done in my past that they were like a big weight holding me back.

He was pulling me up onto His lap with love and giving me a blessing and showering me with the grace of His understanding and saying, “I know why you did all these things. It was because you didn’t know that WE were ONE. You didn’t know that I existed, and that made you feel so bad and angry, that it caused you to rebel against everything else, because in your heart “I” was the most important Goal and without ME, life wasn’t worth anything! So actually, you were always doing RIGHT, by demanding that I show myself to you, or else the world could go to Hell. You are my ‘“most loving son.’””

I was crying, feeling, “How could you ever love me so much?” Everything I’d done was nothing to Him; the only thing that was important to Him was me.

I’d always had a hard relationship with my father because of my bad boy attitude. But the first thing I experienced when I came down was that this was my father was so upset, because he didn’t know. It’s impossible to explain the connection there. But for the first time I understood why my father felt the way he did. When I came back to the U.S. I didn’t have that bad boy complex anymore. And my father was so amazed at how much I had changed.

After the meditation, we went in to eat, and I was so high and in such a different space that the food was heavy for me. I should not have eaten. But they fed us so much food, and I found myself eating two, three courses. The food felt like lead going into me. I instantly felt heavy and sick. I was feeling like I was going to die. I felt so terrible. It was hot. We were sitting around and I heard Dada Shivananda saying to me, “Just do your *mantra*. Whenever you feel any way, just repeat your *mantra*.” And I could hear my *mantra* getting louder in my mind. He repeated this, and it was like my *mantra* was shouting within my mind. And it pulled me right out of my sickness. After five minutes it was completely gone. I felt so light after that, like my food was completely digested.

### Dr. Ram

*Largo, Florida*

I was initiated by Dada Vimalananda in 1969 in St. Louis, and then followed up by Dada Yatiishvarananda-ji afterwards.

Let me say a few words about my Personal Contact (PC). In 1971, I was in St. Louis. We were having a meeting at Dr. Danjoo Ghista’s apartment. Ravi Batra also was there. Danjoo told me he had a dream that I was going to India. I had never thought about going, because I was a new margii, just initiated for a few months. Somehow everything happened so quickly, that within a few days I was in Delhi. From Delhi to Kolkata I had tremendous struggle to get an airline ticket, as the airlines were on strike. On reflection I think Baba put me to many tests, many obstacles on the way to see how much desire or will I had to reach all the way to Kolkata. I was supposed to go to Ranchi and I took a slow night train. In my second-class compartment, there were two youngsters, one from the Philippines, another from France. We were not talking to each other, everyone was afraid to talk, because of visa restrictions, and so forth. Later I found they were going to see Baba also.

Baba was not giving PC until my last of maybe five days in Ranchi. The day before the PC, we had *dharmacakra* in front of Baba. He was sitting at the podium, in a small classroom; 50 or 60 people were there doing kiirtan, which I had never heard before. And I heard later that Baba introduced kiirtan. People were singing Baba Nam Kevalam and jumping up and down; there was no rhythmic movement like there is now. I didn’t know what to do, I tried to imitate the others; no one had explained it to me. After Baba gave darshan, the next day I had PC. Maybe I was expecting too many things in the PC, like many margiis who heard many miraculous things.

So I was slightly disappointed. I don’t want to go into the details, but when I was about to leave the room, He called me back. He was smiling broadly, and said, “You have a question in your mind, whether to stay in India and practice medicine, or go back to America.” He said, “You should go back to America and settle there permanently.”

Baba’s statement was very true. I did have that for a while in my mind. I’d been away from India for five years already. So later my U.S. citizenship became permanent.

Next time I saw Baba physically was in Fiesch. Several margiis from California went in 1978; we made the trip together with Dada Shantabodhananda. Baba was giving PC and people were sitting in line. I was in the line with Kirit, and Baba stopped the line before Kirit. We then followed Baba to Mainz, Germany. We were there a couple of days, Baba giving PC, and the PA told me, you should also sit. I thought he knew I had PC before. Anyway, I sat in the line and my turn came and I went in to see Him. Afterwards, I realized Baba pretended like He never met me before, asking what my name was, etc.

He talked for a long time about the languages of South India, where I came from, Kankoni and Pulu. These are several hundred languages there. Baba had never been to that province. But He knew about the history of those languages, and the script they were using. One was my mother tongue. I spoke Kankoni, a derivative of Sanskrit, totally a Dravidian language, and Baba seemed to know all about those languages. Then He asked me what my hometown, Urapi, was famous for. “A Krsna temple,” I told Baba, but He gently shook His head. Then I thought for a while, and I said, “It’s famous for cooking.” Baba said, “Yes,” and He smiled. Urapi Brahmins were noted for being great cooks. I was single for a long time, and I used to cook for the margiis, as one of my hobbies. I don’t know if Baba was connecting me with that or not.

And I noticed He did not do certain processes that are done in PC, but He blessed me. Then I thought maybe Baba didn’t know I had had it before. But a year later, my brother, who was also a margii, saw Baba in India, and Baba told him he had seen me in Germany. My brother looks quite different from me. Baba knew everything. He said, “You are the second born and got initiation next, and he was the first born and got initiation first.” So Baba would also be pretending sometimes.

Baba loves devotees so much. It’s not possible for devotees really to reciprocate. You always wonder at how little you are able to reciprocate. Sometimes Baba creates circumstances just to please you, and other times He does such great things for you, the devotee, yet He won’t appear as directly responsible for it. And sometimes you realize this years later, but you won’t know it at the time. In that way Baba is a pretender.

I tried to go into medical practice in Palo Alto, but was not successful, so I closed that practice after six months. I had some brand new EKG equipment that I gave to

Kolkata Dada, for Baba. I knew Baba had some abnormality in His electrocardiogram, and I thought perhaps it could be in His house and they could use it to check Him.

When I went to India the following year I heard from Dada that Baba was asking them, “Why you are not doing EKG on me?” every day scolding them. The problem was I did not know the electric cycle was different in Kolkata, and it needed an adaptor. So I went with Dada to the market and went to many shops. Finally we found an adaptor. Finally an EKG was done on Baba with that machine. He wanted me to feel good about it, to make His son happy, His devotee happy. He never had an EKG afterwards, He was making all those scenes just for me.

And another thing, Baba had some eye problems. Dada Shamitananda from Ananda Kanan was sending medicines. I was buying medicines and he was shipping them to Kolkata. Three times we sent eye ointment; that was the request specifically, that Baba needs antibiotic ointment. And the second time I thought this was excessive and harmful to use antibiotic eye ointment, so I wrote a letter and told Shamitananda that it has side effects and it was not a good idea. But why did they not consult an opthalmologist? The next time when Shamitananda called, he said Baba was using so many tubes of it, and to please send more. So I thought maybe Baba had dry conjunctiva which is not uncommon in older age. I included one tube of artificial tears which keeps the eye moist, also in ointment form. And when Baba used this, He said, “I want to use only this one, not any of the others.”

It is strange why Baba waited so long. He knew what was going on, He knew His own people around Him were adding to the problem. I heard the same thing when He had a foot problem; they were going on with homeopathy and naturopathy, and not using allopathy. Finally Baba scolded them, “Call an allopathic physician.” And when he came, the foot was getting gangrene and they had to do minor surgery, and treat a sugar problem also. So Baba lets things happen, roll, to work out naturally. I felt good that He was using continuously the artificial tears, the thing He needed all along. I think we were not really taking care of Baba. Although it was not that easy, because He was playing a great drama also.

### Madhusudan

*Charlotte, North Carolina*

I was initiated in April 1971 in Philadelphia. A lecture was held at an old Quaker church. I went because my roommate who had some contact with margiis told me there would be some realized soul who can answer all your problems. I was having a lot of problems at that time.

I was very opposed to yoga and meditation and used to argue with people about how useless it was. I was in a very contemplative mood, and my roommate said, “Why don’t you come?” and I thought, “Well, why not? I’m not going to join, I’m just going to watch.”

As soon as I walked in the building – the retreat had not even started yet – I was hit by this vibration that I couldn’t understand. It was like going through a door into another universe. All of a sudden you’re in the clouds. It was something very strange. I started talking with margiis, and I was so inspired by them, and thought, “I don’t know who these people are and what they are doing, but they must be doing something right.”

I had been studying philosophy for years in college and I had come up with some new theories of philosophy no one in college could understand, even my professor. And talking to the margiis they were already talking about the same ideas I had.

Here was a whole organization dedicated to the principles I had somehow come up with. I was overwhelmed. But I couldn’t understand any thing about meditation and yoga, it didn’t make any sense to me. I was waiting for this holy man to show up and solve all my problems. I expected a man in robes with a long gray beard. But the acarya was a family man, in a business suit, carrying a briefcase. It was very strange. I didn’t want to do anything. Kiirtan – what was this crazy dance? Meditation, it just gave me a headache. Finally I gave in and went for initiation. I was last in the line. The old margiis were coming for lesson review. They were going in to sit with Ac. Raghava-ji. It was hot and uncomfortable. I didn’t know what I was doing there. Finally I got initiation. That afternoon we went and sat in the graveyard and did meditation, which I thought very strange. The thing that really impressed me was a lecture on *Yama and Niyama*. I thought this was a revolutionary organization; if people followed these principles, capitalism was

going to die. I had been a political activist. They weren’t talking about Prout in those days, but *aparigraha* inspired me.

The retreat was over, and I went back, and got really frustrated with the political movement. I hadn’t gone to *dharmacakra* for two weeks after initiation. The unit secretary called me and said they were having Baba’s birthday celebration. His name was Satiish, now known as Ac. Shambhushivananda. I didn’t really want to go but somehow I went with my roommate to Baba’s birthday celebration. We did kiirtan and meditation all day. I can’t say I was terribly inspired, but something vibrated in me. But I started going regularly. And a strange thing happened. Whenever I started thinking about something, it would happen. I’d be sitting on the couch, thinking I needed to meet somebody and that person would walk in the door. “This is a very unusual thing, this meditation,” I thought.

I graduated, and there was going to be the first sectorial retreat, in Kansas. I wanted to hitchhike across the country, but wasn’t really planning to go to the retreat. I went out on the road, and hitchhiked to Boston, and found the *jagrti*, and some good margiis who brought me to the retreat. Dada Yatiishvarananda was there, and he completely changed my life. He said, “You’re going to India in December; you’re going for teacher training.” What? I don’t want to be a teacher. I don’t even know who Baba is.

Somehow, after the retreat I went to California and lived in the *jagrti* for a couple of months, and somehow in my mind the desire came to go to India. I’m not even sure how I got the money. There was a big plane of margiis going the first week of December, 1971. Most of them were the most established margiis. I was like a baby compared to them. I still didn’t know why I was going to India. But I felt I had to do it.

We arrived in Patna, put our bags down, and it was time for general darshan. Baba came out, and sat down on a nice chair. Who is this man? I was looking, looking.
