In all my travels I had a feeling that Baba was guiding me. But I didn’t know who or what was Baba. So the question I had now was, is this the same Baba that has been guiding me? I was trying to get the vibration and feeling, and I say, “Yes, this is the same Baba.” Kiirtan was going on. Then the kiirtan stopped, and four brothers came out dressed in lungis, carrying blazing torches and daggers. They started doing *tandava*. Dancers ready!

Baba was in a certain posture. During kiirtan He was looking and smiling, and everybody was in ecstasy. As soon as *tandava* started Baba sat in a very serious pose, holding His head up, and looking under His glasses. Ta ta tin ta, Baba Nam Kevalam.

The second day, Baba Himself explained. He called us all up close, 50 people sitting around Baba’s feet. Baba started explaining, “Pull up your lungi or your pants above your knee.” He pointed to the part of the foot for each call instruction, Ta Ta din. The forces of darkness versus the forces of light. Life and death. The skull and the torch. Baba said that in a Western country, you can use a plastic skull, or plastic snake, if you like. In the left hand, the force of death, and in the right hand a weapon. The weapon is not the destroyer of life, but it supports life, it’s dynamic.

We weren’t able to go to morning darshan because Baba said we couldn’t until we got PC. Everyone would have to have lessons reviewed, have first lesson, and memorize *Yama* and *Niyama*, 16 Points, 15 Shiilas and Supreme Command. We’d never heard of some of this before. I was completely clashed. “What is all this stuff? It’s not worth it.” But everyone else was doing it so finally I got in the flow. I got my first lesson from Dada Pranavananda.

One of the evening darshans was remarkable. It was really special. During *tandava* He was His normal serious self, but during kiirtan Baba would look around and search out each margii and do namaskar, one by one. Oh, that was nice. But I didn’t know how I would react if Baba looked at me. I was a little scared. The night before I stood very close to Baba, and Baba asked me, “Did you like the *tandava*?” I was so shocked that Baba would talk to me. To me Baba was something very great, incomprehensible.

Now I started to become tense, agitated. I was watching the daggers and skulls and everything, and thinking, “Who is Baba, really?” Then I saw on the wall above Baba a lizard about a foot long. I had been a little interested in the occult, and the lizard is an occult symbol. And I started to really get afraid. Then the thought came in my mind, like a flash, that Baba is Shiva, come to destroy the world. And I was shaking, terrified.

And just then Baba did namaskar to me and smiled. I don’t know what happened. All I remember was, the answer to that statement came in my mind, “Yes, you’re right, but don’t worry about it, it’s OK.”

And Baba took all the fear, I had always been worried about nuclear destruction and the end of the world, and all that, and Baba took all of that fear out of my mind. And after that I just started crying uncontrollably for the next couple of hours. I was so overwhelmed. I never forgot that lesson. Baba really took something from me that day, like a big burden.

After that I got PC. We finally passed the test. It was *ekadashi* (fasting day), and we were all lined up waiting to go into Baba’s room. And Baba said, “No, let them do their fasting, and the next day they will get PC.” We had never done fasting before. A lot of the practices were there, but nothing was very formalized. We had to learn a lot of new things – *laungota*, all these things. The next day – was it Dec. 15? – brothers were going in and coming out with such bliss on their faces. I was really nervous. I got called and went in. Baba gave PC in the *jagrti*, not His house. I went inside and Baba was reclining on the bed.

He said, “Come here, come here, sit down little boy.” I didn’t want to come so close. I was so nervous, I didn’t know what to do.

Then Baba said, “What is your name, little boy? And who is your father?” Why does He want to know who my father is? It was almost like Baba was talking with Himself, and I was just watching.

I said, “You are my father.”

And Baba said, “Yes, and you are my son,” and He rubbed His hand on my cheeks. I was in some kind of daze. It was the softest thing I had ever felt in my life, almost like wind, not touch.

“Little boy, what do you want to do in this life?”

“I want to do something good, to help people.”

“Do you have the moral courage to do this?”

“Yes, Baba.” I had no idea what moral courage was and why Baba was asking this question. I learned that later. Baba touched me on the forehead. “OK, you can go.”

Was that all? I got up and forgot the door was locked, and it didn’t open. The guard on the other side was unlocking, and I was pulling, and the door opened fast and hit me in the head. I turned around and looked at Baba. And Baba had such a compassionate look on His face. It was so funny, so amazing.

And that was it. It was so simple, but those words come back to me all the time. When you try to do something good, you face so many obstacles, and you need moral courage. Those few words were all I needed.

### Diinabandhu

*Oakland, California*

My name is Dale Brazosky. While I was with Ananda Marga, Baba had given me the name Diinabandhu. In 1969, I took what I thought would be a one-year leave of absence from a PhD program – it was actually close to 20 years before I went back to graduate school. I have taken blessings and initiations in those years. Most of my teachers have been from the Tibetan Buddhist tradition.

In 1970 I was on my way to India and right before I went, somebody invited me to meet Vimalananda, who was an Ananda Marga monk in the United States.

I told him that I was visiting India and asked if I could visit Anandamurti and would he be there? He answered, “Oh, there are so many dadas there in India, yes, go ahead, why not?”

My original impulse to visit India was not to see Anandamurti but I’d had this experience and it was in the back of my mind at least to go visit him while I was there.

So I went and spent a month with a very strong yogi in Ceylon and then was drawn very strongly to Tiruvannamalai, the Ramana Maharshi ashram. When I arrived, one of the first people I met was an English fellow named Philip, and I said, “What are you doing here? How’d you get here?”

And he said, “Well, I just came back from Ranchi where I was with Ananda Marga.”

And I said, “I was thinking of going to Ranchi.”

He said, “Oh, you must go, you must go!”

I arrived at Ranchi, a very pleasant hill station where the headquarters of AM were located. In the meantime, my legs had become infected with boils and when I arrived at Ranchi I could hardly stand. I was in so much pain. And when I arrived, there were probably a hundred or so *avadhutas* or *acaryas* in the yard dancing in expectation of something or other – remember, I’d just arrived – and some monk came to me and said, “What can I do for you?”

And I said, “Well, the first thing you can do is get me a doctor – I can hardly stand.”

And he said, “Oh, don’t worry about that. What are you doing here?”

I said, “I’ve come to meet the teacher, the guru.” And he said, “Well, OK, fine, there’s a good chance you’ll become a full time *sannyasi*…”

And I said, “I don’t know about that!”

And then suddenly all the monks were drawn to one part of the courtyard where there was a short, immaculately white-dressed man, heavy, you could say even a little fat, maybe 5 feet tall or 5’ 2”. The attention was all focused on Him and He kind of swept through the courtyard towards a car, and when He got in the car these hundred or so monks started chanting, “Baba Nam Kevalam” in some sort of ecstasy. Remember, I’m coming from a path of self-enquiry, which did not have outwardly ecstatic manifestations – inwardly, yes. This Anandamurti, as this gentleman turned out to be, could have been a college professor, could have been a government bureaucrat – in appearance – and yet there was an enormous response to Him just walking through the courtyard.

At that point I was given some medical care for my legs and was told what the procedure was to be given personal contact, which was an audience with Anandamurti. I didn’t pay much attention to the classes on philosophy, I think I memorized something. I got a haircut, my hair was long and matted, and they said if you’re going to see Baba you’re going to have to cut your hair and I figured it’s not such a great sacrifice. So I got a haircut and went in for my initial interview, and there was really an aura of great power – also some humor – but mostly a great power, in Anandamurti.

He said, “I can see that you’re very concerned with your personal liberation but I don’t see anything in your past history that shows you want to dedicate yourself to serving others. Even the higher primates take responsibility for taking care of a family, and you only seem concerned with your own *samadhi* and ecstasy. That’s not very good.”

At this point, I wasn’t uncomfortable, there was just kind of a sense of awe. Anandamurti had an enormous field of power around him. He said, “Are you not my son?” And at that point I was in a sort of state of shock – He could’ve said, “Are you a monkey?” and I would’ve said yes, so I said, “Yes, Baba I’m your son.” He touched me

on my forehead and my heart and said something like, “Now you’ll live to serve humanity.” And that was the end of the audience.

After that there was a group audience – the first one I had been allowed to attend because you had to have a personal interview first. And He immediately said, “Stand up, little boy, young man.”

He asked, “Do you have all the powers of *Parama Purusa*?”

And I said, “No.”

He asked, “Do you want all the powers of *Parama Purusa*?”

I said something like, “I don’t know.” And He called me over and said, “Keep your contact with me and put your other hand out.”

Remember this is the first time I’d been to a group audience, and all the audience was focused on me. And He called an Indian practitioner over, Vijay Kumar, and told me, “Keep your contact with me,” and Vijay took my other hand and there was a bolt of energy and Vijay’s eyes rolled back and he fell to the ground in some sort of trance. Baba looked at me and said, “I thought you said you were an ordinary little boy.” He had asked me if I was an ordinary little boy and I had said yes.

He said, “What I just saw was quite extraordinary. Keep your contact with *Parama Purusa*.” Again there was this identification, which is not unusual in *Tantra*, of the teacher with the almighty himself or herself. It was a little foreign to me, but He said, “You keep your contact with *Parama Purusa* and you’ll have all the *siddhis*, all the powers of the universe.” That was the end of that darshan.

That night I spent the whole evening sick – vomiting, diarrhea – I felt like there was some sort of purification. I was so sick I didn’t go to the audience the next day. And I was told that Baba walked in and said, “Where’s that extraordinary little boy?” I’m maybe a foot taller than Baba but everybody around there was a little boy. And He was told, “He was sick all night, Baba,” and He just laughed and dismissed the darshan and left. So that was my first day with Baba.

I didn’t take very seriously the dos and don’ts, the moral statements or the meditation. I was continuing my own practice of enquiry rather than the concentration practices of Ananda Marga. I was drawn to a particular yogi who’s since deceased,

Nityananda, who had been one of the first monks of AM and he told me his story. When he first met Baba, he was doing meditation in the mountains and Baba touched him and he had a vision of many lifetimes meditating in the mountains. Baba told him the reason he was still incarnating was that he didn’t extend himself to others. Baba then touched him and put him into a state of *samadhi* that had evaded him until that time and he became a student and eventually an *avadhuta*. While it was hard to get him to sit down to give teachings, he was the only monk that I was drawn to for instruction, so Nityananda gave me the various lessons.

As time evolved there was a lot of attention from Anandamurti towards me. There were a lot of darshans focused on something to do with me or my inner process and sometimes nobody knew it but Baba. I remember there was time I woke up in the morning and I had a roommate at the time – Vishok, Vikash? – anyway he was a young man from Philadelphia. I just decided that he was one of the stupidest persons I had ever met in my life, and all morning I kept thinking to myself that I came halfway around the world and have to have a roommate who’s a complete idiot. These things arise in the mind when you’re doing practices. I remember brushing my teeth, thinking about this fellow, “Why am I stuck with this roommate?” Doing my meditation, “…stuck with this roommate…”

I didn’t tell anybody. And then at darshan, Anandamurti said, “You know, there are many ways to concentrate. What we want to do is ideate and concentrate on *Parama Purusa*. But occasionally we have other kinds of ideation, like we wake up in the morning and we think, “How stupid is my roommate!” Or we brush our teeth and we think, “What an idiot is my roommate! And all morning we just think what an idiot is…” and I think he even said the name of Vishok. And He looked at me and said, “That is not the way we want to concentrate on our spiritual life!” And I knew he was reflecting on the mental content of the morning.

There was a DMC in Ranchi. The monks had spent 14 days building these giant tents and structures. The first day of the DMC, the first darshan was a very short one, and this is the way it was translated to me. “Sometimes we have to work very hard and then

*Parama Purusa* destroys everything to make us stronger and then we have to start from scratch and work even harder.”

And He left the darshan. It was a warm, sunny day and an English fellow and I took a walk. We wanted to go for a swim at a bathing ghat about a mile away. Before we got to the ghat there was suddenly an enormous storm – one of the worst storms I’d ever seen in my life. We couldn’t even see each other.

We walked back to the grounds and discovered that the two weeks worth of work – building those giant structures, the concession stands, food kitchens – had been totally ripped up by the storm. This was connected to Anandamurti saying that sometimes we work for two weeks or more and everything is destroyed. And He gave the monks 24 hours to restore everything. I’m quite convinced it was His *siddhi*, or power, that, for whatever teaching purposes, created this enormous storm and wind that tore down two weeks worth of work.

There was some trouble at the time. Some townspeople in Ranchi had wanted to attend and had been refused entry because it was, again, the policy that to attend this darshan you had to be already initiated into Ananda Marga – which is not unreasonable. You know, there are a lot of teachings that are just not open to the public, and not just in Ananda Marga. But they came back with a mob of a hundred or two hundred people and started beating people up, pulling them off rickshaws, beating up women, beating up Americans.

I was on a rickshaw with a European or American brother and was saying, “You know, I don’t get this fight for dharma. I came here to be peaceful and we keep hearing that we have to fight to defend against immorality. I don’t accept this, that you have to fight or take physically strong action in your spiritual life.”

We hadn’t heard about the townspeople beating up margiis at this point, but as we were going along we came upon a crowd of margiis saying, “Hide! There’s a group of people beating us up!” So this was relayed to us just after this European or American student had been saying he couldn’t imagine any circumstances in which you would have to physically fight to defend yourself or to defend others.

By the end of that night, the newspapers blamed the tension on Ananda Marga and there were hundreds of people ready to break into Anandamurti’s house. And I guess at that point, the headquarters was moved to Patna. I remember we were woken up at 2 in the morning and given two minutes to dress and gather our belongings before being thrown into taxis and vans and we were moved to Patna from Ranchi.

There was another time, some local, maybe a margii, stole a hundred dollars from me. I was outraged – coming from the other side of the world and having someone steal money from me. And maybe Baba was told, maybe He wasn’t, but in the darshan that day He said, “You know, there are two kinds of wrong in the world. One is doing something you shouldn’t do. Let’s say somebody’s family is starving so he steals a hundred dollars from an American. That’s not good, but the motivation was to take care of one’s family. There’s a much worse crime than that and that’s if you have the capacity to serve others and you say you only care about your own spiritual evolution and you don’t do it.” And He shook his finger at me saying that my crime about not being concerned about changing the conditions that would force somebody, or at least force somebody in their mind, to steal, was a worse crime than to steal.

I didn’t really speak about my roots, but Baba was speaking about the need to fight against injustice and out of the blue He said, “Diinabandhu, why did your grandparents come out of Russia?”

I said, “To escape a repressive Czarist regime.”

He replied, “That is an example of the political inequality and unfairness that Prout and Ananda Marga wish to correct in the world.” He was just revealing to me that He knew why my grandparents left Russia.

There was a time when most of the Americans and Europeans were getting bouts of hepatitis and would have to spend six weeks in bed or in a hospital getting more and more grouchy. Hepatitis somehow affects the liver and makes you irrational and angry. And I suddenly had hepatitis. But I decided that I wasn’t going to spend six weeks lying

in bed and at that night darshan, Anandamurti stopped and suddenly looked at me and said, “Jaundice. Take care of it.”

But as He said it I felt a healing presence, and the next day I was riding around town on a motorcycle. So I felt like He was giving me the energy to not have to spend six weeks lying in bed. I felt that He gave me energy so that I could deal with it myself.

Baba was always very loving towards me. He’d given me the name Diinabandhu and when He would say it he would shake and nod His head and charge up every cell in my body. There were a lot of people there that feared him. He could be very intimidating but I never felt fear with Him. It was almost as if – and I almost hate to say this – it was like all of these outer forms, 16 points of conduct and this and that, it was as if for the two of us, it was theater, it was a joke. And I never took it seriously and He never took it seriously in relation to me. In my sense, it didn’t matter what I did. With His relationship, He was inside of me and I was inside of Him.

I guess a significant thing in my personal history was, one day an Ananda Marga monk asked me if I would write an article for one of their publications. They knew I was trained as an academic philosopher. But I quickly dismissed the idea and told him I didn’t come to India to write and be in my mind – he should find somebody else. I don’t know if the monks told Baba that I wasn’t interested or not. In darshan I would usually sit in the front row right in front of Baba, but this darshan I decided to sit in back. But there were no seats in back, and the only seat was at His feet.

At the time, He looked right at me and said, “It’s good that you don’t use creative gifts to become rich and famous. But if you have a creative gift and you say, ‘I just want to maintain my own *samadhi*, I don’t want to use this creative gift to serve others’ – let’s say one has a creative gift as an intellectual or a philosopher but he doesn’t want to use that gift, only spend time in silence – such a man is worse than a dead man. If you have creative gifts, you have to use them.”

So I started writing. I started working on a thesis I hadn’t finished because I had dropped out of academics. It was on freedom – the relationship of and demand for social justice and inner freedom. I finished it and realized that Ananda Marga, with its mixture

of inner meditation, outer social action, and spiritual economic theory – well, I realized if I had finished my thesis a year before I would have had to invent something similar to Ananda Marga philosophy.

This started me writing a series of articles, most of them in the local Patna paper. Every week they would publish another article about Ananda Marga, meditation and social service – and I wrote those under the name Dale Brazosky. Now this was during a time of great tension, and even a war between India and Pakistan, so it became very popular journalism around the country because they thought Brazosky was a Russian. Russia was a great hero of India at the time, so they said, “Oh, look, a Russian is writing about Ananda Marga!”

At the same time, I was involved with Public Relations because I was out in the media. I was also Baba’s son’s schoolteacher and tutor at the time. So somehow a man named Chopra, a famous photojournalist, contacted me and he wanted to do a story in Bombay Illustrated Weekly about Ananda Marga. My understanding was that it was to be a positive story. So we spent some time giving him background and even arranged for him to take a picture of Baba, which was unheard of at the time. Baba was responsive perhaps because He felt that if I thought it was a good idea, He would do it. There was a picture of me as well, teaching school.

When the article came out, about this time two things happened. The owner of the newspaper who had been publishing these big Sunday articles of Dale Brazosky’s met with me and let me know he was coming under enormous government pressure not to publish anything more favorable about Ananda Marga. He was almost afraid to see me anymore – something had changed. Also, before the Bombay Illustrated Weekly article was published, Chopra let me know that suddenly there was tremendous political pressure on him and the article wasn’t particularly favorable towards Ananda Marga. Bombay Illustrated Weekly was the largest English language magazine in India so millions of people read it. And when the article came out it was really questioning, “Who is this man?” and there was a lot of controversy – not just about me but about Ananda Marga in general. The article was really about Ananda Marga. Suddenly there was a whole political change of tone.

At that point I went to Ranchi to visit friends. I went to the Yoganananda Center there where I had a friend and he told me he had been very worried about me and Ananda Marga. He said that one of the charges against Ananda Marga had been that they had found weapons and explosives in Anandamurti’s house after they left Ranchi several months before. He said, “I was so worried about you, Diinabandhu, being involved with an organization with all these guns and rifles and bombs.” And then he said he met someone in town who told him that the locals had forced him to sneak these weapons into Anandamurti’s house. So he wanted to tell me that he was no longer worried about me but to also let me know that there was some kind of campaign to discredit Ananda Marga.

At a certain point, in October maybe, Baba was thrown in jail. I went to Puri at that time and picked up an English language daily paper and found an article that said something like, “Hypnotized Monks do Guru’s Bidding.” It was the first time that I read something that really portrayed Ananda Marga as some sort of evil cult. It hadn’t really occurred to me, you know. The things that had arisen about arms and explosives in the paper, I had been told that someone admitted planting arms. But it was really shocking how Ananda Marga was being portrayed in the media. There really was a very strong campaign at this point.

I had a couple of darshans with Anandamurti’s wife. She was a very strong, very loving woman. The scuttlebutt within Ananda Marga was that she was always telling monks they needed to meditate more and Anandamurti was always telling them they needed to do more social justice action. And there was this tension between the two of them. Many of the monks were very drawn to Ma and yet they couldn’t fulfill her wishes and Baba’s. One was saying get out there and work – you don’t need so much time for meditation. And the other one was saying meditate more. In Ananda Marga, this was just like a cosmic drama. They were really like one entity.

Politically things were pretty intense at this point. So many stories were circulating: that the government had infiltrated Ananda Marga, and that if there were murders, they had done it to discredit Ananda Marga. Or, that Baba had nothing to do

with any of this, but power-hungry monks had done it. The type of meditation they were given could lead to power before wisdom, and Baba had just let it play out.

At one darshan He said something that related to this. He said, “You know, *Parama Purusa* knows the minds of all beings, and He knows there is the potential in people’s minds to do great evil. But He will not take any action as long as these seeds are just seeds. He has to let things play themselves out.” So that can be interpreted that He knew what was going on, but He wasn’t going to do anything about it, they had to play out.

At this point, I really wasn’t questioning whether it was true or not true. One avadhuta was saying, “I don’t really know if it’s true or not, but Baba has completely won my heart and it doesn’t matter.” Other people were saying that it was true, there was evil in the organization and He was responsible. And other people saying it was all a government plot and Ma’s impurities led her to take part. So it was really a Zen koan. But inside myself, independent of that, I was wondering whether it was time for me to leave.

In one of the last darshans with Baba, I was aware of a tendency in myself to manifest *siddhis* and to stop growing inside. You get certain powers and you play with these powers. Later on I saw that Baba was really speaking to the heart of something I had to deal with. He told me to stand up. “Diinabandhu, do you want *Parama Purusa* or do you want all the occult powers?”

I said, “I want *Parama Purusa*.” He asked me again. I said, “I want *Parama Purusa*.” Then He said, “Everyone come touch Diinabandhu’s feet, because Diinabandhu is a great *siddha* who has all the powers. Don’t you?”

I was sitting there, thinking, “What’s going on?” While He was working with me, Vijay Kumar, whom I could safely say was playing with *siddhis* more than I was – Baba was working him inside out. Vijay was almost crying from whatever Baba was doing with him; He was also working internally with Vijay.

Baba said, “Diinabandhu, forget the occult powers, just merge your mind with the Supreme Lord. Don’t be a fool, just go for oneness with God, don’t be caught with occult powers.”

Then there was a darshan when He turned to me, and He knew my mind, feeling that maybe I was leaving. I felt that something inside of me was untouched, that I had had this lesson in power, and that power was not necessarily compassion and empathy. It’s not His fault, I’m just saying that the *siddhis* and power emphasis were overriding something else I needed to do. And inside, I was thinking, “I think I’m leaving.”

He said “Diinabandhu, my Diinabandhu, always my Diinabandhu.” That was the last time I saw Him. He was put in jail the next day.

And we went and met some of the supposed detractors, one who said he had known for months beforehand that all this was going on. And I said, “Well, if you knew for months, why didn’t you say anything?”

And he said, “You have no idea of the pain I was in.”

I went to see Neem Karoli. I felt I was being brought under Neem Karoli and Anandamayi’s wing. I remember at darshan, everyone was murmuring and pointing at me, that he was with Ananda Marga. I went to Patna to get my things so I could go back to be with Neem Karoli.

In Patna I ran into Tom Barefoot. And while Tom and I were talking, I felt this extraordinary inner darshan that I knew was Anandamurti just filling me inside and out with bliss and presence. And at that point I decided I was staying with Ananda Marga, I didn’t care what these stories were. I planned to come back to America; I wanted nothing to interfere with that relationship with Anandamurti. To not have any more questions. On my way to Delhi, five minutes outside the station of Allahabad all power went out on the train; there was a riot going on. People screaming, punching. I said, “I accept,” and I got off the train in Allahabad. In the time with Neem Karoli I began to establish a relationship with him. And this time when I went to Delhi, it was with the idea that I had left Ananda Marga.

A couple remembrances of teaching were very valuable to me. At one point Baba was speaking about people trying to expand their minds to *Parama Purusa*. And He said very clearly, that was not the way He was suggesting people meditate. Instead of bringing your mind to a point and expanding it, bring your mind to a point and then at that *bindu*

have the awareness that *Parama Purusa* is infinite and totally surrounds you with infinite love and awareness encompasses you. Instead of trying to make yourself so big, make yourself tiny and small and then realize that only God, only *Parama Purusa* is there. That was a very useful darshan. Just being a point and then *Parama Purusa* is naturally there with His grace.

Another time He was speaking of the Buddha. Maybe not the Buddha that I understand, but He remarked once that all these people were coming to the Buddha with all this negativity, and Buddha would say, “It does not affect me.” Then Baba said people come to *Parama Purusa* with all this negativity and He doesn’t say it doesn’t touch me, He consumes it and takes it upon Himself to purify. Actually the role of Buddha is the same. The point is the true guru takes upon Himself the suffering of others rather than remains untouched by it.

My own experience? Baba was a vast guru. I left, I’m not doing His practice. But I will always have respect for Him. He is such a great teacher that what’s fact and what’s fiction in the political history of Ananda Marga is almost irrelevant. You can say, “How can we understand it?” But the point is He had such compassion, and such energy and such power and such clarity that I think it really doesn’t matter. If someone feels relationship in their heart with their guru, and they are continually growing, and their capacity for service is continually growing, that’s all that matters. I just don’t want it construed that I’m saying stay away; that’s not what I’m saying. I left not because of any facts, it was just time for me to move on. The way I was relating in Ananda Marga, the sense of power and ecstasy was the overriding feeling, and it was correct for me to go to another path to deal with it. I don’t have judgments about Ananda Marga. I hope some of the stories I told are of some use.

Moti and Reshma &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Los Angeles, California

Reshma: I saw Baba from the age of twelve. He used to come very often to Delhi. My mother was one of the main cooks for Baba when He came to Delhi. She used to leave us at home to cook for Him. Once my brother was very sick, and she used to leave us at five and go to Baba’s quarters, but come back during the day. She would bring a

little milk, *prasad*, and feed my brother. The neighbors used to say, “Why she is leaving her sick child?” But he became all right. My mother was a very quiet lady, and she never expressed herself to Baba. Once Baba went to the gate, and then came back, as if He had left something in the house. He did namaskar to her, and blessed her. But first Baba asked, “Ask me whatever you want.” And she said nothing.

After these blessings we suffered a lot. In 1975, the Emergency came. My father had to leave Delhi and go to Bombay. When the police couldn’t find my father, they told my mother they would arrest her. I was quite sick, and the doctor advised us to leave the place. So we left for Bombay. After few months, my father was arrested. During that time, we were homeless. We stayed with various uncles or cousins, and met on the weekend. Finally they arrested my mother and took her to Delhi, and my father was in another jail. So, we faced more separation.

At that time the jailer told my mother, “If you sign this letter against Baba, I will release you right away. And I know it’s your daughter’s wedding, and you have to be present.”

And my mother said, “Even if you take my head off right now, I will never sign that paper.”

The jailer said, “What about your daughter’s marriage?”

She said, “Well, if she is going to get married, she will, and if not, she won’t. And I am not going to bother about her marriage.” The jailer became red with anger, and kept her in a very bad condition in the jail.

I got married, and then met my mother in the jail. After four months the Emergency was over, and they were all released.

Actually my mother was a great fighter. She was very bold, and not afraid of anything. The only reason we left during the Emergency was because of me; I was very sick. Otherwise she was willing to fight with all the bullies, and they had no guts to enter her house when she was there. Maybe Baba’s grace was on her. She was quite a fanatic, I would say [laughs]. There were times when I would fight with my mother and say, “Oh, if Baba is God, why do we have to suffer all the time? We’ve only seen suffering.” And she would say, “But He is God, and I can only pray that one day you will get all the things you want in this world.” And believe it or not, I did.

I remember that Baba squeezed us so much. I used to wonder is this right? But you know when you’re totally down, and everything’s finished, suddenly He brings you up. This is what I could never understand, but now I do.

When I was 11 or 12, Baba used to be sitting very nearby. But we used to just look at Him, and think that if He is God, He should have like these extra arms and everything, like in Indian mythology. Everyone was saying He is God, and what was it actually? He looked like such an ordinary man, He can’t be God. And my mother used to say, “You know, He’s the biggest actor. He acts very well.”

Moti: Every year we used to go to Kolkata for DMC. My wife insisted that I should have Personal Contact. Keshavananda told Baba that we had come. And Baba told him, “Moti loves me a lot, and Reshma loves me a lot. And I also love them very much. But they quarrel among themselves very much.” This was the reply that came through Dada. “Do you fight, or not? Baba said, ‘You both fight a lot, and you also both love me very much, and I love both of you.’”

Reshma: I had many problems with Moti. I used to pray to Baba, “It’s because of you, you got me married.” Baba didn’t come to me, but in the room where I used to sleep, I could smell some very good fragrance of flowers. I got up, and there was nothing in the room, and I don’t keep any plants there. I woke Moti and told him Baba was here, and he said, “Oh, you’re sleeping.”

I said, “No, I know this was Baba.” Things then became better between us.

Moti: Since Baba stayed in our house, one dada said the vibration in this house would remain for hundreds of years. So I enjoy going to do sadhana in my Bombay residence, which is as good as Baba’s quarters.

Reshma: Our whole family had a lot of love and respect for our grandmother, Ananda Bharati. [Editor’s note: Didi Ananda Bharatiji became an *acarya* and *avadhutika* after fulfilling her responsibilities as a family person.]

She used to follow one guru, and had prepared herself to follow him her whole life. But suddenly he died, and she became very upset. She did not know what to do. She had gone to stay in her daughter’s house in Udaipur. Mr. Mangal Bihari-ji was there. He happened to tell my aunt – Auntie’s daughter, “I have one saint coming to my house, why don’t you come tomorrow?”

That night my grandmother had a dream of Baba, and dreamt that her previous guru said to her, “He is your actual guru.” She saw one man in orange robe and one man in white. The next day she went to Mangal Bihari’s house and she saw the same dada and she saw Baba. There and then she got initiated. Then she immediately left to become a didi. Then my mother and father and all children took initiation.

I remember Baba used to wear very thick glasses. We never liked those frames, and thought they were too heavy. So Moti said, “Baba should wear very light frames; they will look better.”

Moti made golden glasses for Baba, very nice ones, and took them to Tiljala. Dada said, “He’s not going to wear such glasses,” but Moti said, “No, it is my desire that He wear them once.”

And Baba did wear them, two or three times. So He fulfilled our desire. Whenever we went there we would take some material for His clothes, and give it to the didis. In that way, I felt very happy that something was accepted of ours for Baba.

Once I said I was going to give a garland to Baba. We used to all wait outside for Him to come.

I said, “I am going to do it, even if a VSS guard kicks me on the hand, I don’t care.” You have to be very quick. I was looking everywhere, and as soon as Baba came I went there quickly and gave the garland to Him. Baba spoke something in Bengali, softly, and I couldn’t understand Him. Then I wanted to touch His feet. But before I could touch His feet, the guard came and shooed me away. My *acarya* Ananda Gita said, “You got scared of the VSS; you should’ve just taken the chance and touched Baba’s feet.”

I wouldn’t say He’s my guru, I would say He is my everything: father, mother, friend. I fight with Him, mentally. When I’m happy I’m happy with Him. I talk to Him. He’s like my own family member.

Ashoka Mahesvarii

I was introduced to Ananda Marga in 1964, when I was going into my second year of engineering school, in Pelani. My *acarya* was Shankaranandaji; he’s still my acarya. He used to come to Pelani. There were a number of margiis there, devotees of Baba, who used to tell me about meditation and yoga, how it helps to improve concentration, improve your grades. And that’s what attracted me to it first, improve my grades, and also the exercise part. So I took initiation. I was really not practicing that seriously.

But in December 1964, some of my friends who were margiis decided to take a tour of Bihar. They asked me to join them, and I agreed because I wanted to see Bihar, parts of North India I had not seen. Also in Bihar there was a famine, and they were doing relief work, and I thought this was a good purpose. We took a train and reached Vithya. They did not mention they would be seeing Baba.

Very close to Nepal, about 30 miles, there was a *Dharma Maha Cakra* going on. My friends showed me a few things, how to do *sastaung pranam*, and also reminded me of my *acarya’s* name, since Baba used to ask those things, and they put me in the room. I was expecting Baba to be in orange dress, a *sannyasi*, but to my surprise Baba was a little, chubby, ordinary-looking person. It made me feel at home, because he was a family man. Then He talked to me, asked my *acarya’s* name, where I was from. At that point, He grabbed my ears, and did His usual punishment, that you’ve been a bad boy, but He didn’t punish me much. He put me in His lap. It was such a joy, such a blissful state, I thought, “Here I am with this cosmos, this guy who knows everything about me.”

I asked Baba some question. At that time there was lot of controversy about whether there was life on Mars. NASA was doing a lot of research. So I asked Baba whether there was life anywhere else in the solar system. And He very smartly chose not to respond. He deferred the question, because He wanted us to find out through scientific

means. He certainly knew that there is no life, and now we know that there is no life there at the present time.

Then we toured with Baba, saw the place where He was living and grew up in Jamalpur. Mangal Biharji and myself and another acarya spent a lot of time at the *jagrti* in Jamalpur, a good part of time on Baba’s field trip. Baba was a good family person, taking care of His wife, and His mother, and also taking care of organizational duty. In Jamalpur there was a margii named Bindeshvari-ji. Once he came to see Baba, but on the way to see Him, he passed away. So the other margiis requested Baba to give him life, and Baba reluctantly agreed. This man was in his mid-60s, but after Baba gave him life again, he behaved like a baby. He would laugh hysterically, and do different tricks on people. He would touch people on their forehead, and put them into *samadhi*. He would ask Baba, “Who is the bad boy?” And Baba said, “You are the bad boy.” Baba was kidding him. He was an amazing personality. He had a job but whenever Baba showed up, he would be there, and when Baba would leave, people would hang around him and go into *samadhi*.

I had the good fortune to have two field walks with Baba, and in each I learned something unique. Baba asked me the meaning of my name, Ashoka. There was a great king, Ashoka. Baba said, ‘shok’ means sorrow, and ‘a’ means one who never has sorrow. I never knew that. Also He asked, “What is the capital of Ashoka?” I had forgotten. So Baba asked me to touch His hand. I touched His hand, and all of a sudden, the thought came in my mind that it was Pataliputra, or Patna, the same as became Baba’s capital later.

Baba liked students, and He encouraged them to complete their studies. After this I wanted to become a *sannyasi* too, like many of my colleagues. But my *acarya* and my family discouraged it. They said it was a tough path, that I needed to finish my studies and then decide. I did complete my studies, and meditation helped me. I used to be in the bottom ten percent of my class, and I came into the top five percent in a year or two. So it helped not only spiritually, but also professionally. I saw Baba and took His blessing before coming to the U. S. I have been here since 1971.

I spent a lot of time in Jamalpur with Baba, and also in Ranchi. In Pelani there were about 20 margiis. We would go to see Baba in New Delhi, and the students would be massaging Baba. He used to give special time to students because they were His favorites. Kiirtan was not yet introduced. Baba’s photos were restricted, and were not allowed easily. There’s a famous story from those days. Baba was travelling in a train, and there were photographers who wanted to take Baba’s picture. They couldn’t get a good photo, and got frustrated. So the dadas asked Baba why. Baba explained the photography principle. He said the object which is being photographed must be dimmer than the camera. What was happening was whenever they were trying to take Baba’s photo with a flash, Baba Himself made an even bigger flash, and they couldn’t take the picture. Then Baba started to slowly allow the taking of His photo, limited to devotees.

Baba always spoke about ideology. He didn’t go for His own personal glamour or personal image. He could’ve been a very beautiful personality like Krsna, but He chose to be just a very ordinary-looking person. His mission was to establish more than His personal thing. We see Him as our guru, and may choose to use His photo, that’s an option; but the important thing He gave is the *pratik*, and the system of meditation, which is scientific. It was very appealing to the engineering students, as it was so scientific. And not only did He put old wine in new bottles, but He gave a lot of new things. *Pratik*, *tandava*, *kaoshikii*, Renaissance Universal club, all introduced as we were growing up. We thought this might be the end, but Baba kept giving until the day He passed away. Prabhat Samgiit passed number 5000.

His whole life was phenomenal. If you look at His teaching, here is a great personality who could be winning the Nobel Prize, if you put all His wealth together, but He kept Himself very quiet. He came quietly into this world, and He went very quietly. But He created an enormous spiritual wave, and spiritual people following Him all over world. Baba used to kid, when it was a small organization, He used to say how Ananda Marga would spread, with acaryas from all over world, from all backgrounds and religions. We didn’t believe Him. But Ananda Marga had already spread around the world by the time Baba went into prison, and it spread even faster when He was in prison.

There are so many beautiful experiences of Baba. Just today I had one. While having my lesson revision, it took me back to Baba’s lap again, the same thing that happened to me when I was 17. Now I’m 54. It put me in a beautiful state. Also, I had pain on the left side, and that disappeared. I was feeling immense bliss today.

Baba always kept a low profile, no show or pomp. He wanted people to grow spiritually; that was the most important thing. Other things will follow, service to suffering humanity will bring liberation of the self. I find tremendous energy from the dadas and didis, and margiis, who are the most beautiful people. Even my wife, who was not a margii, has become a more devoted margii than I by reading Baba’s books and meeting acaryas. We need to do more and more social service and Baba’s work.

### Nandinii

*Tucson, Arizona*

To my recollection, Ananda Marga started in the U. S. in 1969, when the U. S. was in incredible political upheaval. It was also a time when a lot of people were starting to think about the metaphysical world. There were many people in the counterculture, antiwar, and hippie movements. They could not understand some of the traditional trappings of their churches. I think about 600 people were initiated in a short time in Carbondale. *Dharmacakra* was so large that some people had to wait in the hallway. Lots of university people, nontraditional, and some traditional people came. Many of us were teaching classes and getting very involved. It was so big back then that we couldn’t anticipate how many people would show up at DC.

I was initiated Oct. 31, 1969, while living in Carbondale, Illinois. I was living with some friends, and we had wanted to learn how to meditate and get into metaphysical things for a long time. It was Halloween, and my friend suggested we go for a walk. There were people running around with costumes, and it was a little bizarre.

There were four of us walking down the street. We came upon this house with nice green grass with an Indian man sitting in the yard. He called us over and asked if we wanted to learn how to become yogis, and we thought, sure, since we weren’t doing anything else that evening. We went inside the house and there was a dada dressed in orange. He said that he had just come from India and was initiating people all over the U. S. He asked if we would like to learn how to meditate.

We each went with him individually into the room, and got our *mantras* and learned a little about Ananda Marga. Then we sat around and talked, and he said that we should meditate every morning and evening for 30 minutes. Dada came the following Sunday with the Indian man we had met, who turned out to be Ravi Batra, and we had group meditation. There were just six of us. Then we started to have larger meditations, up to 150 people some times.

One night we were at Didi Ananda Mitra’s house (before she became a didi), and Dada Vimalananda said that we were going to meditate for four hours. Since we had been margiis for such a short time we thought maybe it was normal. So we sat down and

meditated for four hours, and when we came out of meditation we sang songs, talked and had a meal. We started to meditate a least a couple of hours a day instead of half an hour. After some time we mentioned it to Dada, and he said, “Oh, half an hour twice a day is enough. I just wanted to meditate for four hours that night.” Dada initiated so many people that we had to rent a gymnasium at the university for *dharmacakras*.

It stayed that large for some time, and many dadas and didis came. Until that time Ananda Marga was very loosely woven, and then began to be more formalized. We were happy to see that. We were also happy to see that dadas and didis were traveling because we got to see them more regularly. And AM continued to grow into the 1980s when we established the national retreat site in Ananda Kanan. Then in the early ’90s there weren’t quite as many people. I always think that even though there are not as many people who stayed in Ananda Marga, the people who have stayed are the people who are really hardcore and truly believe in Baba and His work and His mission. I believe that will continue throughout the new millenium. The people who really love Baba will continue to come.

At the last summer retreat I sat next to a teenage girl. I asked her how she came to AM. She said that she was at the university and went to an Ananda Marga class given by a dada.

“How did it seem to you?” I asked.

She said, “I just had to have more. There is something for every aspect of my being in AM.” She said that Prout, Women’s Welfare Department, service projects, meditation, asanas, “all made perfect sense. It was just what I was supposed to do.” I thought it was beautiful because I didn’t know how different people see AM now – I know it has changed a great deal. I was greatly affirmed by seeing this young person joining Ananda Marga.

### Guru Gopal Singh

*Bernardsville, New Jersey*

I was initiated by Ac. Maheshvariji, who was working for the Bihar Electricity Board at Dhanbad office. It was August 1963 when I took initiation. I went to Jamalpur for Baba’s darshan. We stayed there for three days and it was very exciting to be with Baba. I had Personal Contact at least three times and field walk with Baba all three days. The happiness and the enjoyment were too great to describe. After the first PC I was feeling intense happiness and as if I was in a totally different world.

After our return from Jamalpur I started a new life. All my weaknesses vanished. My biggest problems were fear, lack of self-confidence and getting nervous during exams. These problems vanished and I got tremendous self-confidence with the feeling that the impossible does not exist. After a few months I went to Jamalpur for the *Tattvika* class given by Ac. Dasharathji. I was so lucky that most of the time Baba was teaching our classes. My writing power was very fast and I took all the notes from Baba. I use to go for *pracar* work every weekend and evening with friends.

In 1964 and 1965 I had opportunities to go to Ananda Nagar several times, and also to Jamalpur. Baba was always kind to give PCs all the time. He discussed a lot about Buddha, Netaji and world history. His description of Krishna was so lively, as if it was happening in front of Him.

I had lost my father in 1962 when I was only 17 years old, but after meeting Baba in my first PC when He took me in His lap, I just felt, “I am sitting in my father’s lap.”

Baba has said so many things that it is difficult to write. My ambition is to see every human being living happily, meeting all the basic needs. I do not want to see poverty and hunger. Only Baba will guide us to achieve this goal.

# CHAPTER TWO:
## COME CLOSER, STILL CLOSER

> “That which makes the mind soft and strong and strenuous, so that it may keep itself in a balanced state even in the condition of pain, that which perpetually creates a pleasant feeling within, is called love. Devotion is identical with love. The moment devotion is aroused, the love of God comes.”

Ananda Marga’s growth surged in New York Sector in the early 1970s. Service projects, cooperatives, and yoga and meditation classes sprang up in many parts of the sector. But in India, controversy swirled around the Ananda Marga organization. There, the movement’s revolutionary merger of spirituality and social justice apparently threatened the Indian government. Internal tensions also threatened the organization’s cohesion. Baba was imprisoned, as were thousands of Indian margiis. It appeared to be a dark period. And yet from His jail cell, with patience and extraordinary fortitude, Baba continued to run the organization, which went underground in India, but which in many other countries flourished. Protests against Baba’s unjust imprisonment galvanized support in New York Sector. And margiis began traveling to India to visit Baba in prison, often facing persecution from the Indian authorities. It was a unique time, one that challenged margiis to hold firmly to their ideology. As depicted in this chapter’s stories, Baba often demonstrated who was really running the show.

### Bharat

*San Antonio, Texas*

In 1972, I was in Atlanta, married for the first time, but my wife and I were separated. I was dating one girl who was interested in psychics, and spiritual things. We drove to Florida on my birthday and ended up seeing a psychic there. I was not into anything to do with meditation; I was into having a party. My girlfriend wanted to talk to this lady psychic.

I said, “I don’t want to hear about it, it’s all fake.”

But she went in to see the psychic, who said to her, “I don’t want to talk to you, I want to talk to him.”

I said, “Oh, you just want my money.”

But I went inside, and she looked at my palm, and asked me a few questions.

She said, “You know, you have a terrible fear of death. I see the number two. In two years, two months, and two days, you will die.”

“This is all fake,” I thought.

“But it won’t be the death you imagine,” she went on. “What will happen is, the person that you are now is going to die. And that’s what I’m supposed to tell you.”

After this, the army was going to send me to Wichita for training. I called the colonel and said, “I’m a single man, I don’t want to go to Wichita. I want to go some place where I can have a good time.”

So they sent me to Thailand. After a month, I decided I wanted to live off-base. I found a beautiful house on the ocean, and I bought an old car. I had learned to play the guitar, so I would sit and play music by the sea. One night, a GI said there’s somebody at the base giving a lecture on yoga. It was Dada Pinakpani, now Pranakrsnananda, giving a lecture. I was not at all interested in any of it, but I went.

There were military guys at the lecture, and everyone was drinking and smoking. The thing that impressed me was, none of it fazed Dada. He continued his talk. And for some reason, I became interested. After the lecture, he came to my house, and I became initiated into Ananda Marga. At that time, Baba was in jail. When some of the *acaryas* got out of jail, they would come to Thailand. I had this stream of *acaryas* coming to my

house. We would play music, and go to Bangkok and do social service. We worked in the slums of Klong Toey [crying], and that’s where I got interested.

When my military time was over – I was married again by then – I went to India. At that time, Baba was in jail. I went into the jail, and Baba said, “Develop your full mental, physical and spiritual potentialities” [crying]. And He said, “Tell Liila to do her duty properly and faithfully” [crying]. And that was on July 4, 1974, and it was exactly what the psychic had said: two years, two months and two days. So that was a pretty amazing story.

When I went to see Baba another time, in prison, around 1976, He had been poisoned. After that, he fasted for many years. All He had daily was a glass of Ovaltine. I was really curious, as a physician. How does anybody survive? So I went to the jail with Arjuna from L.A. We went to see the jail doctor. You would think that Baba would have some spiritual kind of doctor, who would say all sorts of great things, because he’s had this contact everyday with the guru. We went to see the doctor, and it’s exactly the opposite, he’s got a cigarette in his mouth, he’s got a drink in his hand. He asks us, “Do you want some whiskey?”

And we said, “Hey, what’s going on? This is crazy. Here this guru is writing songs, fasting, doing all this stuff in jail, and what is this guy doing?” So Arjuna put on his yoga hat and started telling the doctor, “You know, alcohol really brings you down, and Baba can teach you meditation…”

The man said, “Every morning I go into Baba’s room, sit down, and He says that I am liberated. My soul is liberated, and I do not have to do anything. I can smoke, I can drink. He says, ‘You’re a liberated soul.’”

The doctor continued. “One night I was drinking a lot and they came and said, ‘Baba’s calling you.’” His house was next to the jail. “So I went to the cell and Baba was gone. Where could He go? And I looked on the wall and I saw Shiva dancing, doing the *tandava* dance. Baba was gone. The next day, Baba was there again.”

“Every time I drink I see Shiva,” the doctor said. “Baba’s not my guru. I tell everyone that Shiva is my guru. He helps me.”

But then he said, “I go and talk to Baba for an hour or so, and then I go to the market, and I meet Ramananda and I tell him everything that Baba said, and then he tells me things to tell Baba, and I go back and tell him.”

This was Baba’s drama of how to get information in and out. He was in prison, and He was still running the organization. His Personal Assistant was meeting this jail doctor, and the jail doctor was never suspected of having anything to do with Ananda Marga because he’s an alcoholic, and he’s smoking, and he says Shiva’s his guru, not Baba. So this whole time this drama was going on and nobody knows. Later I learned that this jail doctor stopped drinking and is now very devoted and spiritual.

The interesting thing is that Baba’s eyes were sparkling, and He grabbed my wrist very tight, and said, “My legs are weak, but my heart is very strong.” You knew this was just a game for Him to fast. He was really thin. I think what happened was after that he developed diabetes. With starvation you can develop diabetes. I think the fasting contributed to that, there is evidence of starvation precipitating that. Somebody’s told me since that this jail doctor is now very devoted and spiritual, that he changed.

I saw Baba again at Lake Gardens in 1979. Around that time my time in the military was ending. I didn’t know whether I should continue in the military or get out. So I went to Lake Gardens, and they were doing personal contact with Baba. Somehow, I ended up seeing Baba. I asked Him, “What should I do? Should I leave the military?”

All the dadas said, “Hey, don’t ask Baba those questions. That’s a stupid thing” [laughs].

Baba said, “In 48 days you’ll know the answer.”

Everyone said, “What’s He talking about?”

So I gave namaskar, and I left. I was on temporary duty in the Philippines. I used to go to sister Nillie and Roshan’s house, in Mandalay City. We would do meditation, and then eat. That particular night, I had a very nice meditation, and I sat at the table to do a little more before the meal. And my mind just went. Out. I’d never felt any experience like that before. They laid me on the sofa and for 30-45 minutes, I was gone. As soon as I started coming around, I would say, “Clinic, clinic.”

“What are you talking about?”

“Clinic?” I don’t know, I was just saying clinic.

“What happened?”

“Great meditation.”

It was some kind of *samadhi* or something. I just felt wonderful, like I was flying. I started thinking that maybe Baba was telling me I needed to leave the military and start some clinic. So I moved to San Antonio, and in the poor part of the city, built a clinic for dialysis. And really I think that clinic has been a highlight of my ability to keep supporting a lot of programs. It was very successful. In some way, this was Baba’s way of telling me to start a clinic.

Another time we came through Nepal. I wanted my children to see Baba. One of my children had gotten lost in the train station; we realized he wasn’t with us, and had to go back and find him. He was just standing there. They were good travelers. Later I got off the bus and stepped into a mudhole up to my waist. We finally made it to Kolkata, and when we got there, everyone said, “Baba’s leaving, Baba’s leaving.” His car was ready. Then someone said, “Baba said get Bharat up here quick.” I’d just gotten there! So I went and He said, “Africa is in great economic depression. You must continue to assist them. They are not the dark continent because of the color of their skin, but because they are in darkness.” This is when we started programs in Burkina Faso, in the early ’80s.

### Malati

*Scottsdale, Arizona*

In the early ’70s I was active in a little Congregational Church in White Plains, and some margiis came there doing *pracar*. They cooked a vegetarian meal, and gave a presentation. I felt a connection to them, partly because I was trained in anthropology, and one of them had majored in that. They invited me to a *dharmacakra* in New York. Someone asked me if I would like to learn meditation, and I asked them what was involved, and they said not very much, it was free. So I was initiated, and I don’t remember the name of the *acarya*. Jitendra-ji became the *acarya* I connected with.

I wanted to meet Baba, and I felt I was always connected not only with Baba, but a higher power through Him. I guess I’m one of the people who has made an effort to keep my Christian religious connection. I started out in a Quaker school, which introduced me to sitting quietly, but later I was in contact with more traditional Christianity. I had a friend in India who was a margii, who went to see Baba. When Baba saw him He made the sign of the cross. I was glad to hear that.

Through the Episcopal Church I was later connected with, I met an Indian parapsychologist named K Das. She knew about Ananda Marga and invited me to go to India, and part of her idea was to wean me away from AM. Her family was outside Kolkata, a Methodist Hindu family. The son had been told by a psychic that he was going to meet an American woman, and that was going to change his whole way of life. They kind of adopted me as a family member. I wanted to see Baba in the jail in Patna, and the son accompanied me. He was not a margii, but accepted that the teachings were relevant to him. He had been used to drinking a lot, and he did change his lifestyle, but when we reached Patna I think some of the margiis thought I had fallen into the hands of a CBI agent, as he looked like that. But he was very thoughtful.

The whole jail had become like an ashram, and most of the jailers had become devotees. Sisters had to go in in pairs. I was paired with a Norwegian sister named Arunima. When we saw Baba, I felt in His presence a very great sort of acceptance. There were some things I felt I hadn’t handled very well with this Indian family, and it seemed as if nothing mattered to Him. I had been told that there was one question that

was best to ask Baba, and that was ‘What is the best way to increase one’s devotion?’ I knew you could only see Baba every two weeks, and had to decide what to do in between. The margiis were helping at the site of some flooding. There were a lot of choices to make.

Anyway, when I saw Baba I asked that question, and Baba said there were two ways of doing service; one internal service through meditation, and the other external service. Baba looked from me to Arunima, and addressed each of us. He said to me, “You have so many questions, and you haven’t asked any of them, and I have answered them all.” And I realized what He said about the two parts of service gave me a clue about how to decide what to do.

Arunima asked Him about her relationship to the head of Prout in Norway, who she was working with. It was a question of marriage, and she asked Baba what He thought. And Baba said, “I am in favor of all good things.”

Then He turned to me and said, “When you think about marriage you should ask yourself, ‘Do I feel like cooking, and cooking, and cooking?’ And then of course there will be children.”

And I thought to myself, I was in my early ’50s, and of course there won’t be children. I thought, “Baba, you know my age, you know everything, OK.” I found that very useful. What He had said relieved me, in terms of relationships, It wasn’t that I was to say, “Do I really love this person, but just, do I feel like cooking?” [laughs]. And I heard later that the following week He met with a group of American margii women, and told them that what they needed was to be liberated from the kitchen. And that reinforced this message. Those were the things He said that most impressed me. I realized that He did know what I was thinking.

I did see Baba again, at Ananda Nagar. It was great to be with all these devotees in this beautiful but less-developed place. At the end of DMC, thousands of us were walking down to the railroad. And at checkpoints people were asking your name. And someone said, “You didn’t give them your name, did you?” Yes, I did. Later as I was about to leave India, the immigration called for a “Mr. Mudd” and pulled me off the plane, and I had an interesting experience. But I felt no fear, and felt that the guidance of the Supreme was with me. And from the time I was initiated I always felt that if I needed

human contact, the right person would come in my life, whether it was a child, or an old person. Things seemed to flow in a happier and gentler way.

One experience led to a dream in which Baba appeared. I had been teaching at a Mohawk school (Way of the True Human Being). I was on the tribal welfare committee for Ananda Marga, when Master Units were being developed. I fell into being able to get some land in New York which now is owned partly by AM, and the rest by the Mohawk Nation. A lot of margiis felt a connection with the land there, on the Canadian border, near St. Regis Reservation. It reminded them of Ananda Nagar, and had a special quality. I had a dream that I was sitting in the house, which had been rebuilt, and there were other margiis there. A knock came on the door, and in came this man. He had a Sikh turban on His head, and it fell off, and I recognized Him as Baba. I still feel that that place will blossom as a Women’s Welfare Department Master Unit.

Recently I’ve heard some things about Kirlian photography. Vishvamitra had been back to Jamaica eight years after Baba visited there, and knew the place where Baba was sitting, and photographed it. There was a great circle of light around that place, which is how he had seen it when Baba was there. He suggested that when a great personage was there, there would be positive *microvita* around [the smallest entity uniting spirit and matter, according to Baba]. I suppose this could be part of the explanation of these pictures.

I’m very grateful for how meditation and social service has transformed my life. I think that the heightening of awareness about the importance of consciousness, and experiencing the positive effects of the spiritual practices, as well as social transformation, is so clearly needed in our society. I do keep in touch with people in the Catholic Worker movement, and I sometimes feel if they had more inkling of the philosophy of social change Baba has given us, it would heighten the impact.

### Arjuna

*Los Angeles, California*

I was initiated on August 8, 1971, by Ac. Yatiishvarananda Avt. The day I took initiation there were 45 other people in line. It was in the door and out. The whole process took about two minutes at the most. But Baba took care of me, and I’ve survived. The first experience I had with Baba was at a retreat in November ’71. At the end of it, I was still thinking ‘Is this Ananda Marga for me?’ We had just finished the final *akhanda kiirtan*, and I was kind of staring into space, thinking. I had this thought in my mind, “Baba, give me a sign that I belong here.” At that second I was staring at the piano, and all of a sudden one note on the piano pressed down. That was my first experience that Baba was there.

I went to a second retreat after that, in the spring. After the final *akhanda kiirtan*, again I was just sitting there, not thinking about anything in particular. But there was a guitar next to me, and as I stared at the guitar, it plucked a note. This is just a very simple story, but it shows how in the simplest ways Baba can show you that there’s more to this world than what you actually see.

I had been in AM for two years. I was doing my sadhana each day, going to *dharmacakra* (DC) each week, but keeping my distance from being drawn in too much. One thing I was very adverse to was having a Sanskrit name. I just didn’t want to go that far. One day at DC at the *jagrti* in Santa Monica I was doing sadhana, and somehow Baba came to me and called me Arjuna, or somehow that name came. The following week Dadaji Jagadeva – he was a dada from Holland – came to the *jagrti*, and he wanted to have a consultation with me, so we talked after DC, and he said, ”You have to have a name now. I want to give you third lesson and I also want to give you a name.” Then the thought came in my mind he was going to give the name Arjuna to me because that’s what Baba had said to me. And as soon as he sat down with me, he asked me “Do you want your name now?” and I said fine. It was Arjuna.

I decided to go see Baba in prison in India, in July, 1977. The first time I tried to see Him there was a lot of red tape involved. We had to go to Bankipur jail and give them our passports. They said to come back in a week and we would probably be able to see Baba. In one week, Dr. Bharat had just flown in from Thailand, and we were told we would see Baba together. I had met Didi Ananda Mitra the evening before. She said that when you see Baba, especially the first time, you have to do everything perfectly, bathe properly, do 16 Points perfectly, wear a clean shirt.

In the morning I got up and had a nice clean blue shirt on and white pants and we left the Patna *jagrti* and bought garlands. It turned out that it was a fasting day. I had heard from other people who had seen Baba in jail that He always gives you *prasad* in jail. So this big clash came in my mind – should I accept and eat the food on a fast day, even if it’s from Baba? There was no one there to guide me, but internally I just made the decision in sadhana that if Baba gave *prasad* to me, I would say thank you and just keep it in my pocket, and I would be strict on this day.

We went in to the jail cell, and this was the first time I had seen Baba. He was lying on His cot. As soon as we came in, He hopped up. He looked so thin. In the only picture we had of Baba in those days, He was a strong healthy person. But now he reminded me of somebody that looked like Gandhi. It was extremely hot there. We were led in by the warden and there were one or two CBI people in the jail there too, and they were taking down our passport info.

The first thing we did was *sastaung pranam*, and Baba called us over and we went right up to Him. He started to put His hand on my neck, and was massaging my neck and higher *cakras*. At first we looked deeply into each other’s eyes, and then He told me to close my eyes. After I closed my eyes He said, “Open your mouth.” So I did that, and He just grabbed one of these Indian sweets and forced the whole thing in my mouth. When I opened my eyes up He was laughing, because He knew what I had been thinking. So this was Baba as the jokester. Something that I had taken so seriously He made a big joke about like it wasn’t that important. The guru decides what you’re going to do.

Then He spoke with Bharat some, and He told me what my duty was. He said it was to seek out support from and unite the *sadvipras* of the world, so they can help the

suffering humanity. I felt really positive about that. I had felt my main purpose in AM was *dharma pracar* and I was actively teaching.

I asked Him about my relationship with my wife. We had gone on separate vacations, so I asked Him what I should do with my marriage, and He just stared into space. He didn’t answer. Then I thought, “OK, I’ll rephrase the question.”

I said, “Baba, there are a lot of margiis in New York Sector who love you so much, but they are struggling so much in their marriages. What can you say to those people who are in family life in New York Sector to help keep their marriages together?”

He thought again, and looked out into space again, and then He answered in a strong way. He said that marriage is an all-round fight, and how can we build a world and a society if we can’t keep our small societies together? He wasn’t in a joking mood here, He was very serious. Now whenever I see or talk to family people who are struggling, this is the first thing I tell them, that this is what Baba said; if we want to build a universal family, then we have to fight to keep our small family together.

Then we asked Him the question, “Baba, how can you have been fasting all these years in prison? How do you do it and still stay strong?” And Baba said that through a yogic technique, He can extract the energy from the air and the sunlight. Then He made a muscle with His arm and said, “If the rest of the world would fast like I am doing, there wouldn’t be a food crisis in the world” [laughs]. There He was back joking around.

We had garlanded Him when we first came in, and He gave us the garlands and also a jar of *prasad*. Until this day, I have kept the garland and the shirt and the *prasad*. They took us to the warden’s office and I had this thought in my mind – I had seen this very nice man but it really hadn’t hit me, who I’d seen. I was always trained as an intellectual, and it hadn’t really dawned on me. So as the warden and the CBI people were questioning us, I just closed my eyes there, and for the first time in my life, I had an experience of pure white light, total peace, just absorption in a whole different world. It’s a thought I think of every day.

That was the deepest spiritual experience I’ve ever had, sitting there, closing my eyes. I had that feeling for several minutes, and could not believe I was having that experience. I actually consciously tried to think of something negative, but that positive feeling had so taken over my mind I couldn’t even will my mind to think any doubt, any

negative feeling. That was quite an experience for me. Then the warden started talking about his experiences with Baba, as he saw we were very inspired. He said that although he was not a margii per se, he’d watched Baba in jail every day over all these years, and one night, he went into Baba’s cell, and Baba wasn’t there on the bed. Instead, all he could see was Baba doing *tandava* on the ceiling.

I went to Nepal for three weeks, and when I returned to Patna, I had the strongest desire to be alone with Baba in jail. On August 14, I went to the jail, but all of a sudden I felt sicker in the stomach than I had ever felt in my life. I was sweating profusely outside the jail and I could barely move. And I was cramping terribly. I wandered around looking for a bathroom, and suddenly I stumbled into the jail warden’s home outside the prison. He remembered me and let me use his bathroom, and then, feeling a bit better, I went back to the *jagrti*.

The next day was August 15, Indian Independence Day, and Dadaji Ramananda said that Baba would only be seeing Indian margiis that day after 8 a.m. I was feeling the urgency to see Him, since my ticket back to the States was in only a few days. Feeling much stronger I went to Bankipur Jail at 7:30 in the morning. No one was outside and I simply went up to the front gate asking if I could see my Guru, Shrii Shrii Anandamurtiji. The guard nodded his head and said come right in as if Baba was expecting me and there was no problem. This time they didn’t even check my passport but just brought me directly to Baba’s cell. It was an unbelievable feeling of being guided by Baba.

This second time I saw Baba in Bankipur jail, I remember being asked by my Sectorial Secretary to ask Him certain questions about how He felt about homosexuality. I said, “There are a number of gay margiis in New York Sector who love you a lot. And want to know how you feel about homosexuality.”

Baba paused, and He spoke these words. He said, “If you eat too much you’ll be fat, if you sleep too much you’ll be lethargic. If you bathe too much, you’ll get pneumonia. And if you have too much sex, it will create psycho-physical disparallelism.” (I’m not sure if He said psycho-physical or psycho-spiritual). “The goal is for the

microcosm to merge into the macrocosm. Do what is natural in your life. Search inside the deepest core of your heart and you’ll find the answer. Do what is natural.”

When I told this to the *acaryas* afterwards, they said, “Well, this is Baba’s way of saying homosexuality is unnatural, because Baba wouldn’t be the type of guru to say something is unnatural, he would just say do what is natural.” But then when I explained what Baba said to the gay margiis here, they all felt Baba was talking to them and saying that what they were doing is natural.

At that time there was a party in India called Janata Party. There was one man, an old freedom fighter named J.P. Narayan who was being asked to take charge of that party. He was on kidney dialysis. I asked Baba what He thought of this person, and of the party. Baba said basically it was old wine in a new bottle. So we shouldn’t pay them much attention. Then He said, “Don’t be like an owl. All these people who criticize Ananda Marga are afraid to see the light. They live in the darkness, just like an owl. They only want to come out at night.”

Towards the end, Baba was massaging my neck and head. I asked him if I could massage His feet. He said, “If you like.” I was massaging His feet, and closing my eyes and trying to merge, and at the end I put His feet up to my *ajina cakra*. Then He said, “That’s enough.” I did *sastaung pranam*, and went out.

### Devinistha

*Northhampton, Massachusetts*

I was initiated in December 1971. I first met Baba in jail, in December ’77. I went with Cirasmita and Kalyanii and Cirasmita’s daughter, who was 13 months old at the time.

We went to Patna, and we thought we’d pretend we were tourists, but of course no tourists go to Patna. We got a room in a hotel and didn’t tell anyone we were with Ananda Marga. The next morning we told a rickshaw driver we wanted to go to Bankipur jail, and all down the street we could hear, “Bankipur jail, Bankipur jail.” Everyone was saying, “They’re margiis, they’re margiis.”

So we went to the jail, and they told us we had to get permission to get in. At the office, they took our application and said, “We don’t know when you’ll get approved.” Every day we went back and asked if we got approved, and they always said, no. Finally we started getting pushy, and we didn’t understand that there was any system, we just knew we only had a certain amount of time in India, and we wanted to see Baba. So we started staying there, we said we would not leave until we saw Him, and we started doing kiirtan all day long.
