The officer would try to meet with people but we would not leave. Finally he told us our approval was there, and we picked it up and we went to the jail. And they wouldn’t let us in. There was a crowd of people there, the man at the gate didn’t understand English very well, and he wouldn’t let us in. We didn’t know what to do. Suddenly, Yatish Coley, who is now Dada Shambushivananda, walked out of the crowd and said, “Give me your passes, I’ll get you in.” He took our passes, went up to the guard, and we were let in. We turned around to thank him, and he was already gone.

Inside the jail we had to wait a long time. It was hot and there were other people also waiting there. Finally they started organizing people to go in; I got paired up with a sister from Europe. At that time Baba couldn’t see very well, and it seemed like He was focusing on her. She was talking with Him, and I was sitting back and thinking, “Well, eventually He will talk to me. I’m just going to look at Baba and see how He looks, and memorize His face so I can remember it in my meditation.”

So she talked to Baba, and all the time Baba talked about how important Prout was, and how we should be Proutists, and how if Prout did not achieve its goals, then Ananda Marga could not continue to exist. He talked about how if people did not have food and clothing, then they would not be able to do meditation. I had not had a chance to eat that day, and I had fasted the day before, but I was thinking, “I wonder if Baba will notice that I hadn’t eaten.”

At one point He turned and said, “If you do not take food, how can you do sadhana?” [laughs]. He was just talking about Prout, and yet it was the only thing He directed towards me, so it was interesting.

Then the guard started saying it was time for us to go, and I thought, “I thought Baba was going to talk to me but He only talked to her.” Baba had given us *prasad* at the beginning, hard candy, and I was curious to know whether He would touch a sister. He put His hands on mine and opened them up, and then He just rested His hands on mine. So I knew, “Oh, Baba’s not afraid to touch me, Baba feels comfortable.” We did *guru puja*, and at the end Baba touched each of us on our forehead, and that was the end of my first visit with Baba, it probably lasted about ten minutes.

As soon as we got out of the jail, we immediately applied for passes to see Baba again. Then I went to DMS in Delhi. We came back on an overnight train and got back to the hotel and someone said we had passes waiting for us. We hardly had time to change our clothes before we rushed off to get our passes.

This time the three of us went in together. Baba was very open this time. At the beginning He said, “Tell me your story.” And it was like an opportunity for each of us to ask Him a question or tell Him something. Kalyanii started to tell Him some good news, and Baba shook His head, like, “This is not what I’m looking for.”

He turned to Cirasmita, and she said, “Baba, I just want to love more,” and Baba nodded His head, then turned to Kalyanii, who said, “Baba, I just want more bliss,” and Baba nodded His head, and turned to me. The night before I had been sitting in a hotel room, and put a table in the room, and was doing kiirtan round and round, until I finally fell over in bliss on my bed. And I had been wanting to ask Baba for a new name, and Baba had said to me internally, “When you merge in me, your name is Purusa.” I had

gotten the answer to my question, which was, when you merge in Him, names don’t matter. But I could think of nothing else to ask Him, so I said, “I want a new name.”

He nodded His head as if to say that was as important as love and bliss. And I thought to myself, “They want love and bliss, and I want a name?” So then He was laying down on His side, and put His head on his arms, and He was making these sounds, like, “Hmmm,” and we’re sitting there, our hearts opening up so much because Baba’s making these sweet sounds like a baby. Then He opened up His eyes and turned to each of us, and turned to Cirasmita and said, “Do you know *pranayama*?” and she said, “Yes,” and He nodded His head.

He turned to Kalyanii and asked, “Do you know how to do *japa*?”

“Yes, Baba,” and He nodded.

He turned to me and said, “You will get a new name at the proper time.” Then Kalyanii asked Baba what women could do in labor to reduce the pain. And He talked about the different *cakras*, and how if you had pain in your leg, then the lower *cakras* are where you concentrate; as you go up the body, the higher the pain, the higher the *cakra* you concentrate on. But, for childbirth, He said, the woman should concentrate on *ajina cakra*, or better yet, *ista cakra*. A couple of weeks later He gave a birth meditation for sisters in labor.

I asked a question about Women’s Welfare Department (WWD). At that time we were trying to get WWD going in the States. “It’s very confusing in the States. People think we will have separate dharmacakras, that the brothers and sisters will be divided.”

Baba said, “Of course there will be adjustment according to time, place and person. WWD is simply an organization to serve the needs of women, and women have special needs in society.” He seemed to refer to women who were abused by their husbands as people who could get help from WWD. Then He said WWD could help men, and referred to the men who abused women as those who could also get help. And He said that while WWD could take help from men – men could help in their projects – that WWD should take no direction from them, should decide their own direction. He made clear that how WWD worked in the U.S. or other parts of the world might be different than India. And again we got to do *guru puja*, and then we were ushered out.

I want to tell a story about my early time in AM. After I began back at college, my meditation began to be very hard for me, a struggle. One of the dadas, a Filipino named Dada Jiitendra, was coming through. But Dada was very busy initiating people. The only time he could get was when he was taking food just before he left town. So he set me down at the table, and turned to me and the first thing he said to me was, “How is your meditation?”

“Well, not that good, Dada.”

“You must sit for at least half an hour each time.” I was like, right, I can’t even sit for fifteen minutes.

And he said, “Why don’t you come with me to Vancouver?” He was driving from Bellingham to Vancouver, about a two-hour drive. We were driving in the car together, and it broke down.

And Dada said, “Well, this often happens to me. Even planes sometimes don’t get off the ground, because if I don’t do my meditation, things will break down, and I’ll have my chance to do meditation. Let’s all go and do meditation.” So we went and sat down in the grass, and I had no problem with my meditation, I was feeling blissful. And then someone came back and said, “The car’s working,” and we all went back to the car and drove off again. After that I had no problem sitting for half an hour. That meditation on the side of the hill sort of cured my problem of being too restless to do meditation.

Another time, I was an LFT, and we were riding in a step van. I was sitting behind the front seats, on the floor, there were two lanes going each way, and a signal ahead, which was green. We were going 40-50 m.p.h. and suddenly a car pulled in and stopped right in the middle of the roadway, blocking both lanes. Our driver slammed on the brake and skidded past the other car into the turning lane. We were all yelling “Baba, Baba, Baba!” And the car stopped and where the dashboard had been completely covered with things – a timer, keys, and a Baba photo – after we stopped everything from there flew off except the Baba picture. It was not tied down or anything, but it stayed there.

Another time I was riding a bicycle, coming down the road and a car was coming towards me. It started to turn and would have hit me, and I pulled into the wrong lane. An old man was driving and still he didn’t see me. I was falling off the bike. And I said,

“Baba!” And as soon as I said, “Baba,” the car stopped. Another time I was riding a bike and this dog came running out, barking. I was really scared and I shouted “Baba!” and the dog went running away. These are several of my little individual Baba stories, about when I called on Him in my life.

Shiva Genosoa, New York

My first meeting with Baba occurred indirectly, in a way that might be difficult for many to comprehend. It was September of 1972, when I was in the last phase of completing my doctoral thesis at the University of Minnesota. Quite unexpectedly, I enrolled in a yoga class, and I was not only surprised but amazed to find how relaxing and satisfying the class was for me. I attended each yoga class eagerly, feeling that the yoga was becoming a capstone experience for my doctoral education.

By December, I was initiated and in full swing. I decided to give back what I had received. I would simply teach a yoga class; nothing else, no guru stuff, which had little appeal for me and my imagined students. I knew very little about Baba at that time, except that He was in prison. Yet I also felt that I needed to say something about Baba, even though I did not personally know Him, for I knew, even then, that He was the force behind my instruction and initiation. I had been told that Baba had advised margiis not to talk about Him but rather to talk about dharma instead – advice that I found refreshing, since the world seemed to be awash with gurus in Rolls Royces who were only too happy to talk about themselves.

So it seemed even more fitting to say something about a man who said, “Don’t talk about me.” I decided to make my statement about Baba without words. I made a small wooden lattice, slung it over my shoulders, and wrote a sign inside the lattice (which was meant to represent prison bars): “BABA IS IN PRISON.” I wore this lattice and sign wherever I went, and I only took it off when I bathed and went to sleep. The effect of this nonverbal communication was surprising. Many people asked, when they saw the sign, “Who is Baba?”

After I made a brief reply – something to the effect that He was a spiritual teacher who was in prison because He opposed the policies of Indira Gandhi – many people expressed concern and wished him well. Others, who took a little more time with me,

commented, “This is quite interesting. Your teacher is saying, perhaps to us all, that the only place for innocence in our corrupt world is jail. Your teacher is quite something!” “Yes,” I would respond, all the while realizing that I did not know the half of it.

So this was my first meeting with Baba: what I call “lower case” Baba. I introduced Baba to people on the street; they introduced Baba to me. For me, even at that time, Baba was universal. “He” was everywhere – no magic guru stuff. My parents taught me as much, only their word for it was God. So I did something for Baba, and something was done for me. Though indirect, it was sufficient. Since I thought I never needed a spiritual teacher, I felt nurtured by the comments I heard, as well as by the many sights and sounds around me. These included the golden sunsets that illuminated the steel-grey skies of the Minnesota winters, the envelope of silence and reflection that accompanied my student days, and the faith that all things would work out, though I did not know how or why. I was to live four years with this understanding of Baba. I felt that I didn’t need anything more, though I was curious who this man in India was.

My next meeting with Baba was what I called “upper case” Baba. I met this form of Baba in an extraordinary meditation experience. After a lovely kiirtan with several brothers and sisters who were also learning yoga in the Minneapolis *jagrti*, I sat down to meditate. Soon all kinds of strange and wonderful sensations accompanied my meditation. To put it simply, it felt as if the sun were bursting in my heart. This experience lasted for some time, and I was quite overcome by it, as were my brothers and sisters, who were unable to ignore my involuntary cries, and who were also somehow profoundly moved by it.

This was my first meeting with this form of Baba – pure and simple; direct, not indirect; inside, not outside, through report or comment from others. In the days that followed, I began to realize that Guru is enlightened ecstasy, and resides within. This stage of realization was also sufficient for me. I felt I didn’t need anything more. But that created another question. Who was this “little brown man in India” whom I had never met physically? What was the connection between what I felt, “upper case Baba,” and the historical personality who lived far away, in a country I never dreamed of visiting?

I decided to work for Baba’s Mission in 1974. For four years, I worked and experienced an enormous amount of turmoil. Death and rebirth were frequent

occurrences; they were as regular as the seasons. Before I was to meet Baba physically, I was to meet Him again, in deep sadness, in an experience I call “dream” Baba. What set this in motion was a painful divorce. My former wife left both our spiritual path and our marriage, and my life, I felt, was shattered (though it had actually been liberated in a way that I could not understand). I suffered for months, not understanding why I was in such pain. Many of my friends were surprised, as if this were something that I should be able to take in stride. Only my family realized how deeply hurt and disoriented I was.

One night, as I was dreaming, I saw a man who looked like Baba. The dream figure turned and began to talk to me. He reminded me of the path I had walked in former lifetimes. He showed me many masters who had guided me in previous lives. As He called their names, in languages that were strange to me, I saw their luminous and exquisitely beautiful faces appear before me, and I recognized them and was breathless at this miraculous sight. They were so beautiful! Their names were strangely familiar, though I did not have the impression that I had ever heard of them or read about them. Then Baba said to me, “It is time for you to come to me. You must stop grieving and move.”

I woke up in a sweat, profoundly altered. I had already planned to go to Taiwan to study acupuncture. Incredulous, I thought that this might be the language of my unconscious encouraging me to follow through with my plan. The distance between me and that little brown man in India was closing. A few short months later, after traveling across our lovely land – from the Eastern Appalachians, to the Rockies, Arizona, the Grand Canyon, and the welcoming coasts and beaches of California – I found myself on a flight to Taiwan. The acupuncture adventure fizzled out – it was too impractical for my age and time schedule. Then surprising news came: Baba had just been released from prison.

I was encouraged to go see him, and I decided to make the attempt, even though many margiis had been stopped by hostile Indian immigration officers. A few days later, I stepped down on the tarmac of Dum Dum, Kolkata’s airport, and I immediately sensed that this would be friendly ground for me. As chance would have it, I met Dada Vijayananda on the transfer bus from the airport and traveled with him to attend my first darshan with Baba that evening. I will never forget my first impression of the physical

form of Baba – the external Baba, the little man in India about whom I had heard so much. My immediate impressions of him were twofold: first, I sensed that He could be very tough, a kind of wrestler, a person who could and would endure; second, I sensed that He was almost impenetrable, too difficult for me to fathom – very deep, perhaps even cagey – but not dangerous, just uncanny. I had never met anyone like this in my life.

I soon realized, as I heard Him talk, surrounded by so many devotees, that I would never be able to really know Him as a person, the way so many of the Indian margiis had. So I settled for what I felt was my next best option: to get to know Him through the dadas and didis who apparently knew Him well – or at least knew Him better than I. It was a good strategy, in retrospect, for three things were working at once for me: lower case Baba, upper case Baba, and just plain Baba, this strange little man sitting in front of me, the real hyphen who was playing a funny game that was somehow beyond me. In my personal contact with Him, I discovered Him to be like silk: I sat in front of Him, and I felt like a caveman looking blindly into the light. He was full of all kinds of jokes, corrections, and tenderness. I felt very comfortable in His presence, which is something like the silence that envelopes you now as you read these words.

I decided to follow Baba around India as he made a three-month tour – Kolkata, Delhi, Bombay, Madras. I made His schedule mine, and my visa seemed to agree with the plan. I learned so much from this journey, filled as it was with Baba’s steady presence, which buffered the many jolts and shocks of the road, the normal deprivations, illnesses, and disappointments that accompany all travel. I also had the helpful advice of a few senior dadas, who looked after me as if I were their younger brother. Eventually, at the end of the journey, I was expelled from India by vigilant Indian authorities, who were also playing their game, which was actually quite unpleasant. What had happened in all of this was a deepening and unfolding of experience that time would turn into a treasure, once I had the opportunity to sift through everything and integrate it more fully. This treasure, I found, would enable me to understand and overcome the many challenges that lay ahead.

Without the experience of what I call triple Baba (lower case, upper case, and dream case, with “just plain” thrown in for extra measure – the proof of grace, if you will), who knows where I would be today, if I even would be today? This is impossible to

answer. But as I sum it up, I return to something quite simple: my cup has continually been filled with more, though I have not sought more. Not once, but three times – and then again, for good measure. It reminds me of something my first astrology teacher taught me: everything important in the birth chart is revealed in at least three ways. Take it from there – and my best wishes to you! Dharma leads to guru. Be true to what you know, what is true will come to know you.

### Guhan

*Englewood, Colorado*

My first spiritual experience was in the year 1966 or 1967. I was about 20 years old and attending junior college near the Los Altos *jagrti*. I was caught up in the habits of my culture, i.e., smoking, drinking, dating, recreational drugs etc. I’d had a falling out with my Catholic upbringing and at this time I was not practicing any religion or spiritual discipline.

One evening I was in my room, a small concrete basement in a modest home. As I sat on the edge of the bed, I felt myself falling into a kind of trance. My head and senses seemed to melt away like candle wax and I found myself in my heart *cakra*. It was a glowing white color, and I was in a wonderful state of bliss. There was a person, a man, sitting in my heart *cakra* opposite me and some distance away. As I looked at him, I noticed waves of energy emanating from his body and coming towards me. And then I noticed his shoes. He was wearing penny-loafers and for some reason I kept looking at his shoes, at his feet.

I have no idea of time in this experience, as it was beyond time but as the experience started to fade, I felt anxious and upset because I realized I was coming back to the earthly plane. And then in the next moment, I realized I had to come back and finish my work, due to *samskara*. So here I was, back on the earthly plane, reality. The bliss was gone. I was saying to myself, “What was that?” And I realized I could not share this experience with anyone, not my mother, not my friends. They would not understand or would think I was crazy.

Fast forward now about four or five years. I was living in the Oregon woods. Dharmaputra, my high school friend, was living in the Palo Alto *jagrti* and invited me down to see a yoga monk from India. So I packed up all my stuff, arrived in the Bay area, was initiated by Dada Yatiishvarananda and moved into the *jagrti* with DP and other margiis.

After several weeks of living there, one day I found myself all alone in the *dharmacakra* room. I was standing at the mantle of the fireplace near a *pratik* and picture of Baba. All of a sudden, I looked at Baba’s picture and said, “Oh, my God! There you

are!” It was the same person who had been in my heart *cakra* several years before. I turned and looked back and in a flash I saw the whole arc of my life and I thought, “Who is doing this? It’s not me. I may think I am but someone else is.” And I realized that God was doing everything!

I was a margii living in the Palo Alto *jagrti* near Stanford from 1971 on. Years went by. Many margiis traveled to India to see Baba or try to see Him, as He was in jail. You could fly to India from the USA for $450 at that time on Air India. I was content and felt my time would come to meet Him. And it did. It was spring of 1978. After finishing some work, I found myself alone in New York in March. I arranged a flight to Bombay. Upon arrival there and after going through the initial culture shock of India, I proceeded to Patna where Baba was incarcerated in the Bankipore jail. I met Asutosha from Canada there and several other margiis trying to see Baba in the prison. I learned that we had to receive permission from the district magistrate to visit Baba, so I proceeded to his office in Patna. This Indian government official was not at all happy to see me.

“Why do you foreigners come so far to see a criminal?” he said. I kept quiet. After he was through venting, I told him I would like to visit Baba in the jail and I also would like permission for Asutosha. He told me to leave our passports and come back on Tuesday. This was on Friday. I thought it strange that in the midst of deportations, harassments and persecution of Ananda Marga and its leader and members, I could just walk right up to a district magistrate, declare myself a margii and ask to see Baba, a prisoner, without being arrested myself! And even stranger, when I returned to his office on Tuesday, he gave me two permits to see Baba, one for myself and one for Asutosha.

In great anticipation, we headed to the jail to see Baba. When we arrived there, the prison superintendent and the guards were even more upset with us than the district magistrate. Our presence infuriated them! They told us point blank that we could not see Baba. They put us in a room and told us to wait. After some time, they came and led us to Baba’s cell. My heart was in my throat! I was nervous. I was finally going to see my guru. They led us to the cloth door of the cell. I found myself in front, and didn’t want to be in front. I hesitated for a moment and then pulled back the curtain and entered Baba’s cell.

He was lying on a cot looking directly toward me and giving namaskar. As I walked toward Him with a garland in hand, a clear ancient voice in my mind said, “Oh my God, He is in the human form!”

I reached Him and placed a garland around Him and Asutosha did the same. We did namaskar and then Asutosha spoke first saying, “Baba, I want to massage your feet.” Baba said OK and Asutosha moved to the end of the cot and started to give Him a foot massage. All of a sudden, I found myself face to face with my guru. Baba reached out and touched my cheek and gazed into my eyes for a few moments. I could only match His gaze for a short time and then I closed my eyes. It was so special.

He then asked our names and our duties. When I said my duty was VSS, He said, “You are a very small boy with a very big job to do.”

Then He rolled over on the cot to His other side and glanced out the small cell window and said, “Didi Uma, Dada Gagan and Dada Lokesh, they are the flame of my New Year’s *vanii*.”

Then He rolled back and faced us again and said, “Prout is the need of the hour. He reached out and touched my cheek again and gazed into my eyes. I could only hold His glance for a short time and then I closed my eyes again. He invited us to say something. I had two questions.

“Baba,” I asked, “is Subash Chandra Bose still alive?”

He glanced around the cell and looked at the CBI agent sitting in the corner and then back at me and said, “Now is not the proper time to answer that question.” Then He laughed and in His hearty laughter, I got the answer!

Then I asked Him about the origin of the swastika, a symbol which is very controversial in the West. Baba said, “Lord Shiva created the swastika.” Using His hands and fingers, He described the different aspects and meaning of it. Our time with Him came to a close. The most beautiful time for me was when Baba said, “Now you do *guru puja*.” Baba sat in a certain *mudra* or posture. We did it, and Baba received it. Then He said we must go now, go back and do our work. As we were leaving Baba was giving *prasad*, this sweet candy, this rock candy, just too much of it. It was falling all around, too much of it, my two hands couldn’t hold it all. When I returned to my hotel room and I

was all alone again, I was so happy. I felt I was one of the luckiest people in the whole world!

Then I thought – the candy! I was looking in my pockets, saying, “Where is it? He gave me all this rock candy.” Finally, I picked out a little handful. I thought to myself, “Oh God, that was Baba’s vibration.” He gave me candy, and I thought it was all this candy, but it was Baba! His vibration. It was just a little bit of candy! [laughs].

### Gayatrii

*Highland Heights, Kentucky*

In 1969 I was initiated and began to read His books. Even to read the words in His books – the words of His discourses – even to see those words put me into an enthralled state, a state of intoxication, and a state of divine excitement, of spiritual ecstasy. Why? Because, I could feel, they were the words of GOD. GOD is who? Generator, Operator, Destroyer. He is all these things.

In October 1971, I waited outside on the tarmac of the airport in Cochin, Kerala, India, for the plane to arrive which was carrying my Lord. It was the first time to see Him in real physical form. Waiting and watching, waiting and watching – and lo, He appeared at the open door of the plane. Slowly, He came down the steps. Then He walked, quickly, doing namaskar as He moved. Inexplicably, I also moved, and was running silently behind Him, keeping up with Him, unbearably attracted to a Stranger who was also my Greatest Love. As no others ran, I stopped. Then a kindly brother told me, “Go ahead, run.” So I ran still faster to catch Him – that Elusive One, that Glorious One, in simple white cotton clothes and black spectacles. But – He moved faster! In the next moment, He was sitting in silence, hands folded in namaskar.

One by one we filed past the Most Illustrious One, most humble, most sweet love of my heart. As I passed, I gazed long and deep at this Stranger, this Unknown Traveler, taking in every detail of His form – the shape of His head, His hair combed back straight and smooth, His eyes hiding behind large glasses. As I gazed at His silent, motionless form, I knew. He knows all. He feels all. He loves all. He controls all. He is Master of the Universe. I knew, there was nothing about me that He did not know. As I gazed, I knew these facts. And receiving these facts, I felt such an unbearable thrill, such divine excitement – how to bear it! How to keep still! I wanted to scream to the sky His true identity. But, I moved on. I walked on.

Then high fever came and I could not rise from the bed. I wept for missing Him, missing His darshan, one day and then the next. Then my loving brother came and made sweet *mosambee* juice for me, and told me, “Baba was talking about you! He was saying, ‘She is my small, small child!’”

I asked my brother many times, “Are you sure? Are you sure?”

“Yes!” he said, “Baba was talking about you with so much love, so much affection!” Next day I determined to go, fever or no. Morning darshan was held. I sat in the front, and sang a song I had written a few weeks after initiation. I sang, ‘O Baba mine, Thou art so dear, O Baba mine, Thou art so sweet, O take me in Thy loving arms, and give me all devotion to Thee. I see Thee walking in the forest, I see Thee sitting in the field, I see Thee smiling, eyes half closed. So full of love, that’s how I see Thee…’

I sang this song, full of shyness. Then Baba came. I was completely transfixed. I only remember how beautiful, how magnificent He looked, and how happy and content I felt to do *Guru Puja* (the process of surrendering body, mind and soul unto His effulgence) to Him for the first time.

Then my Baba left. My Baba was in prison for so many years, in such unbearable conditions, in heat and squalor, in a small cell. He was so far away. But, He was also so close. Once I was at the train station in Madras saying goodbye to my elder brother. Suddenly Baba was in front of me, smiling. Stunned, I moved my gaze. But He was still there. Wherever I gazed, Baba remained! I felt so much bliss at seeing Him everywhere – I could hardly move, hardly speak! That was in 1973.

Years rolled by. I was in Houghton, Michigan, a tiny town in America. Baba was so far, far away. Suddenly, in 1978, Baba was free! I sent Him so many packets of flower and vegetable seeds – more than 100 packets. One day somebody told me that Baba acknowledged the seeds. He said that 80% of the seeds bore fruit. I was confused and asked my neighbor, a gardener about it. He said it was an excellent percentage, if 80% came up, and I was so thrilled. Baba wanted me to know that He loved the flowers as much as I did – more even – and that He had taken personal care of their growth! I was so happy.

Later that day I went to the department store. As I waited to pay the cashier, suddenly, Baba was in front of me. I could not believe it! And again, when I changed the direction of my gaze, He was there again! Everywhere I looked, there was Baba. I began crying uncontrollably, there in the store. It was too much; it was too overwhelming. I felt too undeserving of His grace, of His love. Why, when, where and how He gave His darshan – only He knows.

# Sudhiir

*Ananda Kanan, Missouri*

I was in San Francisco. You might say I'd reached the end of my material voyage, that I couldn't find happiness that way. I was always searching for something that was real. Somehow I got hold of a copy of *Autobiography of a Yogi*, and from then decided that yoga was the path for me.

I decided to look for some way to learn yoga. I heard there were a couple of groups, and one was Ananda Marga. The other group wanted to charge money, and I didn't feel it was right to charge money for something so integral, so I tried AM. The first time I visited there was a big commotion going on, because Madhusudhan had just gotten back from India, where he had had a lot of personal contact with Baba. He had this incredible vibration, had spent so much time with Baba that he practically could light up the room at night. He got everyone else excited. A retreat in Hutchinson, Kansas, was coming up. I went home first, and had meat three times a day at my mother's house. Then I went to this retreat and that was goodbye to carnivorism.

I got initiated the first of the year, 1971 and it changed my life. I was really desperate up to that point, saw no point in going on, no purpose to life. How could I live without a purpose? Once I knew that yoga was my purpose, everything just kept getting better.

I heard that Dada Yatiishvarananda was coming to the *jagrti* to visit, so I went there. I had been doing *nama mantra* for a couple of months. I felt I was sufficiently advanced to get first lesson. He stopped me before I entered the room, and said, "Who are you and how long have you been meditating?"

"Well, Dada, I've been meditating for a couple of months. Can I have first lesson?"

"No, it's out of the question, get out of here" [laughs].

I was so clashed out that I decided to leave AM forever. I went hitchhiking around and ended up in this place. I meditated there, and switched my mantra a couple of times, because I was experimenting, meditating under a fig tree, living this country life. After a

couple of months, I decided to go to the Bay area, where I saw a notice for sleeping bag cleaning, which I needed to do. I went to the place, and when the woman opened the door, she said, “You’re a margii.”

“How do you know?”

“You just look like a margii.” It was Cirismita, who lived in Berkeley, and she said, “There’s this retreat happening, you have to go.”

I said, “Oh, I’ve heard this before, I don’t really want to go.”

But something inside made me pause, and I said to myself, “I’ll go to the retreat, and if it doesn’t look good to me, and it probably won’t, I’ll go on to the Rainbow Gathering.”

So I went to Kansas again, and got so inspired, I decided to sign up for the Ananda Marga Universal Relief Team (AMURT). Dada Yatiishvarananda was recruiting people for the first AMURT team. They would get training, and then, it seemed to me like we were going to be parachuted into the trouble spots of the earth to save humanity! Which is what I wanted to do.

I went to Wichita for the training. Madhavi was the trainer in charge, and she was giving people all kinds of experiences. We were going to preschools, and homes, working with the retarded and the elderly, and all different kinds of social service groups, so I got all this exposure to these things which were completely new to me, and I had many experiences in a short period of time. Plus first aid training, so that we could be this SWAT team of AMURT. Meanwhile Dada started throwing lessons at me. I didn’t want any lessons from this man, and now he wanted to give me all these lessons, all the way up to sixth lesson. Because who knows, we’d be in Afghanistan or somewhere, and who knows what lesson we’d need [laughs].

Toward the end of our training, Baba had said we were supposed to start an LFT training program. Madhurii, who is now Didi Ananda Mitra, was to be the trainer. This was 1973.

We got a very good training from Madhurii and Tom Barefoot, now Giridhara. They put us through everything they could think of, a combination of bliss and clash. I couldn’t wait to leave. We all got postings, as they were thinking about filling needs for

AM in various places around the country. I was assigned to be the editor of the Crimson Dawn newsletter in Wichita.

At that time Baba had just been put in prison. Everyone at the sectorial office started going around the country attending all these protests and vigils for Baba in Washington, D.C., and Atlanta. As LFTs we were also supposed to be informing all the margiis about Baba. At a rally in Washington, I met the person whom I would marry. We started a RAWA *jagrti* in Atlanta, the first RAWA project in the sector. Also at the time we were starting Seva Ananda, a whole foods store, which later evolved into Seva Ananda Natural Foods co-op, which became the largest natural foods warehouse in the southeast of the U.S.

People were saying you have to go see Baba, they’re now allowing people to visit Him in prison. This was a change. Before only a few top *acaryas*, and lawyers, etc., could see Him. Now, general margiis started to go see Baba.

In August, 1977, I had my first meeting with Baba in prison in Patna. I went with a few margiis who later all became *acaryas*. We had to go through a whole procedure to get permission. I was very nervous.

I saw Baba, and was so shocked. I’d heard he’d been fasting and was very weak, but He looked like a frail old person lying on his cot. Words can’t express seeing Him in that state. The person I’d waited so long to see [crying].

He was saying, “Come closer, come closer,” so I ended up the first one in, and I came closest to His head, and He kept saying, “Come closer, come closer,” it just didn’t seem to be close enough. He gave me permission to get so close that I was able to put my arm around His shoulders. It seems funny to think about it – the most incredible being in the universe.

I felt He was frail; I felt like comforting Him. I put my arm around His shoulder [crying]. I had no idea at the time how lucky I was to be able to do that. And I’m sure that was part of Baba’s plan, to do this at the time I was so dumb and naive about everything. It just seemed like such a natural thing to want to do, to want to put your arm around your Baba.

I don’t remember everything that happened, because from that point on my mind was pretty much gone. I was experiencing, I wasn’t thinking. I do remember asking Him to say something about Renaissance Artists and Writers Association (RAWA.) I’m interested in art, I’m an artist. He really liked the question.

He said, “Renaissance Universal (RU) is like this” and made motions like two cymbals clashing together, “to create clash in the mind, and to bring people, by powdering down the mind, closer to *Parama Purusa*.”

Then He said, “RAWA is to attract people’s hearts to love *Parama Purusa*, to get closer, by the attraction of love through art.” That was kind of the end of my time with my arm around Baba. I understood it was time for me to move aside so the other brothers could get close to Him.

You could barely hear Him, His voice was so low and quiet. I went to His feet, and I started massaging His feet [crying]. I spent the rest of the time massaging His feet, and didn’t really hear anything else. I can’t even remember who the other people were or what they were saying. We just sort of floated out. He gave us some candy, or *prasad*, to give to other people. And He said, “Come again, please. Come again and again.”

I went back to Patna, and went to see Baba a second time. When I went to get permission in the office, the officer said, “You again?” I thought it was over, but he just laughed, and gave me the permission. So I went back in the cell with Baba again. It was *Shravanii Purnima*, and Baba said, “You’ve come on a most auspicious occasion.” He made a big deal about it, and said, “I want to convey a message to the margiis, so remember the words.”

It was something like, “Let my sons and daughters be loved by all good people of the universe.” He explained that *Shravanii Purnima* is when the star Shravana, symbolizing the devotee, is closest to the moon, symbolizing the Beloved. It’s the closest proximity of the devotee and the guru. That’s exactly what was happening at the moment. We were this close to Baba [crying]. I’m only just beginning to understand what really happened there.

Afterwards, we did our *sastaung pranam* and said our goodbyes, and all the dadas said, “What happened?”

“Well,” we said, “He gave some words.”

“Oh, a *vani* from Baba!”

So they took us to Baba’s house, and somebody typed up the message from Baba. We got to see all His clothes and shoes, His slippers and cane, everything just the way it was when He was taken to the prison years before.

Arpana Devi Detroit, Michigan

I went to see Baba in jail in 1977. Circumstances orchestrated the situation so that I had to enter Baba’s jail cell first out of our group of seven margiis. I was quite apprehensive about seeing Baba, knowing of His omniscience and fearing exposure of faults, insecurities, imperfections, etc.

When I entered the cell, Baba was propped up, doing namaskar (first, as always). His presence was overwhelming. I met, not the omniscient “judge” I feared, but a being radiating pure and unconditional love. I knew, without a doubt, that He was incapable of any thought, perception, or expression of any kind that was not for my welfare. All self-consciousness melted away like the darkness disappears in the presence of light. Absolute love and trust remained, and as I flew toward home, I felt that the plane was taking me away from where I wanted to be, my true home.

After seeing Baba, I had several Baba dreams. The common theme in them was that I was able to connect with Baba when I was deviating from “the crowd,” or doing something I wasn’t supposed to do.

In one dream I was with a group in a big house with many stairways, and doorways with VSS guards everywhere. Our guide took us to see Baba, and I realized after we had left the room and were leaving the house, that I hadn’t actually seen or connected with Baba. I ducked away from the group, edging along walls and ducking away from guards into doorways, working my way back toward Baba’s room. I opened many doors, and finally found His room.

He was sitting at a desk facing me, in an obviously important and private meeting. He looked up, became very angry, and in a terrible voice said, “What are you doing here?”

I just said His name, and did *pranam*. When I looked up, He was beaming a big smile at me.

In another dream, I was in the New York City airport, with an enthusiastic crowd of margiis waiting to see Baba. We were on a ground floor terminal, and VSS guards announced that we had to all go to another floor. I ducked away and stood alone in the corridor. I heard footsteps, and Baba and a companion rushed by with a briefcase in hand with their heads down. After He passed me, I called His name and He turned to cast His eternal smile at me.

### Svarupa

*Albuquerque, New Mexico*

I was initiated in 1973 by Dada Shishir Kumar.

I was 18, and searching for something. One particular day, I came home from work, feeling it was some low point in my life. Baba talks about something called *samviit*, which is that peculiar force that awakens all of a sudden your desire for spirituality. It happened when I came home from work, and was lying on my bed, listening to the radio. The radio had a spiritual discourse from Ram Dass. He was a spiritual teacher, an American, and he had a guru.

Almost instantly, from the moment I heard his voice, and heard him speak about spirituality, and *Tantra*, very much like Baba's philosophy, from that very second it awakened this desire within me. I knew I had to find something to fulfill this desire. I didn't know where to look or where to turn. The first thing I found was a free magazine at a university, with an advertisement for some free classes. I called, and it turned out to be a *jagrti*. The classes were over, but they invited me to come anyway. I went, and it was my first *dharmacakra* (DC). Over the next few months I continued going, and still had this great admiration for Ram Dass. He had a book out.

At one of the DCs, I came and there was a large crowd there. I didn't know what was going on. We were waiting for DC, and a large number of people came in, all dressed in white. It turned out to be a group of people accompanying Ram Dass. That night he had given a speech in Albuquerque, and some margiis were there and invited him to *dharmacakra*. To me that was a great experience. It happened to me many times in my life that Baba fulfills desires, if you're following His path. Even before I was margii, it was the closing of some spiritual circle that I needed to close. Ram Dass was really my first spiritual teacher, and Baba brought him to the dharmacakra for me. In fact, I had my first spiritual experience in sadhana, a fantastic experience. Ram Dass is a great yogi, and has deep spiritual insight. Anyway, that sort of closed a chapter for me.

In 1977 or 1976, I had one experience. A lot of margiis had the same experience. It was at a sectorial seminar in Oklahoma. Lots of people were there, during a *dharmacakra*, in a large hall. They had just finished showing Baba's video. Baba finished

giving the DMC talk, and they stopped the picture with Baba doing his *mudra*. Then everyone went into doing sadhana, a long sadhana.

Something curious happened. Many times people have individual experiences in sadhana, jumping or making noise. This particular time maybe not the whole group, but certain parts of the whole group shared the same strange experience. First people started jumping. That settled down, and then people started laughing, chuckling. People couldn’t control it, and it was like waves of this strange behavior going across the crowd. And then people started crying. And then for some of us, smells came into the auditorium.

Later that same year, I went to India the first time, Oct. ’77, and He was still in prison. In between visits to the prison, a few margiis decided to go to Jamalpur. The first place we visited was the school. The dada there started showing us around. In a courtyard corner they used to have darshans. We went into a room and he said, “This is where Baba used to give darshan.” As soon as I walked into this room, the same smell as I’d smelled at that *dharmacakra* came to me. There was no incense burning.

My first visit with Baba was in prison. You had to make multiple trips to the prison authorities to get the papers, and then had to come back until they saw fit to see you. Eventually you’d get an appointment and go to the prison. Then at the prison you went through the process of being fingerprinted and interviewed by the authorities and so forth, and that’s when a lot of people were blacklisted, they wouldn’t let you back in after that. But after that you were able to see Baba.

His cell was open to the courtyard, with no bars, just a cloth hanging over the door, but there were guards. When we saw Baba it was not alone, but with several margiis, and a guard. But the guard stood back and after a while, in Baba’s presence, you didn’t even notice the guard. When you came in the doorway Baba was lying on His cot. I heard a lot of stories about how emaciated Baba was, but because this was the end of ’77, somehow His health had come back.

We did *pranam*, and Baba asked each of us our names, and our organizational positions. I was working in an Ananda Marga co-op in Albuquerque, and doing some related work, part of Food and Care Dept. Even now we do work in Food and Care. It was a nice experience, because Baba said, “Oh, that’s very good.”

Then He asked everyone if they had any questions. I didn’t really have one. My impressions of that and of the second visit, were just about being in Baba’s presence. One of my reasons for going was to just understand who Baba was, and what He really meant. I knew from His books and from sadhana who my guru was, but still I wasn’t 100 percent sure. Seeing His eyes I no longer had any doubts. In prison, He gave you very direct focus; you could look directly into His eyes. You felt a sort of bottomlessness in Baba’s eyes that went on forever. We were there for half an hour. He blessed us, put His hand on each of our heads, gave us candy, and we did *guru puja*.

It was a very strange time in India, because magazines had all these strange articles on Ananda Marga. AM was being accused of murder, and all kinds of horrible things, in their equivalent of TIME magazine. You couldn’t go to a newsstand without seeing these things. It was a crazy time to be there.

On the trip to Jamalpur, there were CBI agents following margiis on the train. One gentleman asked me why I was going to Jamalpur. Then he tried to convince me that I shouldn’t go. Later I realized this was someone following margiis and trying to keep them from going to their destinations. As soon as I got off someone met me at the platform and started asking the same questions. We had a lot of experiences like that, and we had to take a resolve to get through it all. In retrospect it was *tantric*, really, having to go through struggles in order to maintain devotion.

### Cakreshvara

*Seattle, Washington*

It has been nearly twenty-five years from the one time that I met Baba, to the time that I am now writing this account. The memory however is as if this were only yesterday. May this heartfelt story be preserved and added to the garland of His incomprehensible legacy. By my word, these events are in truth and from personal experience.

A group of us had finished training in Sweden and were about to be sent into the world to realize His mission. I had undergone severe clash over memorizing endless *shlokas* without having translations… so many meaningless nonsense syllables to my analytic mind. I began to have severe resentment over what I thought was Indian-style clash for the sake of clash. At any rate, the training was about over and it was hinted that I would be taking a posting as RAWA Secretary in Europe. As a former studio musician, this was the closest prospect to heaven on earth. I had toured the continent, spoken with RAWA devotees and was formulating plans for a professional recording center.

Our reverend trainer took best care for our wellbeing. He intuitively analyzed the nature of our karmas and sent his recommendations to Baba as to where our talents could best be utilized. It was, however, the Lord’s pleasure as to where we ultimately arrived.

Before leaving for our postings, the trainer said that all of his recommendations for our assignments were approved by Baba except for one of us. One was to be posted to Central Office in India. He would not tell us who this was until training was finalized. There was much excitement and conjecture. I was secretly thinking that as much as I had mentally blasphemed the very inventor of the *shloka*, the Sanskrit language, and everything remotely associated with Indian culture, at least that person was not me. As it turned out, when the postings were revealed, my position as Berlin Sector RAWA Secretary was scratched out in simple pencil by the Lord’s own hand. In the place was His direction, “Central Office.” I was in a mixed state of unbelief and reverie.

Baba was in the Patna jail at the time. The *jagrti* was alive with an international assembly of margiis from over the world, coming to pay respect to their Beloved.

One brother Hanuman was there also awaiting darshan. Hanuman was distressed over his Sanskrit name. This brother was in the final phase of formal study as a homeopathic physician and was given the name Hanuman by an acarya in Europe. I noted the similarity with the name Hahneman, the father of modern homeopathy. This brother knew little about Indian mythology, but was told that Hanuman was a venerable figure of great strength, unfailing devotion and intellect. He was happy with this until asked by an Indian dada what his name was. When he replied Hanuman, the dada said, “Achaa! Hanuman is the name of a big monkey.”

The brother was clashed to the core and was resolute that he would ask Baba for a new name. As time progressed, it was arranged that I was to see Baba with Hanuman and the two didis who had rescued me from the train station.

Before going into the jail, we had to submit to processing by the jail authorities. This was not good, since the CBI and communist factions were using the darshan of margiis to gather data on all who came to see Baba. The four of us were in the office of the jail superintendent, reluctantly giving information required before we were allowed to see Him. In the office were a couple officials and one individual whose vibration was so crude that I tried even to avoid any eye contact with him. The aura of this person was nearly black. I was instead concentrating with amazement on the superintendent. Every other word out of his mouth was about Baba. He was not a margii, but was immersed in the thought that his jail was the one that held Baba. Baba said this and Baba did that. With each repetition of His holy name, I felt a sanctifying impact upon the jailer’s mind. We collectively celebrated His auspicious presence. I gained a glimpse then of how He must be working toward the salvation of scattered souls in realms beyond the manifest structure of His organization.

As we were cleared to enter His room, the jailer forced an introduction to the crude individual sitting to his left whom I had tried to avoid even looking at. I forget his name.

“This is the personal physician we have assigned to care for Baba.” I felt a cold sickness pass through me. The thought of his hands touching Baba caused a deep revulsion in me, except for the conviction that our Lord was transcendent to the physical, and loved this crude entity equally as much as His love for the purest saint.

Through the courtyards and passages, we were led to a small, dark, and unventilated cell. A line of seven or eight CBI agents stood with hands folded, at rigid and paranoid attention against the east wall. The Lord lay on a cot against the west wall facing north, parallel to the line of agents. We filed into the shrine, myself first, followed by brother Hanuman, followed by the two didis.

We had seen so many photographs of Baba, the blissful stout figure with His benevolent smile. The shock was unbearable in person. The only thing I recognized was His thick black glasses. His subtle fair body was wasted to mere bones under the consequence of prolonged fasting. This was more than I could bear. We all fell to the floor in *sastaung pranam* and the didis sobbed uncontrollably in pain over His physical state.

After moments, He gently bade us to rise up. There were a couple glass bottles under His cot with sugar rock candy in them. He opened a bottle and distributed some of the candy to us as *prasad*.

In the darshan that followed, my Baba poured such loving attention on me. As I had the good fortune to be the first in line, I was merely inches from His face. He stroked my head and hair. I held His hand as He explained the meaning of my name. Baba became quite grave in mood and told me that I would be destined for considerable suffering in the next several months, and that drinking water would be a problem. He made several predictions about hardships I would face by way of trying to prepare me. In later contemplation, it was as if He were witnessing all time, and that I was merely a player in the events that were ordained to happen. Once I misunderstood the intention of His soft voice and He corrected my thinking, demonstrating that He was omnipresent even in my innermost thoughts.

Baba talked about the suffering of people in the region I would eventually be posted to, and exhorted me to do something to help them. His cosmic gaze was looking beyond the small room. He was witnessing something far away, and in that moment I realized the yogic dispassion of a great saint. Any lesser mortal would have been ensconced in the physical suffering Baba was going through. His focus was, however, not on His own physical hardship, but on the plight of His children in some distant place.

During the darshan, Baba would on occasion address or reprimand the CBI agents behind us. They would jerk to attention on His slightest address. “Yes, Baba, Yes, Baba, we will do. What you are telling is correct.” These people who were posted to intimidate and harass Baba were reduced to mere toys at His whim. I had heard that these agents had to be continually changed since they either fled in fear or became devotees.

In one awkward moment, I was holding His wrist tightly. As He talked, He slowly moved His head and eyes, staring at my tightly clenched hand. When I realized it, I thought, “Oh my God, I am holding the Lord of the universe hostage.” I released my grip and He looked back at me. At that time I had one other incidental misgiving. The entire focus of the darshan was on me. Baba gave fleeting acknowledgement to the other three saints, but all of His love and attention was being poured directly onto me. I felt uncomfortable for the other three who were being practically ignored by Baba.

Before the darshan, we were strictly told by Central officials that we were to ask nothing personal of Baba. I am not the most subtle of entities, neither then nor now. At my first chance, I begged the Lord to grant me purity and devotion to do His work. He looked away from me and said, “Those who want will get.” By His infinite Grace, devotion has come. Purity I am still waiting for, and will no doubt achieve this blessing in some future life as I struggle to know Him.

Baba’s attention was briefly turned to brother Hanuman, who immediately asked Baba for a new name. Baba closed His eyes for a moment and said, “You are now Shudodhana. Shudodhana was the father of Buddha. The meaning is one who earns his livelihood by moral means.”

Shudodhana was most relieved. Baba then gave respectful greeting to the two didis who knelt at the far end of the cot by Baba’s feet.

Our time was finished and we were asked to leave by the jail officials. We stayed on as long as we could, ignoring the badgering requests to leave. Finally, we could not stay longer. We exited the small cell in the order that we had come in.

This is difficult to explain, but outside the jail cell, back into the mundane sunlight, I was putting on my shoes, which were left at the door of the shrine. Intuitional realization overcame me that I was leaving His physical presence, but that I could not go anywhere beyond the radius of His guidance. The shoes would carry me to places as of

yet unknown, but I could never walk outside of Him. In bliss or adversity, He would always be there with me.

Two days later in the Patna *jagrti*, I walked into a room to find Shudodhana in a terrible mental state. He did not even know that I had come in, as he was writing on several pieces of paper, talking to himself in a fretful way. I could see three pages filled with notes and a fourth page about half-filled. At that moment also, the two didis by chance walked into the room. There we were, together again.

I was wanting to share our experience with Baba, but was concerned about whatever problem was causing Shudodhana so much anguish. I asked him what he was doing and if there was some help I could give. It seems that Shudodhana was so lost in the Lord during darshan that he had no idea of the Sanskrit name given to him by Baba. He had been in this room for over three hours trying to write down every possible name he could imagine.

For this, I was happy to help. I told him that I had clearly heard and remembered every word Baba said to him. Shudodhana looked as if the weight of the universe had been removed. I proceeded to tell him that his name was Shudodhana, the name of Buddha’s father, and it meant one who earned his living by moral means. Shudodhana thanked me profusely almost to the point of tears. We were smiling and laughing with joy. My reverie was only broken when I looked at the two didis who were frowning and obviously upset about something.

I quickly gathered my composure and asked them what was wrong. The first didi said that there must be something wrong with either my ears or my mind and probably both. She had heard Baba’s conversation with the brother and He had given a completely different name and explanation. This was entirely bizarre, and we only had time to begin arguing when the second didi broke into the fray. She informed us that we were both crazy because she had heard an entirely different name and different translation. As the two didis and myself argued at an escalating volume, poor Shudodhana sat still, more depressed than ever. I was becoming upset, because whatever these two women heard was not what Baba had said.

Conduct rules out the door, I yelled at them to close their mouths for a minute. I informed them that if anyone heard correctly what Baba had said in His soft voice, it was me, since I was sitting closest to His face. They should understand that being at the other end of the cot, it would be possible for them to not have heard correctly.

There was silence for a moment and I felt that my point had been accepted and the matter was closed. This was not over, however. One didi countered with both anger and amazement. What was I saying? She was the first in line and was sitting at the head of the cot while I was at the end near Baba’s feet. Baba was talking so softly, how could I have heard anything? The second didi entered the battle claiming that she was at the head of the cot and the other three of us were toward the far end. Poor Shudodhana was entirely drowned out in his claim that he was the first to enter the cell. All the yelling continued for a couple minutes and then in a split second, there was complete silence. We all came to a profound realization in the same instant.

We knew then that Baba was carrying on four independent darshans at the same time. As we calmed down and explained our subjective experience, each of us perceived that we were the first one to enter Baba’s room followed by the others. Each of us sat directly in front of Baba, only inches from His face. Each also felt that the entire encompassing love and attention of our Guru was focused exclusively on us personally, and that the other three were largely ignored somewhere far away in the dark room by Baba’s feet.

Such was and is His cosmic, and sometimes humorous, drama. We learned from this that while He is guarding the welfare of countless beings, He is at the same time caring for each one of us as if there were no other devotee in the universe for Him. Each devotee can rightfully say that He is mine and mine alone.

I remained in the Central Office for a while and did some work there until an appropriate posting was found. When one dada in the Rajasthan province was reposted to Africa, I was assigned to take his position as principal in an Ananda Marga school. I remembered Baba’s serious warning about drinking water and I asked one Central worker about the conditions where I would be going. He was somewhat perplexed, since he said

the region in general was known for the purity and quality of the water. Surely Baba must have been speaking figuratively.

The long train ride at last began to Bikaner, near the India-Pakistan border. The heat was severe and at every stop on the way, I would cautiously get down from the train to test the drinking water. To my relief, the dada was right. As I traveled closer to Rajasthan, the water became more pure. I was wondering what Baba could have meant since I was experiencing the opposite of what I heard Him say. My mind listened to the rhythmic clatter of the rail tracks as I thought about the different words our small group heard in Darshan. This would just have to pass as another of His mysteries.

The train finally arrived in the desert city. I got down from my cabin and went to the water fountain for a cool drink. To my shock the water was like ocean water and I spit it out onto the ground. Water in this area had to be drawn up in stages from a hundred meters below the surface and was laden with salt and minerals to the point of being toxic. My body underwent extreme shock over the next weeks. I could feel the kidneys in my back inflamed and becoming rigid as I tried to adjust to the saline water. I would travel many miles on advice from the local residents that if I would go to a certain place, there would be a well that had water with less salt. All were the same and my health was deteriorating.

Baba removed me from Rajasthan after a tour in the local area. I was arrested in Batinda for being a Margii and was mentally abused for 24 hours by the police and CBI. Because they had no grounds to keep me longer and possibly because of my American passport, I was let go and was saved from the physical torture that had been endured by so many other margiis. As the train was leaving for Bikaner, the CBI changed their minds about letting me go and sent an agent on board to detain me. The agent grabbed my arm and a fight began. In the struggle, I was able to throw the agent off of the moving train. I was quite happy with myself that I had the strength and warrior spirit to conquer the enemy.

My celebration was short-lived, however. The Batinda officials had reported the incident to the Bikaner authorities while I was still in route. Needless to say, I had a warm welcoming party waiting for me at the Bikaner station. I was immediately arrested. It was very late at night and I had two sympathizers among the local police who allowed

me to stay in the school for the night. They would come at daybreak for the formal arrest. After they left, I contacted the local margiis who got me on a night train for Delhi. They threw me into a vacant cabin, which was odd for any train berth on an Indian train to be empty. In a few minutes, about ten soldiers arrived and told me to get out of the cabin since it was reserved for the military. I was very ill with malaria and could hardly rise up. I remembered telling them that I was sick from some deadly contagious disease that the locals feared. The soldiers fled in terror and I was able to get some rest in solitude.

Over the next several days, every *jagrti* or headquarters I went to had been just visited by the police looking for me. I was able to make it to Patna one last time for a train headed east and eventually out of India.

It was late in the evening and the sun had set. Circumstances were such that I could not have seen Baba in the jail, and was feeling too unworthy to be in His physical presence. I felt that I had been a complete failure. Nothing had been accomplished but taking food and being sick and causing so much trouble to the Margiis. I had to talk to Baba, though. Even if He knew all things, I had to explain.

Outside the jail, there was a small wooden fence or rail that separated the city walkway from the high stone walls. The inside perimeter was a bare, cleared area of stones and dirt. I climbed over the railing and knelt by the stone wall. The heat of the sun was still strongly radiating from the rock as I laid my hands on the wall and broke into tears talking to my Guru on the other side. So many things I said and explained. After some time, I did Guru Puja to Him through the stone wall before leaving. I mentally offered up a white and a red flower to Baba and asked His forgiveness for my shortcomings.

After a long Namaskar, I noticed a subtle perfume in the air and opened my eyes. To my amazement, now in place between myself and the wall was a large plant of some kind, having two large, fragrant flowers. One was deep red, and one the purest white. He had given His reply and I knew that I was to shed the past and move on fearlessly. I picked the two flowers and pressed them gently in a notebook. There was only time left to catch the train.

Although faded now from the years, I have kept the flowers. The sacred memory of His presence has not faded. It is my hope that others may find this story of historical or

inspirational value. My purpose in telling it is then complete. God's Grace be with you all.

### Kamala

*Eugene, Oregon*

In April 1978, I went to India to see Baba while He was still in jail. After going through all the bureaucratic red tape and getting permission to go in, I found out that Baba was protesting at that time and not seeing anyone. Of course I was completely devastated. I went back to Baba’s house in Patna and spoke to Dada Ramananda who was Baba’s Personal Assistant at that time. He told me I could write a letter to Baba and he would take it to Him.

That night I wrote to Baba and with the letter I enclosed a picture of my two sons, who at the time were about one and three years old. Dada took the picture and letter to Baba and brought back a reply. Baba said that He was very sorry that He could not see me at this time and hoped that I would return again to see Him. On the back of the picture He wrote in His own handwriting, “With love, Baba.”

I was very touched to have the picture, and as *samskara* would have it, the picture was misplaced some time after I returned to the United States. You can imagine that I looked everywhere for it for years but it seemed to have vanished.

In 1999, I had a health crisis involving non-Hodgkin’s lymphoma. I had to make critical decisions as to which therapy I would undergo. I decided with much trepidation to have chemotherapy. A few days before I was to start treatment I was at the house of my ex-husband. He had been with me in India in 1978 to see Baba. He was looking through some old envelopes of pictures in his closet and to everyone’s amazement he discovered the picture of our sons, which Baba had signed! I was surprised beyond words. It seemed to be the sign I was waiting for. I felt that Baba had reached out from the invisible world and handed me a message saying He loved me.

I am happy to say that I am now three years cancer-free and very happy to be alive to tell this story to others. Baba Nam Kevalam.

### Kirit

*San Francisco Bay area, California*

I was initiated in 1975 in the Los Altos Hills *jagrti*. I had not seen Baba, I had seen only one of His pictures. I had only read Human Society Part I and I was struggling very hard to learn Idea and Ideology. Those were difficult days. I was struggling to understand who Baba was. Although I did not know anything about Him I was very impressed by His books and I felt a very strong vibration – even at that time I felt a strong cosmic vibration attracting me in His books.

Soon thereafter, I started seeing Him in my dreams. I saw Him come and give very specific messages in a collective environment. Much, much later I found out this is how He used to give His discourses. And every single time the message has come true later on. Those messages related to many, many things about the world situation – economics, things related to energy crises, things related to drought and water crises.

I wanted to see Him very badly, so in 1978 I finally managed to go to India. At that time Baba was in jail. Every time I made an effort to see Him, He created a situation whereby I could not. For example, when I had the opportunity to go see Him He had a struggle with the jail authority and stopped seeing anybody. I struggled and yet I still could not see Him. So I was very disappointed and I spent, altogether, almost three months trying to see Him.

Different dadas said to me, “Why are you hanging around here? Why don’t you go to Ananda Nagar and Kolkata, see some different places and see what’s happening with Ananda Marga? You should go out and see the senior devotees of Baba.”

So I went to see Naginaji, Chandranathji, all these great disciples of Baba. Then I again came back to Patna hoping to see Baba but still I kept failing.

Eventually they said that I should go to Ananda Nagar. I spent some time there and at that time Dada Amitanandaji was there and I became close to him. We became good friends and spent a lot of time doing sadhana and visited many villages.

From there he suggested that I go to Kolkata so I went for a visit. After some there time Dada Pranavanandaji suggested I should go to Bangalore because there was a *Dharma Maha Sammelan* (DMS) going on there.

I went and spent a good amount of time in Bangalore. And again I came back traveling through Patna to see if I would get a chance to see Baba but again Baba refused to see anybody.

Meanwhile I developed an infection. I was staying in the office in Patna. There were no conveniences there. It was a very difficult environment and I was very sick. There was hardly anybody there to help me or take care. I was struggling.

Then one family *acarya*, Rasmohanji, saw me and said, “You should not stay here. Come to my house until you become better.” He took me to his house and I lay in bed trying to recover my health. This is the time something happened. What had happened during my whole journey was this: the Central Bureau of Intelligence (CBI) police had somehow gotten my name as being a prominent margii visiting India, but they did not know where I was. They were looking for me to deport me. The day I left Patna to go to Ananda Nagar was the evening there was a raid on the market, and many foreign margiis were deported. I spent some time in Ananda Nagar, and the day I left, the CBI heard that I was in Ananda Nagar, and they came to check on me. That was the same day I left for Kolkata. I was in Kolkata for a very short time. And once again, the day I left, that was the day they were inquiring about me. They traced me to Bangalore. I was walking around among the CBI officers, not knowing they were looking for me, and they never found me. I finally came back to Patna, staying at this *acarya’s* house, and they traced me there. I was in bed and I could hear what was going on.

They said to the *acarya*, “We are looking for a foreign margii.”

“No, there is no foreign margii here.”

They searched the whole house, upside down, everywhere, boxes, briefcases, suitcases, and finally they came to the bed where I was sleeping.

“Who is this?”

“This is one of my colleagues from Gujurat, he is sick, lying down.”

And they left. Much later I found out they had been looking for me. But by some strange coincidence, and some strange guidance, I was always one step ahead of India’s intelligence force.

Eventually Baba came out of the jail. We won the case in July 1978. I was in Ananda Nagar at the time. We were all very joyful. We had a lot of *akhanda kiirtan*, and

I immediately went to Patna. But I found out that Baba was not coming out because of some other charges pending. Again I was disappointed and returned to Ananda Nagar. I felt I would not get the chance to see Baba. Already three months had gone by. My visa would be up very soon. I was very sad, that in all this time I was not able to see Him.

Again we heard the news on the radio that Baba’s bail had been granted, and He would come out. I rushed back to Patna, arriving a few days before He was to be released. I wanted to present something to Baba, and was wondering what I should give. All of a sudden, a silver glass for drinking water just came into my mind. I thought, “Yes, this is what I want to give Baba.” I went to the market and bought such a cup, and sent it to Baba. Baba was very happy, but said there was no need for such things here. I thought it was strange that Baba did not accept my gift.

But Ramananda said, “No, He is just saying that He won’t need it in jail.” Sure enough, Baba came out, and that was the first time I saw Him, in the car when He was coming back. That scene I will never forget. So many people, an ocean of people. The streets were closed in Patna, the railway station was overflowing with margiis, the whole town had completely changed. When I saw Baba the first time, it was an unbelievable experience, because everything I had been feeling was there in front of me suddenly. It’s like you’re working for something for a very long time, and finally it arrives. You almost don’t believe it.

He immediately started giving two darshans a day. We were all enjoying very much, and we were all struggling to get as close as we could. Baba was still in a wheelchair, as He had been fasting for more than five years. In the Patna house, I got a place right in front of Baba. I was overpowered with emotion and devotion. I tried to open my eyes, but I could not, and could not control my tears; they were flowing nonstop. As soon as I opened my eyes, Baba was scanning all the margiis with His head. He smiled at me. I wiped my tears. When His face was coming this way, I would barely open my eyes, and dry my tears. But after He looked at me and smiled, there would be so many tears.

Again and again, for forty-five minutes, this game went on – I could not control my tears, and He also could not quit looking at me and smiling. Then He finished His

discourse. Ramananda-ji pushed His chair, and had a strict order not to let anyone touch Baba. All the margiis wanted to touch His feet, but Baba was trying to regain His health, and he didn’t want to burden Baba with the *samskaras* of devotees. The VSS guards were also protecting Baba. But Baba’s wheelchair came very near and stopped, and I just put my head on His knee, and cried. Finally He gave me a blessing.

Next day I left. I was on the train journey to Bombay for three or four days, and for the entire time I was in a totally blissful spiritual state of mind. My mind was completely oblivious to the material world; it did not care about it, it had no concern for it. The train malfunctioned on the way, extending the time. I was completely isolated from everybody, and I did not mix with people. So it was very nice solitude on the train.

Another time in 1978, I was struggling so hard to get personal contact with Baba. But Ramananda had said Baba’s health was not good, to let Him regain His health first. I said, “I understand, but I want you to know that I am requesting.” I requested many dadas to take care of my request to see Baba before I went back to the U.S.A. Many dadas were my good friends for the three months I was there. Everything I had, I gave to my friends. But when I needed these dadas to take my message to Baba, all friends disappeared. And by that time, I had nothing left in terms of donations to give. All of a sudden I had no friends because I had no money left.

I was standing in front of Baba’s house, and some Prout meeting was going on. I didn’t care which trade it was, I just wanted to see Baba.

I finally said, “I give up. This is not my house, because I will not be treated this way in my house.”

I told this to Baba, “I don’t belong here.” With tears in my eyes I turned around and said, “I am not coming to this house again.” I must have walked five or ten steps, crying, having given up hope to see Baba in person. All of a sudden, Dada Dhyaneshananda-ji came running from inside Baba’s house.

“Come, come, you’re being called inside!”

“Who are you? I did not request you anything.”

“I don’t know, you are being called inside. Somebody sent for you.” To this day I am not sure what exactly happened. He dragged me inside, and there was a meeting going on – a UPSF (Universal Proutist Student Federation) meeting.

Baba was sitting on a cot, and I got the very best position in front of Baba. First Baba asked, “Who is not UPSF? This is a UPSF meeting.”

He was smiling. This was my first encounter with Baba closely. I was afraid to stand up, that I would be thrown out. So I said, internally, “It’s up to you Baba, you know I am not UPSF.” I surrendered. Baba smiled for a while. That was my first close contact with Baba, and He talked about many things, including certain things that applied directly to me. I wanted to dedicate my life to Baba’s mission. I was thinking to become a doctor, that way I could be independent.

Baba at that time talked about the essential elements for a successful revolution. I still remember the three points He mentioned. Morality, a strong spiritual/moral foundation must be there. Second, you should never lose sight of who is being served, never lose track of humanity. Third, pinnacled intellect. You must start with your foundation, a broad foundation, and build more pointed and specific to an apex, to specific points. Your intellect must be sharp and pinnacled. Then He looked around and said, “And some people think that ‘I am an engineer, and I will become a doctor,’ but this is not proper pinnacled intellect.”

At that time I knew that everything I was considering was nothing. Baba gave me the indication that I should not go for these medical things. So I did not go to medical school.

### Giridhara (2)

On Christmas Day of 1971 I boarded a plane for Delhi. I was traveling alone, planning to attend DMC and see Baba for the first time.
