When I reached New Delhi, I remember seeing the surging crowds of taxi wallahs outside the airport. I almost turned back into the airport – the intensity of the crowd was so great. Finally I got a cab to South Extension and we had trouble finding the place, until I saw an *avadhuta* in uniform and got directions from him. I stayed there for two nights and then I had to move to the Prasads’ house, because there were rumors of a police raid and I would be deported if they found a foreigner there. I recall that the second morning was *ekadashi* and the whole South Extension crew, who were publishing a Prout (Progressive Utilization Theory) newspaper, all got up well before sunrise and made a huge breakfast of cooked foods. They said that they had to work so hard that they needed to do this to have enough energy.

We got word that Baba had been arrested at His house in Patna and that DMC was cancelled. Sandiipa and I took a 2<sup>nd</sup> class sleeper with those little shelving units high up for beds. It was winter and very cold as the train had no heat. After 24 hours on the train, we got off at Patna Station and took a bicycle rickshaw to Pataliputra Colony. I remember when I saw the foreigners’ *jagriti*, I was so impressed with the margiis; they looked like they were all saints. Their faces were all shining. When I stepped up onto the porch, I felt like I had stepped onto something that was moving. The vibration was wonderful.

Baba was being held in jail in a small town about three hours back towards Delhi. The train was hours late and it was about 11 p.m. when the Assam Mail train arrived. We mostly stood in this crowded compartment and held on. Three hours later we stopped in a small station that had no electric power and hence no lights. It was completely dark. A dada that I didn’t know motioned for me to get off the train. I got off and placed my hand on my belt pack and found the zipper open and my passport, tickets and money all missing.

I was quite upset as I was aware that the CBI was after foreign margiis, and I was afraid that with no identification, money or ticket I was going to be in real trouble. I laid myself down wrapped only in a blanket on the cold floor. I was very cold and could not

sleep. I lay there alternating between great fear and worry about losing everything and great excitement that I might see Baba in the morning.

This back and forth drama continued until the sun was rising. Finally I was able to say, “OK, Lord, if you want me to come to you naked and completely without my Western resources, so be it.” I found contentment and held that feeling. The group of margiis hired several rickshaws and we rode out to the edge of the small village. The ride in the morning air was beautiful. It was very green and cool. We rode to a large building where we got down and waited for several hours on the grass in front of it. There must have been 200 margiis and lots of dadas and didis there.

Suddenly the door opened and Baba was standing in the door. Everyone jumped close to the door, but I was rooted to the ground on top of a three-foot wall. I could see Baba clearly. I saw Him and I saw His smile. I saw the white light coming from His smile. This white light enveloped me and my mind turned white for a while, then it turned orange and then it turned back to white. I knew instantly that this was the same Baba that I had seen in my sadhana back in the States. This was the same Baba. I knew that He was my guru. Baba then said to the margiis, “You all do your duty, you know what that is. Do kiirtan every spare moment.”

I later felt that these were His instructions to us for the long period that He would remain in prison. I was in a blissful daze, very exhilarated at seeing Baba and I also knew that I had gotten all I needed. We milled around for awhile and everyone felt the special spiritual vibration. Then we took rickshaws back to the train station and caught a train to Patna in the afternoon.

When we arrived back in Patna, I went to the Station Master to report the theft of my things and he quizzed me about who I was and said I was a very lucky man. He removed an envelope from his drawer and in the envelope was my passport, tickets and all my traveler’s checks and U.S. dollars. Only the rupees were missing. Everyone agreed that this was an unheard of miracle in India. No one ever recovers stolen property. Later we learned from a margii who worked for the CBI that the CBI had had me pickpocketed to see who was this new guy in town.

Baba had just given the 16 Points a few weeks before He was arrested and so this was an important topic. Kamala and I decided to interview Dada Pranavananda and others and put together a small book on the 16 Points. We carefully obtained information on each of the points and typed up a small book. A few months after I left India it came out as “How an Ananda Margii Should Live.” It was very useful for me to have carefully gone over the 16 Points shortly after it was given as I was able to note down some fine points such as touching the big toe to the ground outside the other ankle and bending the knee during kiirtan.

Finally it was decided to hold a three-week *Akhanda Kiirtan* to try to get Baba out of jail. This was held in the darshan hall in the overseas margiis *jagrti*, which was in the room next to where I slept. We had three shifts for the kiirtan. After two days of this non-stop kiirtan I started to wish I could escape, since everywhere in our house the sound of kiirtan was there. You slept to kiirtan, ate to kiirtan, everything, all the time. After about five days of frustration, the feeling changed, and I started to love the flow. Many days I did kiirtan the whole eight-hour shift. It permeated everything.

Finally the day for the ending of the three-week kiirtan came and we prepared for a large crowd. Many workers and margiis came for the final hours. The room was packed. The vibration was so strong that rings of golden light could be seen coming out from the center of the circle. You could tell by the vibration, the high pitch of the singing voices and the heads tilted back that the *bhava* was very, very strong. Margiis would fall over in samadhi and we would carry them out to the porch, out of the way of the dancers.

Near the very end of the kiirtan, Dada Pranavananda who was in charge of ending the kiirtan and leading the chants was going slower and slower around the mandap. His head was tilting further and further back. He was leaning on the dada behind him who was helping him to stay upright. Finally he could no longer stand and he lay on the floor as the kiirtan finished. We sat for sadhana and then this small faraway voice could be heard trying to do his duty and sing the *Samgacchadvam* chant. The voice sounded so distant.

The energy released from the kiirtan was amazing. During sadhana I felt someone behind me touch my spine at my *anahata cakra* and I felt as if a blockage was removed and I had an amazing sadhana. After the sadhana was over, I turned around and found a

small dada with a very sweet expression. We smiled at each other and I said, “Thank you.”

Baba was transferred from the jail to Patna Medical College Hospital. This was very good as the margiis were allowed to gather in the courtyard of the hospital and to sing kiirtan and have His darshan. He was housed on the second floor near the end of a long wing. Baba would come out and sit in a chair on the porch for some time in the morning and afternoon.

One day there were many margiis and workers present and Baba was obviously in a special mood. He looked very playful. He was smiling down at us and then he would give a meaningful look at the guard in full uniform standing at attention with the rifle guarding Him. He looked down at us and over at the soldier several times and then suddenly he raised his right hand in a *mudra* (I think the *mudra* of fascination with the first and little fingers raised and the rest in a loose fist). He shook this *mudra* for 5-10 seconds and looked intently at the soldier. Then we all watched as the soldier just fell over and collapsed on the floor. Baba looked down at us with a look of satisfaction as if to say, “You see!” The other guards dragged him into the guard’s room next door. Later the guard was seen looking out of the window at Baba with a look of total affection and bliss. The guard was transferred as he could no longer maintain his disciplined manner in Baba’s presence. It was clear that Baba was having His fun and showing the margiis something as well.

On another occasion there were many margiis and workers as there was a rumor that Baba was going to be taken to another building for an X-ray. Everyone wanted to give namaskar to Baba and see Him up close. I took my place on the stone steps about 2/3 of the way down the second flight of stairs near the bottom. I had been feeling very much part of the New Age spiritual movement and I had this idea that I did not like prostrations to the Guru. Loving namaskars, yes, but prostration seemed too much for me. As Baba came down the stairs and came near to me, I don’t know what happened. I found myself laying in *sastaung pranam* on the stone steps. I did not know that I had done this, I did not feel any pain from falling onto the edges of the stone steps. I only felt peace and

bliss in my heart. I knew from that second that the only position to be in Baba’s presence was in *sastaung pranam*.

I eventually stood up and mingled with the margiis and workers. Everyone was in an excited state, as Baba had showered us with strong energy. There was a rumor that Baba would come back to the stairs in about a half an hour and so all but one very small brother from South India and I went off for a short walk either along the Ganges River which was just behind the hospital or out in the market outside the hospital. This brother and I were both just too blissful to walk. We stood right by the curb where the small van had been parked when Baba got in. In five minutes the car with Baba returned and we were the only two margiis present.

When Baba got out of the car we both did *sastaung pranam*, the dada grasped Baba’s left foot and I grasped Baba’s right foot. Baba of course could not walk and he stood there, gesturing with His hands, with a big smile saying things like, “My devotees, what can I do…”

This went on for maybe two minutes, and then He spoke to us and asked us to let Him go so that His guards could do their duty. Reluctantly, we released our gentle grasp and Baba went up the stairs with His guards. My hands smelled like roses or some sweet flower. I did not wash my hands for several days as the smell stayed sweet for several days. These events greatly deepened my devotion for Baba. I was starting to feel and experience *bhakti*.

On one other day at the hospital there were only three margiis present – myself, brother Dharmaputra from California, and brother Abhik Kumar (later Dada Abhidevananda). Baba was sitting up on the porch as usual but there was something in the air. The three of us did not talk or make any plan, we just all three walked nonchalantly toward the far end of the building as if we were planning a walk down to the river. When we got to the end of the building we all three turned and started up the stairs. I know that my *guru mantra* was going continuously very fast inside me.

We resolutely walked up the two flights of stairs and stepped out onto the upper porch. The distance must have been at least 20 meters. All three of us started walking down the porch toward Baba. As we started to approach where Baba was sitting, the

guards became aware of us and started to turn toward us and to move toward us. There were the two plainclothes guards and the guard in uniform with the rifle. As they turned toward us I could see that they had looks of complete horror on their faces, because they were moving in very slow motion. We walked right through them and fell to our knees at Baba’s feet.

Again without any discussion or planning we started *guru puja*. I had my hands and head on Baba’s right knee, Dharmaputra was next to me and had his hands and head in Baba’s lap. Abhika Kumar had his hands on Baba’s left knee. The guards continued to try to reach us, but they could only move in slow motion and could not reach us despite being only a few feet away. They were in some kind of time warp.

We completed all the verses of *guru puja* and Baba was very, very happy. Light was coming from His face. Finally He said that we should allow these guards to do their duty and we should leave now. We namaskared to Baba and then very slowly started to move away from Him and back slowly down the porch toward the stairs. The guards came back to normal movement but they were so confused by their experiences that despite having weapons they could only shoo us away with their hands as we slowly backed down the porch.

When I was about to leave Patna, I went to the hospital to see Baba sitting on the porch in the morning and to take my leave of Him. I was feeling totally fulfilled as I rode the rickshaw downtown. I felt that He had given me everything that I needed and that I was ready to depart. I had not felt any pain of separation; I felt fulfilled. But when I looked up at Baba and said my mental goodbye, my heart suddenly became filled with a feeling that I had never felt before – the pain of leaving and a sense of loss or longing for Him. I knew what this was and I appreciated that He had given this to me also. I was very intellectual at the time, but I got the feeling of devotion and the deep pain of leaving Him.

In the spring, we had a wonderful Vermont Diocese Retreat at a Quaker Camp in Plymouth Union, Vermont. I remember teaching *tandava* to the margiis and the kiirtan Saturday night was amazing. The Quakers are very spiritual people and this camp had been a center for them for 50 years at that time. When we did kiirtan the vibration was

very high and many people, myself included saw numerous bright blue balls of light floating up near the corners of the room. I felt that these luminous bodies were around the spiritual vibration of the center and attracted by the strong kiirtan. Over 60 margiis attended the retreat, all of them local. This was a period of great activity in Ananda Marga in the Northeast.

Soon, Madhurii established an intensive LFT training program in Los Altos Hills, which I attended. Soon we had many dedicated people available to start projects and do pracar all over the country. This dynamic training made many LFTs and WTs and really accelerated the pace of Ananda Marga in New York Sector. Many of the LFTs are now leaders in Ananda Marga.

The training center was called AMRIT (Ananda Marga Retreat for Intensive Training) and was a resource for margiis all over the San Francisco Bay Area. Madhurii also wanted to do publications, so we established AMRIT Publications. We produced *Baba’s Grace*, *The Great Universe*, *Teaching Asanas*, and many smaller booklets for classes and *pracar*. These books were produced at night after the trainees had gone to sleep.

We started the ERAWS training center in the *jagrti* in Washington D.C. to provide a more focused LFT training for margiis who wanted to do social service. I was transferred to RU Secretary. We decided to set up an Office in Carbondale and Akhanda Darshan and I moved there. The Renaissance Universal Journal was conceived there, becoming a very good publication for several years. We scheduled what was called the Problem of the Day Seminar. We brought in eight Proutists and margiis and picked a couple of topics. The eight broke into small groups; each one researched the problem chosen and presented a final paper to the group after a week of research and discussion on Prout’s solution.

The pressure to try to get Baba out of jail increased. This was the time of the Emergency and things looked tough. We wanted to organize a large protest march and contact as many government officials as we could to try to get some help for Ananda Marga and others in India who were being arrested and jailed without charges, as well as

to try to get Baba released. We brought about 60 people to Washington and we all jammed into the house there, sleeping like sardines packed tight. We organized the Universal Freedom March, which went from the Washington Monument to the Capitol building. We were not very successful in building coalitions, but it was a good learning experience and we built a team of hard-working margiis who have continued to do Ananda Marga work since then.

I was in charge of Baba's Release in New York Sector. After the march we kept a team together in Washington to work with the State Department and the news media to try and keep the Indian Emergency and Baba's case in the forefront.

I was sent to Nepal to try to secure travel documents and asylum in Europe for several workers who had escaped to Nepal. I spent a couple of very good weeks in Kathmandu with Shivananadaji, Chitgatanandaji and others. I was staying in an apartment with several trainees who had had to leave India. Shivananda would take us to special places for sadhana and he greatly encouraged us in our sadhana.

I returned via Nairobi where Ramanandaji had been expelled from the country. There was starvation in some of the tribal areas and Dada was intent on publicizing this to raise funds to help them. The government was concerned that news of starvation might keep tourists away and wanted the news suppressed. It was wonderful to attend *dharmacakra* in Africa and see our universal culture and the beautiful colorations of the local cultures blending in.

I went on to Geneva, where I met with the United Nations High Commission on Refugees and obtained some travel documents for the dadas in Kathmandu and some support in helping to get them out. I then went to London, where I met with Amnesty International to get their help as well. I was in ten countries in 30 days. I had a layover in Tashkent in Central Russia. I remember that the people I saw at the airport had a grey aura as if they had no life or creativity in left in them. I saw this as an image of what Communism had done to their culture and their individual spirit. It was the most depressing place I had ever experienced.

After returning from this trip we continued to work in Washington to get Baba released. Cirasmita, who was an LFT, and I, got married on Baba's Birthday at the *jagrti* in Washington D.C. Soon after that we moved back to Vermont.

### Chandra Shekhara

*St. Petersburg, Florida*

In February, 1975, I was in India working on Baba’s defense work. I met with the lawyers and attended a court session. The day of the court appearance there were large numbers of guards surrounding the courtroom that was just outside Bankipur Central Jail. After court the lawyers left and I went to try to see Baba. Although it was the regular day for people to visit Baba, no one else was being let in because of the court activities and because there were security concerns related to a large Communist rally being held in Patna that day.

I went to the jailer’s office and in fairly short order was let into the jail and led to Baba’s cell. I had to leave my pen and paper in the jail office, though, which concerned me since Dada Pranavanandaji had asked me to get posting instructions from Baba for a number of new acaryas who were waiting in Kolkata until they had their new postings.

It is funny what you remember about such times. The first thing I recalled as I started to enter Baba’s cell was the sight of little birds flitting through openings around the top of the room. I thought that these were fortunate little beings. After salutations to Baba, He called me closer and I sat by His cot and was able to hold onto His arm. Baba was lying on His side and was covered by a lightweight shawl. He had a slight cold on that day.

I don’t remember everything that transpired but several things still stand out. First, I got all jumbled up with the postings. Although I’d memorized the names of the *acaryas* to be posted, Baba quickly gave both the location and specific task for each acarya. He did it so quickly that it was very hard for me to keep everything straight. I hope they all reached the places they were supposed to be.

During the course of our time together Baba mentioned Indira Gandhi and said that she was not a bad person but that she was not doing good things and should be told to change this.

At one point I was saying something and Baba closed His eyes. Somehow I knew I should be quiet so I stopped and internally said my *Guru Mantra*. Just as I did this, a big smile appeared on Baba’s face. It was a very special moment.

Soon the guards said it was time to leave and I had to part from Baba. After leaving the cell and walking back to the jailer’s office, I remembered stories that people told about wonderful scents that people sometimes smelled in Baba’s presence. While I was in the cell I didn’t notice anything unusual, but I smelled my hands that had been touching Baba’s arm. There was a beautiful floral fragrance – not something I’d ever smelled before and I felt quite blessed by Baba.

Three and a half years later I saw Baba again, this time in Jamaica. Although there are many wonderful stories about Baba in Jamaica, three stand out for me.

The first was when Baba arrived at the airport. My wife Kalyani and I had arrived just a short time before Baba’s plane was due to arrive. We were the last on our flight to go through the immigration check and the room was empty. Kalyani thought it would be a good idea to stay there so we could see Baba without lots of other people around. So we sat down and waited. One of the Jamaican officials came over and said we wouldn’t be able to stay in the room. I told him that my wife was expecting (this was true) and asked if we could just sit there awhile. The official gladly obliged. It was a short time later that Baba’s plane arrived and just a few minutes before a large group of *acaryas* rounded a corner and Baba was in the middle of them. As He turned the corner we gave namaskar to Him and He stopped and gave a long, beautiful namaskar to us. The energy was so intense and wonderful that I couldn’t stand up.

The second story involves my personal contact that took place on Baba’s first full day in Jamaica. After entering Baba’s room and doing *pranam*, Baba called me near Him and asked my name. After I said my name, Baba told me that although I might be a very small boy yet I could do great things. He said that even though an atom is small it still possesses great energy. Baba then said a blessing in Sanskrit and touched the top of my head. It felt as if a fountain of energy began bubbling up from the top of my head – it was a wonderful feeling. And then the personal contact was over.

The final story from Jamaica involved my desire to hear Baba say “*Parama Purusa*.” I always felt that when Baba said this it had special force because He knew directly of what He spoke. Well, one of Baba’s talks turned out to deal with *Parama Purusa* the Great. Not only did I greatly enjoy the talk, but my duty during the *Dharma*

*Maha Cakra* time was to transcribe the tapes of Baba’s talks so that a written version could be distributed to the margiis. In this way I got to listen through the talk many times and got to hear Baba say *Parama Purusa* until my heart was content.

# CHAPTER THREE:

## I THINK EVERYONE ENJOYED THEMSELVES?

> “*For Jinaniis (those who want to realize God only with intellect), the path of Dharma is a razor’s edge, but for Sadhakas (spiritual practitioners), it is like a flower. Since God is attainable only by devotion, therefore He is with you.*”

Following an international outcry and independent judicial inquiries, and a change in the political climate of India, Baba was cleared of all charges and released from prison in 1978. Soon afterwards He embarked on a world tour. Although not allowed into the United States for political reasons, He did visit Jamaica, and many margiis attended His darshans and *Dharma Maha Cakras* there. He also traveled to Switzerland, Iceland, Germany and other European countries, and again North American margiis made the journey to be with Him.

### Vishvamitra

*Asheville, North Carolina*

My first contact with Baba was when He came to Jamaica in 1979. I had gone to India in 1978 and spent six weeks trying to see Baba – this was towards the end of His time in jail – and I was not successful. I had extended my stay there, was quite sick with dysentery, and I had to leave and come back to my job and family. Baba was released three weeks after I left. It was quite meaningful that after all that effort to see Him, finally we were going to see Him in Jamaica. My mother and son went with me.

The setting was so lovely, an employee-owned cooperative hotel. All the margiis were staying there, high on a mountain. Baba was staying in a margii’s house down below. He came up in His car, and the first time I saw Him, He was ascending the stairs to the lecture hall. My dear friend, Shaktideva was Baba’s bodyguard and he came leading Baba up the stairs. Each day Baba gave darshan in a very intimate setting in that hotel. Many of Baba’s lectures were very well-suited for this Western audience. Baba spoke of the many psychic issues He knew many Westerners were beset with. His darshans went straight to the heart, trying to help us work through some of these issues that plagued the Western mind.

Daily, we would go down to His house and see Him come out for field walk. The occasion finally arose when I was to see Baba and have PC. I went in the room and did *sastaung pranam*. Baba was seated across the room on His bed. I sat, and Baba said, “Come closer, my son.” It took about four times of His coaxing me until I was seated as close as I could possibly get, next to the bed. Baba began by asking my name and duty. Then He said to do something great for the suffering humanity, something concrete. I was looking up at Baba, probably in awe, and Baba wanted to help me relax. And He said, “Remember, something small can be great,” and gestured with His hand. I was overwhelmed with that charge to do something concrete for humanity. To accentuate the point He reached over with His hand and pushed the corners of my mouth up and said, “And smile a little!” It’s been easier to smile since.

This was most of the communication in PC. But really it was about the tremendous vibration of love and affection that Baba radiated that was so overwhelming.

I went back outside and sat in meditation, feeling this overwhelming urge to be closer and closer to Baba. My mind was only riveted on that, how to get field walk, to be with Baba as much as I could. One opportunity came up the next day. Dada Ramananda said “We need to get visas for the trip to South America – will you go down and get visas for this party of 12?” And I thought, “Well, yes, this is an opportunity to do something for Baba. Of course.”

So I went to the consulate to get the visas, and it occurred to me to get a visa for myself, in case the opportunity arose that I could go. I wasted no time in asking Dada if it was possible if I could go with Baba and His party, not thinking about any other circumstances, that my son and mother were there, and I had a job. Amazingly, he had always been quite reserved with me about trying to see Baba in India, and now he said, “Yes, it will be fine, if the pilot says it’s okay.” That just fired my imagination, but knowing it was all in His hands. I was in a total state of surrender.

I was given opportunity to go on field walk at the University of West Indies. I was walking alongside Baba, who said that this university will play a great role in the Caribbean *samaj*, and Jamaica will play a great role in this part of the world, because of its openness. We noted a mural on the wall which reflected the multi-cultural theme Baba was talking about; it had people of all the islands, like it was the hub of some melting pot of the islands. Baba was an extremely fast walker, to get exercise, but He was constantly educating us about culture and the environment, plants, the place we were in, and its place in human society.

DMC was scheduled that night. The feeling was even more exaggerated because we felt Baba was going to be leaving soon. I had the opportunity to do *tandava* that night, with torch and dagger. My mother was there, and my son, and they were totally enthralled by the vibration of Baba. Baba gave His talk, which was aimed right at the heart of all the listeners. When Baba gave the *mudra*, there were many people crying, screaming, going into *samadhi*; the vibration simply knocked many people over.

For me, I felt this tremendous warming sensation in my chest, and felt as if my body was lifted off the floor. Looking at Baba, it was as if He were enveloped in light, the room was filled with that light, and I was in some other consciousness, blissful. When

He left the room, He left very slowly, and as He walked along the open side of the building, He stopped and spoke with people, giving namaskar. It was not like many of the DMCs I saw later in India; Baba lingered. The feeling was He couldn’t resist so much love coming from the margiis, such an intense exchange. And we all bathed in that.

And then Baba finally left. We were told that Baba was leaving the next day and there would be a going-away darshan at the airport. There was a private plane coming from Caracas. Oddly enough, the airport was on strike, all the personnel gone, and the airport belonged to Ananda Marga, and Baba, with nobody else there. It was a celebratory mood, and Baba gave a brief talk. Then Baba and His party were leaving for Caracas. I had my bag with me just in case the pilot said I could go. Finally the pilot came out and was inspecting the plane. I said to him, “Sir, is it possible I could go with this party?”

“No, it’s not possible, we’re overweight already.”

Then Yatiishvarananda said to him, “But he’s a vegetarian, he’s very light!” The pilot laughed but he didn’t give any ground, “No, I’m sorry.” Then everyone started getting on the plane.

I was standing there and something inside said, “Get on.” So I got on the plane. I was standing inside and I saw the baggage area, and I just sat on top of some bags. The copilot came back and saw me, and said nothing, and closed the door. The plane started taxiing. Then the pilot looked in the rearview mirror and said, “Hey you.” I thought he was about to throw me on the tarmac. He says, “Come forward in the plane so we can balance the weight.” So I came forward and the only seat left was just across the aisle from Baba. So I sat there, enjoying His grace, being with Him. He was fulfilling my desire.

Ramananda was telling the pilot in the cockpit that he need not be concerned about this flight. “Who is that man in white back there?” the pilot asked. In terms that the pilot could understand the stature of this gentleman Ramananda said, “[He’s like] Jesus Christ. He’s flying this plane, so you need not worry.” So we had this marvelous flight through this star-glittering night across the Caribbean.

We arrived at Caracas early in the morning. We were met with a beautiful South American kiirtan reverberating off the walls of the airport.

On field walk the next day we went to a botanical garden. They opened the rear entrance, so we had it to ourselves, compliments of Baba. Baba was walking with the assistance of Dada Ramananda, and He said, “I have to take the assistance of someone because of my eyesight. You know they tried to poison me in prison, but they did not succeed, did they?”

Then He looked up and pointed at a plant about thirty yards away. He began to wheel off the genus, species, and said the Portuguese brought that plant here, and He talked about its medicinal value. He went on like this, pointing out plants, whether they were they domestic or imported, their qualities.

We came to a beautiful waterfall. I was carrying Baba’s chair, and He said, “I’ll sit for awhile.” I looked up at the waterfall and I couldn’t understand where all this water came from, since there was no watershed. I had always had the question, “Does Baba know everything I’m thinking; is He with me all the time?”

And Baba said, “My son, do you know the sources of water from a waterfall?”

“I know some.”

“What are they?”

“There’s snowfall and rainfall, and also rivulets and springs.”

“Yes, those are two sources. What is the third source?” He asked with a twinkle in His eye.

Then He answered, “The third source is in an area where there is a very high water table and small undulating hills, the water breaks out at the highest point. It’s called the artesian principle.”

And then He said, with that smile that melts one, “Now, my son, do you understand?” So many things I understood then.

He began a discourse about the derivation of the word water, in Greek and Latin. He talked about the language tree, and bifurcation of language, which later He wrote about. Afterwards a didi and I sat down and said, “What are these field walks all about?” We decided Baba was teaching us about our relationship to everything: to society and nature; natural remedies; the impact society has on the environment; and the potential for

working together – a better cooperation with nature and between people. Baba was opening our eyes, expanding our minds about what the potential was.

There were only about 40 margiis in Caracas. I had the chance to go again on another field walk. This time we went to a zoological garden. Baba talked about the role of Caracas and Venezuela. He said they will play a role in fostering world government because of their openness and universal mind, that they were in a good position, not so identified with politics like other parts of South America and the U.S. We were taking notes.

The first stop we made was where the lions and tigers were. “What family do these animals belong to?” Baba asked.

“Baba, they belong to the cat family.”

“Is that so?” And He pointed over to the lions. “Notice the nature of their head, it’s angular,” and He described the lion’s intestinal structure, it’s claws.

He said, “Look at the tiger, it has a rounded bony structure, in contrast to the lion. Does not the lion run in packs? And the tiger, does it have the same behavior? No, it’s a solitary animal. How is it that these animals belong to the same family?”

“We don’t know, Baba, that’s the way they are classified.”

He said, “No, this animal belongs to the canine family, and the tiger belongs to the feline.” So Baba was giving us a lesson in rational zoology, teaching us how to think. Since then I’ve read a zoological treatise on this, that there’s a continuum between cats and dogs, and with some types you can hardly tell the difference.

In the reptile house, He was pointing out snakes. He asked me the name of a particular snake, and I happened to know it. He said, “Oh, you know so much about reptiles.”

We were walking by the alligators, and an egret was picking the meat out of the teeth of an alligator. One of the South Americans said, “Do you know what they call this animal?” pointing to the alligator.

“No.”

“Baba, we call it Baba.”

Then Baba said of Himself, “But this Baba is not so dangerous.” He continued, “You see, that bird and alligator have a pact; the bird takes the meat and the alligator gets its teeth cleaned. This is a good example of coordinated cooperation.”

We walked by the seals and sea lions. Baba explained, “Although they have rudimentary flippers, they once were land animals, and they evolved back into the sea to escape predators.” And in my mind I was thinking, that must include whales, too. I was thinking about the harvesting of blue whales by the Japanese; they were threatened with extinction. I said, “Baba, are the blue whales about to become extinct?”

And He looked at me and said, “My boy, people with blue eyes and blond hair will one day be extinct. People will come here from another planet and observe the ruins of our civilization. And they will say there was once a very advanced civilization here that built very tall buildings and had advanced technology. But they couldn’t learn to live together. My son, do you want that history should read this way?”

And I said, “No, Baba.”

“Then you will have to perform your mission.”

Baba is saying to us that we are writing history. The life of a spiritualist or sadhaka is one of some social action, and some self-realization. And we can make a difference. We must do something within the scope of our capacity.

Baba gave DMC there, in Caracas. I didn’t experience the same vibration as Jamaica. There was a similar vibration, but not as intense as the first time. But one of the other things that did occur was a personal message from Baba.

An *acarya* came out and said to me, “We asked Baba a question about society and what our role was in dealing with social problems, and He told us we should talk to you Vishvamitra, because you have a good understanding of social dynamics.” It was a special message; Baba was saying, “Use your talent, Vishvamitra, in dealing with the social environment, and to correct social ills.” So I took that to heart.

And soon Baba was leaving again. We went to the airport, and it was a very sweet departure. There was something lighter about it. The feeling was that though Baba’s body was going on this plane, Baba was still with us, that we would never be separated. That was my personal feeling. Baba always taught me, that the relationship was like hide and

seek: you want to see Him, but the physical body wasn’t always available. His darshan wasn’t always available. But the vibration that He was giving if you surrender, in your sadhana, was always there. He wanted us to relate to who He really was, on the deepest level, Anandamurti, embodiment of bliss. And not to fawn on the physical, although that was precious to us, and we miss that.

**Jiivesh**
*Blacksburg, Virginia*

My first physical encounter with Baba was in September of 1979 in Kingston, Jamaica. After attending several of the most beautiful darshans with only about 200 other devotees, I had the opportunity for Personal Contact (PC). I had been intoxicated by Baba’s beauty and purity. I had heard all the stories but the reality was overwhelming. He was so attractive that I could not take my eyes off of Him.

After one darshan, I was told by a close senior *avadhuta* that Baba would give PC upon His return to the house where He was staying and I should run down there immediately. Without any further thought I took off running for Him. I was not alone in this endeavor; however, I had some athletic background, which may have helped. But surprisingly, one brother, an unathletic accountant, finished ahead of me after running through a thick wooded area instead of down the winding roads. We were both in the front of a group of wild-eyed devotees running as if their lives depended on it. This brother was bleeding, he had lost his glasses, his shirt was torn and his ankle sprained – but what desire! He later became a Whole-Timer (WT).

Anyway, I arrived at the house and was second to see Baba until several senior WTs brought a few brothers to the front of the line. This was especially deflating for me because two of these brothers were from my unit and all three of us had shared a bit of ego tension; needless to say both of them were placed right in front of me. I couldn’t stop these feelings of jealousy but didn’t have long to entertain them, as I was soon grilled by Baba’s Personal Assistant, Dada Ramanandaji, where I swore to do (or that I had done) whatever he asked or required of me.

I meditated while waiting to go into His room (I was drenched in sweat and my legs were throbbing but I would not move for half-bath or anything else), hoping that nothing would prevent me from having an opportunity to see Him. I was surprised when each of my two fellow unit brothers came out from PC looking very normal. I didn’t have time to think long about this puzzling reaction because Ramanandaji pulled me close and asked if I knew about *sastaung pranam* and, after replying yes, I was literally pushed through the door of His room.

So there I was, sweaty and nervous. I awkwardly performed a sprawling *sastaung pranam*. When I sat up, I namaskared Baba very slowly and carefully. He said sweetly, “Come closer, come closer, my boy.”

I moved forward and sat as close to Him as etiquette would allow. Baba was radiantly smiling and sitting on the edge of His bed. He asked my name and where I was from. I played along and told Him, internally thinking how beautiful He was and how I never wanted to leave His presence. He paused and then again said, “Come closer, my little boy.”

When Baba asked me to come closer a second time (I was already sitting close to and in front of Him) I moved up so that my knees slid under the bed and my nose was an inch from His feet. Without thinking I began to massage His feet. They were so soft and I was intoxicated by His fragrance – I was really out of my mind. He permitted me to massage His feet for several minutes without talking; it was an ecstatic experience.

He then said softly that all over our planet so many are suffering. His voice was filled with pain. “You must become an ideal boy and serve the suffering humanity.”

I said, “I will, Baba.”

Then He placed His hand on my head, His thumb was at my *trikuti* and the palm of His hand rested on my *sahasrara cakra*. I experienced this in what seemed like slow motion. He closed His eyes and repeated a lengthy *shloka*. I was watching His face but when I felt energy moving up my spine I closed my eyes and experienced a powerful burst up to the top of my head. After maybe 20 or 30 seconds, He finished the *shloka* and brought His hands together in namaskar. I returned the namaskar and then returned to massage His feet with the top of my head blissfully on fire. After a few minutes, I was told my time was up by someone outside the room.

I backed out of the room namaskaring with my eyes on Him. Once outside I did not look at anyone, and went and sat down in the front yard of the house and meditated for much of the afternoon, internally replaying His voice, His appearance, His fragrance and His touch and feel. From that day forward I have continuously experienced the most pleasant “burning” sensation at my *sahasrara cakra* and it is activated with my *mantra*, with His image, or with the thought of Him.

### Arati

*Ithaca, New York*

Baba somehow stole His way into my life and captured my heart when I was 21. I had not met Him, but had only heard about Him. There was something about His picture and about Baba Nam Kevalam that had a magnetic pull for me. From the first time I heard Baba’s name something in me stirred. I was a student at the University of Stony Brook in Long Island.

Dada Yatiishvarananda was giving a talk at someone’s house. I went there with a couple of friends. I remember hearing about Baba and wanting right then and there to get up and learn meditation. Strange sensations overcame me and my friends drew me back to reality. My friends were not affected at all by the talk and so we left. It wasn’t until several months later that I again had the opportunity to come in contact with Ananda Marga, and this time I took initiation.

That was in 1972. I did not meet Baba until 1979. As a young margii I had some Baba dreams. There was one where Baba was in wrestling clothes and was wrestling. I thought this was an absurd dream. Soon after that dream I heard that Baba had a fondness for wrestling and would have his workers wrestle for Him and sometimes with Him.

In 1973, as a new margii, I moved into the *jagrti* in Boston and worked on organizing retreats and did office work for Ananda Marga. I soon met my husband, and we got married and over the next four years had two children. When Baba was released from jail we went to Jamaica with our children where I met Baba in person for the first time.

It was everything you would expect it to have been – the culmination of years of devotion, and the depths of clash and despair. In my life experience Baba has brought me closer to Him mostly through much clash. The clash was that I was not able to get Personal Contact and my husband was told he was “disqualified” from attending darshan, as he was an ex-wholetimer. But through this little drama Baba accomplished much with me.

It is hard to put into words my experiences while in Jamaica. They were all so subtle – Baba playing with me, answering my thoughts, my small wishes, teaching me

things through serendipitous happenings. It is too much to write and to others it would be so simple and insignificant. But for me they were interactions through which He communicated with me... such as looking into His eyes, seeing His pain when he hurt His shoulder, finding myself alone on the street with Him when He left darshan.

I wanted to be alone with Him. PC was not to be for me. But during one darshan my four-year-old daughter insisted she had to leave and go to the bathroom. I tried to put her off, but in the end I had to leave the darshan with her to avoid her having an "accident." As we were returning we saw that Baba was leaving the building and heading for His car with one assistant. I immediately ran with my daughter and stood by the car door and gave Baba my namaskar. He fulfilled my wish to be alone with Him.

My husband and I were extremely sad because due to the disqualification rules, he was not permitted to attend darshan. But even this Baba tempered two times. Once we were all outside the darshan hall. Baba arrived one hour early for darshan. The guards were frantic that the room should be full before Baba entered, so they ordered everyone to enter the darshan hall immediately. There was no time to check passes. So my husband and the other disqualified margiis ran in with everyone else. So He fulfilled my wish to sit as a family in darshan.

Another time we were at Baba's house with the children. Everyone was ordered to go the darshan hall. But we did not leave, because my husband was not permitted to attend and I was needing help with the children so I was not able to go alone. So we just rested outside under the trees by Baba's house. Baba was still inside, because He hadn't left for the darshan hall yet. All the other margiis were gone, except for one other family with two children. For some reason the children decided to pick flowers for Baba. Before we knew it, without having thought about it, we were all lined up outside Baba's house and every child had a flower except for my son who was only eight months old.

Baba came out and noticed the children and was in a very happy mood. He bent down and one by one took the flower from each of the children and gave them His namaskar. Next to the children, my husband and I were standing and my husband was holding our son, whose name at the time was Arjuna (later changed to Kapil). Baba went right up to Arjuna and said, "And how about you, little boy?" meaning, does he have a flower too?

Arjuna, who was just an infant and normally extremely shy of strangers, starting leaning forward towards Baba until their faces were an inch apart. Then his little body started to shake, and from somewhere deep inside emerged a very loud and resonate “B - A - B - A” as if spoken by a very strong grown man. We were all amazed and Baba threw back his head and started laughing with joy. Then He took the flowers he had collected from the three little girls and gave them to Arjuna by placing them in my husband’s hand. I still have the flowers till this day. Later that day we were told that Baba shared the story with His workers.

Another experience I will share was my experience of DMC. My husband was not permitted to attend, so he stayed back with our sleeping daughter since DMC was to be at midnight. I went with my baby son. I was experiencing tremendous despair that my whole family could not attend, and my mental peace was disturbed and my thoughts were painful. I sat in the back of the room holding my infant son and weeping most of the time. Baba entered and gave His DMC and then it was time for His *mudra*. Many people were shouting and falling into states of bliss. What happened to me was so simple and yet it made an extremely deep impression on me. My mind entered into a deep state of peace. All my negative thoughts and feelings were gone and I entered in a space of total stillness and quietness.

I did not have another opportunity to see Baba for ten more years. In 1989, I traveled to India alone without my family. I was not there for very long but during that brief visit Baba completely pulverized my soul. What a magician… just a little contact with Him can completely transform us. Again for me the contacts were subtle and personal. I did not have PC, nor did I have a conversation with Baba. I remember setting Him four tests, privately, by which I would know that He knew I was there, and He passed all four of them. I got to see Him often when He went out for field walk several times a day. We had a special darshan for Westerners and I sat in the front right before Him. That day I learned that darshan is not about our seeing Baba, but about Baba seeing us. By His looking at us, we are transformed.

I experienced many things about Baba during that visit. I saw His sweetest joy and smile, and I heard His deafening storms as I sat outside His door for hours. I learned the ins and outs of what got one past a guard and into the part of the building where Baba’s quarters were. During that brief visit, He plunged my mind into the state of the Void and He set my small being with its attachments and desires aflame in a devouring and all-consuming internal fire. While so many people have stories of actual physical contact and conversations, I cannot boast of those. But I can certainly attest to deep internal transformation from interactions only visible to Him and myself.

When it was time for me to leave India, just then Baba came out for field walk. There was a large gathering and I was standing in the back. By some magic the group seemed to part and there was a distinct path for me to walk down which would place me right outside the window of Baba’s car. Without a moment’s hesitation I walked down that little path and then the crowd closed in again. I was standing right outside Baba’s car window. There was no one else on that side of the car. Baba first turned and namaskared the large group out of the other window and then turned toward the center. It was not clear whether or not He would turn my way, as I was the only one on that side of the car. At that moment some thoughts entered my mind about some work I might do for Him. I wondered if Baba wanted me to do that work. I thought that if He does He will surely turn in my direction and give me a namaskar. At that moment He turned in my direction and looked directly at me and gave me a deep namaskar. I felt a wave overcome me. Baba’s car drove out and I sat on the ground and did sadhana. I feel He blessed me on that day to do some work for Him.

A few months before Baba left His physical body, I became quite ill and used to cry for days on end without knowing the cause. I knew that Baba was not well, and I heard He might be coming to the U.S. for treatment, so I decided to knit a green wool scarf for Him. I would knit and cry, not knowing why. One morning I had a dream early in the morning of Baba and a black spot had come to sit on His perfectly pure white attire. I was extremely disturbed by the dream. Later that day I heard that Baba had left. That very day I stopped crying. I now knew why I had been crying all those months and now my tears were all used up.

He comes now and again in a dream... when my desire is intense. I keep a diary of my dreams... they are very simple... just sweet moments with Baba very close.

### Jyotishvar

*Los Angeles, California*

I was initiated by Ac. Yatiishvarananda Avt. in Carbondale, Illinois, which was a very big unit back then. I'll tell about the experiences I had in Iceland. It convinced me that Baba had very special powers.

It was part of Baba's tour of Europe. I was very lucky to be in Volunteers in Social Service (VSS) at the time. I had always been interested in martial arts. VSS was just getting started, and since Baba was coming to the West, we realized that Baba would need security, a bodyguard, crowd control. I was living in Los Angeles with Kamalakanta. We were training hard every day, rigorous about our sadhana and training.

We both flew to Europe. Dada Shantabodhananda was in charge of New York Sector VSS. He assigned me to be in charge of all security there – it was a tremendous opportunity, a blessing, and I was completely dedicated to it. We organized all the guards around Baba's house 24 hours a day. The crowds were very large, and security was very important. We were always checking on the security, and would sneak up on Baba's house in the middle of the night to try to catch the guards off-guard. I went on all the field walks myself, with four VSS guards around Baba.

One day Dada Shantabodhananda brought me to Baba's house and said, “Wait right here, don't move!” He went in and then ran out, excited, and said, “Baba wants you to be His bodyguard!” I was so excited. Any time Baba left His house to go on field walk, or go to darshan, I was always at His side. I carried a bag with a thermos of milk and water – a time-honored tradition of Baba's field walk supplies. I was lucky to be able to serve Baba like this.

In the night I shone the flashlight for Baba wherever He walked. At the time of darshan it was kind of like being a Secret Service agent for the president. You were the closest person to Baba, and looking out at the crowd constantly, ready to die for Baba. There was no question, if shots were fired, you'd stand in front of Baba and take them. There was no doubt in my mind that I would have done that, or any of the other VSS guards also. We were experiencing a tremendous exalted state, it was like a kind of *samadhi*, but we could function, we could do everything, we were 150 percent, not

spaced out or drunken. We enjoyed tremendously, but we were working overtime always, solving problems. It was the greatest moment in my life.

Then we got to Iceland. There was a karate dojo right across from where Baba stayed. I trained there everyday and got to know those brothers there. I was very much into my martial arts then, and still am. We arranged to have DMC at the karate dojo.

During *Dharma Maha Cakra* (DMC) someone had to guard Baba’s house. One VSS brother would make a sacrifice not to go to DMC but stay and guard Baba’s house, all alone. That brother always got some kind of special blessing. This was the first time that no one volunteered for that duty. The dadas took me aside and said, “Jyotishvar, you’ve got to take this duty, you’ve been to so many DMCs. You can be Baba’s bodyguard the whole time He is here, and go on all field walks, but we have to ask that you guard Baba’s house during DMC.”

I don’t know, I was so selfish. I think today I would volunteer, but I was young, and I refused repeatedly. I still can’t believe I did that. I even asked all the karate guys I was training with if they would guard Baba’s house. They said they’d make a ring around Baba’s house and no one would enter. And I told the dadas, but they said it had to be a margii. None of the karate guys wanted to get initiated. So I would have to do it. I was very bitter about it.

I was always in a frenzy while organizing the security, but now while everyone went to the DMC, with all this tremendous energy, everyone running to the dojo. I was the only person there, and I became crazy. I was pacing wildly around the room; I couldn’t imagine how much pain I was in. I don’t know why, I just resented it so much. I was supposed to guard the door, but I went inside Baba’s room. As soon as I walked in, I was just caught up in the energy. The room was thick with energy, really amazing. I went up to Baba’s bed. And there were His shoes. His shoes were sitting there, really well worn. He had gone to the DMC wearing the slippers we had given Him. I sat down there, abandoning my duty – anyone could have broken in – but I sat down there and put my hands in His shoes.

Just in that second, I don’t know if I went in *samadhi* or what, I don’t recall becoming unconscious, just at that moment I heard all this racket outside, and I thought, “Oh no, there’s an attack on Baba’s house, and I’m not doing my duty!” So I got up and

went outside to my post and the whole room was filled with margiis, coming back from the DMC. But not enough time had passed for it. I was completely confused. It couldn’t have been more than five or ten minutes, in my mind. Baba came in, and people were shouting “*Parama Pita Baba Ki!*” Baba was just about to go through the door, and He stopped. I was trying to look very VSS but I was completely disorganized. Baba turned to the crowd. He said, “I think everyone enjoyed themselves?”

And He looked right over at me, I was inches away from Him, and He looked right in my eyes, and said, “I think everyone enjoyed himself?” He had a big smile, looking at me, and then He disappeared. Then all the dadas came up and said, “You see, Baba gave you a special blessing.” But they didn’t know that two hours had been erased from my mind.

At the very end of Baba’s visit there, we were at the airport, and there were very few margiis there. Baba was flying on to Europe. I was unable to get a seat on the same plane, but I saw that Baba was sitting in the lobby waiting. I went and sat there with two or three margiis. Baba didn’t say anything. Some Indian dadas were there and Baba spoke to them in some language.

After this He looked down at us and said, “What language was I speaking?” Nobody knew.

I didn’t know but I said tentatively, “Hindi?”

Baba put His face down close to mine and said, “What?”

“Hindi?”

He didn’t respond directly but sat up very happy and pleased, so I guessed that I had guessed right. Then He looked at me and held out His five fingers and said, “You know so much about languages. Which languages do you know? English?”

I didn’t speak any other languages at the time. But Baba seemed very intent that He wanted to be able to count on His five fingers all the languages I could speak. At that time I could only speak English, so I thought it was strange. But as the years rolled by, I developed a very strong interest in foreign languages. I lived in Germany and became fluent in German, then I studied Chinese in Taiwan and became conversational, then moved to Japan where I have lived for eight years and learned Japanese.

So I need one more language to fulfill Baba’s prediction. That’s what’s great about Baba. He could have told me anything. “Oh, your mother’s name is Betty and your father’s name is William, you were born in Illinois” – He could have told me anything and I would have gone, “Wow, this guy is a magician.” He didn’t do that; He said something that on the surface looked like He made a mistake. He didn’t try to impress me. It was to leave an indelible mark that now twelve years later I don’t forget. And also perhaps influenced the course of my life.

After Baba left I went back to where He had stayed. There were so many things there that Baba had used, towels, a brush. And the margiis and dadas had just cleaned it out. But left on Baba’s bed was this foam mattress, so I thought, “I’ll sleep on this mattress.” I slept on it that night and it was fairly wonderful; I had extraordinary dreams. I also got a small hand towel. I was playing the violin at the time, and I used to use that to keep my instrument in. I also found a disposable razor that Baba had used. I kept it and used it for months, every day. I would tell people I’d been using this disposable razor every day for four months and it was still sharp as ever. To me it was a kind of miracle.

**Aditi**

*Albuquerque, New Mexico*

We saw Baba in Jamaica! I had never seen Baba before. I came to Ananda Marga, really enjoying the meditation, but very ambivalent about gurus, or anything devotional. I came from a very scientific, analytical family, and I have a scientific career. And I was known as being quite skeptical about those things.

But I strongly wanted to meet Baba, to maintain my skepticism, probably. It was a big deal that He was coming. We were living near Denver, the only margiis there, and they forgot to call us. Finally someone remembered and told us. And we were on a plane in 24 hours, which was exciting and chaotic and a little crazy.

Seeing Baba at the airport, I’d never experienced anything like it. It was late at night, He came down, and we all ran madly towards His plane. I’m sure there were security people there but it didn’t make any difference, we were all out on the runway. One tall brother suddenly flung his arms back and fell straight back, and I was watching his head bounce on the cement. I was going, “Ohmigosh, he’s dead!” And Dada Daneshananda was running by and I said, “Dada!” He looked, and said, “Oh, don’t worry, he’s in *samadhi*,” and we both ran on. And the brother actually was fine later. We were all so excited that people were falling into *samadhi*, and the fact that we could kind of consider that normal, shows what state we were in.

We were all crowded around Him, and Baba seemed to be almost in a light. The biggest thing for us, was that we had our marriage blessed. We had requested that long before we went.

It was one of the reasons we got married then. We were hoping, though it wasn’t guaranteed. In the way all sorts of crazy things happen to people, it wasn’t made easy. First Dada said, “No, it just isn’t going to happen.” And then he said “tomorrow” and it didn’t happen. And then, he said that after a darshan Baba had agreed to do a few of them. Baba went for field walk, and we went down to His house, far from where the darshan house was. Darshan wasn’t supposed to start until 1 p.m. And while we were there someone ran up and said, “Baba’s starting darshan!”

We were hoping that we were going to have wonderful sadhana beforehand, that it was going to be this perfect experience. And in fact we were rushing up the hill, our sides hurting, and I was crying, because I cry easily, and we were saying, “We’re going to miss it!” Running and running. We got there and He started darshan, and we were supposed to have had our marriage blessed before darshan.

We were on one side of the hall, outside, knowing we’d missed our big opportunity. It was really important to me. We were watching Baba, and He finished darshan, and started to get up, and someone came in and whispered in His ear. He looked around, and they’re whispering, and He’s shaking His head. Then He nodded, and they let us come in.

I’d given up by then by about the third shake of His head. I was still crying. But the actual experience, I don’t remember much. He said something about Shiva and Parvati. My only memory was watching His hands, and looking at His rings, and it was a wonderful memory. Those were Baba’s hands. And He gave me the garland, we’d made a garland, and said, “This is your wedding property, so keep it safe.” We still have it around a picture of Baba. It was the first marriage blessing in North America.

In ’84 we saw Baba for about two seconds in Allahabad, and then at DMC in Kanpur and in Agra. We were there to see Baba, and we knew He was in that town, but had no idea how to find him. We finally went out, and bumped into an *acarya*. He made arrangements for us to go to where Baba was. We went by night, dressed in Indian clothes, by bicycle rickshaw. We got there and got just a glimpse of Baba, because it was all over. But I could look through a door and see Baba lying there. And after all that drama, it was very heartening. Then we got His tour schedule, and could see Him in Kanpur. Kanpur was a small gathering, maybe 100 people. I remember doing kiirtan in the tent and opening my eyes and Baba staring right at me. It was such an intimate DMC. I guess everyone has different type of *samskaras* with Baba, and for us a lot of them were all these crazy experiences just trying to see Baba. You could read in the old days how you had to go through all these trials and tribulations to see guru. For a lot of margiis, you’d have to go through all kinds of terrible things – running from the police, being fingerprinted, questioned, and deported.

In 1986 we didn’t even make it out of the airport, because they had our names. We had our new baby with us. Once I got to see Him I didn’t so much care about what kind of interaction I had. In ’89 it was so busy you had to wait and wait for hours to get close to Him, although He did keep giving darshans on Sunday. I went while He was giving lecture on *Shabda Cayanika*, one of the letters, and afterwards Dada Vijayananda translated for us. I managed to give Baba a garland on that trip.

In the darshans in India we never heard a word of English. But it was wonderful not to have to pay attention to the words. In Jamaica I felt like I had to pay attention, but in India I could just watch Him, His expressions, what He did with his hands, his head. It was so nice. You could pay attention to Him instead.

### Satish

*Cuba*

I was only 14 years old when Baba visited Jamaica. It was an experience that no one present will forget. I remember that we made all the preparations in August – there were margiis from all over New York Sector. I was a member of the Volunteers in Social Service (VSS) team. We rehearsed all security measures. We stayed up all night to make sure that our *tandava* was perfect. Our VSS dress was all in good shape.

Wow, it was then announced that Baba would not be coming to Jamaica. How disappointed we were! With tears in our eyes, the margiis from Latin America, Guatemala, and Mexico who had saved so much to come to Jamaica were not able to see their Guru. They all left in disappointment.

In the next two weeks, the news came. Baba would definitely be in Jamaica. I am not sure how those margiis who had saved to come to Jamaica once were able to find a second way to be back in Jamaica on Baba’s arrival. Baba’s arrival was dramatic. Three days before His arrival the meteorologist announced that it would rain for the next five days, and sure enough it started raining.

I remember that Brigadish (Maurice Rhodes) was on his way to the airport to pick up Baba; he would be Baba’s chauffeur. We were all drenched in rain. It was two hours before Baba’s arrival and like a flash of thunder, it stopped raining. All was well. The sun shone at 5 p.m. (which was not normal on a rainy day in Jamaica). All smiled at Baba’s arrival. There at the airport Baba stepped forward and we were all blessed with His love.

It was at the airport that Baba smiled at me and gave me His namaskar. I was a VSS worker and so I was not able to respond in the way I wanted to respond. I was disappointed as I tried to have Personal Contact on four occasions but was not able to do so as I had VSS duties.

In August 1987, I was an LFT in VSS in New York and I had to report in India. At my first reporting I was all prepared to have Personal Contact but again I faced Baba’s play. There would be no more PC as duties were not accomplished in all sectors, so I returned to New York City.

I was disappointed. I thought that Baba did not want to bless me, so I would continue but with much pain in my heart, disappointment and a little anger. It was then in October, 1982, that my acarya, Dada Shankarananda, sent me again to India with one mission – to have PC. He instructed me well. “Do not return to New York Sector until you get PC,” he said, “This is your mission and this is your privilege and this will happen only once in your life.”

I went back to India with one conviction: PC or never to return from India. Those of us who know Dada Shankarananda know of his lion heart and penetrating instructions and force, so I yielded. I arrived in India and after three days I had conjunctivitis, so to my disappointment I was not able to see Baba for two weeks. How I suffered. At last the day came unexpectedly. I was summoned. Then it happened suddenly. I really was there in front of Baba’s room. I had so many thoughts in my head. What will Baba say to me? I had so many questions to ask my guru. There were so many small thoughts running around in my head.

Then I was in front of my guru. Baba beckoned me to come close to Him. He asked me my name and I told Him.

Then He closed His eyes and a few minutes later with eyes closed He said, “Satish, my boy, do you remember when I was in Jamaica at the airport and I looked around and smiled at you?”

I was dumbfounded. All my questions were gone. What more could I have asked when Baba could identify that while He was in Jamaica He smiled at one of His little sons. My mind was transformed into a ball of love and devotion. My guru was with me in all His glory and universal might… and with all His love.

He looked at me and said, “I know that you have many doubts. Do you have any doubts now, my little boy?”

Lost for words, I said, “No, my Baba.”

He said, “You have done a few wrong things in your life. Do you want punishment?”

I said, “Yes, Baba.”

He then waved His stick over my head and said, “You are my little boy, how can I punish you? Go and do work to help save the world. You are one of Baba’s sons.”

He gave me His blessings and I left His room with a new vision and might. It was then that I knew that I had to do something to save the world. I have been trying to do all that I can. Now I am in Cuba, doing His work in the actions that I have made commitments to.

### Gopi

*Monterrey, Mexico*

I entered Ananda Marga in 1974 in the Phillippines, when I was just starting at university. I was brought up in a very strict Catholic family, in a very strict school. After being there for about 13 years, I was searching for something more. One day at the university I saw a dada and I just followed him. I didn't know what he was going to do. And he invited us in to a conference room to talk, and said, "Just for a moment, close your eyes and try to meditate." I didn't even know what meditation was. I remember I just closed my eyes and said, "Please Lord, show me the way." And from that moment, saying those words, everything changed in my life. For some reason I just wanted to be in the yoga house, just wanted to do meditation. Everything changed.

I saw Baba's picture, and thought, "I've been looking for Him for such a long time." I decided to become a Local Full Timer (LFT).

Baba's Personal Assistant, Dada Ramanandaji was touring the Philippines with Dada Adveshanandaji. They passed by my province, Cebu, and I met them. Dada told me, "Come with us." Without asking my parent's permission, I went. I felt Baba manifesting His presence, His love, so much through other people. When the dadas went for *kapalik*, they would always call Gori, one of the margiis, and Gopi, my name, "Gori-Gopi, come with us." I didn't know what *kapalik sadhana* was; I would go to the cemetery but I was not afraid. This was the start of my Ananda Marga experience.

I was a margii for seven years before I saw Baba physically. I had no words to explain what I felt when I saw Him. Someone you have waited for, felt His presence, but not known physically, and suddenly you see Him, and He looks so simple.

There was a field walk. They gave a schedule of who could walk with Baba. For some reason we arrived in a place and Baba didn't like it. We went back. And I felt so depressed, that my chance was over. But the following day Baba was going to go to a convent in Jamaica, and they called me for field walk. This walk was filmed, and when I saw it again, I couldn't believe I was given the chance in this life to be near the guru. I always wanted to be near Baba, but so many people also wanted to and you couldn't be so near Him. As we were walking through the gardens of the convent, Baba started to say

that some flowers in Jamaica actually came from Asia, and people have brought them to America. He started to talk about some fruits. I was the only Filipina there.

He was saying, “There is one jackfruit… who knows Tagalog?”

I was so shocked. And then He looked at me, “What is the Tagalog word for jackfruit?” And before I could answer, He said, “Lanka, lanka?”

And I said, “Yes, Baba.”

Then He started talking about other fruits. There was a fruit named chilemoya here. And He said, “This one in the Philippines, what is the name of that fruit?” And He again turned to me, and before I could answer, He said, “Apis, apis.”

“Yes, Baba.”

They were walking back to His car, got in the car and Ramanandaji called me. At that time, my name was Guruvati. He turned to Baba and said my name, “Guruvati, Baba.” Baba said, “Hmm, Guruvati. Such a small girl, and she knows so many things!”

### Vishala

*New Haven, Connecticut*

In 1977 I was living in Vermont and I took a meditation class with Cirasmita. One thing I remember very strongly is that after my first meditation class I had a dream, and in the dream I was dying. I was feeling total annihilation, like I was dying. All of a sudden I heard “Baba Nam Kevalam,” all this kiirtan singing in my mind. And I just held on to that, and it was very beautiful and peaceful.

The next week I went to meditation class and told them that I thought I had this problem, and told them what happened. And Cirasmita started laughing, “You have one class and you have this experience!”

Later that summer I had another experience. I had many bad habits, a lot of American-style partying and drugs. I had stopped these things when I started meditating, but I still had those kinds of friends. And one night a group of friends came to my house and said, “Come out with us, we’re going to go partying.” A margii sister was living next door to me, it so happens, and she just looked at me, and I felt this intense thing – later I understood what it was – I felt this “No.”

I became extremely afraid, and I said “no” to these people, and slammed the door. And I got afraid and went into my room and put the covers over my head. They kept knocking on the door to get me to go out with them, but I said no. And that night they were all killed in a crash, and I was saved. I hadn’t even been initiated yet. I feel that Baba came through and told me, “No, don’t do that.” I felt that something was happening to me, I didn’t know what it was. I started meditating, going to *dharmacakra*.

Anyway, I went back to college. I was 19, and I still hadn’t been initiated by an *acarya*. I’d already been meditating twice a day for a year, and I got initiated and received first lesson. Baba had been in jail, but He got out of jail, and the following fall He was coming to Jamaica. So everyone was going. I was sick, I had pneumonia, but somehow I had this force in my mind that said, “I’m going to go.” I was a very young margii but I had this pull to go. My roommate was a margii and she was going to go, too. Then I thought, “I can’t go, I’ve had pneumonia, I’m in school, I have all these courses.”

But that night, there were robbers outside our window, trying to get in, and suddenly I was like, “OK, I’m going to go, let’s leave.” As if Baba was making me go.

We got to Jamaica. I was really sick, and young; it was totally crazy. In the beginning my feeling about Baba was “Who is this person? Why am I here? He’s trying to manipulate everybody.” I had this ego. “He doesn’t know me, He doesn’t know who I am. Why am I doing this?” All these thoughts were coming in my mind.

In the middle of the next darshan He gave, He stopped, looked over and pointed at me, and said, “And you.” Everyone around me fell over, and I just felt it was like Baba took me at that moment. And you, you will be my devotee, too. In one look and one word, I felt He captured my heart for the rest of my life.

Before that everyone wanted Personal Contact, and I was so-so about it. But after that I was like, “I want PC!” and I was chasing Didi around trying to arrange it. Baba never gave me that much personal attention when He was in His body. But He gave me a few subtle things that lasted for the rest of my life. Very small things, but they always had to do with my spiritual practices. And I could watch Baba.

One time we were doing *kaoshikii*, and I noticed that people were not doing it properly, and Baba stopped it, saying “No, that’s not proper.” I was like, “I have to learn how to do it, and from now on I’ll do it twice a day.” And I feel like Baba needed me to know at that moment how to do *kaoshikii*, because it was going to affect my health for the rest of my life.

We went to the airport, and stationed ourselves purposely to be where Baba was going to walk in. Some people had bought some French fries. And looking at it, I thought that this was not sentient food. They were fried in lard or something, and somehow I knew. But I thought, “Well, I’ll just take it,” and I ate it.

Right after that, I knew I’d done something very wrong. I shouldn’t have done that. And just then Baba came in, and looked at us, and immediately turned around and went into the bathroom. And it was like we had stationed ourselves for Baba but we had done this one thing that was not right, and He was in a subtle way training us to do the right thing. Not only giving this unbelievable love, but teaching us how thin the line is in

*Tantra*, and how important it is to be very careful about what you’re doing. And I really brought that with me to this day, and I think about that sort of thin line.

Baba is that very thin line, of love but also *Tantra*, and you have to be very disciplined if you’re going to be with Him.

### Sadhana

*Madison, Wisconsin*

I was ripe for the picking by my Guru Baba. In 1973 I was 24 years old. But the seeds that grew into that ripe fruit were planted back when I was 13 years old. That summer I fell quite unexpectedly into a deep pit of depression. The lights went out on any sense of meaning, hope or joy. I simply couldn’t feel anything inside but a bleak emptiness. Running from it into reading alone in my room day and night, I found escape but no solace. I certainly didn’t feel I could tell anyone what was going on.

So I waited it out until the distraction of school resumed, seeming to bring me out of it enough to go on acting as if I were OK, but something in me had changed forever. I had even, before this time, had a deep sense that this world was not as it ought to be, that human life should be full of peace and harmony. But I had seen enough to know all of life was not this way. As the daughter of a U. S. diplomat, I had traveled all my life until then, living in foreign cultures for several years at a time. Fascinating, varied and friendly as other cultures were, I just knew something was not as it should be. In the natural world was the only resonance of that pure harmony I longed for, so I often escaped to the trees and the blue sky.

As I grew older and more sophisticated, I found some meaning in creative expression through music and poetry. It was in playing music, improvising, with others that I experienced letting go my own limited, earth-bound consciousness to fly on the wings of an expanded Self at one with others. But these moments never lasted long before I would be dropped back into my unhappiness.
