Being young at the time of the civil rights movement and in college at the height of the anti-Vietnam War movement, I was able to channel my unhappiness into efforts to change the world. While making my efforts to have an outer impact, I was also reading Taoist and Zen approaches to inner realities of mind and consciousness. These spoke of the kind of inner freedom that rang a bell with me, offering some lasting peace and a way out of the existential emptiness.

In the fall of 1972, after two years of living and traveling overseas, I returned to the town where I had gone to college to settle down and find a job. The depression hit full force.

When He finally came into my life, the match with Baba and His ideology could not have been more perfect. Being a Guru who equally emphasizes spiritual progress and social change, His teachings fit like a glove. I can remember, in my early reading of Prout, how I felt I was reading my own ideas articulated by someone, as if He had read my mind. This relationship with Baba started quietly. I attended a meditation class with my sister. I signed up without realizing it was Ananda Marga. The synchronicities were there in that the margiis had come to the school where I was teaching a hatha yoga class to offer that one of their acaryas give a lecture to my students. The talk had been confusing, but the friendliness and sincerity of the local margiis who brought him stuck with me. One of them, Jinaneshvar, became my meditation class teacher, my best friend and eventually my husband.

The real, profound impact of the meditation itself began when I received initiation from Dada Jitendra in 1973. I could feel I had been handed, with this technique (first lesson) a truly powerful tool for inner transformation. But I still did not realize the depth of my connection with Him. He was, to me, simply the man who perfected these meditation tools and this philosophy I admired. But to love Him or see Him as a manifestation of Cosmic Consciousness was still far away.

In my early days as a margii, I felt my inner skeptic holding me back from fully embracing this as my path. I had spent years distrusting ‘isms’ and outer sources of authority. Yet the power with which the meditation worked to center my mind and the new sense of joy it brought won me over, eventually. My first week-long national retreat introduced me to longer three-times-a-day meditations. I found these tedious and hard to sit through, yet when I returned home several dramatic changes occurred, during two *dharmacakras* in particular. First, I experienced a total lifting of the anxious, depressed, empty feelings that had been with me since adolescence. These were replaced by a sense of surety that a source within me was ever-present and all I ever needed.

The very next time I attended a group meditation (having had an incredibly joyful week) I experienced diving deep into that source and breaking through into a revelation of my complete oneness with all of life, infused with my love for it all. The inner

transformation worked by these meditations convinced me I had found my spiritual home.

Yet who Baba was and how He related to these experiences still confused me. I had observed the older margiis ecstatic with love for Him and this felt very foreign to me. Not only did I see myself as too intellectual to be a *bhakta*, but I had no personal experience of Baba to make me feel drawn to Him. I had discussed these doubts with Jinaneshvar at my first retreat and his support to trust my sincere intentions and my own inner process was reassuring. I felt less out of place and risked another national retreat six months later. I came to the next year’s retreat determined to know more about who this guru was to me. Did I really have a personal relationship with Him? If so, why didn’t I feel it?

In my determination to have an answer I decided at the retreat’s *akhanda kiirtan* to do non-stop kiirtan myself until something happened to let me know Baba was there for me too. After just two hours I suddenly felt these powerful surges of bliss rolling up my spine and spreading throughout my body. They continued whenever I meditated for weeks after, although I tried to suppress them out of embarrassment at the movements they caused. So I truly felt I had been answered and ever after that, when I needed Baba’s help or just to know He was with me, I found Him ready to respond within me. He began to win my heart as well as my mind.

At the time I first encountered Ananda Marga I was also becoming involved with the man who was to become my first husband. Had his interest in spirituality been as strong as mine or had I been married before meeting Baba, this story would have been very different.

However, by the time I realized how much Baba meant to me, I was already deeply invested in a relationship with someone who, while supportive of meditation and of Ananda Marga superficially, turned out to be quite threatened by my love for Baba. So what did I do with this difficulty? I agonized, struggled and suffered. I could not let go of either of these loves of mine. I sought countless compromises, ways to include him in Ananda Marga activities, ways to explain and justify my choices. It was very painful to

have to hide so profound an inner relationship as I had developed with Baba from my own partner for fear of his judgment or rejection.

The year before we made the commitment to marriage, the struggle was particularly intense. I kept questioning if I was supposed to give up relationships altogether and take *acarya* vows in order to work full time for Baba’s mission. My pull to work for and please my Divine Father was very intense by then. On the horns of this dilemma, I kept struggling and asking for guidance from Baba. It came in a most unique way. At the time, Baba was in jail and few margiis could get in to see Him. I had not tried to go to India. It was quite dangerous. Yet one night, I awoke, or so it felt, fully alert and conscious, in a line waiting to see Baba outside His jail cell. I pinched myself to know I was not dreaming.

Full of excitement and some apprehension as to how it would feel to finally meet my Guru in person, I waited my turn in line. At last I was let in and knelt beside the cot where He lay, covered by a light blanket. As I bowed my head in greeting, He lay still and then returned the greeting with eyes half closed. He reached out and took my hand so lovingly and drew me nearer, up closer to His face. Holding His hand, I felt enveloped in my love for Him. I felt His love flowing freely towards me as well. Then I knew what I wanted to ask. “What about my relationship with...?”

“Do whatever you can for him and leave the rest to me,” came the reply.

I felt so blessed and reluctant to leave; yet the experience was beginning to fragment and slip back into the normal realms of time and space. Baba handed me some rock candy from a jar by His cot and then I was back in my bed, sleeping again. Although I did not know how to take this message from Baba at the time, in the months that followed I felt more and more that I needed to see this commitment through, not change altogether, or so Baba’s message seemed to imply. I can see now the karmic reasons why we were together and I honor Baba’s gentle message to stick it out. My experience of Baba is that He has asked of me that I undergo some very challenging undertakings in my life, this relationship being one of them.

One more time, in 1982, Baba again came to guide me and to ask of me a new challenge. I was almost 34 years old and married to my non-margii friend. I had been unclear about my readiness or even my willingness to have a child, never having had strong yearnings in that direction. I had been asking Baba for guidance, but receiving no clarity. Then one day, while I was at a meeting in the local hospital, an overwhelming urge to meditate came over me. I left the meeting as quickly as I could and went to the meditation room provided by the hospital. As soon as I closed my eyes, I saw Baba seated before me. What He told me was that if I wanted to attain the highest form of bhakti where I would love *Parama Purusa* only for the pleasure it gives Him, not for my own sake, that I should have a child. I had always wondered how I could ever attain that form of *bhakti*. It seemed so far from where I was. Here was His answer. I immediately took the resolve to follow His guidance. There was no longer a doubt or moment of hesitation. And Baba did His part. Later on when there were obstacles to fulfilling this decision on the physical plane He removed them so that my daughter could be born in 1983.

As deep as my bond with Baba had become, I was to find that there was yet a deeper experience of the profound bond of love between us waiting for me in Jamaica. In the fall of 1979 North and Central American margiis gathered in a lovely hilltop retreat center outside Kingston for four of the most blissful days I have ever experienced. When my plane landed, Baba had already arrived and gone to His house below us in the valley. Margiis were streaming in from all over the continent, 250-300 in total. That evening, we had our first, long-awaited, darshan. After we were seated and singing *bhajans*, Baba entered the hall. I could not believe how glowing and beautiful He looked to me and His voice was so deep and powerful. From then on my heart was lost to Him.

I ran down to His house every morning at dawn to sing kiirtan, outside, for Him. We crowded outside His door every afternoon to see Him come out and go on field walk. We sat for darshan morning and afternoon in utter bliss at His nearness. Every moment of those days I felt as if I were in heaven and I only wanted to be near Him. To my eyes, every particle of dust was dancing in love because of His presence. He was truly my Krishna and I his *gopi*.

On the third day, I was finally able to have personal contact with nine other sisters. It was not easy to get on the list for PC, but I was determined and had the help of a friend so at last I could sit before Baba and receive His direct blessing. He asked my name, and told me, “You know what it means? Meditation.” He then reached out and pressed my crown *cakra* with His finger. I could feel it vibrate. But most of all I could hardly stay in my right mind. Being near Him, the whole world felt unreal, ephemeral and only light shone within me.

The last night of His stay was to be DMC. The hall was packed and I got a place about halfway back, not near enough for my liking, but still where I could see Him. At one point during the evening I realized He was looking directly at me. My mind couldn’t accept the attention to me and denied it could be, until later when the friend next to me said, “He was looking at someone over here, wasn’t He?”

When I felt Baba looking at me, I fell into a deep meditation and had a powerful vision of Baba sitting at the center of the universe and me dancing around Him with the clear message that throughout eternity, now and always, this is where I truly am. In the midst of lifetimes of experience, now, past and future, I have always been and will always be in His orbit. I am never far, always in His presence. Behind the day-to-day experiences, this is what is most real. How sweet and profound that moment was.

We went to the airport late that night to see Him off to South America. Singing and crying, my eyes were glued to Him until his plane was out of sight. I could not believe the bittersweet longing of separation could be so strong. And then I went back to my daily life, but something in my relationship with Baba was changed, a level of devotion and love for Him was there that I found hard to contain, it was so powerful. In the years that followed, I lost that feeling, even though my devotion to Him stayed strong. It surfaced for a brief moment in 1990 when there was a hope He would come to the U.S. and I could see Him again, but, again I felt so swept away by the feeling that I could not hold on to it. And then He left His body a few months later.

A few years ago, He came to me in a dream. I sat with Him while He sang some of His songs to me and then as He got up to leave, that blissful feeling from Jamaica swept over me and He said, “Don’t forget this feeling.” So, by His grace, it is not far

from me anymore, buried in some inaccessible recess of my heart, but available when I open to feel the bond of love He showed me back there in Vrindavan, in Jamaica.

### Pashupati

*Raleigh, North Carolina*

One or two days after receiving Personal Contact (Jamaica, 1979) I found myself talking with Dadaji Ramananda about AMURT work. He was very pleasant, and very sensible and practical – a delight to work with. We initially met outside, and then in his room, which was across the hall from Baba’s room. At one point, a gong or bell was heard, and Ramanandaji immediately got up, said “Baba,” and walked out into Baba’s room.

Baba had some of His laundry (a shirt He’d worn) sitting on Ramanandaji’s floor. I took the liberty of smelling it, to see if the sandalwood odor I’d always heard about was permeating it. It didn’t.

Anyway, Dada Ramanandaji shortly came out, and said that Baba would soon be leaving. I took the opportunity to stand in the hall near where He would be passing. He passed just by me, and just after He did, I suddenly said, “Baba!” He turned, looked me straight in the eyes with those doe-eyes of His, and melted my heart. I realized then the softness in those eyes, and how much love He must have for us. This experience stayed with me for quite some time.

After I got home from Jamaica, I realized that I was quite happy. I expected to be a dynamo of energy after meeting Baba, but instead I was merely mellow. Perhaps a bit more *sattvic*. I was already fairly *rajasic*.

Later, I was in India, going to visit Baba in Ananda Nagar in 1986, not having seen Him since 1979 in Jamaica. Although I didn’t recognize myself as craving the presence of His physical body, I found myself running after Him and chasing after His car, like a mindless idiot or a dog on the highway. Very interesting.

There was much waiting around when Baba had reporting scheduled. I remembered that Baba once said that He didn’t mind being late for darshans because the margiis would sing kiirtan while waiting for Him. So I sang kiirtan and did sadhana while waiting.

In reporting, Baba noted that the targets had not been met. He asked, “Should we give punishment?” Some said yes, some said no. Baba said, “It is not proper to give punishment all the time. Let them try harder.”

In DMC, Baba gave a wonderful discourse in three languages. I found my mind wandering somewhat. This was partly because I was jammed between many *avadhutas*, who had graciously allowed me to sit near the front of the 20,000 people there, since I had come all the way from the West. However, it also meant that I had to sit with my knees up to my chest, shoulder to shoulder and elbow to elbow with several other dadas. This physically difficult posture made it difficult to meditate or concentrate.

But at least I could see well, and hear pretty well, too. When Baba gave His *mudra*, I tried to sit in half-lotus to prepare to receive it properly, but to no avail. I heard lots of people screaming and jumping around me, but I could only envy them. Then, some of the dadas got up to follow Baba as He left the dais. Finally I got to sit in proper position. I did *dhyana* (sixth lesson), and suddenly my mind got very light, and felt encompassed in light.

The feeling persists even as I write this. I did not move for several minutes, but I just enjoyed the bliss. I still do.

At the DMC in May of 1987, I attended AMURT (Ananda Marga Universal Relief Team) global meetings with margiis, dadas and didis. We met in Kimshuk’s bedroom at Baba’s house (Kimshukji is Baba’s adopted son, and is now an *acarya* in his own right.) The meetings ran overtime, due to some people not arriving in time from their various countries. Therefore, we planned to give report to Baba on the day after DMC. However, it turned out that some of the AMURT workers had already made plans to leave Ananda Nagar immediately following DMC, and so the final report meeting could not take place as planned, as Baba demands that all sectors be represented during reporting sessions. That night, in darshan (where Baba gives a more informal talk), Baba spent half the darshan commenting on how some of the workers “left in a hot haste,” and was obviously displeased with the disruption of order. Then He went on with the lecture.

### Vijaya Laksmi

[Editor’s note: Vijaya Laksmi, a great devotee and dynamic worker for Ananda Marga, passed away in 2003. Her selfless service stands as an example. She will be missed.]

I was initiated in 1971 in Los Altos Hills, California. I was raised a Catholic, but after I went to college and into nursing, I had given it up. I thought it wasn’t something I wanted to continue in my life even though I was still very emotionally involved in the spirit of Christ and prayer. I was then married, and living in Berkeley, California. A few years later I began to look at different meditation practices and different psychologies. When my oldest daughter reached nineteen or twenty, she was initiated. I remember I was in the kitchen doing dishes and she said to me, “There is a very special time when a Guru comes to the earth and it has such profound meaning,” and she wanted me to look into it. So I went to Los Altos and did very nice kiirtan. I didn’t stay for DC because I wasn’t yet initiated. After that, I thought it all seemed very beautiful. But I was very hesitant to join another group after being in the Catholic Church. Gradually, I got acquainted with some of the margiis.

Within six or seven months, I was beginning to be acquainted with the whole spiritual path, with Dada pushing me. When I decided to join Ananda Marga, I was especially impressed with the social service and what that meant because that was what I wanted to do. Even before I could realize some of the spiritual aspects of it, I was studying Baba’s social theories. I was very impressed with some of the *acaryas*. We didn’t have any didis around then. The first didi that came was Didi Ananda Rama. She came to train the GV sisters. So, as soon as I became initiated, I gave up meat. That was the easiest thing I ever did. It just seemed automatic. But I kept eating eggs and fish. Then in about eight months or so I was completely vegetarian. After that, things moved very fast. Baba was in jail and we were not able to see Him. But we were very close to Him and we were doing everything we could to help our Guru.

During the time Baba was in jail, I was taking a class with a very politically active teacher. One of our projects was to do something that was a little against the rules or very

activist in nature. So we put a committee together and decided we were going to do a demonstration in San Francisco. We decided the theme of the demonstration was that justice was dead and we were going to have a funeral.

We started down Market St. We had cars with lights and people had black bands. We had a police escort, which was very unusual. As you know, funerals move very slowly with the lights flashing. We proceeded down Market St. with the police escorts, and I remember two or three margiis had those black veils over their faces.

Then we stopped at the Indian embassy and were going to deliver a letter. We wanted to take the coffin in, but they wouldn't let us. So we took the letter, and the officials were just so angry when they saw us there from the window. We were spreading literature and had a megaphone and telling about what happened to Baba. And actually there were two or three TV stations which came to film us, so we were on TV. That was a very unique experience. We were very brave. We wanted to let everyone know about Baba's case.

The first time I saw Him was in Fiesch. I remember that everyone wanted to go and it was such a frenzy getting ready. It was Baba's birthday also. There were so many margiis from the U.S. and all over the world there. I remember I was sitting there after sadhana. People were singing and then shouting "*Param Pita Baba ki!*" The energy and magnetism was just fantastic in that room. And so when He walked in, everyone ran over to see Him including myself. While I'm not a very emotional person usually, I burst into tears as soon as I laid eyes on Him. I did not even know Him, but tears just flowed. It totally surprised me because I just didn't know what was happening to me.

Then we had meditation and darshan for DMC, of course. It was just so beautiful, absolutely the most beautiful thing I had ever seen. I didn't know what was going on, but I knew that there was something so beautiful, so subtle, so special, like nothing I had ever encountered before. I was so overwhelmed with this beautiful spirit, I decided to stay in the DC hall to do meditation. All of a sudden Dada Yatiishvarananda ran into the room and said, "Vijaya Laksmi, I think you are going to be chosen to go on field walk with Baba. Hurry, hurry, you have to hurry because you have to give report on what you do, what you have done in the last year, the last six months, the last three months."

I ran up there. I was fairly new to DMC, and I had on nylon stockings. And to get there, we had to cross a little river, and I didn’t even care. Baba was staying up on top of the hill at a chalet. I was just running and running, carefree and getting all wet and everything. So when we got there, they were busy taking reports. They took the reports and several of us sisters were chosen. That was the first time sisters were chosen to go on field walk. Devinishta was guarding and there were a couple of sisters from Australia. Baba, of course, rode up ahead in a car. So we got to the mountain, and it was so beautiful. The tops of the Alps were cutting through the sky like knives. There were spring flowers on the ground.

Baba came out. They had a little chair for him, and He looked up. I could see the reflection of the mountains in His eyes. He said, “I think this is a good spot.” Then He sat down. We all sat around Him. I was so close that I could have reached out and touched His knee. It was so nice to be there because He was talking about different things. He was talking about how life began on Mars, and the flora and fauna around the place.

Pretty soon He stopped talking and there was a young margii right in front of Him with blond, curly hair. He did namaskar to him and then tousled the curls on his hair. I was thinking, “I wish He would do that to me.” He started talking on the side of the brothers. I thought, “I wonder why He isn’t looking over here at the sisters.” And then immediately a thought came out of my head saying, “I may not know why, but I’m sure He has a reason.” And as soon as I thought that, He turned around and looked at us and began to talk about women.

He gave the very famous discourse about how women had so much power and ability to lead at one time. And when someone introduced you, you didn’t say who is your father and who is your uncle, but who is your grandmother or mother. And then He said something funny, “But we mustn’t get so proud about it, we mustn’t boast about it.” It was beautiful. It was a great experience especially since I wanted Him to do that. As soon as the thought came in my head, He immediately turned to us. I had just surrendered saying, “He knows, and I don’t.” As soon as I said that, He turned, and it all happened.

After that, we came down from the mountain. I was amazed to see how beautiful Baba looked especially after being in jail for so many years. His skin was so golden brown. He seemed strong with a healthy voice. I just couldn’t believe it, but I knew Baba

could do all that. After we came down, they had a car there for Him. I was among the people in the front, and I just hated to see Baba go. I wanted Him to stay for a long time and be with us. All of a sudden He beckoned, saying, “Come.” I wasn’t sure what was happening, but Didi said, “Go, He is talking to you.”

So I went and Devinishta came also because she was GV. I bent down to the car right close to His face, and He said to me, “Always uphold the dignity of women.”

Then He gave namaskar and left. I was so impressed with that because the whole morning had been about sisters and their relationship with Him and their dignity. That field walk was so special for me.

There is another story that happened when He went to Jamaica, and I think it shows Baba’s sense of humor. We were walking along during evening walk and pretty soon a dog started barking and Baba was talking. The *acaryas* started trying to get the dog to stop barking or get rid of it. All of a sudden Baba stopped and said, “Don’t worry, they [the dogs] are doing their duty.”

Another funny little story. I became very close friends with sister Jagadmitra after our initiation. She couldn’t go to Fiesch to see Baba. Her husband was a margii but still ate meat. She called my house one day and my youngest daughter told her I was in Jamaica to see Baba. She was so excited, she just left a note for her husband saying, “There is a roast in the oven, I will be back later.” She didn’t tell her husband anything else. She didn’t tell him that she was going to Jamaica.

There were very beautiful darshans. There was not enough room for the margiis, it was so rushed. We had *dharmacakra*, and that night we took Baba to the airport. He was going to go to Venezuela. We all did kiirtan and *tandava* for Him, and He flew away. The hurricane that happened in Santa Domingo happened immediately after DMC. Baba wanted volunteers, so many AMURTEL and AMURT volunteers were excited to go since we were so charged from Baba’s presence. The first AMURTEL (Ananda Marga Universal Relief Team – Ladies) group was formed in Santa Domingo.

### Prakash (2)

I first saw Baba in Switzerland in May, 1979. What I remember most about Fiesch was personal contact with Baba.

Baba was staying in a little chalet. There was all this chaos outside, as everyone wanted to get inside to see Baba. I was a Proutist; Devinishta had come back from India and said Baba told her, indirectly, we have to be Proutists. Dada Tadbhavananda pulled me into the house and said to Baba’s Personal Assistant (PA) that I should get Personal Contact. It was very sudden, I wasn't prepared, but I was thrust into the room, and was sitting with my legs underneath me. And the first thing Baba said to me was, “Sit in proper position.” He asked my name, and who was my *acarya*.

Then He said, “Do you want to do something little or something great?”

And I said, “Baba, something great!”

“Do you have the moral courage to do it?”

“Yes, Baba.” I was very serious; then He looked at me, reached out and pinched my cheek, and said, “You can smile!” That was all.

I did namaskar, and Baba put His hands on me, and gave a blessing. Then I went outside and I just cried and cried, as if something had been opened up in my heart, as if all the longing, all the pain in my heart had been released. Afterwards – this was on Baba’s birthday, Purnima, May 1979 – afterwards at darshan all I remember is that as Baba was speaking, saying, “No one or nothing in the universe is unimportant.” I felt that that was what happened to me in PC, that Baba removed enough of the barrier between me and Him, that suddenly all those feelings of being little were released. Society tells us, that you’re insignificant – capitalism is based on the idea that you’re nothing – and all of Baba’s mission is about seeing the greatness in everyone.

And with that little touch, Baba did that, said, “Do something great, and have moral courage,” and then the smile, so that while we are being strong and courageous, we do it with joy and happiness. He doesn’t want us to be so serious, so burdened by the heaviness of a great mission. He wants us to feel love and joy and compassion as we’re doing a great work. I remember from LFT training one of the meanings of Brahma is one who makes other great. Baba’s mission is about making others great. We all have to

become great. That touched me so deeply. In darshan I felt Baba was speaking just to me.

“You’re not alone, or insignificant.”

### Devinishta (2)

I went to see Baba in Switzerland, and Baba’s arrival was delayed by a few days. The European margiis had not come yet, and very few people were there to prepare. So we were welcomed with open arms. They needed more people to prepare for Baba’s coming. They gave me money and said to go out and buy fabric, and make a place for Baba to sit. I had only seen Baba in jail, but somehow instinctually I knew how to make the pillows. It felt like it came from another lifetime or something like that. I bought beautiful gold fabric, and some brocade for the pillow in back, and gold velvet, and I made a place for Baba to sit. And they needed someone to make the flag for Baba’s car, so I sewed that. Then they had sheets and pillowcases not the right sizes, so I got to sew Baba’s pillow for Him to sleep on. And I very much enjoyed doing this for Him.

We had a few Prout meetings there, and every day we would wait for Baba to give darshan and field walk. Up to that time, field walk had always been only for brothers. But in Switzerland, some of the sisters felt so sad about it that they were doing a protest, a 24-hour kiirtan, to get a chance also to have personal contact and go on field walk. When Baba found out about it, He said, “Well, why not?” But they decided that the sisters wouldn’t ride in the same car with the brothers. Unfortunately, most sisters didn’t drive. I knew how to drive, and had a U.S. driver’s license, so I was told I could drive, but I had no car, of course. Finally, an Italian brother said I could use his car. But the Italian cars were very slow, and we had to go all over the mountains of Switzerland.

I was told if I drove, I had to be a GV guard. I was a Proutist, but everyone was taking turns being guards. So there I was, dressed in my guard uniform and driving this car. I was flooring the gas, but all the other cars were going too fast, and we dropped behind, and got lost and couldn’t find our way back. We were sad, because we were going on field walk for the first time, and couldn’t get there.

So the next day, they agreed that the brother who owned the car could drive. He was able to keep up with them, and we got there and went on field walk. Baba talked about plants and trees. I never knew exactly what was happening. We were given no training for guarding Baba, and for the first time in my life I was guarding. The other guard wanted me to drop back, and I thought, “Well, my job is to make sure the sisters

don’t bother Baba, so I need to be close to Baba.” So there was this silent struggle between us, he saying, “Get back,” and I saying, “No, I’m going to do my job!” [laughs].

We also got our marriage blessed in Switzerland. We made garlands out of flowers we were able to obtain from a local florist. There were three or four couples waiting to have their marriage blessed, and we sat right down in front of Baba. I was sitting three or four feet from Baba. Baba was up a little higher, so we were looking up at Him. It was a very interesting darshan. You know, whenever you’d see Baba you’d have these different questions, you’d have these concerns about things having to do with you as an individual. For some reason, Baba decided to talk about dance. My husband, Prakash, is a dancer. So we were sitting there and Baba was demonstrating these different *mudras*, and moving His hands around the whole time, talking about dance, and that was so meaningful to my husband.

Then part way through the darshan, Baba picked up one of the pillows I had made. He started rocking it like a baby. We had been trying to get pregnant for a number of years, so one of our inside questions for Baba was, would we ever be able to get pregnant? Baba was picking up the pillow I had made for Him, and rocking it like a baby. And we were able to adopt two children a year or so after that, then were able to give birth to one, and adopted two more after that. If Baba was predicting we would have a baby in our arms, He was right. Baba blessed our marriage like He had many others; He took the garland we had in His own hands, and told us to live like Shiva and Parvati, and then gave the garland to me and told me to take the garland home and take care of it.

I also got to see Baba in personal contact, in Switzerland. I thought it was rather unfortunate the way PC was arranged there. They would just grab us and put us in the room. I was a GV guard, and I had no time to meditate or anything beforehand. It was so frustrating because if Baba wanted our vibrations to be easier to handle, then giving us time to meditate beforehand, and not putting us in in a hurry, seems like it would be better. Anyway I went in, and there were 17 sisters. This was supposed to be personal contact and there were 17 of us. I felt frustrated. I don’t think I had a very nice PC because I didn’t want to be stuffed in there with 17 sisters on the spur of the moment when Baba was getting ready to go to darshan. I wanted Baba to have time when He could really pay attention to each of us. Basically the didi there said our names and our

trades, and Baba said a few words. There wasn’t much more to it. I never told anyone that I was hoping for a chance some time later in India or something to have PC, but that was all I had.

The other two times I went to see Baba were at DMC in India. We had children then who were one, three, and five, and we were one of the first Western families to bring their children to India. I think people were interested to see how we did. When we came, it was just in time for DMC, and Baba had already moved to Tiljala. We stayed in a hotel, and they came to pick us up in a car in the middle of the night [to avoid police detection], and covered us up, all of us who were white. My children are black – they could show their faces because they blended in, but the rest of us had to cover up with shawls. And they dropped us off in Tiljala. We brought a tent with us, which we set up on the rooftop, so our family could stay together.

As we were leaving India that time – we stayed for about five weeks – there weren’t many people at Lake Gardens, so as Baba was going for field walk, the *acaryas* put us in front by the plants so He would see us. And as He passed, I said, “Namaskar, Baba, we’re leaving.” And Baba stopped and gave us a nice namaskar.

“Will you come back?”

“Yes, Baba, we’ll come back.”

When He returned from field walk we saw Him again. There were these double doors He passed through, and as Baba was walking up the stairs, He could tell that people were peeking through the crack in the door. For at least half an hour, He stood on the stairs just so all of us could stand on the stairs and peek at Him. It was sweet.

Having promised Baba that we would come back, the next year we came again – the kids were two, four and six. Again we went to a hotel, and every day we’d spend until noon just getting our laundry done, and then we’d go to see Baba go on field walk. One time Mahajoy couldn’t stop saying namaskar to Baba – she would say, “Namaskar Baba, Namaskar Baba, Namaskar Baba!”

And Baba would stop and say, “Namaskar to all of you.” Baba had this agreement, I think, that He would never not return namaskar to someone. So finally we told our children they were allowed to say namaskar only once each time they saw Baba.

That time they gave us a room at Tiljala with other families. Baba used to do field walk on the roof of His building. If you sat at any of the windows of our building you could see Him walk back and forth. And at night time there was kiirtan; everyone would stand in a window, crowds of people, and you couldn’t carry the same tune from the top to the bottom floor, so you’d have all these tunes going on all at once. And Baba would walk back and forth, and if you were lucky He would stop, and lift up His cane, and do namaskar to you, and then we would go, “Ohhh, Baba.” It was just so wonderful. I would put my youngest child on my back in my backpack, and stand there doing kiirtan, and she would peer over my shoulder to see Baba. It was beautiful.

### Gayatrii (2)

Baba was coming to Jamaica! It was September 1979. By His grace, and due to the kindness of my brother, I was in Baba’s house from 6 a.m. until midnight daily, preparing food for Him! It was His sweet grace. In the beginning I hesitated to cook. Then Madhu said, “Come Gayatrii, you must cook for Baba!” So I began to cook. First I cooked cabbage – the simplest of vegetables, and cooked in the simplest way. It was given to Him for dinner. Next morning, Madhu called out to me, “Oh Gayatrii! Baba loved your cabbage! He talked on and on about the origin of cabbage, the entire history of cabbage, how it spread to the different countries and continents!”

Oh, Baba! How happy I was! Imagine, for Baba to get excited over cabbage! Slowly I cooked more and more items for Him and watched the tray go into His room, and then come out with a clear and sometimes substantial portion missing – I would just jump for joy! On another day, my (spiritual) brother gave me the towel with which Baba had wiped His face. The towel was filled with the most divine heavenly fragrance – it was the scent of purest sandalwood. Never have I smelled such a divine fragrance before!

In 1971 I had had PC (Personal Contact) in Cochin with Baba. But, there in Jamaica, Madhu told me, “You have to have PC!” I decided not to mention to anyone about my Cochin PC. I decided to follow whatever Madhu and Didi Karuna wanted. They organized my (second) PC with Baba! We went in together. It was indescribable. Five or six of us were together with Baba, with PA Dada and Karuna Didi. As my turn came, I went on my knees at His feet. I began crying. It was too much for me, to be so close to Him, even touching Him. My love was too much. My longing was too much. The intimacy was too much. I cried so loudly.

Through all my weeping, Baba was talking so much, but I heard nothing. He was taking apart each syllable of the name – Gayatrii – and giving the Sanskrit origin and translation. But, I was only crying in bliss to be at the feet of my Beloved. Finally, He made me listen.

He said, slowly, “Gayatrii … means… mother… of… the… universe!”

I listened. I heard. I remembered it for life. All those days, I had not asked Baba for anything. But that day, I told Him, in my mind: “Baba, when You return from

fieldwalk, can You not smile at me, as I stand in the line when You walk past?” And then, I forgot my wish. Later on in the evening, Baba came. And as He approached, He was looking directly at me, with such a big smile on His face! I was stunned! (I had forgotten about making this very wish!) Then I was overwhelmed. His love was too much to bear. I had never experienced such love before. It was Infinite love! His love was vaster than the cosmos! To feel it even for a fraction of a second meant my face was covered in tears. Grown men, forty and fifty years old, feeling His love as He passed – they all were weeping! Such was His love. So was it to be loved by Lord Anandamurtiji!

Time moved on. It was 1982, in Patna. Daily, morning and evening, Baba would come out and walk up and down the driveway, next to the garden which was filled three feet high with all sorts of flowers – tuberoses, zinnias, marigolds, chrysanthemums, gladiolas, lilies, asters – Baba surrounded by flowers. It was paradise! Daily, we waited on the verandah for Him to come out and begin His walk. One day He stopped just in front of me, and spoke in English. He said, “This world is for human beings... not for demons.” He spoke so seriously that I remembered these words forever. There was a sister there whose husband had just been killed in the *samaj* movement (Baba’s plan to remove the regional exploitation by outside capitalists). One evening, this sister fell at Baba’s feet, weeping profusely due to the loss of her husband. Then Baba spoke. He told her and everyone present: “Let there be no unhappiness in your hearts. Let everyone be immersed in serving the suffering humanity, by doing *samaj* work for the sake of the Supreme Entity.”

After His walk, Baba would sit in the chair, and all His devotees would dance, singing kiirtan, in bliss, around Him. Many times this occurred. As we danced and sang, Baba sat, so quiet, so silent, mostly with eyes closed. He was enjoying! He was loving the devotees all around Him. Occasionally, He opened His eyes, to look deeply into the eyes of a particular devotee. One must realize that in the infinite depths of His eyes lay the entire universe! It is impossible for mere mortals to comprehend, to fathom, the infinite attributes of the Lord! Can a small ant fathom the intricacies of the human being – the mind and heart of the human being? Similarly, it is impossible for us to comprehend the Lord. But, His love was pouring forth to all, causing some to go into ecstatic trances as they danced. Such days with the Lord cannot be forgotten!

### Uddhava

My name is Uddhava and I'm from Stockholm. I came to Ananda Marga in 1977, more or less by chance. I saw a poster about it, and I joined the meeting. I got initiated the same year. I went to some retreats, and did some kiirtan. I remember the first *akhanda kiirtan* was six hours. I thought, “Oh, these crazy people.” But anyway I joined the kiirtan, and suddenly I felt it was over already. Those six hours were up, and I was lost in Ananda Marga. I tried to leave many times, but I could not.

I met Baba in Stockholm. The first time was in Fiesch. I was not so regular in AM at the time. I was not familiar with the rules, and my English was not good enough to understand what the dadas were saying. But anyhow, I was brought to Baba. I was not ready for any Personal Contact. But they just took me to meet Him. He told me to come and sit here. For me, it was uncomfortable. There was no space. So I lay down on the floor, and did what they told me, and He looked at me. I was looking up. I just saw something very, very beautiful. He looked in my eyes. I will never forget that. His eyes were very strong.

He told me, “You do not know the 16 points.” I tried to say these points. I tried my best, but not well.

But He said, “You do not know the 16 points.”

“Yes, Baba, I do.”

I tried to explain. And then I could not answer.

“You don't know.”

“No, Baba, I don't know.”

Then He showed me something. I saw some ancient times, before we were living, and people were there. As I understood it, this time factor is not as important as we think. I got the feeling that very near and very far is all the same, and also, about the time factor, that very long time back and now is the same. He had this stick, and He told me to put my hands out, and now how many times I should beat you?

He explained, “You have done many things in your life.”

And I didn't know about this stick. I really thought He would hit me hard; He looked very dangerous.

So I said, “OK, you beat me once.”

But He just beat me lightly. Then I said, “OK, 100 times,” and I thought He would really beat me. But He just touched me, and then He gave me some blessing. But these small moments I remember every day. When I came there I thought it was not important, but afterwards it has become more and more so.

**Jagadmitra**

I was initiated by Dada Yatiishvarananda in 1971. I always had a difficult time with sadhana, it was a big change for me, but every time my *acarya* would come he would encourage me. In 1978 we moved to California. I didn’t know anybody in California, but for the first time in many years I was afforded time to do more meditation. I had never really understood Ananda Marga; I was always involved with the people and *acaryas*, but had no real connection with Baba. We were living in Pacific Grove, two to three hours south of Los Altos. One day after meditation, all of a sudden it was as if the curtain opened, and I felt for the first time who was running the show, behind all the organization, behind the *acaryas*, behind everything. It was a very deep experience that day.

The following year, 1979, Baba went to Jamaica. Prior to that, I remember going through a magazine, tearing out articles I wanted to save. I saw this picture with the word ‘Jamaica’ written across it. I had never had a desire to go to Jamaica, I had never traveled, except to the West Coast, and this warm feeling came all over me, as though I was getting sick, as if I had a fever or something, just chills. I didn’t think anything of it, and it passed.

But later that year, Baba was supposed to come to Los Altos. And I didn’t know anyone up there, but I talked to Vijaya Laksmi, who has since become a good friend, to get the details about what I should bring. Bring a fan, she said. So I took three days off work – I made up some story, I couldn’t tell them I was taking off to go see my guru, they would never understand. And of course, Baba couldn’t get into the country. So I went back to Pacific Grove, and realized I had forgotten the fan, which didn’t belong to me. So the next day I called, and talked to Vijaya Laksmi’s daughter. She said, “Oh, she’s not here, she’s gone to Jamaica.”

“What’s she doing in Jamaica?”

“Baba has gone to Jamaica.” And it was like somebody put me in automatic gear. You have to understand how fearful I was in those days, never having gone anywhere by myself, or done anything by myself. My marriage was very shaky at that point, and when I went back I found out they had fired me from my job. And I was just amazed. I thought

to myself, “Jamaica? If I do nothing else in my life – I’ve just lost my job, my marriage is going down the drain – if I do nothing else in my life, I’m going to see the guru.” I left a note on the table. My husband was not vegetarian, and I had a roast in the oven. I put it on the timer, and left a note saying I was going to be gone for a few days. I ran down to the bank, had about $400 to my name, and whatever I had was enough for a ticket to Jamaica. I packed this little suitcase, went to the airport and went standby.

It was as though my mind was just racing and racing, uncontrollably. At about 3 in the morning I was in the airport, and people were sleeping all around me. And reality set in. “Ohmigosh, what am I doing in this airport, across the country, all by myself?” I saw a few familiar faces that I had seen at retreats. Anyway, I made it to Jamaica, after Baba had arrived, and there was no room in the hotel where all the margiis were staying. I didn’t have a pass, so they wouldn’t feed me, I was bitten up with mosquitoes, and didn’t have a room. So I was feeling very badly. I met up with a couple of other women who had met a Jamaican brother who had a car, and he said he would help us find a place to stay. We did find a place to stay, four miles away. Next morning he picked us up, and he said, “Would you like to drive by where Baba is staying?”

So we drove by. I had no clue what was going on, really, we saw all these people sitting on the grass, and they said Baba was in the house. Vijaya Laksmi recognized me, and grabbed my arm and said, “You stand here, and get a good look at Baba.” I did. Baba came out the door, and went for a walk. A little later, they announced the bus to take people to darshan was broken down, so we should take taxis.

I was not a known margii, I didn’t have a posting. One dada came and said he needed someone to do some typing, and I volunteered. He took me to the hotel. We were leaving and Vijaya Laksmi said, “No, she can’t go; she needs to see Baba.”

Dada turned to one didi and said, “Didi, will you take responsibility to see that she sees Baba?” She replied yes. So we went up, but the hotel office was locked, and we couldn’t type. We went back down, and people were starting to leave in taxis. I was not aggressive. People sometimes get very aggressive when it comes to seeing Baba. So people were cramming into these taxicabs, eight to ten people in a cab, to go see Baba. I couldn’t get in. I just sat down on the grass, crying, thinking, “Oh, I’m going to miss the darshan.”

A few minutes later, Didi came by and took me in the house, with some other girls, for personal contact (PC) with Baba! It was so amazing to me that out of all this confusion and turmoil from the time I left California, after I had PC, it was like a quiet sea. Ah. This is why all this was going on. It was such a beautiful experience because so many times you would hear people say, “Oh, I wanted to see Baba and this dada or didi wouldn’t let me.” And I knew that when the desire is so strong, no one can stop you from seeing your guru. It’s the guru who opens the door.

There I was in Jamaica, not knowing anybody. Even somebody asked me, “What’s your posting?” And somebody that I didn’t know spoke up, “Oh, she’s GV” [laughs]. I wasn’t GV, I wasn’t anything. And doors kept opening, moving from here to there, and circumstance-wise Baba allowed it to happen. Well, I was just feeling wonderful. I felt like I’d never felt before. That was a long time ago. Still, He lives right here inside. I miss Him terribly.

I’ve been to India several times to see Him. Once I saw Him, I would be satisfied. The next year I discovered I was not satisfied, and I would go back to India. I brought this little plant, a little cactus, and put it in my purse. I wanted to give it to Baba. Usually you’re not allowed to give something directly to Baba, and usually there’s tons of people there. But when I got to India, we went to the Lake Gardens house. All the way on the airplane I was worried, sometimes crying, what if He’s not there, what if I can’t see Him? I was very emotional. It was exaggerated. I even thought as I was experiencing it that it was a little exaggerated.

But that’s what I was going through, and we got to Lake Gardens and there was nobody there, except this guard at the gate. They let us in. Baba was coming around the side of the house with Dada Ramananda, his personal secretary and Didi Ananda Karuna. I was walking, and I had this plant in my hand. All I was thinking the whole way was, “All I want Him to do is touch my hand. If He does that, I’ll be satisfied forever.” So He came around.

“Namaskar, Baba, I’m from the U.S.” We talked a little. I handed Him the cactus, nobody stopped me, and He went like this over my hand [shows how Baba touched her hand]. Oh! It was… so beautiful. Oh, He was like my father. I kept thinking, “He’s my

father, He wouldn’t not see His child.” You know, I’d come halfway across the world to see Him.

But I think spiritual greed sets in, you know? After that, I was still in India and decided I wanted another personal contact. I couldn’t get enough. Well, that didn’t happen! [laughs]. I kept talking to my acarya, “Dada, I have to get in for another PC.” That didn’t happen, and I was furious. Almost like, on this plane you have a fight with your husband or lover, the emotions were of that intensity.

# CHAPTER FOUR:
## YOU ARE MY REPRESENTATIVES

> “If we want the development of this beautiful universe, we should try to accelerate the speed of spiritual progress more and more... not only in the physical or psychic or spiritual world of human beings, but in the entire living world, and not only the living world, but also both the animate and inanimate worlds...”

In the late 1970s and into the early 1980s, many margiis traveled to India to see Baba and get Personal Contact or attend *Dharma Maha Cakra*. In 1981, Baba offered a once-in-a-lifetime opportunity to His devotees to come to India for *Dharma Samiksa*. In a small crowded room in Kolkata, Baba reviewed the spiritual life of each margii who came for this special program, sometimes commenting on their past or even future. They received His attention, His love, and also His reprimands. He prescribed asanas for physical problems. In the tradition of *Tantra*, He used His cane to punish some, which as many devotees confirmed, removed karmic obstacles. The event transformed many lives.

### Arjuna (2)

In 1981, I went for *Dharma Samiksa*. There were hundreds of people in the office there at Jodhpur Park. People were lined up. I got pinkeye, so I couldn't see Baba for five or six days. I had to stay in this mass of people. I heard stories about how Baba would hit some people with His stick, but most people only got tapped on the hands or hit a few times. The day I went in, the line was arranged like a snake, and slowly you would see what Baba was doing to the people before you.

He would just tap the sisters on the hand, and with the brothers, sometimes He would take a strong swing, but then just tap them. I was kind of expecting that for me. When my turn came, we had our shirts off. I came in front of Baba, and He said, “Put your arms up.”

He looked up and down, was scanning me. I think He asked, had I been a good boy? All of a sudden He started beating me. On each side, He hit me eight times, so there were sixteen times in all. And wow, it really hurt. I jumped each time; I'm not that strong a person! [laughs]. It was painful. Then afterwards, He asked, “Are you going to be a good boy?”

I said, “Yes, Baba” [laughs]. He gave me some asanas, and they were the exact same asanas that my acarya had given me. Those marks were with me for at least two weeks, and the sixteen, to me, had the significance that I should be stronger in 16 Points.

In 1984 I went to India again. Before that time I had thought this *Prabhat Samgiit* was OK, but it didn't really do that much for me. The first day I went to Lake Gardens, two or three sisters were singing *Prabhat Samgiit* as Baba was in this chair in the driveway. That's when it hit me, that *Prabhat Samgiit* was really special. I just cried, it was so sweet, and so beautiful. Just seeing Baba there, hearing it sung so sweetly, it finally made an impression on me.

You know, when I saw Baba in jail the second time, it gave me the feeling that if the guru wants to see you, He'll create the circumstances. I was with Dadaji Devashraddhananda. We flew into Delhi. The following day was some festival, and I really had a desire to see Baba's *mudra* again. I felt we should fly from Delhi to Calcutta. He wanted to take the train. I said, “Dadaji, are you sure there will be time to see Baba on

darshan on the festival day?” He said, “Yeah, we should be there on time.” We took this long train ride with some other acarya and LFT. We got into Howrah station around 2 or 3 p.m. There was all this traffic. We got to Tiljala around 4.

Just as they opened the gate, everybody was coming out of the DC hall. Baba had just given the *mudra*, and everyone was in bliss. We were just five minutes late. From this, you can only laugh, and feel that Baba is in control of everything, and when He wants to see you, He’ll see you, and when He doesn’t that’s fine, too. Every time I went to India to see Baba, every time I do sadhana, it’s all up to Him if He’s going to make His presence known.

In December 1986, I had a revolutionary marriage to sister Sharada from Taiwan. We had met two or three days before, and we decided to just surrender everything to Baba. We had our wedding on the 31st, with 10,000 people there. The following day Baba blessed our marriage. He took our hands and held our hands to His, and He said, “Be like Shiva and Parvati.” That was the first time I had really stared at His hands. They were so big and warm. It was a wonderful feeling for both of us to be in Baba’s hands.

I went to India again in 1987 for Personal Contact (PC). I had PC with Baba at Lake Gardens, upstairs. I went into His room, and did *sastaung pranam*. He called me over, and said, “What is your name? Do you know what Arjuna means?”

I said, “The way it was told to me was one who fights for morality.”

Then He paused, and said, “Arjuna means filled with energy, just bursting with energy.” That struck a sympathetic note in me. He asked me where my father’s father was from. I said, “I think he was from Germany.”

And He said, “No, he was from Northern Russia, from Siberia.” I thought, “Hmm, my dad always told me his parents were from Germany.” So that stuck in my mind.

This PC was short. He said, “Have you been a good boy?”

“I don’t know, Baba.”

“No, you’ve made a lot of mistakes, with women.”

He had his stick, and I thought He was going to beat me again. He told me to put my hands out, and this time He just touched them and said, “Be an ideal boy.” That was my PC with Baba.

When I came back, I saw my father the following Thanksgiving in Florida, and was asking about where my grandparents were from. And he said his father had come from Russia to Germany.

In 1989 Sharada was pregnant, and she was due to have our daughter in November. My dad and mom were still alive in Florida, but I hadn’t heard from my mother since September. Whenever I called, she was sleeping or out. The baby was due around November 19th or 20th, but it didn’t come on time. That week my aunt in Florida called and said my mom only had a day to live. So I was feeling my mother wouldn’t live to see the baby. I asked Sharada if I should go back and see my mom. She said yes.

I flew down, and saw her in the hospital on her deathbed. This was the first time we really had this kind of deep connection. As I held her hands all I was thinking was Baba, giving His *mudra*, and healing. I stayed there one day, and said my goodbye to her. We held hands for hours. And my ideation was stronger than it had ever been. I left the next day, and Sharada went into labor that day. When I called Florida, I learned my mother’s pain all of a sudden was gone; she got out of the hospital and went to Thanksgiving dinner with my family. People couldn’t believe that she got up even. The next day the baby was born. We took pictures. I sent the pictures Federal Express to my mom in Florida. She saw the pictures and then she died. Baba really took care of everything.

### Sadhana (2)

There is one more gift Baba gave me that I must tell about before this story will be complete. That is my trip to India in July 1981 for *Dharma Samiksa*. At the time, I had been married for two years and my daughter was not yet born. As I mentioned, my husband had a fair amount of clash about Baba, so when the call for margiis to come for *Dharma Samiksa* went out, I was not sure how to explain the time and money to him. Asking my husband to come with me seemed the best way to reassure him and he agreed to come with me and several other margii friends. As a result, when we arrived in Kolkata I knew I had a limited number of days to get in to see Baba before my husband would become impatient and want to continue our travels in India.

Dutifully he accompanied me by cab each day to global HQ at dawn to avoid the CBI surveillance, and dutifully he came by cab after dark each night to retrieve me. The hours in between for me were made up of sitting, cramped and sweltering with no food or water, waiting. But in the waiting we sang, meditated and met many wonderful Indian margiis.

Finally Didi Ananda Karuna got me in to see Baba on the third day. I was *Bhukti Pradhan* of our area at the time and so a report had to be given. I had been prepared ahead of time, but as in Jamaica, as soon as I saw Baba my mind emptied. I had been forewarned that He would be strict and serious. Yet with me, Baba was only full of encouragement.

“Good work,” He repeated, “Good work.”

Afterwards, I realized His words were for our whole unit, not me personally. We had been seen as too autonomous as a unit, taking Baba’s inner advice and our interpretations of His words seriously, sometimes challenging organizational positions or policies. So Baba’s positive words let me know we had His support for the way we were doing His work.

But the biggest impact of *Dharma Samiksa* was mysterious. It came after I got home and after some time had passed. First, I lost my desire to be *Bhukti Pradhan* anymore. Then, one day I realized I had been striving to be a ‘good girl,’ to please my spiritual Father, needing His approval. All of that need was just gone, missing from its

familiar place in my psyche. To come home with less organizational energy seemed paradoxical and yet that is what Baba took from me. He is truly a mystery, as He has said, and the way He works to bring us to liberation is also a mystery. To this day, I find it hard to grasp, to comprehend the grace that brought Baba into my life and into my mind and heart. There is nothing I could have done to earn such grace! Baba Kripahi Kevalam!

### Vishvamitra (2)

My next opportunity to see Baba arose in 1981, when my *acarya*, Yatiishvarananda, called me and said, “Prepare to go to India.” I said, “Dada, what’s the urgency?” He told me that in the past great gurus would select one person out of 4 or 500 devotees to give *Dharma Samiksa* to every three or four years. And Baba was giving this to everyone now, but there was no assurance that it would last. I took him seriously, made my arrangements, and took off from my job.

At that time, there was a lot of interference in India from the local government, and CBI. It was sort of a cops and robbers scene. We were moving from hotel to hotel, and many people were being deported. We were very crowded in the small office, with hundreds of people there. We would sit in the larger room and meditate, then our names would be called, and we’d go with 15 or so other people into a smaller room with Baba. Finally I was called in. I sat there and heard all these stories. Baba was revealing personal things about people, things they had done wrong, and giving them punishment. The interesting thing about this was that I knew a lot of those people, and when I left the room I had no memory of what Baba had said about them. And the people I didn’t know at all, I remembered their stories. So Baba was protecting us in wiping clean the memory of the experience.

As I was sitting there, an Italian brother was introduced as Vishvamitra from South Carolina. Baba lavished such affection on him, and the brother got in His lap. I thought, “I wish that was me. After hearing all these stories, I’m probably going to get beaten.” But I felt a responsibility to let someone know that I was Vishvamitra, and not this Italian brother. I told the dada who was taking notes that I was Vishvamitra, and He looked at me, startled, but he went up and told Baba that he had the name wrong. Baba’s finger looked like it was two feet long, sometimes. “You stupid,” He said, and cleared the room.

But finally I got up to see Baba. Baba didn’t say much to me. “Your work is not good,” He said, in a semi-harsh tone. And I knew what He was saying – I wasn’t sending in reports, I was being lax in details in my Ananda Marga work. He motioned me to roll up my shirt, and He gave three licks on both sides of my chest. They were well-placed.

Then He motioned me to come closer, and took my head. Touching my head, He did some *mudras*, and said some *slokas*. Then He took my face in His hands, and looking into my eyes as close as I could get and still stay focused, He smiled this smile that would melt a thousand worlds. He just had this affection, and love, and tenderness. I was enveloped in that. It meant a lot to me, it was focused, and I was enveloped in Him. That remains a useful support in my sixth lesson.

He gave the asanas for me, and they cured many problems I had. I’m just now understanding that He gave me some asanas for problems I would encounter now, this year. He had an infinite capacity to give you what you needed. I sat outside the room doing sadhana, then we heard that Baba was coming out of the room. I stood up, and when Baba walked past me, He looked at me and gave namaskar. There was this tremendous vibration that went through my body, a shower of bliss. His mercy and His grace are infinite.

Baba gave darshan on a Sunday about the meaning of *Dharma Samiksa*, how seldom it was given and how important it was, the burning of the seeds of karma. I’m sure since then I’ve gathered some more *samskaras*, but I’m sure Baba has always helped me whittle them down, keep it at a low roar, so I can do some work while I’m here, in this body, and be His representative.

I went for *Bhukti Pradhan* (BP) reporting in 1984, at Lake Gardens. Baba was constantly taking reporting, throughout His life. Baba took so much personal care with us. This time we were squished into the *dharmacakra* hall at Lake Gardens. The *acaryas* told me to go forward. Baba said, “Let us hear from someone from a poorer country.” They brought a Ghanaian brother forward. I felt the grace of being there close to Baba. He wanted the BPs to know everything about their region of the world – rivers, socioeconomic potentials, what the Marg was doing to help that place – in great detail.

One of the dadas told an interesting story. One day Baba came out and said, “Are you enjoying the showers?” It hadn’t rained, but they knew what He was speaking of. It was invariably the case, singing *Prabhat Samgiit* and waiting to see Baba, that we were swimming in bliss.

Then Sudeva and I got into a strategic position to see Baba when His car came back. He got out again and gave namaskar. He started walking towards the back of the house. We said, “Baba, we invite you to come and visit the margiis in New York Sector.” And He stopped, turned around and walked back.

“You are my representatives, do you understand, my sons? And this influence goes out in ever-increasing circles, like ripples in a pond. And remember, you must come back, again and again and again.”

We understood Baba was saying to exercise every opportunity to be close to Him, not just in the physical sense, but in every moment of ideation during the day. To come back again and again to ideation on Him. He is seeking us even more than we seek Him.

The next time I traveled to India was with Shaktideva on Baba’s birthday in 1987. Baba gave him his old position as VSS guard as he had been in Jamaica. Baba gave a darshan about the existential value of animals. Most animals in the forest have very small mammary glands, but when we domesticate them they develop larger ones. This is not good for the animals. In fact this will lead to the extinction of milk cows, because of the change in hormonal structure. We will have to adopt only synthetic forms of milk. Baba was saying we should be respectful of animals, that their existential value should be protected by us. Shaktideva was doing *kaoshikii*, and one dada was correcting him, and told him to sit out during *tandava*. And I saw Baba turn and say, no, giving the indication, let him do *tandava*. Baba was always guarding the sentiments of His devotees, to make it softer, more spiritual, richer, closer to Him.

The last time I saw Baba was DMC 1989 in Ananda Nagar. I brought two sisters with me from Charleston, who were involved with the AM school in Charleston. By Baba’s Grace these two Western sisters were able to get there without too much hardship. The night of DMC, 30,000 people were there. Dada Haratmananda came up to me and said, “This is your opportunity tonight, Baba will give you an AMURT award. You can express yourself to Him.” I was a bit overwhelmed to go on stage, but I had nothing in my mind to express to Him. I went on stage after a number of other people received awards. I took the plaque from Baba and He gave me a namaskar and the most beautiful smile I’d ever seen. I returned that smile and felt close to Him. I had no idea that would

be the last time I’d see Him. In retrospect I appreciated that opportunity to say goodbye to Him face to face before He left His body.

### Guhan (2)

It was the summer of 1981. I was living in Denver. Dada Y (Yatiishvarananda), more popularly known as Dada Why?, was passing through and announced that there was a special program in India and we should all go! So, I and other margiis traveled to India. Margiis and workers from around the world came and we all crammed into the little building at Jodhpur Park. Baba was giving *Dharma Samiksa*, a special cleansing, to all of us.

It was the most dramatic thing I've ever seen in my life. It was really hot outside, but the room was air-conditioned. As soon as you entered the room, it was like entering heaven. The room was at a slant, and Baba at the lower part of the room. Brothers and sisters were all mixed together. Twenty people were sitting in rows. It was just incredible to observe. There were many *acaryas*, senior *acaryas*, and margiis from all different sectors. They moved up one by one. I was traveling with brother Viniita from California. One by one, we stood before Him and many dramas took place.

Before my turn came, there was time to observe some of these dramas. At one point, a brother from India stood before Baba. Baba told us all to close our eyes. As soon as we did, Baba started beating him like anything. Every time Baba hit him with His stick, He would say 'sex crimes, sex crimes, sex crimes!' And of course, the Indian brother was crying. Then Baba pulled him to His lap and comforted him and told him to be an ideal boy. This incident had me a little concerned! Was Baba punishing him for an actual sex crime or just for being promiscuous? Having been born and raised in California and having had girl friends in high school and college and beyond, I decided not to wait and find out. I turned around and started heading for the door but we were crammed in this room like sardines and it would have created a big scene for me to get out of there! So, with some fear and trepidation, I gradually worked my way up before Baba.

As I stood in front of Him, not knowing what to expect, He waved His stick and looked at me with His X-ray vision. All of a sudden, he became very angry and said, "This boy hasn't done any work! Get Out! Get Out! Get Out!" He yelled. I couldn't believe what was happening. I was dying right there on the spot. I looked around and the

room was in total chaos, as if a tornado had blown through there. All the *acaryas* were looking down or looking the other way or covering their faces. Finally, one of the *acaryas* grabbed me and led me off to a small, dark adjacent room. He told me to sit and meditate and then he left. Sit and meditate? I was sitting there crying like anything. I could've sworn Baba was my guru. “I can't believe He's not my guru,” I thought. But I was resigned to leave.

“He doesn't want me,” I thought. I left the room and went out into the crowd of margiis and acaryas. It was so hot! In the process of packing my gear and getting ready to depart for who knows where, there was an announcement that no one could leave the Jodhpur Park building because the CBI and the communists were harassing and deporting anyone caught leaving the premises. So we all had to stay there until the VSS deemed it safe to leave.

In the meantime, margiis and *acaryas* were ‘in my ear’ saying I had to go back in and face Baba again. I said, “No way, He threw me out!” Dada Yatiishvarananda came up to me saying, “Guhan, you're going back in there.”

“Dada, no, I'm not.” Finally, they coaxed and pushed me back into that little, heavenly air-conditioned room and the process started all over again – all those people getting cleansed, Baba prescribing asanas. It was just the most amazing thing I've ever seen in my life to see Baba sitting there, like a king, a beautiful king, in complete control, guiding us through all this stuff. Anyway, I was so emotionally drained, burned out, I realized I didn't care, I felt surrender. When I came up before Baba, His stick was wavering. He brought His stick forward like He was going to strike me and then He gently tapped my ribs on each side and then pulled me into His lap. I grabbed His feet. He looked at me very seriously and said, “You must help humanity now!” It felt so cool and refreshing in His lap. Then He said, “Go!”

As I left, I realized, “I'm asleep, I'm not realizing what's happening in the world, Baba wants us to serve.” I wasn't doing any work, in the true spiritual sense of service. I was doing my own personal survival stuff in the world, which all of us have to do, but I wasn't doing anything beyond that. I'd like to hope I'm now more active. The more I meditate, the more I'm active to fulfill what He did in *Dharma Samiksa*.

### Bharat (2)

In *Dharma Samiksa*, all I remember is that I was there and all of a sudden I was getting these sticks, all up and down one side and up and down the other. I think no one had as many sticks as I did. I thought maybe I had some trouble ahead, that things were going to happen, and a lot of things did happen. A lot of problems, frustrations, struggle, but also a lot of success. I don’t remember if He said anything, I think He was just busy giving me the sticks. But it didn’t hurt. It was sweet.

Some time in the ’70s, I had another opportunity to meet with Baba, with another mundane question. I had adopted the child of my wife, and her sister was living in the house, and I had a couple of kids. I was the only one who was vegetarian. They were cooking steaks and all this stuff. There was a lot of conflict between the way I was trying to live the Ananda Marga life, and their way of life. I went to see Baba, and rather than just have him say some kind of great thing to me, I said, “Baba, I don’t know what to do, I’m trying to do vegetarian diet, and Liila is not eating it.”

He said, “People are like planets. They follow a certain orbit, a certain path. You have to be patient.”

I said, “Thank you.”

And He was right again, because finally things began to change. I had to make a stand. I ate my food by myself in the kitchen. They felt badly and finally they changed. And it was really good for them, I think. But the most interesting thing about that was that some years later I went to the Philippines, in 1986. I was at a retreat, and a fellow there was an executive in some kind of business.

He said, “Why don’t you stay with me? I’ve got this house.”

I said, well, I appreciated it, but I wanted to stay with the retreat.

“No, You have to, you’ve got to stay with me.”

So I stayed with him. And he started telling me his story, how he can’t leave the country. He wanted to go see Baba so bad and he couldn’t get out of the country. Then he told me he had exactly the same problem with his family that I did. The whole thing, what Baba had told me, was the answer for him. Exactly.

Later, I was making a video of Baba’s speeches at Ananda Nagar. I was there with the camera, and it seemed like everyone was gone. I was sitting there with Baba. Other people were running around, and getting ready for things. We were just sitting there. Twenty thousand people, but here I was. I didn’t know what to say, so I said, “Thank you.”

And He said, “Thank you for patiently waiting.”

He had remembered exactly what He had told me twelve years before.

I think my life has been changed because of this experience. Working with AMURT all these years, I’ve probably been to 30 different places in the last 30 years. I went back to school, really because of this relief work, and got a doctorate in public health. I teach now. I’ve helped support projects all over the world. And I think most of this has been because of this experience. There’ve been so many things that resulted from this contact with Him.

*Dharma Samiksa* kind of speeds up your *samskaras*, so you’ll be a more spiritually realized person in this lifetime. Well, it sure speeded mine up. A lot of things I wish hadn’t happened, did, and I learned from them. But I’m still learning how to deal with things. One time at Ananda Nagar we were having an AMURT meeting. Baba was there and we were giving reports. They asked me to give a report because I had been very involved in a lot of the activities, particularly in Africa. I had to say all these different things.

When I got in front of Baba, I said, “Baba, I just, you know, a lot of these projects, we just don’t have enough money.”

Everyone was saying “No! Don’t talk to Baba about money” [waves his hands].

He was very nice, and said, “If the program is right, the money will come.”

And that’s been a very important comment. In all this relief work, the experience has been, when you’re not successful it’s because they’re not right. They’re not doing the program correctly, haven’t done the homework, they’re not talking with the community, not doing what the community wants. And that’s been the bottom line of what I teach. If you don’t do what people want, deep in their heart, you won’t have a successful program.

I think that He’s still guiding. Because I’m still learning.

### Dr. Ram (2)

I would like to narrate my experience with *Dharma Samiksa* around 1982. I was married in 1977 and my wife was not a margii then. Later she was initiated. But she had a lot of difficulties adapting to margii life and everything that was going on. There was a lot of struggle.

When Dada Yatiishvarananda told me to go to India, I had no idea what was going on. Baba was giving *Dharma Samiksa* to hundreds of people from all over the world. The *jagrti* was jam-packed. We were about 30 people in the room. One by one, margiis stood in front of Baba, and in a few moments He would look at the person and evaluate them totally, at the physical, psychic and spiritual level. Generally He asked questions, and gave asanas or physical punishment or rectification. For brothers a hit with the stick, and for sisters a spanking on the outstretched hand.

When Baba asked me why I did not bring my family along, I had no answer. I thought He knew. My wife did not want to come with me. And my daughter was very young. I could not think of an answer, and I got several strokes on my side. Several dadas in the background said, “Please say something.” So I said to Baba, “I will bring my family next time when I come.” Baba stopped, and took my face in His hand, and squeezed my cheeks. I guess I was a little too serious.

He gave me some instructions about my physical health, and gave some asanas. And then we were taken to the next room where we meditated. At the next *dharmacakra* there was a very different feeling, difficult to explain. It was like we were in a different vibrational world.

Then I had to leave for California. I reached the airport in San Francisco two or three days after the *Dharma Samiksa*. My wife came to meet me at the airport, and I happened to touch her. She was silent, but when we reached home afterwards she told me what happened. When I touched her, she felt a big shock, like electricity all over her body. She felt very blissful, like she had never felt in her life, and she could not explain it. After that, Prathima was almost totally changed, in many respects. Without going into too many details, this was a profound change.

But I could not keep my promise to Baba for another two years. Our second daughter was born, and two years later we all went to India. We went to Khanpur DMC. As soon as we reached the site, Baba was coming out in the car for His daily walk. Lots of margiis were assembled on either side. We were standing somewhere in the back. The car stopped right in front of where Prathima was, and it was only her that He looked at, and He talked to her in Hindi, “Did you just come?” and Prathima said, “Yes, Baba.”
