There were many old margiis there, but Baba only talked to her. And I was thinking maybe this was an acknowledgment that she had come, the promise I made in *Dharma Samiksa*. The following day during DMC, during the *varabhaya mudra*, Prathima had an identical experience with the one she had in the San Francisco airport. I felt Baba was saying her experience was true. It was as if Baba transmitted a blessing through me to her, and He was taking care of the devotee’s family. I kept this story to myself for years, but later I told it.

### Jyotishvar (2)

We heard that Baba was released from jail, and we were so excited. Baba was touring India, holding DMCs. Two margiis said they were going to India, and I went with them. This was 1978. I had never been outside the U.S. I had to get a passport, find my birth certificate, it was all so much trouble.

We were exhausted by the time we got to India. I thought we would go crazy once we reached India; with the crowds and heat, and culture shock, it was very rough. We landed in Bombay and flew to Delhi, and attended the DMC. I felt really happy once I was there. I hadn't slept in days. There were 20,000 or 30,000 people there. There wasn't enough room to put your knees down, and I kept dropping off to sleep and hitting the fellow's head in front of me.

Next day we flew to Bombay, and I was still very disorganized, and was standing at the bus stop for the airport. I would be on the same plane as Baba. I had my wallet in my pocket, and of course someone stole it. I was really upset – I got to the airport, and was almost crying. I had to check on my ticket. A number of margiis were there waiting for Baba to arrive. Suddenly I heard *"Parama Pita Baba Ki!"* I turned and there was Baba right next to me. He had not come in where they expected Him, but in a different door. He walked right up to me, gave namaskar, and then walked off. I had only seen Baba once before, and then He was just a little speck, three football fields away. And now suddenly He was just two feet from me. All my frustration and fatigue came out, and I just cried unashamedly. But in India, with the vibration of a holy man, crying is seen as a great thing. People seemed to be very supportive; people at the counter they thought I was a great devotee or something.

I was holding some bananas which I wanted to give to Baba. I wanted to fight through the crowd and give them to Him, but it was hopeless. It was time for Baba to leave and get on the plane. We went through security. Baba came through and sat down. And we sat on the floor by Baba's feet. He had His eyes closed, and I just stared at Him. I still wanted to give my bananas to Him. Someone asked Him, "Baba, how do you like flying?" some silly question, and Baba didn't respond. Then He opened His eyes and looked right at me, and said, "Are you flying to Bombay?"

“Yes, Baba.”

“And then to Madras also?”

“Yes, Baba.” I was really getting excited. Then we had to take the little bus out to the plane. I had on a tie and was trying to look official. I was worried about being deported, and trying to stay apart from all the hysteria, since this was an easy place for the immigration to catch me, but of course once Baba came I completely let go that idea. I dove in with all the margiis, and was sitting on the floor at Baba’s feet. What could be more obvious? So it was ridiculous trying to look like a normal tourist. I was weeping on the little bus. I was trying not to get too close to Him, because I didn’t feel it was too polite to sit right next to him, you know, “Is this seat taken?” I kept a little distance. But Baba motioned to us to come and sit close. I sat and I was weeping, and then I remembered my bananas. Ramananda read my mind – his mind was like permanently melded with Baba’s – and he just kind of motioned for me not to give them.

But Baba leaned over and said, “You take, you take.”

So I ate a banana, and it was really delicious. I was completely in some kind of walking samadhi. On the plane, I was sitting a little ways away from Baba, just feeling really relaxed and satisfied. Everyone on the plane was staring at me, I was weeping like a child. The lady sitting next to me said, “Your mind is completely gone” [laughs]. That’s such a compliment, in India. This was the best country in the world. I couldn’t have been happier.

We attended the darshans in Bombay, and it was great. But Madras was much better. We were always trying to get PC with Baba. This was a golden opportunity; there were so few margiis. Finally it looked like I would get in. It was a beautiful place, the weather was wonderful, the people so friendly. I went in, did *sastaung pranam*, and He asked who my *acarya* was.

“Do you want to be great?” He asked.

“Put your hands on your chest.” I had to take an oath. I would have said anything really, but it was very simple, that I would do sadhana twice a day. I put my head in His lap, He touched my head, and said, “Let this boy be great.”

Then I was about to leave. Baba was just doing namaskar, and I said “Baba?” and He stopped and quickly looked out and said “Yes?” I was just stalling, trying to delay leaving. “Could I touch your feet?”

He said, smiling, “But I have just touched your head.” And did namaskar. And then I left.

I’ll tell briefly about my *Dharma Samiksa*. I always felt a special warmth from Baba. I never felt any harshness from Him, even in *Dharma Samiksa*. My life since birth was always harsh enough, and Baba always took pity on me. He always treated me like a small child, almost an infant. There was always so much stored-up pain which came out when I was with Him. *Dharma Samiksa* was in August, and it was so much unlike my PC, which had taken place during the winter in Madras. In Kolkata, I became sick with typhoid fever and went in the hospital; I was almost deathly sick. We were really afraid of the police, everything was organized by Baba to make it difficult, and challenge us, and to remove the *samskaras* of countless past lives. So we were suffering greatly. The conditions in the *jagrti* were awful. And we couldn’t leave anyway for fear of getting arrested – some margiis did go out and got arrested. It was so crowded, we were packed in like sardines. We waited and waited. People were getting sick. Many were getting this eye disease, and Baba was very sensitive to that, and so they couldn’t go in for *Dharma Samiksa*.

We had to wait in a line to get there, and sometimes we had to close our eyes. Many people just got a whack. The dadas read out all the good things that people had been doing. I hadn’t done anything really. I got to the front and Dada was saying a bunch of stuff that wasn’t true at all, “He started three schools,” and Baba just stopped him from saying all that. Baba said to me, “Your spiritual life is very good. Your sadhana is good, but your personal life is no good. You’ve done many bad things with that body of yours.”

I had to pull up my shirt, and Baba took His cane, and He whacked and whacked, and it hurt. He started hitting on the same places. I never saw any Western margii get so beat up. Just lashed left and right. Someone outside of the organization could never understand. A couple times He hit me on the hipbone, which was very painful. But I

really believe that He relieved tremendous past karmas, painful deaths, things we didn’t even know we had in store for us. Basically it was a tremendous relief.

I remember I got out of there and waited outside. Baba came out, and they were holding the crowd back, but somehow I got pushed beyond the VSS guards. It was like I was invisible. Nobody could see me; I was walking along as if I was Baba’s personal bodyguard. I was holding my hands right behind Him, like I was trying to touch Baba’s aura. I remember looking at His neck, and the back of His head, walking right along behind Him, like I could just grab Him. I followed Him all the way down the hall, but couldn’t go out for fear of getting arrested. I stopped, and then the crowds just crashed over me. Then I got very sick. I wasn’t really well again for years. I think Baba must have brought out a lot of bad karma.

I felt Baba’s *krpa* working on me, and began doing a lot of work in my unit. Baba just started to work on me, it was like fermenting, like yeast in bread. I tried to go to reporting one time, and for some reason couldn’t get a visa. I saw some kind of fortuneteller where I was living, and she said I would never be able to get into India again. I was afraid I would never be able to see Baba again. I was doing a lot of work for AM at the time, and having tremendous sadhana, and felt like I could go into high *samadhi*, having these tremendous experiences you read about in books. And I felt I must go to see Baba. It was 1989. I was in Japan, saving money. I had been blacklisted in India and was afraid I would get caught.

Someone recommended I go overland from Nepal, so I did that, and got detained there. I had to bribe them. They asked me, “You’re an Ananda Margii aren’t you? Do you know Dada Daneshananda?” I said I didn’t know anyone in India. I kind of convinced them. Finally I gave them some money. I got in, went to Ananda Nagar, and could really feel Baba’s presence there, like He’d incarnated Himself in all the animals and plants and air. It was a full moon, and we had to sneak in. There was some kind of raid going on. We had to sneak out again, ran down to catch the train, and went to Kolkata.

Baba was very sick; it was hard to get into the darshan to see Him, but I finally got in. DMC was going to happen soon. Then Baba suddenly had a heart problem, and had to go to the hospital. We weren’t supposed to go to the hospital, but we went anyway, brought some flowers, and wrote a little note. We gave them to the driver. He came out

and said Baba was very excited about these flowers and wanted to know who brought them. He gave us His namaskar. I never saw Baba again after that.

### Jagadmitra (2)

Later on in my life I went for *Dharma Samiksa*. Actually I had just come back from India, and Dada Nityashuddhananda, the trainer here, called and said go to India. I said, “Dada, I’ve just come back from India.” He said, “Baba’s giving *Dharma Samiksa*, go to India.” Dr. Ram was here, and he called me, and said we should go. I said OK. I didn’t have any money at all at that time. I told my husband. I said, “I have to go to India, Baba wants to see me.”

He said, “You’re crazy.”

“I have to go, I have to see Baba again.” I had this friend I had taught school with. We used to always have these philosophical conversations. And so when I thought who could I ask for money, I thought of Bill Popovich. I called, and the person said, “Oh, I haven’t seen Billy for a long time, but his sister lives in…” and she gave me that number. And you know the feeling I had while all this was going on was the same feeling I had when the Jamaica thing was happening. My mind was racing. I felt like life was moving very quickly. I called his sister. “Hi, my name is and I’m looking for BP.” “Oh, well, Bill happens to be sitting right here” [laughs]. I said, “Hi. Bill, this is Gerry. Can you lend me $1500? I have to go to India.” He said, “Yeah, I’ll wire it to you.” So off I went to *Dharma Samiksa*.

I made many trips to India. I thought, there’s nothing better I can do with my life than go to India and see Baba. I was there when He was writing these songs, and I remember as a musician, being very frustrated because I couldn’t understand the words to the songs; they were all in Bengali. One day, Baba came out. It was always a choice whether you were going to stand on that side of the car, or this side – which side is Baba going to get out on? I stood on this side, and of course He came out the other side. I remember thinking strongly, “Why doesn’t He do anything in English?”

So Baba came out, and He looked across the car, and started singing this English song, ”Tiny Green Island.” And I thought, “Oh, He wrote a song just for me!” It was funny because the friend who was with me said, “No, He wrote that for me.”

“No, for me!” [laughs].

There were so many beautiful experiences like that. When I had such strong desires, He always gave me what I wanted. I think that’s the most beautiful part.

When I went to India for *Dharma Samiksa* in 1981, it was a situation where I was on the list and got bumped off. Not just that day, but to another day. Finally I went to see Dada Yatiishvarananda, my *acarya*, and told him I must see Baba, and so I finally did. But they sent me out again; Baba said only 12 people in the room at a time. I was the 13th. I was sent out again. Then at the last minute they called me back in.

They had three rows of people, brothers and sisters mixed. Baba was seeing each individually and prescribing asanas. I didn’t know exactly what *Dharma Samiksa* was, but I knew any personal contact with Baba was worth whatever I had to go through. Vijaya Laksmi was before me. Baba was scolding her, because she wasn’t doing enough work [laughs]. Oh my goodness, Vijaya did so much work! She was a nurse; she was very strong.

I never did any work. Even when I went to retreats, I never went to organizational meetings, but I went to every meeting where they talked about disciple-guru relationship, or anything like that. I just couldn’t get enough of that. And I’m a musician, I wasn’t a nurse. My service is playing, I guess. So I went up. Didi Ananda Karuna was there, instructing me what to do. And Baba said, “And you have done no work!” He took the stick and hit my hand three times, and then told Didi the asanas for me.

I left and went to meditate. The very interesting thing for me was, I had these three bruises on my hand. I’m a pianist; I’ve always had a strong passion for the piano. But once I started to meditate, and after I had PC, the music had kind of taken a second place, and meditation was becoming stronger and stronger; I was losing the desire to play. Well, after He hit my hand, it kind of opened up this door, for all these latent *samskaras*, musical *samskaras*.

Vijaya and Dr. Ram and I had taken a suite at a hotel; we were all back there meditating. While I was meditating, all of a sudden I felt this jolt, and I opened my eyes. It was so vivid, as if someone had taken a piece of paper and ripped it in two, and I felt separated from my husband, but I didn’t know what that meant. It was very real, but I didn’t know how to interpret it. So after I got back to the States, in two to three weeks, a letter came, and of such a nature that it destroyed the relationship. So what I had felt, I

didn’t know it was going to manifest on the physical plane, but it did. But after that, the music began. I started playing more and more, and studying at Stanford and San Francisco Conservatory, and gave several concerts.

The music just seemed to blossom. I always wondered if that striking my hands had anything to do with that, you know? I was kind of frightened in *Dharma Samiksa*, mainly because Baba was giving punishment to some people, and I understood that later to be a blessing. But it was very, very beautiful to be that close, just that close, and to have Baba giving you personal instructions. Ananda Marga had already gotten so big that my relationship with Baba was mainly on another level. It was not like I could say, “Hi, Baba” and He’d say, “Hi, Jagadmitra.” So it was very special to be that close to Baba and have Him give asanas.

Baba’s been the love of my love, the highlight of my life. I’m very thankful.

### Puspahrdaya

*Montpelier, Vermont*

In 1981, Baba began calling margiis to come for a special opportunity, *Dharma Samiksa*. My husband and our friend Pavan were able to go almost immediately, in June. I had just begun running a children’s program I was committed to through mid-August, and bought a ticket for the first day I’d be available, August 17^th. My husband and Pavan, like many other margiis, had wonderful experiences traveling to, waiting for, receiving, and then reflecting on the gifts Baba gave them. I was very excited to have my turn coming up. And then, perhaps a week before it was my time to go, Baba ended *Dharma Samiksa*. No more margiis were to come for it.

I was much disappointed. The travel agent in New York who booked so many margiis’ travel to India, an Indian man himself, was most sympathetic and apologized for the non-refundable fee, but of course, that is not what saddened me. The acaryas and margiis said, “Maybe Baba will start up again, let us see.” So we waited and wondered.

At a regional retreat in Ithaca the next month we were told that Baba wanted a First Global Conference to be held very soon. It would be in October, in Germany, and we thought, maybe Baba will come, He wants all the margiis from around the world to gather. Surely He will come.

But before even all these thoughts were voiced, I heard in my own mind, “I will go. Baba wants me to come whether He will be there physically or not. I want to please Him. I will go.” I arranged travel right away.

I recall going to Kennedy airport with my parents. My mother, who had met only a few other margiis, picked out some family people on a check-in line and suggested that they may be going where I was going, “because they look so peaceful.” This seemed funny but not uncharacteristic of my mother, who was known to see the objects of her affection and thought in the faces around her. Sure enough, though, they were margiis from the Carolinas, and I enjoyed traveling with them on the overnight flight.

When, after various interesting legs of the journey, we arrived in Russelsheim, Mainz, Germany, I was delighted by the conference site, a castle that was now a hostel,

with lovely gardens around it and situated near a train station. Bikes and bike racks were everywhere, and everything seemed so lively and friendly.

I started to meet margiis from all over the world, and there was a particular thrill to our first *dharmacakra* as the conference officially convened. There was still a question in our hearts, in our conversation and in the air, “Will Baba come?” as we went about diving into devotional, ideological, cultural and organizational programs with brothers and sisters from every continent.

By the end of our first day, which of course was bridged to the day before it by a sleepless night in transit and a time change, I was exhausted and depressed. Looking to settle down in a room stuffed with noisy sisters, I thought, “Why did I come? Was this a big mistake?!” But rest, the kindness of those around me and the wonderful morning muesli with warm milk soon revived my body and spirits.

At some point during the conference, it was arranged that every sector would have a group meeting with the respected Central office *acarya*, Dada Vijayananda. This was to be an intimate conversation between the margiis and Dadaji, in which we could ask him questions based on our own concerns. It was an intriguing and exciting prospect. When the appointed time came, our own Dada Yatiishvarananda, New York Sectorial Secretary, hosted the gathering, sitting with Dada Vijayananda before us in a large room. Because so many New York Sector margiis were fortunate enough to attend, we filled the room, sitting in rows and columns following some kiirtan and sadhana. I remember brother Sudeva from New Haven sitting behind me and to my right, toward the center of the room.

Dada V’s presence was strong and intense and compelling. His brilliance, devotion and integrity were unmistakable. I was very much aware of the unique opportunity this was for us, to discourse with one of Baba’s closest workers. And I was concerned about whether I would get a chance to ask a question, and what that question should be. Similarly, as the meeting progressed, I was restless when a sister I didn’t know, a new margii (compared to my five years at that point), asked about how important it was for her family to be vegetarian. I hardly paid attention to Dada’s thoughtful reply because of the noise in my own head – “What’s so important about that right now?? This dada could answer questions of the greatest subtlety! Let’s not waste time here. I want

MY turn!” Shortly following this internal ego-fest, I was called on by Dada Y to direct my question to Dada V.

I took *guru mantra* and asked, “What do you do when you have negative thoughts? I mean, I know we should take ideation and try to overcome negative tendencies by cultivating the opposite, positive tendencies, but wha…”

“You should speak in shorter sentences and do longer asanas!” Dada Vijayananda cut me off with controlled thunder. A bolt of energy went up my spine, jerking erect my head, which suddenly had the sensation of expanding with great speed, and I felt my whole body and head filled with tremendous force that was pushing against the borders of my entire being. I sat with my eyes closed, tears running down my face, feeling that Baba Himself was taking over, filling me, penetrating every organ and cell with His presence, stretching my body and nerves and whole physical, mental and spiritual existence to become one with Him.

I was only vaguely aware of the discourse continuing around me, in which Dada was explicating the tendency of people with narrow faces to use too many words, and so on and so forth. A couple of sisters around me responded to my shaking and tears, and to the palpable charge in the room, by starting to touch me, offering comfort. But I told them, “No, no, I’m fine, this is good!” Indeed, DadaYatiishvarananda injected a kind of joke to this effect: “In three thousand years no one has given *Dharma Samiksa* but Baba, and now Dada is joining in!”

But I knew, and I believe from those who approached me afterwards, that others knew, the truth behind both dadas’ words. I had a determined desire for *Dharma Samiksa*. I had gone to Germany only to please Baba. And in response, Baba had come to meet His little girl half way to satisfy my heart’s longing for His touch, His instruction, His blessing.

# CHAPTER FIVE:

## DID YOU COME ALONE?

> “The devotional sentiment is the highest and most valuable treasure of humanity. This element of devotion must be preserved carefully. Because it is such a tender inner asset, to preserve it from the onslaughts of materialism, one must build a protective force around it... a proper philosophy which will establish the correct harmony between the spiritual and material worlds, and be a perennial source of inspiration for the onward movement of society.”

The ’80s witnessed a burst of speed and dynamism in Baba’s work, and in the organization as a whole. Baba began composing *Prabhat Samgiit*, a body of devotional songs of incredible beauty and lyricism that eventually numbered 5018. Many devotees recall magical evenings singing these songs at Baba’s home in Kolkata. He developed the philosophy of Neohumanism, which rationally explained the interconnectedness of all life. Baba began to place emphasis on the spiritual and service communities called Master Units, especially the main MU, Ananda Nagar. And He continued to give Personal Contact. Through His glance, His touch, the hide and seek game of affection, devotees came closer and closer to Him.

### Dhyanesh

*Austin, Texas*

My name is Dhyanesh, and my *acarya’s* name is Daneshananda. He initiated me in April 1980 in Denver, Colorado. Before my initiation, I was looking for my guru for seven years. In the university, I had read the *Autobiography of a Yogi* by Yogananda, and was receiving the Self-Realization Fellowship lessons every week – one a week – and I would look forward to these lessons so much.

I learned *sattvic* diet, and I learned all the asanas, and I learned *Yama* and *Niyama*, and so many things, and I changed my lifestyle, fortunately, by His grace. And in landscape architecture school I was designing ecological spiritual villages. My professors thought I was a little bit crazy... but I had a strong intuitional feeling that some spiritual master was training me for the future.

And even though I know Yogananda taught me so much, I also had a strong feeling that he was preparing me for my true guru – all intuition.

In 1980 I was living in Denver, and I had had many glimpses of Ananda Marga, but just a little here and there. I went to the AMUS, an Ananda Marga Universal Store that opened next to my house in South Denver, and I started associating with margiis. They were telling me Baba stories, and I liked the stories I heard about Baba so much, and their service projects also impressed me.

Anyway, Dada Daneshananda was giving a lecture at the University of Denver, and I went and was initiated, and he told me I could only do *nama mantra*. At this time I had many lessons in Kriya yoga, and I was very inspired and strict with my practices, so that was difficult for me to only do *nama mantra*, but even with *nama mantra* I had a blissful meditation.

Every day I would go to the sectorial office in Denver – every day I would go see my dada and we would talk, and after a few days he gave me first lesson, and after a couple weeks he gave me second lesson. After I got second lesson, I was doing midnight meditation in my room in front of my *puja* table. On my *puja* table I had a photo of Yogananda and I had a photo of Baba, because Yogananda was my guru for seven or eight years. In the meditation I looked in the Baba photo with all my heart and I asked

him, “Are you my master? Are you the teacher I’ve been waiting for? Are you the one that I’ve been feeling?”

Then I fell asleep in my meditation, and Baba came to me in a dream that was not a dream: Baba came to me through my subconscious mind. He looked right at me, and His two eyes looked right into my eyes and mine into His. I felt so much love coming up inside of me, and I told Him how much I loved Him. Now this shocked me to hear myself say this because before this time I had never felt love like this before; I didn’t even particularly like the idea of a guru. I felt we all had a personal connection with God inside and why do we need a guru?

But, my soul, my inner self, felt this love for Him, and I told Him with my words how much I loved Him. This was the first key, to open the door. Then I felt Baba taking me with Him, and in this subconscious state I said my *guru mantra* completely and exactly, and then I started saying my *ista mantra* over and over and over again. Baba was teaching me how to surrender myself using the *ista mantra*. Then Baba’s skin became like a vessel filled with liquid gold light, and with the *ista mantra* I went into Baba, I was absorbed into Baba. I went from subconscious to superconscious, I bypassed the conscious mind. This was His way for me. He answered my question beautifully, directly, clearly. When I came out of this after some time – and even to this day I remember it – all the cells in my body were vibrated. I was changed. I didn’t eat for days, and I felt so vibrated and inspired that He had answered my question.

It wasn’t so long after that that I quit my job and sold my house and was on my way to *Dharma Samiksa*. Our Sectorial Secretary was disappointed I quit my job: “Why does everyone change so fast?” he said. But anyway, I was on my way to *Dharma Samiksa* and I got halfway there and Baba stopped, He went into isolation. I was told to stay in Europe; Baba would come for the first world convention. At that time I was able to visit many *jagrtis* and I decided strongly inside myself I was not ready yet, so I went to LFT training.

I worked as an LFT for one and a half years in San Antonio. By His grace we did many wonderful service projects. I worked in Wyoming and Denver also. But then I was

re-posted, and asked to run the New York sector office until July. Then I was re-posted in Kolkata as an LFT and I was thrilled. Every night I got to go see Baba; this was in 1983. That was the most special time in my life. He taught me so much; every morning I would feel His vibration. Even though I was on one side of Kolkata and Baba was on the other side of Kolkata, I could feel His vibration. Even to this day I feel the vibration – His mood I should say. I was not allowed to go every night– there were communist rallies or something like this, but most of the time I went.

Even though I was in His special aura in Lake Gardens, my mind was sometimes here and sometimes there. Only once out of every two weeks or so did He allow my mind into a more special subtle plane, to get closer to Him. One night I had a fever and I was in a particularly devotional mood. And that night Baba came back from field walk and He sat on a chair in the garden and all of us sat around Him, and they started to play the *Prabhat Samgiit* song that He composed that day.

I started to sway in a devotional state with the music and Him in front of me. And the thought came to my mind that… Baba had used this concept that all the unit beings are dancing around the nucleus of the universe. We are all at our own orbits and at our own rates of speed but we are all orbiting around *Parama Purusa*, or this nucleus of *Purushottama*. So in my mind I had that thought that I was swaying, I was dancing to the rhythms of life around the nucleus of the universe inside Baba’s form in front of me. And when I had that thought, while the music was playing, Baba turned and looked and me and said, “Yes,” like that. A very blissful vibration was happening.

But then, and this is the big lesson of the story, I noticed that Dada Yatiishvarananda, my Sectorial Secretary, was standing some distance away near the garage and watching me be with Baba. He was watching me, and my relationship with Him. Well, this disturbed my mind because now somebody was watching me. I don’t know what it is – ego or whatever comes up, but it ruined the vibration. But a thought came to me, and in my mind I said, “No! My whole life I’ve been worried about what other people think! What other people feel! About being accepted.”

Maybe being number six out of seven children, it’s been competitive. Prestige, Baba calls it. Whatever it is, I had this strong thought: “No! My whole life I’ve been worried about what other people think. No more. Baba, it’s only you and me in the whole

universe.” Just that personal relationship. The second I had that thought, Baba turned to me and said, “Very good!” Like that, while the music was playing [laughs].

So much grace He gave me. This is what Baba calls the subjective approach in life. We get so caught up in the objective world and our position in it that we lose the closest one, and this was the lesson He was teaching me. The music stopped and Baba got up. I had a garland for Him that day. I think I was leaving the next day for somewhere. Baba got up and turned away from me and was going out, and I was behind Him and I had this thought in my mind, “With this garland, Baba, I’m giving you my all: my physical, my mental, and my spiritual being I’m giving you.”

And Baba stopped, and He turned all the way around, and He let me put the garland over His head, and He said, “Very good.”

This was a wonderful experience for me. All of these experiences are such special grace of the Lord. All of our souls love Him, and this was like news to my conscious mind, that my soul, my inner self, was already in love with Him. He was teaching me how to think, and how to feel, basically. I always asked, “Let me love you more.” That was the only thing I’d ever ask of Him. And I think it’s important that we spiritual aspirants ask this. Baba said we should only ask one question: guide my mind onto the path of bliss. It’s similar: let me love you more.

### Jayamati

*San Diego, California*

I had the very strong desire to see Baba, to see His physical form. I remember when I lived in Denver and Baba was planning to come to the New York Sectorial Office. It was an exciting time, everyone working hard to prepare. It was so wonderful that Baba was coming to us. My sister Dipanii, who is a margii, too, and I were planning to go camping up in the Colorado mountains for about five days and then come back, and Baba would be coming to Denver. We stopped on the way to the mountains at a friend’s house, and he told us that it looked like Baba would not be coming to Denver. They would not let Him in the country and He was going to land in San Francisco and we could see Him there.

Our friend was flying out and he lent us money and we decided to drive out in our little Volkswagen instead of going to the mountains. Our parents thought we were in the mountains. By Baba’s grace it was an uneventful trip, and I felt like I was on a straight path to the Guru. My desire to see Him was so strong. When we got to the Los Altos *jagrti*, it looked like the U.S. officials would not even let Baba off the plane. It would be so wonderful if Baba could even step on the United States soil, so uplifting for this country. I did not get to see Him. I remember meditating before His picture and just crying my heart out. My heart was broken, it was a loss that I had never experienced. I wanted to see Him, to be in His physical presence so much.

About three or so years later, I got the chance to go to India and see Baba. I was so one-pointed, that I knew that I was going to see Him. Everyone else was going, “Oh, what if we can’t get into Tiljila; what if we get deported?” all this drama stuff around seeing Baba. I knew that I would see Him. I remember flying on an airplane and a dada pointing out the Himalayas. I was not interested. I only wanted to see Baba. In Kolkata, I had a strong desire to see Baba’s garden. I was at Baba’s house and I was really stressed out with the travel. I walked through the gardens, and felt much peace. A dada scolded me afterwards, saying, “Didn’t you see the sign that no one was allowed to walk through there?” I honestly did not see the sign.

When I finally arrived at the sisters’ *jagrti* in Tiljila in the middle of the night, I sat down and did meditation. Baba appeared in my mind’s eye and namaskared and welcomed me there. He knew that I had traveled all this way to see Him. The next morning a didi came and told me to hurry and come see Baba. He was walking on His verandah. I hurried, and then, finally I saw His physical presence. It felt like time stopped. I do not know how long I was there. All I could say was that it felt like I was home. I had finally come home. He would walk back and forth on His verandah, and I just wanted to keep seeing His form again and again. It felt like the world took on a beautiful, shining quality.

After that, I would do anything to be in His presence. I went and sat near the place where He had given darshan, and had the clearest and strongest meditation ever. At the *Dharma Maha Chakra* ceremony, when Baba did the *mudra*, I felt a strong jerk in the bottom of my spine and I cried and cried during *Guru Puja*. I remember crying a lot in India, and just opening my heart. In my lifetime, of all the things that I have done, the one thing that I am glad that I did was go see Baba while He was still in His physical form.

A few other experiences: I was in Alaska, sitting in a bar, and I remember asking the waitress for a pen, and I wrote Baba’s name on my hand. That night I almost had a very bad thing happen to me, but it didn’t and I knew that Baba was looking after me.

I had a very vivid dream that Baba had healed me. In this dream I got a piece of paper that Baba had written on and it said that he would take the contaminated matter from my fallopian tubes and put it in His pancreas. (I had been dealing with years of infertility). We did finally adopt a beautiful boy, Narada.

When Baba left His physical body, I tried to be sad, but Baba wouldn’t let me. I remember crying before His picture. Then He would fill me up with such bliss, that I could no longer be sad. When I got the book in the mail commemorating Baba, and it showed the picture of Him on the funeral pyre, then it seemed so real and I cried and cried and cried. For a whole year I kept saying that I wish Baba was still here in His physical form. I had a dream that I was looking at a Baba picture and there was an empty chair where Baba was supposed to be sitting.

In another dream I was looking at a Baba photo and there was this light around Baba, and a dada told me that was Baba’s light body. I knew that Baba was in his light body. He had not gone anywhere.

### Yogendra

*Cuernavaca, Mexico*

When I make a retrospective of my life, I notice that Baba has been always near me and I realize that all experiences that I have had with the Guru possess an immense value. Nevertheless, there was one that began in a normal workday when suddenly the telephone in my office rang. It was Dada Brahmananda from New York who said, “Yogendra, Namaskar, I call you to tell you that you must go to India since Baba is giving personal contact and it is a great opportunity that you shouldn’t waste. So prepare everything you need for the trip: money, passport, visa, etc. and as soon as you are ready, let me know.”

As on many other times when a dada or didi called to tell me the same and I would always say that perhaps one day I would do it, on this occasion it was no exception – I continued my activities as if nothing had happened. In reality in my mind a trip to India was the same as if it were to another planet.

Some days later, Dada called me again asking about my advances on the preparation for the trip, which in reality were zero. For that reason, Dada got a little angry and told me that it was an opportunity that I might never have again. Despite the insistence of my *acarya*, I continued with my daily normal routine and my wife Malati and I decided to go to Mexico City for the next weekend to visit my parents. I remember that we arrived on Saturday evening and after dinner we chatted for a while and later on we went to sleep. Then, as we say in Mexico, “Here comes the best.”

I started dreaming that I was walking on a street on a big and populous city. People seemed to be like the people of my own town but there was a difference about them, and the houses and buildings were different also. I asked where I was but when I spoke to the people they would not understand me. Suddenly something on the ground took my attention; it was the metallic cover of a drain from the water system of the city which had something written in unknown characters to me, but that I had seen once in a book. And then I came to realize that these were words in Bengali or Hindi and I thought, “Well, I’m in India, I don’t know how but I’m here and as Baba is in India, what I have to do is to go to visit Him.”

I arrived at a house where there were many didis, dadas and people from all over the world, all of them waiting for an opportunity to see the Guru. I went into the house and sat on the floor of a pretty big room where most of the people were youngsters of different nationalities waiting for personal contact with Baba. When one of them would enter and come back from being with the Master, all of us would run and ask what He told him.

It was then that my turn came and I went into the room where He was seated on an armchair dressed in white, looking somewhere else. Then it was I said to myself, “Is He Baba? I don’t feel anything seeing Him.” In that moment He looked at me and a bright light came out from His eyes which blinded me and I saw myself in front of a huge mountain, maybe it was a volcano. I was in front of Him without being able to say a word. With a sweet but powerful voice that I have never heard again He told me, “I am not only in the human beings, I am also in the plants, and in everything that surrounds them.”

Suddenly I woke up with the great desire to meditate. I sat down, closed my eyes and a flood of energy started to run through my body and such an extraordinary happiness was felt in my whole being that tears broke out, as they never had before. I couldn’t stop them and this extreme happiness seemed interminable. I don’t know how long I stayed like that, I just know that when I came to “normality” I had only one thought, “I have to go to India to see Baba at any price.”

Next day we returned to Cuernavaca and right away I started to gather money and on Tuesday I applied for a visa that was given to me the next day. I spoke with the people I was working for as an architect and as much as I could, I left everything taken care of, things that were pending and situations that now when I think of them, I’m surprised I left because I was tied up in my job. On Friday I packed, prepared my documents and the money I had gathered and said goodbye to Malati. I drove north toward Dallas, and arrived there next day in the evening. From the first telephone I saw, I called Dada Brahmananda who was in another city, and when he answered the phone he started scolding me saying, “Yogendra, you don’t understand, you will never have an opportunity like this…”

“Dada, I’m in Dallas.”

“How come in Dallas, it can’t be?”

“Yes, Dada, I just arrived and that’s why I’m calling you.”

“Well, now what you ought to do is call Ravi Batra and when you are with him you’ll give him the money you brought and he’ll arrange everything so you can go to India as soon as possible.”

Once I hung up, I followed the instructions, bought a map of the city and soon I met Ravi Batra who took care that nothing was missing. It was done in a short time because he already knew that my flight to India would leave next Monday morning from Dallas to New York and from there to India. On Monday Ravi went early to the Prout office and took me to the airport where I continued my trip to see Baba.

I arrived in New Delhi at night and even though it was a strange place I felt secure. From the beginning I felt somehow that I knew and had possibly been in this place. I took a taxi, which drove me to an area where a *jagrti* was. I was nicely welcomed by a dada who happily took me to a room where I spent the night willing to be close to my master. Next morning I woke up early and after doing my routine I went to the airport where I received bad news.

Because it was Diipavali (the light festival, a season with great movement, almost like our Christmas season for people who travel during those days), all the flights to Kolkata were saturated. I was told that they would put my name in the waiting list and that in three or four days they would confirm my flight. I felt bad because my time was limited and this was very inconvenient. When I returned to the *jagrti* Dada told me that I could try to go by train, though I might have the same problem and also traveling by train was a lot slower.

I went to my room to do kiirtan and meditation, and that helped me to calm down. I decided to go for a walk and see a little bit of New Delhi. I took some reference points so I wouldn’t get lost and I left. In a few minutes I was in a zone where there were a lot of people and buildings that looked oddly familiar to me. As I was walking I looked down and I saw a drain with a metal cover with strange language characters written on it. My body shivered with the sensation that I had lived that experience before. A sense of impotence took possession of me; I felt that even when I was so close to Baba I couldn’t see or be with Him.

The next day I returned to the airline office to see if the situation had changed; however, I knew it was the same, and I had no possibilities of traveling at least in the next three days. Nevertheless, I wasn’t defeated so I tried some other possibilities; I explained to them that I had traveled from the other side of the world just to see my father. I asked to speak with someone who had the power of decision. The lady that was taking care of my situation looked at the system for a while and then said, “Look, just now a cancellation was made; since this is a special case I’ll give you the seat, but you must hurry because the plane leaves in less than two hours.”

Nervously and at the same time happy, I accepted, thanked her for the attention and took a taxi to the *jagrti* to pick up my things. I was excited when I entered the *jagrti* in a rush and Dada asked me what was going on. I explained to him that in a few minutes my flight to Kolkata was leaving, and an uproar burst out with everybody who helped me with my luggage and called a taxi since I had limited time to get to the airport. Everything went fine after I arrived at the airport.

In a few hours I was in Kolkata; from there I went to Tiljala, where I asked for Baba, and I was told to go to Lake Gardens. I began to look for Dada Haratmananda who was the New York Sectorial Secretary. When I saw him he was very surprised and gave a place in his room so I could rest. At that moment the least I wanted was rest, so I told Dada that I wanted to go to Lake Gardens. Dada asked how much money I had. I showed it to him and he said, “Oh! Yogendra, that’s very little money, how did you get here?”

Dada gave me a few rupees and directions to go to Lake Gardens. I took my briefcase and I left trying to not forget anything. I walked for a while until I heard my name; two dadas were calling me – one of them was very tall and the other was very short, they were always together and they also were going to see Baba. They told me that we could go together and while walking one of them asked me if this was my first time in India, and if I had never seen Baba, he recommended I do half meditation, to repeat my *mantra* mentally until we arrived at His house.

At Lake Gardens there were lots of people, dadas, didis and margiis from all over the world. I walked around for a while and then someone announced that Baba was about to come out to take His afternoon walk; our excitement became more intense. The didis

started to sing *Prabhat Samgiita* and a dada told me, “Sing even when you don’t know the lyrics; just follow the melody.”

When the Master came out, He gave us His Namaskar. I must admit that my emotion didn’t increase – I was very happy but I wasn’t altered. Some brothers and sisters were crying. I would have liked to feel like them but it just didn’t happen. Anyway I was happy and I stayed there waiting for Him to return from His walk so I could see Him again. Once everything calmed down, I introduced myself to some dadas including Baba’s assistant Dada Keshavananda who was very polite with me all the time and made me feel at home. He then took me to a room which wasn’t very big, and where some youngsters from all over the world were sitting on the floor waiting for personal contact with Baba. I talked to some of them and they told me that they were from different continents. I noticed then, that when someone came out from being with Baba, everybody in the room asked him what the Master told him.

The days were passing by in that way, and some people told that they had been waiting for months to have Personal Contact. Some others would come and leave, and others just weren’t allowed to see Baba. I remember a brother who was refused to have an interview, even when he explained he had good relations with wealthy people, he had to return to his country without accomplishing his purpose. There was another brother who had already entered Baba’s room but he wasn’t wearing his *pratik*, and he had to come out, crying. There were other cases when Baba asked something in Bengali to Spanish and English speakers and they couldn’t answer, and the interview was interrupted. For that reason a dada started to give us an elementary course of Bengali, in order that we could answer questions like: what’s your name? How old are you?

Every night that passed we were told to return to Tiljala to have some rest and to return the next day in the morning.

One day we heard that Baba was coming to Tiljala; the atmosphere turned festive. However, that day I was called to see Him, and when my turn came, a dada came out and said that was all for that day. My preoccupation increased because I had to return to Mexico due to the great amount of work I had there. My mind was crowded with thoughts like, “What if my interview is in two months?” “What am I going to do if I’m not received?” and “What about the job I left undone?”

Each day that passed my preoccupation increased, and sometimes I could not concentrate while meditating. One day I returned to Lake Gardens and for some reason that I never knew I was allowed to sleep at Baba’s house. That night at dawn a young dada that I had never seen before and I never saw again, woke me up saying, “Yogendra, you are 29 years old.” I answered him that I was 33 and he told me, “You are 29 and if Baba asks you about your age you’re going to tell Him that, otherwise he won’t give you personal contact.”

I answered him, “Yes, Dada, I understand.” He left and I went back to sleep. When I woke up I felt sick, I had a fever and I was tired. Anyway I did my best to wake up and took a shower; that day was fasting so I went to the waiting room. I felt even more sick, and tried to meditate but my preoccupations didn’t allow me to concentrate so I decided to analyze my situation and make a definitive decision.

“I’m in India, I’m close to Baba, I see Him everyday, I meditate near Him, we have had *dharmacakra* in front of him, what else do I want? I should enjoy every moment of the happiness I receive just being near Baba; I have overcome the worst part already. Baba, I just ask for the patience to wait; it doesn’t matter if I have to wait one week or two or even to wait months for You to receive me, I put myself in your hands.”

I tried to meditate again. My mind was clear now, and a great tranquility emerged until I felt encouraged with the idea of staying there until I had personal contact. Once I finished my sadhana, I went to the garden for a while. It was a beautiful day, the sky had an intense blue, there were very few clouds but the shapes they had were very beautiful, the birds were singing and they demonstrated joy, and being calmed, I returned to the waiting room. I still had a fever but I felt a lot better now.

Maybe an hour or less passed when a dada called me and began asking, “Did you take a shower today? Are your clothes clean? Are you wearing *laungota*? Are you wearing a *pratik*?” I answered yes to every question and then he said, “Yogendra, you are going to see Baba right now.”

My tranquility turned to nervousness. I went upstairs and at the entrance of the room was another dada who gave me some instructions and opened the door. Baba was there totally dressed in white sitting in an armchair, and He was looking at me firmly. I walked toward Him and gave Him a Namaskar and my *Sastaung Pranam* and suddenly

this thought came in to my mind: “*Tava Dravyam Jagat Guru Tuvyameva Samarpaye*” [To the guru I offer everything]. I stood up and continued walking. Baba answered my salute and asked me in English,

“WHAT’S YOUR NAME, MY SON?”

“Yogendra, Baba.”

“HOW OLD ARE YOU?”

I thought about my *Guru Mantra* and said, “Twenty-nine years old, Baba.”

“OH! YOU ARE A VERY LITTLE BOY.” I noticed by His smile that He knew my real age.

“COME CLOSE TO ME AND SIT DOWN HERE.” He pointed at a place in front of Him. I was so close to Him that if I moved my arm I could touch His foot. My nervousness increased and the fear of breaking up the interview made me ask, “Baba, can I touch you?”

Without saying a word He just raised His hand and made a gesture telling me to wait.

“WHERE ARE YOU FROM?”

“I am from Mexico, Baba.”

“MEXICO! YOU LIVE IN A PLACE SURROUNDED BY VOLCANOS. IT’S A WEALTHY COUNTRY BUT BECAUSE OF THE GOVERNMENT CORRUPTION, IT HAS BEEN SUNK IN A CONSTANT ECONOMIC CRISIS. YOU SPEAK SPANISH AND IT IS A LANGUAGE WHICH IS ORIGINALLY FROM A REGION NEAR VALENCIA, SPAIN.”

As He was telling me each phrase I would answer yes by moving my head because I was entering in a state that wouldn’t let me do any other thing, just look at Baba. My eyes were only for Him.

“YOGENDRA, YOU FORGOT THE RELATIONSHIP BETWEEN BROTHERS AND SISTERS. YOU WANT TO BE RESPECTED BUT YOU DON’T RESPECT THEM. TELL ME WHY YOU HAVE BEHAVED LIKE THAT ALL YOUR LIFE.”

“Baba, I think it is because I’m weak.”

“NO, YOU HAVE DONE SO BECAUSE YOU THINK NOBODY IS LOOKING AT YOU, BUT I HAVE ALWAYS BEEN OBSERVING YOU, ALL THE TIME. BECAUSE OF ALL THOSE THINGS AND FOR YOUR MISBEHAVIOR, DO YOU THINK YOU DESERVE A PUNISHMENT?”

“Yes, Baba.” For that, He was asking me with words I didn’t understand, and immediately He changed them into synonyms easier to understand.

“TAKE OFF YOUR SHIRT.”

Then He said something I didn’t understand, took His rod and raising it, He said, “HOW MANY DO YOU THINK YOU DESERVE?”

“I don’t know Baba, whatever you like.”

“TEN, TWENTY, OR THIRTY?”

“Thirty, Baba.”

“THIRTY ON ONE SIDE OR FIFTEEN IN ONE SIDE AND FIFTEEN IN THE OTHER?” He said, pointing to each one of my shoulders.

Believing that it was going to hurt a lot, I asked Him for fifteen and fifteen. Baba raised again His rod, took it back as if to gather strength to hit harder. I closed my eyes and felt a slight touch on my left side.

“WHEN A BAD THOUGHT COMES TO YOUR MIND, JUST REPEAT, ‘OH BABA!’ AND THAT THOUGHT WILL VANISH, BECOMING SOMETHING POSITIVE.”

“THIS IS ALL, NOW COME CLOSE TO ME.”

Opening His arms I came close to Him and I touched His hands, they were as soft as a baby’s skin. I touched His feet and He let me caress them. He put his hands on my head and pressing the crown of my head, He said some words, maybe it was a *mantra*; I can’t exactly explain what happened. I felt as if I was floating with Baba and only He and I existed. I held His hands again and looked at Him. A great love emanated from Baba, I felt protected, secure, and without any fear.

Little by little He let go my hands and said, “NOW, YOU ARE GOING TO PROMISE ME THAT YOU’RE GOING TO BE THE BEST OF MY SONS.”

“I’m going to try, Baba.”

“NO, YOU’RE GOING TO PROMISE ME.”

“And if I can’t?”

“PROMISE IT!”

“Yes, Baba, I promise you.”

He said an oath, asked me to repeat it and when I finished I could barely listen to what He said.

“NOW YOU CAN LEAVE.”

I stood up and walking backwards I moved away until I reached the door, I don’t know if I gave Him my Namaskar, I just remember that I knelt down again in front of Him and left the room. Outside I kept standing for a moment, and the dada who had given me the last instructions came close and asked me, “What did Baba tell you?”

I wanted to answer him but I couldn’t, it was impossible. Instead, I could only say, “Baba is God” and then I couldn’t articulate a word. A tremendous happy sensation started and tears began to come out without being able to stop them. The dada told me to sit down and meditate just by the side of Baba’s room. Again that energy and happiness overflowed through my body, I lost the notion of the place I was in and let myself go until I was totally calmed. I was meditating for about fifteen or twenty minutes. I went down the stairs and another dada asked me about what Baba had told me. I wanted to answer him but I couldn’t. I couldn’t talk and the torrent of happiness and crying invaded me again. The dada helped me, telling me to sit down and meditate more. I don’t know how long I stayed there. That intense happiness possessed me again and only after a long period of time could I incorporate myself. The dadas came but they didn’t ask questions. There was one who came and embraced me and took to the garden because he knew that other margiis were anxious to know about my encounter with the Guru and I wasn’t in a condition to answer to anybody.

As soon as I came out to the garden I saw the plants, it seemed as though they were beating, I could almost feel the circulation of life in them. I heard the birds and they seemed to accompany a kiirtan with its songs; suddenly I looked at the sky, beautiful blue and the few clouds that decorated it as if it was a monumental piece of art and inside my mind the remembrance of that powerful but at the same time sweet voice which was telling me,

> “I AM NOT ONLY IN THE HUMAN BEINGS, I AM ALSO IN THE PLANTS, ANIMALS AND IN EVERYTHING THAT SURROUNDS THEM.”

### Jyotisa

*Flagstaff, Arizona*

During the summer of 1972, after my freshman year at college, I found myself exploring spiritual ideas. I read the autobiography of an Indian yogi who had lived many years in America. It fascinated me to discover that there exists a science of spirituality, a path one can follow to the spiritual heights. I was eager to undertake its practice. The problem was that I felt that I needed a true guru, one who was fully realized. This presented a problem as the possibility of meeting such a guru seemed remote, at best. I fell into a state of despair. For weeks on end my waking hours were haunted by a spiritual longing unfamiliar to me. One day while walking down the street I was unable to contain my pain and I burst into tears.

Shielding my eyes from other pedestrians I continued walking ahead, going nowhere in particular, my tears falling softly onto the concrete sidewalk. Suddenly a colorful hand-drawn poster caught my eye. I stopped and read it. It announced an upcoming lecture on yoga and meditation sponsored by Ananda Marga. I felt surprisingly hopeful, and resolved to attend the lecture two days hence.

The day of the lecture I left my home hours early to allow sufficient time to arrive at the lecture site. I had to hitchhike as I did not own a car and the bus was abysmally inefficient. While walking to the corner where I intended to solicit a ride I was seized with misgivings. How was I to know if this was a path for me? I decided to put it to a test. I mentally resolved that if God was real and if this was a path I should follow, then the first car which passed me on the road must pick me up and take me to the door of the lecture. This was no small demand as the lecture site was 12 or more miles away and a few miles off the main road.

I strode to the corner and with my back to the traffic I stuck out my thumb. Instantly a car screeched to a halt. I turned my head to see a silver Porsche sports car at my side. The door swung open. “Get in,” the professionally dressed young man driving the car suggested as I was standing with my thumb extended and my mouth open.

“Where are you going?” he inquired as we pulled away. “Mission St. exit,” I replied, giving the name of the freeway exit where I expected to be dropped off.

“But where are you going?” he persisted. Relenting, I replied, “I’m going to a lecture on meditation being held at the Unitarian Church.” Despite my protestations, he insisted on driving me miles out of his way to the door of the church. I was stunned yet elated. As I stood on the steps of the locked church (I was the first to arrive), I reflected on my experience. What did it mean? Was I being superstitious? Had I intuited the course of events and so placed my demands in keeping with them? Was it real?

I enjoyed the lecture and went the next day to meet with Dada to learn meditation. Feeling heady from my experience of the previous day I decided to repeat the test and again resolved that the first car must take me to my destination. I walked to the same spot as before and put out my thumb. Whoosh! Whoosh! The first two cars passed without stopping. My spirits sank. The next car, a small pick-up truck, pulled up beside me.

“Where are you going?” the driver asked as I climbed into the bed of the truck.

“Anapumu street,” I replied, giving neither the number nor a cross street.

Feeling somewhat crestfallen I reflected on my recent experiences as I sat alone in the rear of the pick-up. The more I thought about it, the more foolish I felt. If there was a God, most certainly this God was aware of my needs. Why was I making such trivial demands and tests? I should resolve to place my desires in line with that of the supreme rather than try to manipulate God. I decided then and there that since I had an intellect I should use it to make decisions even about spiritual matters.

Absorbed in these thoughts, I paid no attention to where my ride was going. Suddenly, the truck stopped at the curb. “Hey, get out!” the driver yelled at me. Waving my thanks I hopped to the ground as the truck sped away. I wondered how many blocks or miles I would have to walk to get to the desired address. Looking around I checked a house for a street number. To my astonishment, I was directly in front of the house I was looking for! I had been taken directly to my destination by a driver who knew only the street name and not the number. He had merely stopped at what was a convenient place in a busy part of town.

“Good enough!” I laughed as I walked up to the house to meet with Dadaji. I felt as if I were in a cosmic play being directed by a force I could not see.

My desire to meet Baba grew year by year. About six months after Baba was released from prison, I had a curious experience in my meditation. When I would sit to meditate I would experience an image of Baba. In this experience He was walking towards me, His hands together in spiritual greeting, and His gaze meeting mine. Somehow He was always walking towards me and yet He never reached me. He was always just a few steps away. It became impossible to meditate. If I had an hour to meditate, 55 minutes would be spent absorbed in this vision. In the remaining five minutes I would perform all my meditation lessons and run out the door to work.

This experience had continued for three to four weeks when an *acarya* came to visit my area. Feeling guilty concerning the lack of time I had been spending on my meditation lessons I asked the *acarya* what to do. He simply said “It’s OK. Don’t worry about it.” Immediately thereafter the experience stopped. I went on with my life and forgot that it ever happened.

I missed the DMC in Jamaica and was crushed as I felt I had missed the opportunity of a lifetime. Late the next summer one new Indian dadajii toured through my area. Feeling the pangs of my desire to see Baba and hearing how I had missed Jamaica he said, “You must go to India this winter for DMC. You must go!” Somehow, at that moment, I felt that somehow I not only could go, but I would go to India that winter. I had no savings and minimal income. I took my car to the credit union in an effort to take a loan against it. This car was a joke; it was always breaking down and having problems. Somehow, incredibly, the loan officer gave me a loan many times the value of the car. I suddenly had the money for my trip. I called Dada Yatiishvaranandaji and found that others would be going to India that winter under his guidance. Wonderful! I made arrangements to meet with this group in New York around December 20 and from there we would fly to India via Italy. I arranged my passport and visa as the time quickly passed.

To the best of my memory there were 18 of us total in the group going to India, most of us general margiis, with a handful of *acaryas*. We flew to Bombay and then took a domestic flight to Kolkata, where Baba lived and worked. We arrived at the *jagrti* in the early evening. It was my first experience in the tropics. I slept on the roof of the *jagrti*. Baba’s house was less than a mile away. One dadaji showed me the rickshaw

stand down the block where I could get a rickshaw to Baba’s house. He told me how much to pay the driver. I bathed, did sadhana and asanas and went down to engage a rickshaw. I only had to say “Baba’s house” and the rickshaw driver motioned onto the seat. As I was bicycled through the small winding streets of Kolkata I was filled with a wonderful anticipation. I reflected on my past eight years as an Ananda Margii. I remembered that first lecture, my first retreats, and the years of longing to see Baba. These thoughts were mixed with the scents and feelings of the warm tropical evening, the sounds of people walking and talking in small groups, the smells of people cooking in their homes.

Without warning the rickshaw stopped. I dismounted and paid the driver. A small door opened in the large gate and I was admitted onto the premises by a young man in a guard uniform. I found myself in a driveway where a small car was parked headed outward. There were many margiis from around the world milling about talking softly or just waiting. Hundreds of plants and trees were planted in the ground and in pots hanging from the walls and on the porches. There were a few statues of historical spiritual figures and small signs hanging from many plants which I learned later were Bengali poems. I had only a few minutes to take this in when suddenly two guards standing near the corner of the house snapped to attention. In just a moment everyone standing there reverently made a path to the car through which Baba was to pass.

And then Baba appeared around the corner. A guard walked backwards in front of Him making sure His way was clear and there were no hazards in His path. Baba was wearing a simple white *dhoti*, the traditional Bengali dress. I was some distance away from Him on the other side of the car. I could see Him stop and talk with an occasional person as He proceeded along. The entire route to His car from the time He came around the corner until He got in His car was only 15 feet or so. He got in the car, the gates opened, and He, along with those chosen for the field walk, were driven away by the driver. They were followed by a larger vehicle filled with more guards.

“Well,” I thought, “so that was Baba.” I wasn’t sure what to think, and somehow I had expected more. After the car left, everyone started milling around again as it would be some time before Baba returned. I noted that there was one spot just near the corner of the house where, if a person were standing there, Baba would walk right past them as

there was no room for anyone to block the view. I immediately went over and stood in that spot. I didn’t care how long it would be before Baba returned, I wanted to see Him close up.

After 50 minutes or so the sound of a car horn honking was heard coming up the street. The guards swung the gate open. Everyone suddenly shoved and pushed to get a good position to greet Baba. I was positioned so that I would be the last person Baba would pass before reaching the side yard and heading into the house. Baba got out of the car. He was so happy. He stood and with His palms together in spiritual greeting, He smiled at the crowd. He greeted everyone, turning slowly from His left to His right. Being the farthest to Baba’s right I watched as His pleasant smile passed across the crowd. Suddenly Baba’s gaze met mine. His face lit into a broad smile as if He were greeting an old friend. Holding my gaze He started to walk towards me. His hands together, closer and closer He came. I was so shocked I didn’t know what to do. I tried to hold His gaze but my eyes kept rolling upwards into my head. Closer and closer He stepped and then, inches before colliding with me, He turned to the side, laughed quietly, and proceeded on His way.

Gazing at His retreating form I heard Him reply to His personal assistant (Ramanandaji), “Really? Eighteen of them? From America?”

Suddenly He turned around and faced me again. He walked up to me, smiling, and to my bewilderment addressed me.

“So,” He said, “you have come from America?” I stood staring mutely at Baba. A voice in the back of mind was screaming “Baba is talking to you! Answer Him!”

But before I could collect my wits enough to put forth, an answer Didi Anandarama, a nun who was standing next to me and also part of our entourage, said, “Yes, Baba.”

Baba smiled an acknowledgement to her response and again looked at me.

“So tell me,” He began slowly. “Did you come directly to Kolkata or did you come first to Bombay and then to Calcutta?” Such a simple question and yet somehow I became completely confused. Before I could put together a rational response Didi again answered, “Baba, we came first to Bombay and then to Kolkata.” Baba again smiled an acknowledgement to her response and looked once more at me.

“So tell me,” He said. “Did you arrive this morning or did you arrive this afternoon?” Now this was a very simple question. But in my perplexity I struggled. We had arrived in the morning in Bombay, in the afternoon in Kolkata, and in the evening at the *jagrti*. Once again, before I could put together an answer, Didi said, “Baba, we arrived this evening.”

Without removing His gaze from me Baba said, “This evening? Then you have arrived just now.” And when He said “Just now,” He reached out with His right hand and touched me very quickly, yet very softly, between my left eye and ear. Then He laughed quietly, turned, and walked away.

As soon as Baba disappeared into His house, people were all grabbing me. “What happened?” “Did Baba touch you?” “What was all that about?” Other than confirming that Baba had touched me, I had nothing to say. I had no idea what was going on. I felt happy, yet confused, and somewhat as if I were floating. It was not until many months after my return home that I remembered the experience in my meditation where Ba’ba’ would walk towards me and I realized that He had recreated this experience for me.

We were some of the first to arrive at Ananda Nagar for the DMC (Jan. ’81). After a few days many people would arrive, but for the time being we had the entire place, and Baba, to ourselves. Baba was paying a lot of attention to us and being very sweet. One of the first nights a small group of us, maybe 10 or 12, were waiting at Baba’s house for Him to return from field walk. It was dusk and at that time Baba’s house was very small with no gardens or other development. Just before Baba returned, Dada Karunananda suddenly closed the door to Baba’s house and brought out a chair. He said, “Perhaps we can convince Baba to have a short darshan with us.” And when Baba returned, He agreed, taking His place on the small wooden chair as we sat assembled before Him. There was a gentle breeze and the stars were just starting to appear. Baba sat still in a contemplative mood. I remember how beautiful and perfect He seemed. After some minutes He said, “Would the margiis from Italia please lead us in a kiirtan in which the brothers will dance and sing while the sisters sit and sing.”

One Italian brother with a guitar led the kiirtan as we followed His instructions. Baba simply sat as we did kiirtan, absorbed, smiling gently, eyes closed, in His cosmic mood. After ten minutes or so the kiirtan ended, Baba stood, gave us namaskar, and proceeded into His home. I had so grievously missed Baba for so many years that to have this special experience thrilled me then as the memory of it does today.

One curious thing I remember from this same time is simply that Baba used to look at me. As He was walking past, not infrequently He would stop, turn His head and gaze at me from the corner of His eye. His look held a mystic significance to me that I can never express in words, but it had to do with our relationship with one another. Baba often gazed at me in this way and touched me many times. In fact, one day in front of Baba’s quarters in Ananda Nagar after He had gone into His house, Dada Karunananda (whom I had never met or spoken to) came up to me and in an excited voice said, “Baba likes you! Baba likes you!”

On one of the first mornings after our arrival in Ananda Nagar I was at Baba’s quarters waiting for His return from the field walk. I noticed some carved pillars of stone about five feet long lying on the ground. I was told that they had been found somewhere in the local area and were going to be installed in Baba’s yard. Shortly thereafter, Baba returned. Instead of going directly into His house, he wandered over by the stone pillars which appeared to be some kind of statues. I ventured nearby. Suddenly Baba started talking about the statues. Only myself and two European brothers were in attendance.

Baba said something to the effect of, “Many years ago this entire area was home to many large herds of cattle which roamed about. The bulls of the herds were admired by the people of that time for their strength and virility. And so the people created deities which were half human and half bull.” Baba then described how during different eras of history, different styles of sculpting were used. In one era the figures were depicted clothed and in another era the figures were depicted “in the nude”. Baba then asked, “Are these figures clothed or are they in the nude?” (Baba said the word “nude” with an extremely long “u” sound, “nuude”.)

I looked at the statues but, other than recognizing that they had heads, I couldn’t be sure what I was seeing. Baba continued. “They are clothed,” He stated in answer to

His own question. He went on to say that this fact meant that these statues were from a certain era (which I can’t remember).

“In fact,” Baba said, looking up, “these statues were made during the reign of two brothers who were rulers of a kingdom 800 years ago.” Gazing across the countryside Baba continued. “And their kingdom was located eight kilometers away,” he said while extending his arm and pointing, “just there.” The three of us gazed into the distance in the direction indicated (down river and approximately 30 degrees to the right). I could see only endless rice paddies, mustard fields, and small villages. I felt as though Baba was gazing through time and seeing the kingdom before him. Then Baba lowered His arm, nodded His head, and proceeded to His house.

At the time I thought that Baba was telling something which was well known to the *acaryas* and Indian margiis. I came to find out later that Baba rarely repeated such information, and that every day on His field walks He talked constantly of the local history, fauna, flora, agriculture, art, anything and everything. If those He was speaking to did not write it down, the information was lost. I don’t know who those other European brothers were or whether they would remember this event. I did not write down the information when it was fresh in my mind and therefore do not remember the names of the eras described or the name of the kingdom. These statue pillars were placed at the sides of the drive entering the complex of Baba’s residence there at Ananda Nagar, a location they still grace to this day. This may be the only written account of their origins.

A few interesting events occurred during the time when we returned to Kolkata after DMC before returning home. We were getting more used to Baba, He was so sweet and approachable that people were becoming very friendly with Him. So one day Baba came out, and someone asked Him, “How are you, Baba?”

He replied with a question, “How should you reply if someone inquires ‘How are you’?” Then again, “What should you say if someone asks ‘How are you’?” Answering Himself He said, “You should say ‘I am trembling with energy and dancing in divine ecstasy! I am TREMBLING with energy and DANCING in divine ecstasy!!’”

So saying, Baba smiled and proceeded on his field walk. On His return Vishvadeva spoke out. “Baba,” he said, “How are you?”

Baba paused, smiled and said “Better than best. BETTER than best!”

Another night Baba had been ill. I am under the impression that this happened not infrequently after DMC. He missed some field walks and was taking some ayurvedic medicine. One day, Vishvadeva said, “Baba, are you feeling better?”

And Baba stopped, and then He started laughing. He started laughing the most hysterical laugh you’ve ever heard. Ha ha ha ha ha! Just laughing and laughing in this belly laugh. He never answered and just walked in the house continuously laughing this contagious laugh. We all started laughing. We never had any idea what it meant. Somehow this question of “Are you better?” was very amusing to Baba.

Permission was required before taking a photo of Baba. This was both to maintain the sanctity of the environment and also to prevent unauthorized use of His photo. One day, as Baba was leaving for field walk a European margii raised his camera and snapped a shot of Baba.

It was rather blatant, everyone noticed. As Baba got into His car and departed one of the dadas in charge of security approached this brother. “I’m sorry,” he said. “Unauthorized photos of Baba are not allowed. Please give me the film.”

The brother refused and held up his hands, indicating he didn’t have any film. The dada repeated the request more firmly. Soon an argument had broken out. Everyone felt very awkward, as this margii’s attitude towards the dada was very disrespectful. No one knew what to do.

Suddenly a motor horn sounded. Even though He had only been gone for five minutes Baba was returning! This was unprecedented. During the few weeks I had been there, Baba had never returned like this before. The gates swung open and Baba’s car pulled into the middle of the drive. Baba said not a word nor gave any sign. He merely looked upward into the ceiling of the car and sat still. You could have heard a pin drop. Everyone looked at the dada and the margii. The dada put out his hand and said quietly, “Give me the film.” That man ran into the house and got the film from his wife to whom he had surreptitiously slipped it. As soon as the film was in the dada’s hands Baba nodded His head and His car backed out of the drive, returning to the scheduled field walk. That man and his wife were forbidden to enter the compound for a few days

following. After travelling half way around the world to see Baba this was a painful punishment. In a few days, having learned their lesson, they were allowed back in and all was forgotten.

I do not believe that Baba cared about the photo. I think He returned primarily to protect the dignity of the dadaji who was being treated disrespectfully. In any event it was remarkable to witness first hand not only Baba’s omniscience (there was no way for Him to know what was transpiring after He left the compound), but also the depth of His commitment to the workers of Ananda Marga.

### Krsna

*San Francisco, California*

I am currently the *Bhukti Pradhan* of the East Bay area in the San Francisco Bay area. I was initiated in 1975 by *Acarya Adveshananda Avadhuta* in the city of Manila in the Philippines.

My realization of Baba since I first saw Him until I had Personal Contact in 1988 is that Baba – what we know as Baba – is not a person, not a human being. It is a creation that allows us to realize ourselves. This I first realized when I arrived at Baba’s house from the airport in 1982.

At that time I did not think that I could make the trip to India though I had been longing to see Baba for a long time. At one point in my sadhana I had this desire, so I spoke to what I thought was God, speaking to Baba internally, “If you are really the Lord of the Universe, the distance from the Philippines to Kolkata is very small. The universe is so big and the distance from Philippines to Kolkata is very small, so perhaps you can make it even smaller.”

I was working for an American computer company, and in ten days my office informed me that they were going to send me to Thailand to train other people in manufacturing computers, because I was a manufacturing engineer.

So after my stay in Thailand I quickly went to Kolkata. There were about 200-250 people there and we were packed to the edge of the room. When I first saw Baba I thought, “This is an old man. But I’m here anyway, so there must be something.”
