What was unusual about that time was that after the darshan, Baba was singing the chants before what was to be the *varabhaya mudra* – He was going to do the *varabhaya mudra* in a Sunday darshan. And the margiis were all very excited.

And we were all at the back, squished back at the window, and the moment He did *varabhaya mudra*, there was like a spiritual wave – it started at the top, the margiis started crying and then after about three or four seconds it hit us at the back. There was like a force coming and all the margiis were crying and I thought, “OK, this isn’t an old man after all.”

I had about two weeks to spend in Kolkata and I was advised by dadas that before I left, I should take leave of Baba and give Him a garland. So I bought a garland from the market and before I left, Dada Arjunananda, the Personal Assistant (PA) of Baba said I should give the garland to him. And when Baba was coming out to His car in the morning Dada said, “Baba, Krsna from Maharlika would like to take leave of you.”

And Baba stopped and said, “Krsna from Maharlika – yes, I know him. He has a lot of energy.”

And then He took the garland and as He went to his car I spoke to Him. I said, “Baba,” and He put his face very close to mine and said, “Yes?”

His face was so close that I could see through His thick glasses, I could see His eyes. The eyes, they were so unbelievable, I have not ever seen eyes like that – they were so bright and shiny!

And I said, “Baba,” and He said, “Yes?”

“Do you have any message for the people in Maharlika, for the margiis in Maharlika?”

And He said, “Yes. Tell them to do all the good they can for the suffering humanity.”

I said, “OK, Baba. I will tell them.” And then I left. That was my first brief and wonderful encounter with Baba.

Then I had the opportunity to go again to Kolkata, by Baba’s grace, in 1988. This was the time of *microvita* and Baba was giving all kinds of demonstrations on *microvita* among wholetimers – some of whom I knew. It was very interesting, because in one of the demonstrations, our beloved Dada Nityasatyananda was there, and Baba was doing a demonstration with Dada.

It was for LFT brothers only, and Baba said, “Let an advanced soul come forward.” And GS was looking and tapped Dada Nityasatyananda on the shoulder and said, “Come here.” And then Baba asked Dada Nityasatyananda to undress.

Baba then took his stick and touched different parts of Dada’s body and said, “I am now taking out your shame, your shyness, your fear. Now tell me, do you feel any shyness, any shame or fear?”

And Dada said, “No, Baba.”

Baba also said at that particular darshan that *Parama Purusa* can liberate anybody, liberate the entire universe if He wants to. He can take all the propensities and liberate anybody if He wants to. But He doesn’t want to do it, for the evolution of the universe. And I think that was a time that I had a lot of inner conflict with the organization – “Why are we doing this? Why are we doing that?” But from this darshan I learned that this is all the play of *Parama Purusa* and He wants all of this to happen. If He wanted, He could get rid of all of this and collapse the entire universe but *Parama Purusa* doesn’t want to do that. He wants the evolution of the universe to keep on going.

The circumstances by which I got my PC were also very funny in a way because I went there as a Prout LFT. But at that time Baba was objecting to the Prout organization because it wasn’t performing very well. A lot of the Prout acaryas had their acaryaship removed and there was no PC for Prout LFTs. So I was waiting for more than ten days – that was in March of 1988 – to get my PC, and my name was on the list but I wasn’t allowed to go further because of the so-called ban on Prout workers, because they had not been performing well, in Baba’s eyes.

So I went to the senior central worker of ERAWS and told him, “You know, I’ve been here and I’m actually married with two children but I’m still working full time for the organization. This is the second time I’m working for the organization but I’m married and sacrificing and then you tell me that I cannot see Baba.”

And this central ERAWS dada said, “OK. Your name is Krsna Kumar and you are an ERAWS worker. Tomorrow you will see Baba.” I think he was touched by my sincerity and the next day I was next in line to see Baba – ten in the morning, March 10, 1988.

When I came into the room. I did *sastaung pranam* to Baba and He said, “Come here my boy. Come close.”

He asked me, “Did you come here alone?”

And my first knee-jerk reaction was to say, “Yes, Baba.”

Then He said, “Really?”

And then I remember coming through Bangkok airport on my way to Kolkata, I was in line to get a ticket and on the left side of me was a man who looked exactly like Baba. And as this was flashing through my mind as Baba was asking me this question I thought, “No, no, no – this can’t be! This is too much!” I had looked at his baggage and he was some kind of doctor. The man wasn’t paying attention to me at all – glasses, white kurta, and everything and he was also in line for his ticket. I thought, “No, no, it can’t be! I mean this is too much!”

And then Baba asked again, “Did you come here alone?”

And I said, “No, Baba. I don’t think I came here alone.”

Then Baba said, “Come closer.” And Baba took my head and massaged it. I don’t know, maybe He was correcting some loose screws! Then He asked me to put my hands like this, and He asked me to promise to follow 16 points closely and to do all the good I can for the entire cosmological order.

“OK, Baba. I promise.”

The first time I saw Him He said to do good for the entire humanity. The second time I saw Him He said to do good for the ENTIRE cosmological order! Sometimes I can’t even take care of myself and now He asked me this promise! But that was my PC with Baba.

And all through that time I had very good experiences and I realized that as you come closer to Baba you realize that it’s not a human being. I had many experiences that as I was looking at Baba, especially during darshan, I did not see a person. It was like an outline of a person but if I looked through it, inside that outline was the entire universe.

**Vishvadeva**
*Eugene, Oregon*

I was initiated in 1971 by Ac. Yatiishvarananda in the U.S.

From 1980 through 1990 every winter I went to India. The third time I went I decided I preferred spending more time with Baba with less people there and not going to the big DMC. And so I left in November; it was winter time. Before I left I called the sectorial office and asked if there was anything I should be aware of.

The dada who answered said, “If you like, you can bring a plant.” This was fairly early in the time that Baba was asking margiis to bring plants to India. So even though I wanted to be a *tantrika*, and be brave, I also thought, “If I bring a plant, I may get trouble from the immigration, they may even know I’m a margii, who else is bringing plants? So, I’m not going to do it, I’m just going to go.”

My flight stopped in New York City, and at the airport to my surprise Dada Yatiishvarananda was walking by, coming back from India. He asked me what I was doing. He said, “I hope you’re bringing a plant. Baba will be very upset if you don’t bring a plant.”

“Oh.”

This was Sunday, it was very cold and there was snow in New York, there was no place in the airport that sold plants, so I thought, I really somehow have to get a plant. I went outside the airport and looked around. I couldn’t really find anything outside the airport, but I found some pine cones that had fallen down, and I thought at least these are seeds, maybe this will be good enough. I stuffed them in the bottom of my sleeping bag, and thought, “I’ll see what happens.”

I arrived in Kolkata and at Baba’s house saw Dada Ramananda. He asked, “Have you brought a plant?”

I said, “Dada, I brought these pine cones.”

He shook his head and said, “No, this is not a plant.”

I started to feel a little bad that I hadn’t brought a plant, first of all that I’d been a little cowardly, and I thought it was not considered very good that I didn’t bring a plant. I

saw Baba, and didn’t get a chance to talk with him, but Dada informed me that Baba was disappointed that I hadn’t brought a plant.

Baba was a bit ill at the time, and they hadn’t had Sunday darshan for a few weeks. Sunday came and all of a sudden, though no one was ready, no one expected that Baba was going to give darshan, suddenly Baba was coming for darshan.

All was in turmoil. Usually there would be some margiis dancing *kaoshikii* and *tandava*. On my first trip I had danced *tandava* in front of Baba, and I wanted to do it again. I usually enjoyed doing it at *Dharma Maha Cakra* and it assured me a good seat near Baba. I hadn’t ever danced *kaoshikii* before Baba, and I didn’t know it very well. It was new at that time. I thought I should do *kaoshikii* but I didn’t have the proper clothes – there was a uniform for it. Two Indian brothers in normal clothes were going to dance, so I thought I could also do it, and I volunteered. I was still feeling kind of bad that I hadn’t brought the plant, but I thought, “OK, I’m going to dance *kaoshikii* before Baba.”

It started – I was inexperienced with *kaoshikii*, and also inexperienced with the rhythm of the didi who was leading it; it seemed very sort of herky-jerky to me and I couldn’t follow. When I was supposed to go up, I went down, when I was supposed to go left, I went right. I was bumping into a post there. I did everything completely wrong.

After it was over, we sat down, Baba said something in Bengali, and everyone laughed. I wondered what Baba had said. I asked one dada very quietly.

“Baba said that in spite of numerous defects, it was still very nice.”

Somehow when He said that, I got a very special feeling, I felt very loved. Because I realized that even though I was not a perfect yogi, that I was not as brave as I could be, I had made some effort and Baba still loved me and loved everyone in spite of all our imperfections. I couldn’t stop laughing and smiling for some time after that.

I try to remember that experience whenever I become too self-critical and tend to be negative, because a yogi needs to be positive, and not feel that “I have this defect, that defect, God doesn’t love me, I’m not going to be able to achieve my goal.” And so that has been helpful to me.

When I became a margii in 1971, I was aware that most people had Sanskrit names. But I felt this should not be some kind of fad – “Oh, I got initiated, and I’m going

to have a Sanskrit name, and I’m going to go play yogi for a year and then I’m going to do something else.” No. It was a full three years before I felt ready to get a Sanskrit name. Also, I had a secret desire to get a name from Baba, that was another reason I was waiting, but Baba at the time was in jail and we didn’t know when He was going to get out.

So given those uncertain circumstances I found one *acarya* whom I felt comfortable with, and liked a lot, and said to him, “Can you give me a name?”

However, I still had some prejudices about Sanskrit names. I told him there was a certain sort of name that I didn’t want to get. He said he would think about it for a few days. And after one day, he said, “I’ve got the first part, but I don’t have the second part yet. I’m still working on it.”

Finally he said, “I have the complete name, your name is Vishvashakti.” The power, the force of the universe, that’s a very powerful name. I liked that name, but at the back of my mind, I had the thought that Shakti is the female principle, is that really a correct Sanskrit name for a male?

A number of years later when I saw Baba for the first time, He asked me my name. I had Personal Contact, but afterwards I was always thinking, “Is there something wrong with my name? I would like Baba to tell me about my name.”

It was always in the back of my mind. Also, basically as a trick to see if I could get some attention from Baba, I asked Dada Ramananda if he would ask Baba if my name was correct. Each time he assured me my name was fine. I went each winter to India, had many interactions with Baba, and He never said anything at all about my name.

I went again and as I got there, some margiis were saying some people had gotten a second PC. I knew the rule was that margiis got one PC in a lifetime. And sometimes it was difficult to get even one PC—you had to promise to start ten schools and do long sadhana and all these things—and other times it was fairly easy. I had never thought I could get a second one, and when I heard this the wheels started turning in my mind.

There was one brother who had gone to *acarya* training, and left, and had changed his name and somehow had gotten in to see Baba for a second PC. Another brother from

Italy had by trickery managed to get a second PC. I thought, if these people are getting PC, I should maybe consider it, too.

The Sectorial Secretary (SS) of Berlin Sector came up to me and asked, “Have you ever had PC?” I was in a dilemma. I don’t like to lie; in the long run being straightforward will always be the best policy. But I didn’t want to be stupid, either. I didn’t want to miss a chance for another PC because of my attachment to always being honest.

So I just mumbled. I didn’t say anything one way or the other. When I came for my first PC there was a different Personal Assistant (PA) to Baba. The General Secretary (GS) was also different, and neither of them were there, nor was my SS. No one knew, except Baba, that I had had PC. I asked several *acaryas* whom I respected what I should do, and they all said, “When it comes to personal matters with Baba, there are no rules. If Baba wants to give you PC, He will. If He doesn’t, He won’t. Give it a try.”

So a few days went by, and one dada said, “Come, Baba’s going to give PC this morning, we’ll wait together.” While I was waiting, one brother who had just had PC came in and I asked him how his PC was. “Baba gave me some long vow to take, I don’t remember it.” In my mind I thought, “What kind of idiot is this, his guru is giving him a serious vow to take, and he doesn’t even remember it?”

Then we went to Baba’s house. I was sitting there, thinking it looks like I will get PC. The SS came out and said, “Baba does not accept your name.”

And I thought, “Oh, how I have humiliated myself. Baba doesn’t accept my name for PC. I should never have tried to play this game. Baba is probably upset with me.”

Then he said, “Baba says your name should be Vishvadeva. That Vishvashakti is not the proper name.”

All of a sudden I went from the depths to the heights, and then I was inside with Baba. And He asked me, “What’s your name?”

I said, “Vishvadeva, Baba.”

And He said, “Very good name!”

I started to laugh and cry at the same time. It was so funny. You just gave me the name, Baba, you should approve of it as a good name! Baba gave me a very long vow to take, and I do remember it included such things as to serve humanity, and help the world,

but I also couldn’t remember the exact vow, it was too long, and I felt more humble than when I had criticized the other brother.

Baba also asked if I deserved punishment, and I said yes, and He asked me to raise my arms. But then Baba said, “No, you don’t need to have punishment, you’re sincere.” I don’t know if that was good or bad, because it probably would have removed some of my *samskaras*, but it was Baba’s will and I accept it as such.

It was only a long time after that I remembered the story of how I had originally asked for my name, that I had always wanted Baba to give me a name.

I had said to the dada, “Please don’t give me a name with a Deva, I don’t like that.” So at the same time, I had prevented myself from getting the right name, and Baba had fulfilled my desires by giving me a name. It completed a cycle, and I could only laugh how after all those years all those things had interconnected at one point.

### Maitri

*Portland, Oregon*

In 1977, I was in the training center in Davao, Philippines. While at the training center, I got very sick with asthma. Our trainer, Dada Cidananda, put me on a strict milk-and-honey diet. I had this stuff three times a day and I loved it, except I was not getting any better at all. In fact, I was getting worse. I did not know at that time that I was very allergic to dairy. It got to the point where I couldn’t lie down anymore. I could only sit up.

One late night, I was having a bad episode and I was sitting down on the bench by the dining table. I was very tired, so I rested my head on my folded arms, which were resting on the table, and tried to sleep. In the same room was a sister trainee who took care of me whenever my asthma attack was bad. She was dozing off also, when she was awakened by something she felt. When she opened her eyes, she saw Baba floating towards me. He was wearing a white shirt and was seemingly enshrouded by white clouds. She said only Baba’s upper torso was visible. She watched Him floating towards where I was and stop by my side. He stooped down, giving an impression of inquiring how I was. Then He floated away and disappeared.

The following day, when she told me about this, I felt blessed. Although the asthma did not go away, I felt happy knowing that Baba cared. I did not finish the wholetimer training because of poor health, but I’m very grateful of the chance to experience life in the training center.

### Mayatiita

*Mankato, Minnesota*

I was initiated in Oct. ’73 by Dada Yatiishvarananda. The first time I went to India was winter of ’78. Dadaji wrote me a nice letter of introduction to increase my chances of getting Personal Contact, which I lost somewhere along the way. So I met Dadaji Tadbhavananda, who wrote me another letter, in which he said, “Mayatiita works more than an LFT.” I found out later that one of the requirements for getting PC was to be an LFT.

Anyway, I arrived in Kolkata, went to Baba’s quarters, and found some of the requirements for getting PC were to be either a college graduate or an LFT. I was still a student at that time, not a college graduate. I’d had a very strong feeling that I was just going to go in and get PC; it had never occurred to me that there might be any complication to my going in to see my Baba. So I started getting a little concerned, and I presented Dada Ramananda with Dada’s letter, which was obviously exaggerated with my accomplishments. He was looking at it with great skepticism [laughs]. I got to know some of the other people there and realized some people had been waiting for months without getting to see Baba. I started feeling some inferiority complex, that maybe I wasn’t good enough to see Him.

So I started doing sadhana, and calling out to Baba, “Baba, if I’ve done something wrong, it’s your responsibility to give me guidance.”

Actually I was just there a very short time, it was my first day there, and when it was time for Baba to give PC, Dada Ramananda called me first. So, it was a very surreal experience for me, but very special. I went into Baba’s room, and Dada had to remind me to do *sastaung pranam*. I did that, then I looked up at Baba; it was almost hard for me to get Him into my focus, for my eyes to comprehend Him, and I felt like I almost floated over to sit in front of Him. He asked my name. Then He said, “Do you want to be a good boy, or a very good boy?”

“Very good boy, Baba.”

“And if I give you punishment for the bad things you’ve done…”

“I’d be grateful, Baba.”

I wasn’t afraid at all. Then I remember Baba just beaming down on me. I was looking up into His face and He had this most beautiful smile, and it seemed a long time that I was looking at Him, just beaming pure love down on me. I had the feeling that love surrounded us in the room, almost like a physical substance you could slice through, the love in the room was just so thick. So that seemed to last for a long time. He had this beautiful look on His face, and then held His hand over my head for a while, then to one side, and then the other side.

He said, “I’ll give you punishment at the proper time. Now go.”

Being very obedient, I left immediately, and sat down outside, and waves and waves of bliss overcame my body, and I had great difficulty just sitting up. I was in the state it seemed for a long time. Finally Baba’s PA called me over and gave me some duties.

Dada Sarvabodhanandaji was there and said it was very, very short. It didn’t seem like that, but the whole experience was probably about two minutes. Of course it was very special to me.

It would be many years before Baba gave me the realization of what had occurred there. It happened very shortly after He left His physical body, which was fairly devastating to me, the first time that I really experienced a loss. In any case, I was at the next retreat. All of us were still grieving. I sat down to do sadhana and a feeling kind of came over me, a similar feeling to when I left Baba’s room. And I just had the feeling that I knew exactly what I needed to do to express the grief in a concrete way, and also as a kind of penance. I felt it was kind of a message, clear to me, what I had to do. So for one year, every Saturday morning, I would maintain silence (as much as my children would allow me) and I also fasted four times a month, instead of my usual two times. I maintained that program for myself for the year. I felt that was Baba giving me the punishment at the proper time.

And I had always wondered what was the meaning of the blessing Baba had given me. I asked my *acarya* if he knew, but he had no idea. The following year I came to the retreat at Ananda Kanan. At the closing ceremony, I closed my eyes to do a little sadhana; there was a nice feeling in the room. Then all of a sudden out of nowhere I got again this feeling of being immediately in Baba’s presence, exactly that sensation as after

PC, that extremely powerful vibration coursing through my body. Then I heard a voice internally, very clearly though very soft… it said, “You will never forget me.”

And after that came the realization that that was the blessing that Baba gave to me in PC, that under any circumstances I would never forget Him. The enormity of that was exactly what I needed. I was living under difficult circumstances, and isolated from the flow of AM, and it could happen very easily. But Baba would not allow it.

On my first trip, I got the opportunity to fly in an airplane with Baba, from Kolkata to Patna. I had the desire to try to get close to Baba some way, but didn’t know how to do it. I was sitting some distance from him, so I made a plan in my head, as soon as we landed to say something to Baba.

He frustrated my plan [laughs]. I got up and went over near Him quickly and said, “Did you have a good flight, Baba?” And He just kind of laughed at me and walked away. So I was actually one of the last ones to get off the flight, and we were to get on a shuttle bus to go to the terminal, and as I got on, Baba was sitting directly in front of me. He gave me a big namaskar, and a big smile.

During that flight, the stewardess would bring sandwiches around. It was a full moon day. She came to Baba, and He said, “No, no, we’re fasting.” So of course everyone else followed Baba’s lead, even the general margiis. And a kind of unworthy thought came to my mind that some of these fellows had never fasted in their lives.

I got kind of greedy for free food also, so when the waitress asked me, “Would you like a sandwich?” I said sure. It was awful. The moral of the story is always follow Baba’s example.

I had come for *Bhukti Pradhan* reporting, to represent Prout. It was a long time until Baba was finally ready to have reporting. There were BPs from all over India, as well as foreigners such as myself. He was asking people things unrelated to their work. At the time Baba was giving discourses on the *cakras*, and the relation between *cakras* and plexii, and what He called *padma*. I had overheard an *acarya* earlier in the day talking about some of the things Baba had given on the subject. Reporting time came, everyone was rushing in, people wanted to be close, there was some pushing and shoving

to get closer to Baba, and that seemed a little bit unseemly to me. I didn’t want to participate and didn’t try to force my way to the front.

But I felt something of a desire to report to Baba. Three other brothers were selected to report; they went in front, and He asked the question, “What is the difference between *cakra*, *padma* and plexus?”

None could answer, and everyone was disturbed. Someone had to ask, “Who here can answer the question?” Suddenly I found myself in front, not sure how I got there, and I raised my hand. The answer had something to do with plexus is something more subtle than a *cakra*, and *padma* is a more poetic kind of expression for the *cakras*. It was close to what Baba was saying earlier.

Baba said, “This boy said something of value, though not very clearly” [laughs]. That reporting session was over.

In Patna, Baba was giving discourses as a followup to the DMC, in a park in a public building. One snake charmer had the misfortune of bringing his snakes for a show, which the margiis enjoyed. But that gave some of them an idea. Of course, every time Baba came out the margiis would perform *tandava* for him. They took this man’s snakes, at least the nonpoisonous ones to hold in the left hand as they were doing *tandava*. It was just really hilarious. They were mostly these middle-aged man, who were quite a bit overweight, and they were holding these poor snakes that were swinging from side to side as they did *tandava*. So, Baba, ever polite, afterwards said, “Very unique.” But then He corrected them, “But if you use snake, you should use venomous snake.”

During the DMC I saw one thing that at the time I didn’t think much of. In Baba’s atmosphere things that would seem miraculous to the common person, you just took in stride and it seemed very normal. I saw a kundalini experience when Baba gave *varabhaya mudra*. One Italian brother – before this Baba had given him a flower from His garland – anyway at that moment this brother shot up like a rocket. Some other spiritual groups talk about floating. He literally shot up like a rocket, with that speed, and landed about 20 feet away in the laps of some *acaryas*. It just seemed like a very normal

thing at the time. Now, I’m thinking that was a fairly unusual kind of occurrence [laughs].

In Jamaica, they put on a very dramatic *tandava* demonstration, with actual flaming torches. Immediately afterwards Baba said “Bravo.” So of course people started applauding. And Baba stopped them, saying, “No, no, no, you don’t applaud at a spiritual function.” Something we should remember.

The sweetest I’ve ever seen Baba, was in Jamaica. It was a very happy vibration there. During the DMC He gave His *mudra*, and it was the first time I’d really experienced the power of His *mudra*. I was put into a state of bliss, and was sobbing for a long time afterwards, from the vibration. It seemed particularly sweet. After He did this *mudra*, He said, “Let everyone be happy, Let everyone see the bright side of things.” Then He got up to leave – usually He leaves very quickly – but there was such a devotional feeling in this room, everybody got up and started calling Baba. So He paused, and it was such a nice interaction, people were enjoying His presence, and it was evident Baba was enjoying the company of the margiis. He was just shaking His head back and forth and had a big smile on His face. That’s one of the images of Baba I keep in my mind.

### Jyotirmayii

[Editor’s note: Sister Jyotirmayii passed away in the summer of 2003. She was living in Ojai, California at the time. An LFT and sincere devotee, she will be missed.]

Before I transition, I’m going to record as many of my experiences as I can since I was blessed by Baba by encountering His Mission on Thanksgiving weekend, 1973. My life instantly changed. I realized the vastness of the whole mystery of “God,” a word I associated up to that time with a limited, divisive concept. I was always searching for a spiritual base, though. When Dada Yatiishvarananda initiated me, I knew that the concept was more than the human mind could grasp, but that was perfectly okay!

Clash went along with my new peace and contentment. My husband and parents went into a reaction from which they never recovered and after seven years, I left my husband. (My children were all grown by then). I was never able to share much of depth with my parents.

My first experience of Baba was a dream, after a difficult day of haranguing by my husband at the time when Baba was imprisoned. Baba was standing in front of a small white cell. He handed me a piece of paper, saying, “Here are recipes for sweets to help you through difficult times…” It was a very special type of paper, “onionskin,” a kind we used to use in drawing classes and rarely see. It was folded meticulously into quarters. I wondered at the significance of this particular paper and its form, so I kept this in my mind. The answer came years later when I was in LFT/Prout training in Los Altos Hills with Dada Nityashuddhananda in 1980. Near the end of the training, he pulled out a sheet of onionskin, neatly folded into quarters and read us the meaning of *Taraka Brahma*.

Shortly after initiation, I had another intense dream. My parents and husband had put me through what seemed to me an inquisition, trying to persuade me to get into some other religion, to give up my choice. My parents left for home. I was crying and feeling depressed. I spent the whole day doing sadhana. My husband had gone back to work in L.A. That night I dreamed I was in a building, looking out the front second-story window, watching Baba get out of a car and walking to the front door. Margiis were

sitting in the large room arranged for darshan, with a platform in the front. Baba walked out and for the first time I saw Him so clearly, in total detail. I’d never seen a full picture of Him, so I didn’t know what He looked like exactly. He was dressed in a creamy white *dhoti*, with a shawl draped about one shoulder. I studied his hands, strong and powerful. He had by His side a staff shaped like a lightning bolt. He was exactly Baba as we know His physical form. He gave a discourse on greed. My parents were sitting on chairs along the wall on my left side, and kept an unrelated conversation running. I turned to them and said, “If you don’t be quiet you’ll have to leave the room.” Baba continued His discourse, then walked to me, and touched me right on my *cakra*. I fell back. This was to be my personal contact – I was never able to receive another, though I tried many times, but this was perfect.

I continued my practices faithfully, as my personal family life continued to be very difficult. We moved to a town 100 miles from L.A., and my husband continued working there, staying away two nights a week. This was Baba’s grace, as I could at last attend *dharmacakra* with a great group of margiis in Santa Barbara. It was a wonderful time on the spiritual level. When I had bad days, I would wake at night with a radiant sensation of loving, supportive energy.

Almost seven years passed, and suddenly things changed. I heard of a Prout training to be held in the Los Altos *jagrti*. I just got in my car and drove up there one afternoon. I must have left a note but I don’t remember! The training session was wonderful. I have always been totally turned off by the inherent weaknesses of our political system, and of capitalism. Prout’s total philosophy makes complete sense to me.

Baba would be coming to the U. S. so there were margiis and *acaryas* from everywhere there, all preparing for this visit. We gardened, prepared a beautiful room for Him, studied, and did long kiirtans. On the night before His arrival, everyone was so tired that we didn’t do an all-night kiirtan. I woke early, and was told that He was refused entry. Such disappointment! I could hardly believe it! I have always felt that if we had done kiirtan all night, He would have come…

After all this, I ended up going back to my family. My husband promised to change, but he couldn’t keep it up for much more than a week. But soon after, I did get to see Baba when He went to Jamaica. That was a beautiful experience, running down many

times a day from the hotel to His house, being so close to Him. The wonderful DCs, hearing and seeing Him at last. And He looked just as I had seen Him in my dream.

The next spring, I left home for good and moved to the Los Altos *jagrti*, where I stayed for over three years. In 1980 I started a new life, free to follow Baba’s path of bliss! Soon more wonderful experiences came – I could finally go to India for DMC. Margiis arranged a flight for just before Christmas and I was able to sell my car for ticket money just in time. I had broken my ankle in November, so I went off in a fiberglass cast from foot to knee. I could walk without any pain, so I was eager to go.

India was immediately “home” to me. I spent the next three months in total bliss to be there, even though it started out with such stress. The two brothers that I went with didn’t think to bring the Central Office address! So we searched fruitlessly for a couple days around Kolkata, then jumped on a madly crowded overnight train to Ranchi. Luckily we found someone who knew of the AM center there, so we joined margiis headed for DMC. The police met us at Pundag Station and took our names, but we then dashed into Ananda Nagar at last to see Baba again.

The next days were filled with such peaceful joy, surrounded by thousand of margiis, squeezed into a tiny space, waiting for darshan, kiirtan, meeting margiis from India and everywhere else. Baba filled our lives.

Suddenly DMC ended. We rushed, packing up and piling into conveyances. Fourteen of us crowded on a jeep and took off, headed north for Bokaro (the steel city). Immediately, the jeep broke down. A big open-backed truck came by, and gave us all a ride. We stayed with a margii family in Bokaro overnight, then a few of us led by Devashisa, rode a bus all day to Jamalpur.

Since reading a series of books on Krsna’s life, I had a great desire to visit Mathura, where he spent so much of His life, but that desire left when I had been in Jamalpur. I was completely satisfied. There was so much to see and feel – the vibration of the early days of Baba’s life and mission. We meditated at the Tiger’s Grave, visited the house where Baba lived – so simple, so peaceful. Baba fulfilled my dreams even to visit Varanasi, where two sisters and I went before returning to Kolkata.

The next three months were filled with so many personal experiences with Baba. In those days, there weren’t such large numbers of margiis and we had much closer

contact with Baba on a daily basis. We all attended morning darshan at the Central office to hear Baba’s discourses, usually in Hindi or Bengali. We waited in front to greet Him when He arrived. In the evening we again waited at His house in Lake Gardens while He went out for field walk. During the day we could stand by the front garden. While He walked among us sometimes He would stop and talk to us.

During this three-month stay, I lived with other margii sisters at the didis’ house and Didi Ananda Bharati, the first *avadhutika*, a loving and inspiring being. We slept in a stone room on straw mats. In the evenings, when we all trekked across Lake Gardens to Baba’s house for field walk, we were always filled with excitement and anticipation. One night, a German margii sister was so anxious that Baba would give her some recognition that she could hardly contain herself. I thought to myself, “Well I know that He won’t notice me!” When Baba returned from His walk, He got out of the car, walked to us, turned right in front of me, looked into my eyes and gave me a deep namaskar!

Another evening, we had seen Baba leave on walk, and I sat on a bench inside the garage space, thinking how tired I was and wondering how I could ever walk all the way back to our room that night and I almost fell asleep. Just then Baba’s car neared the gate and we all jumped up and lined up along the walk. Baba got out, walked up to me, stopped right before me, leaned forward in namaskar, saying “Ahhhh…” with a powerful sighing sound. Jayanta and another Australian brother exclaimed after, “Did you feel that?” We were all uplifted with energy. We flew back to our places that night, laughing with joy. I was totally oblivious to my leg cast.

I spent some more time being near Baba, then thought that I should either go home or travel a bit to satisfy my desire to see more of India. Without making a conscious choice, I found myself on the way to the train ticket office. There I bought a three-week pass for travel and soon left for travels through north India, and thanks to Baba, some memorable experiences – meeting interesting spiritual people, visiting senior margiis and training centers, having challenging clashes. I stayed in Delhi, traveled to Agra, Jaipur and Kathmandu, before returning “home” to Kolkata, the big *samskara* satisfied. There I stayed until late March enjoying being with Baba.

One morning two sisters and I were standing outside a side door into the Central Office when Baba returned from His walk. When He reached the door He stopped before

us and asked, “And what do these sisters say?” We stood there probably with our mouths hanging open, and He answered His question – “They say nothing,” smiled, and went in the door.

He would walk among us in the front garden of His house in those early days, often stopping to speak on different subjects. One day I remember He spoke of languages – Spanish and Catalan and their differences and relationship. (It was, coincidentally, a subject that was of interest to me). Another time, when it was almost time to leave, I told him that I was to return home and asked if He could give a message to American margiis. Baba replied, “Tell them to continue working on all levels – physical, mental, and spiritual.”

The morning came when it was time to leave. Two brothers from Singapore were also taking a taxi to the airport and we were hoping to see Baba and talk to Him one more time. We thought we could if He came directly out instead of slowly making His way out, which He always did. Of course Baba can fulfill any wish, so He came directly out to us so that we could tell Him namaskar.

I stayed in Los Altos until the end of summer of ’83, when Didi Tripti (Girl’s Prout Secretary) asked me to go to Kansas City, Missouri to help Mukti put out a newsletter and work for GP. I took a three-month leave from my job in the Physics Dept. at Stanford University and went off.

After these three months, I ended up quitting my Stanford job (by turning it over to another margii) and moving to Kansas City. Mukti left for *acarya* training and Kamala came. This was a wonderful period of two and a half years of total immersion in Baba’s flow! We put out a newsletter every weekday, writing it, formatting and printing it, then distributing it to colleges and sites around town. We also distributed government surplus commodities to the eligible – tons of cheese, flour, peanut butter, etc. This upset our landlady so much that we had to move so we scraped up money and bought our own house, a three-story late Victorian in a great area for our work. We were involved in community, Latin and Native American issues. They were very exciting times. One of our greatest challenges was the gathering and shipping of plants for Baba’s garden. This

was really a big job! Now when you see the collection at Lake Gardens and Tiljala, you can see the results of margii’s devotional efforts.

During the years of ’83 to ’88, I traveled three more times to India. Since I was an LFT during part of this time, I was there for only two or three weeks each time, either for reporting or winter or May DMC. The first DMC was in Ranchi, where I fulfilled another desire – to dance *kaoshikii* before Baba (even though I had a broken toe this time). During another trip, after Baba had introduced *Prabhat Samgiit*, we all sat around Him in Lake Gardens and sang these beautiful songs to Him. I sat there, singing and crying with joy – a dream come true.

On one of my last visits, I was given a demonstration of His constant attention to each of us. I was feeling very sad that, since there were so many margiis around by this time, we couldn’t feel so close to Him personally and get the attention we’d had. I was staying over in Queens, the WWD office, on the way back to Kansas City. One morning, one *acarya* said that Central office had called, very upset, saying that Baba said a NY sector LFT had been using face powder and drinking alcoholic drinks. They asked Him if the person should be expelled and He said no.

I felt absolutely no connection to this for a short while. Then I remembered. I had gone to Delhi to do my usual buying expedition for our GP office sales. Giita, from Australia, and I would dress up like the usual tourist and I’d put on some makeup! I also remembered having a horrible headache one afternoon and going into a homeopathic store for help. They brought me a teacup of water with a very strong remedy in alcohol. It cured the headache, though! I laughed with such relief in the knowledge that Baba is never unaware of our deepest feelings (and doings)! This one experience seems to sum up how close He is to us always – and reminds me of His playfulness.

There is so much that I could say about my last thirty years as a devotee, so many reasons that I have had for being on this path, but now, at the end, I know that it is all just opening up to being in unconditional love with no boundaries.

**Viveka**
*Albany, New York*

I grew up in a religious family, but went to university and studied science, and science is very agnostic, so my religious faith fell away. I became a little bit disillusioned with science during the turmoil of the late ’60s and early ’70s. I was becoming fascinated with the mind, and consciousness. When I first came to a meditation class in 1972, I was looking for more of the impersonal consciousness, not at all a personal guru. I lost track of my original *acarya*, and about a year later Dada Yatiishvarananda took over being my *acarya* and gave me more lessons.

After I got initiated, I went to *dharmacakra* and retreats for a few months. Then I was traveling with my family, and doing my sadhana on my own, with *nama mantra*. We traveled across the country and reached Berkeley, California. In 1973, I started going to *dharmacakra* there. The energy there was kind of high. I really hadn’t known the format, I was new, and nobody knew who I was. None of the *acaryas* knew me. And I became very clashed out with *guru puja*. Some of the people were being overly demonstrative – they were throwing themselves in front of Baba’s picture. I still had the mind of a scientist, that logical mind. I didn’t want another Jesus in my life, I wanted that consciousness. And it disturbed me very much.

So one afternoon I was trying to put my two-year-old to sleep. Despite the fact I was clashed out, I was always very regular in doing my sadhana twice a day. That day I couldn’t concentrate at all, my mind was all over the place, and I started to daydream. The interesting thing about this experience is how ordinary it was, it was very much an ordinary daydream.

All of a sudden I felt I could see this light in the distance, and it became clearer and clearer, like a camera zooming in. And I saw the light was coming from a man. I knew who it was, I knew it was Baba. He came closer and He was holding up a frame, like a doorframe, and the light was coming out of that. And the thought just sort of came in my mind, spontaneously, “Don’t get hung up on my image, I’m just holding the door open.” This was a time when Baba was half a world away, no acarya knew who I was, but Baba somehow knew it was just what I needed to hear.

My relationship with Baba grew very slowly after that. For a long time that image – that I don’t have to worry about guru, people telling all these Baba stories, and I don’t relate to them – it was OK. But having children in those days – in New York sector there were very few margiis with children – I never really felt that much in the flow. I remember a few years later in Los Altos, there were a lot of activities going on, and I was feeling this kind of separation and loneliness, as if I didn’t fit. And all of a sudden, in the middle of a meal, or something, I just got this image in my mind of a galaxy spiraling out, and in the middle was Baba, swirling right tight with Him were all the dadas and didis, and coming out further were people at different levels of involvement, and I felt myself there, very much at the edge. And the thought came to me, it’s at the edge that I touch the rest of the world, the edge is very important to me. It’s at the edge that I connect.

It took six or seven years before I got sixth lesson. And *Dharma Samiksa* was coming up and I thought maybe I should go, and there was always this thing in my mind, “Oh, I can’t go to India.” All of a sudden *Dharma Samiksa* was stopped, and I promised myself the next time I got the chance I would go.

In 1986, Dada Sumantrananda said, “Baba’s giving Personal Contact (PC) now.” If my wife agrees, I told myself, I’d go. She was reluctant, but said OK. Because of family and work, I only had ten days, including travel time. The first thing I found out was, people had been waiting for months to see Baba. And this was very discouraging.

We were on field walk, and people would all crowd together just to watch Baba get in and out of a car. If you step back from a spiritual perspective, it almost seems kind of silly. And I kind of had that feeling when Baba was coming back. He was not in a good mood, and He had a scowl on His face. He walked slowly, and all of a sudden I thought, “Oh my God, I’ve been following a despot all these years.” It was the same clash I’d had before – do I really need a guru? I was very clashed the first time I saw Baba. But it was all stick it out, what else can one do? I was there in Lake Gardens.

So next walk, I got up there with some trepidation, and He was giving namaskar to this whole line of people, He was giving a sweeping namaskar. And I must’ve been somewhere in the middle, and as His namaskar got to me, I just felt this wave coming out

from Him; it’s so hard to describe. After that, I was up there for every field walk, wanting to get a good spot.

Then the struggle came on, waiting for PC, and I said to the dada, “You’ve got to get me on the list.”

And he said, “These people have been waiting for a long time.” And all of a sudden my service began to look very small. I was discouraged. I was with Vishvamitra in a hotel, and we liked to walk. We were walking, and he told me how he’d gotten scolded the day before. He was at field walk, and as Baba got out of the car, he’d called out “Babaji!” and Baba gave namaskar. But one of the dadas came up and said you should never do that. When you call out, Baba has to respond.

And suddenly a light bulb went on over my head. Here He is, the Lord of the universe, but if we call out, He’s helpless and has to respond. Just as a parent is running the household, when the child calls out, the parent drops everything. He has no choice. And once I realized that, I just went and sat outside His house in sixth lesson for two hours. I said to Him, “Baba, I want to see you. I want to see you.” I got more and more intense. As a result, I got on the list.

The day before I was supposed to leave, I was at the end of the line. It was a long line. One brother would come out, in some other place, and then we’d all move up. Finally I got to the top of the stairs. The Personal Assistant came out and said, “Please come back tomorrow.” My mouth must’ve dropped, because I was leaving the next day. He went in and talked to Baba, some exchange in Bengali, and he came back. OK.

Baba’s room seemed to be filled with light, with blinding light. I seem to remember He had a fish tank, too. I did *sastaung pranam*.

He said, “Come here, my boy.”

Then, “Move back a little further, I need to see all your *cakras*.”

Then He asked for my name, and I was never very good with Sanskrit, so I said Viveka.

“What?”

“Viveka.”

“Spell it.”

“V I V E K.”

“Vivek! Discrimination. Discriminate between what is right and what is wrong. Throw out what is wrong and do what is right.” The name has always had a special meaning to me.

Then He started asking me about my ancestors. What did your father speak? “English.”

“No, your fathers. Your forefathers.”

“Oh, Scottish.”

“Ah, the highlander dialect,” He said, as if that explained everything.

Like everybody, I heard, “You’ve done many misdeeds in the past. I need to punish you.”

“Yes, Baba.” I’d heard that when Baba punishes you, you just feel bliss.

“Raise your hands.” I was ready to feel this blissfulness. He tapped my side.

“I’m going to make this hurt, I’m going to make this really hurt.”

All of a sudden, all this fear came to me. But then He said, “How can I punish my little boy? Come, I’ll give my blessing.”

There’s no way to describe the feeling, it went so deep, deeper than I’d ever felt before. The only thing I can say is I couldn’t stop smiling. I smiled all day. I was totally happy, totally clean, like this is where I should be, and have been all along. It was a feeling that just stayed with me; the most significant thing in my life was that moment.

Every moment I remember that. Since then the relationship has only deepened. When He left, I was shocked, but also encouraged to find that the internal Baba had deepened.

### Cakreshvara (2)

Baba had also given a special blessing to me during PC in the Patna jail. He said, "You will be with us for some years."

The vibration of Baba was more important than the words, as His intent was to impress upon me that I would be with the organization for many more years to come. If I were to visit India now, my experience would be much different. At the time, due to severe illness and harsh external circumstances, Baba's words were more of a curse since I thought that I would be in India for many more years. When I was abruptly expelled from India, it seemed like Baba meant that I would be a WT for many more years. This, too, was not my destiny and I realized that I was to be an active margii for many years.

This prophecy was an assurance several times in the following months after leaving India because I found myself more than once in hostile, life-taking situations. After initial fear, I would remember Baba's words and fight bravely knowing that I could not be killed. Many years had not passed and I felt like one figure in the Mahabharata who could not die. What other mortal human had been given such a guarantee? Some subtle ego arose over this and I would find myself taking unwise chances knowing that no death could come.

In my next tour to Africa, one of my favorite past times was to swim in the ocean. The local population would not enter the water because of man-eating sharks and deadly undertow. I would play in the giant waves and have my clothing sanded from my body by being dragged across the sea floor. One time something very strong grabbed my leg and was about to carry me off to deep water. After an initial fear, I remembered Baba's blessing and scolded the creature that it was powerless to cause me harm. The creature then let go its grip on me and retreated into the depths. This made me only more brave and foolish.

One day of the full moon, the waves were extremely strong. The margiis were with me on the beach and advised me not to enter the water. The transition from the beach to the deep water was abrupt and is largely responsible for the energy of waves. The undertow was obviously very dangerous that day, but I could not be harmed. Many years had not passed. I dove into the 15 foot walls of water and swam out to sea to catch

the waves and “body surf” back to shore. Maybe I would swim fearlessly among the sharks for fun.

The effort to swim out to sea was easy since the undertow did all the work for me. I spent some time riding the huge waves in and letting the sea pull me back out. Getting tired after many waves, I began to swim back to shore. After several failed attempts to reach the land, I knew that I was in trouble. My strength was weakening and the undertow was pulling me farther out to deep sea. Several years had not passed, but I was beginning to sense real fear. The margiis watched from the shore, knowing what was happening, but powerless to do anything.

All strength was gone and I was about to drown. A calm came over me and I surrendered my effort to survive. Some people talk about reliving all the events of their lives at such a moment. I had no such experience, but was in a state of clarity in which the mind was free to race a thousand times faster than normal and to analyze Baba’s words, my arrogance and my changing interpretation of His blessing. Baba could have possibly meant that I would be many years with His mission on some other world since I was about to leave this one. I mentally thought the words, “Into Your hands…”

I raised my arms in kiirtan to disappear into the ocean.

Before I could sink into the darkness, my feet landed on something solid. I was standing on a freak sandbar in the middle of nowhere. The water at this place would have been over 30 meters deep. This caused an instant change of plan from dying in detachment to an all-out fight to survive. The outline of the sandbar was only a few meters across with black depth all around. I managed to bounce back to the sandbar for some time regaining strength to attempt the swim to shore. As I began the swim, there was a momentary favorable shift in the currents as if God’s hands were guiding the waters. I was in the end literally thrown on the shore where I fell unconscious for some hours.

This was a lesson not to tempt the limit of God’s Grace, especially when the ego is involved.

### Ajay

*Phoenix, Arizona*

My *acarya* was first Surendra Kumar, but Dada Daneshananda gave me first lesson in 1976. I was initiated March 26, 1975. I first saw Baba in Jamaica and that was a real trip. It was 1979, and I was in music school at Indiana University.

After music school, I went to Istanbul because that is where the Renaissance Universal training was. There were no LFTs, and they had no money. I had a flute I wanted to sell, but we were waiting for that money to come. We were really suffering there because we had no money. There was myself and two other Turkish guys with Dada. It was very spartan. We had only one or two meals a day. Dada had a credit account with the grocer. He had borrowed money for about two months, and the grocer was getting frustrated because he wasn't getting paid. This dada was staying there and working hard, living on practically nothing.

One day the dada was arrested for overstaying his time. He was supposed to leave every month and go to Greece. And he stayed too long, and they arrested him. I remember I just spent the whole day cleaning the *jagrti*. It was a little disorderly, and I was in a cleaning phase. I spent the whole day cleaning. The others were worrying about what to do, and I said, “Well we don’t have to think too much. It’s a very simple solution. When he is ready, he will get out. We just have to give him nice food and let Baba take care.” Everybody was fretting about it, but just watching me clean. I was very tired. I spent from about three o'clock to nine o’clock in this one little apartment.

I was tired and I was going to wash my hands, and I said to myself, “Baba, I don’t know why I am doing all of this. I am supposed to have a nice LFT training. I’m supposed to have a trainer. I don’t know what this is. If you really love me, you’ll do something to show me you are Supreme.” It was just a thought. I was kind of in a bad mood because nothing was going right. I was having internal clash. I wanted Him to justify why I had given up so much, and why there was nothing to show for it. So I thought, “If you really love me, you will do something to show me you’re Supreme.”

What happened was, my hands were dirty, and I put them in the sink and the water turned on by itself. The water turned on, and when I pulled them out it went off. It

was just like the faucets in the airport. I would put them in and the water would go on. I did this three times. After that I said, “Hey, something is wrong here.” I looked under the sink and everything was okay. Then I turned the water on and it worked.

That was the one experience that convinced me that Baba was much more than I had imagined.

### Anjali

*Los Angeles, California*

I was initiated into Ananda Marga in the Philippines on May 7, 1971, in Baguio. I read in our local newspaper that there was a free orientation conducted by one lawyer margii. I had heard of yoga, and I wondered, “What can this be all about? Anyway, it’s free. For curiosity’s sake, I’m going to find out.”

I found it was so very interesting, and I began at once to discuss with the lawyer all about what he was lecturing about. When the session finished, the lawyer said to me, “When will you be initiated?” Oh, I was caught off guard! I was only curious. I didn’t have any intention to affiliate myself. But there was a retreat coming up, and I decided to go. And that was it, the beginning! It had a great impact in my life; it was a total revolution. Revolution is complete transformation. After the retreat, which I found so very enjoyable, I went home and told my children, “This is something you must know.” After a few months, when another *acarya* came, my four children were initiated.

Things began to crop up. Dada Sumitananda initiated me, and the second day, I was clashed. Oh my God, I was a strict Roman Catholic. Did I just convert, like a Buddhist? Because during meditation we had to sit in lotus position, which was like Buddhists. Did I get converted into another religion?

I rushed to Dada Adveshananda. “Dada, did I get converted? I am a Roman Catholic. I even graduated from convent school.” I was on the verge of dropping out, right then and there.

He said, “No. You’ll be all right.” He pacified me. From then on, I blasted along on the path of *dharma*, with lightning speed, smashing into smithereens the Rock of Gilbraltar of obstacles. Clash and cohesion really functioned in me, stress and relaxation, speed and pause. I coped with all this beautifully. But the struggle was there. Life is never smooth sailing, it’s always systaltic.

I was struggling hard, and after so many years of meditation, I’m still struggling, but most beautifully, savoring every minute of it. After self-purification, I’m right here, hale and sound, spiritually vibrating, to last the whole of this life, beyond, and more, into infinity.

One thing in my memory is the knowledge that I saw the Lord, Shrii Shrii Anandamurti, when He was still alive, in India. Didi Ananda Tapati, a most devoted, dynamic, and conscientious worker of Ananda Marga, took me with her in 1987.

We zoomed to India. I was looking out of the airplane window, through a cloud of foamy bubbles. There were the Himalayas, snow-covered. From Kathmandu, we proceeded to India. At that time, Ananda Margiis were persecuted by the police. Didi had to dress in Nepali clothes, and separate from me, and walk in a separate place. We made it to Tiljala, and entered a vast tent. I had to wear fully covering clothes, the required garment, to see Baba. The tent was jam-packed. We were like sardines. I felt that I would be squashed to death. It’s a wonder, because there were thousands of devotees, and everyone was accommodated. Finally, Didi said, we will be seeing Baba. I occupied a three-foot space on the ground, waiting.

The Lord was announced; Shrii Shrii Anandamurti was coming to the stage. The *acaryas* were all shouting, “*Param Pita Baba Ki*,” in an endless way, and Didi positioned me right in front, and then I soaked in the vibration of that great moment. I was just looking, there was no emotion yet in me. But the Lord was finally seated. The *acaryas* let the young children do *kaoshikii*, and Baba was fondly looking at them. I was still adjusting to my environment, squished in a tiny space, among a vast multitude.

All at once – I had only heard of this – Baba was giving the *mudra*. I received that *mudra*, and it created a great impact in me. Later, back home, I said that *mudra* will last me this lifetime, and beyond, into infinity. It was the greatest thing that happened to me.

**Prema Priya**

When I try to think of what Baba story to tell, I wonder if I should tell of my personal visits when I saw Him in India? But my experiences with Baba started long before I ever saw His face: when I searched for Him day and night; when I called out to the heavens, “Where, oh where, is my Lord?”; when I cried and cried, and asked, ”Why couldn’t I have been born when my Lord walked the earth. Oh, how much I would love to sit at His feet and listen to His stories and follow Him everywhere.”

One night He came to me, and my bedroom filled with light. He spoke to me and I was assured that He indeed was here! I knew my Lord was on earth but not where or in what form. I was delighted and in my heart I felt at peace. My questions had been answered and I knew from that moment on that my life was being guided by the unseen Hand of my Lord.

Two years passed before I was led to Ananda Marga, took initiation in 1972, and saw the face of my Guru who had guided me! I am a simple housewife and mother; I am not aware of the reason that His shower of Grace has rained so sweetly on my life so many times. I know that if the longing for Him gets so strong that I can think of nothing else for nights and days, then He just has a way of coming into my life and flooding me with His waves of Bliss to let me know that He is very near. And His Love is all there is.

My first trip to India was an eventful one for me. I had anticipated this meeting with my Guru for many years and my heart was soaring faster than the airplanes that brought me to Him. I arrived in Kolkata and the taxi brought me and another margii to Tiljala. I was told Baba was inside the compound in Ananda Nagar so off I sped to get a glimpse of Him, along with a crowd of other margiis. We sort of flowed together on our hurried walks, sometimes feet barely touching the ground beneath us. My mind was already well ahead of my body, wondering if He would grant us a glimpse of His sweet form.

We climbed up on a roof to get a view of Him in the next building. We could see Him through a small window as He strolled back and forth across the room. I found a place to sit and then fixed my gaze on His magnificent form. Yes, the sweetest face I had

ever seen. A faint smile dancing upon His lips. Joy overwhelmed me and no words could express His sweetness. After some time He left the quarters there and in a whirlwind of orange *acaryas* He rode away in the most incredibly beautiful black car. And the chase was on. The game began. How to see Him? Where? How to get a better glimpse? How to let Him know that I am for Him alone?

Baba had just left for Lake Gardens. We, of course, followed, hoping to catch sight of Him again when He went on field walk later in the day. There was always a sea of margiis and I was having a hard time seeing when Baba came out of the house and into the car. My mind was spinning for a solution to this knotty problem. I tried to push up front but could not make it. I tried to secure a front spot long before Baba’s arrival. This was impossible; I was becoming desperate to see Him. When He came out from the car after field walk I was in the back of the crowd and next to the garage. The garage was empty and as I looked inside, I saw a window! I ran to it and stood looking out, because the very path that Baba used to walk into the house came right in front of this window.

My heart pounded with anticipation as I watched Him walk towards me. I quickly gave my namaskar and started crying, “Baba, Baba.” I knew it was He who had granted my strong desire to see Him. He stopped right in front of the window, looked at me, gave Namaskar back and then walked on with a smile. I reeled in His ecstasy, laughing. How He plays with His devotees! I have found that it is in the small matters that He reveals His Divine guidance.

### Vinit

*Berry Creek, California*

I want to tell how I got into Ananda Marga. It was during the late ’60s when the general trend was towards Eastern philosophy. This came at a good time in my life, because I was not satisfied with the religion that I was involved in; the answers weren’t there. I was attending a community college, and met a brother who was in AM. The more I heard and read about it, the more it just made sense to me. There weren’t any questions I had that weren’t answered by it.

My first experience with guru was in a dream. I was raised Catholic. It gave me a good spiritual background, of God, but it wasn’t quite enough. Studying the practices of Ananda Marga, in the back of my mind I still had pictures of the Pope, so that was not letting me be free with this meditation. Then I had a dream: I was in Rome, in the catacombs, where in the past they did their practices so they wouldn’t be persecuted. I was at a mass, and the Pope was saying mass. I saw him from behind, then he turned around with a big smile on his face, and it was the guru. So I thought, wow, there’s something to this, this is a direct contact through a dream.

Shortly after that, margiis were going to India – Baba was giving a special grace. I didn’t know what it was about at the time, I just had the intuitive feeling to go, to see this guru. We were waiting outside this building where Baba was going to come. Baba came by in His car. I had never had any direct spiritual experience before, outside that dream, but when this vehicle drove by, the tears came. I could not stop crying; it was just an overwhelming thing for me. I said, “This is really something.”

A few days later I was told to give a report. I didn’t know what the report would be, even, since I was a new margii. I was supposed to tell what my duties were. Well, I didn’t have any duties. So all this time, three days waiting, I had so many anxieties about what I was going to tell Him. I didn’t even know what a report is. I was nervous, what was I going to say to this guru? Well, I understood He was omnipresent, so I knew He was going to know what I was thinking, and what the *acaryas* were going to tell Him about my duties.

There I was in front of the guru, and I felt completely stripped of everything. I felt like a skeleton in front of Him, I was so exposed. And still thinking, what was I going to say in my report? And the guru let me off the hook by asking, “Was this a satisfactory report?” And I said, “No.” So He let me off the hook.

The next thing I knew, and it was a quick process because there were so many margiis waiting, the *acaryas* grabbed me, and threw me on the guru’s lap. I was rubbing His feet, He’s rubbing my back. I lost all contact with everything, except I felt like I was a flower, a rose to be exact, being sucked up into a vacuum. The point was, everything left me, I didn’t feel anything. Next thing I was being picked up, pulled away for the next person to come in. It was a beautiful experience. Just feeling like I left the plane of the universe, a totally beautiful feeling. It actually took me years to experience it, and I’m still experiencing it. At that time I had just left the planet, completely spaced out.

But I had heard stories about people’s experiences with Baba, that He was joking, all these fun stories. So if you can believe, I felt cheated that Baba was not joking with me, that He was all business-like. A few months later, I still had this feeling, “Well, I really didn’t see the guru laughing, that funny nature.”

One night I had a dream. Baba pulled up in a pickup truck and asked, “Who wants to go to town?” Me, being humble, or maybe not feeling worthy, said, “Let someone else go.” But nobody else wanted to go. So I said, “Baba, I’ll go with you.”

So I jumped in and we took off. Then someone else came out of the house and wanted to go too. I said, “Baba, John wants to come too.” Baba said, “OK,” and He’s smiling all this time. So Baba backed up, I opened the door, and just as John was about to get in, Baba floored this thing, peeled a strip of rubber, and took off. And He was laughing and giggling. So I got my joking Baba through a dream.

### Tapan Mallick

*Hockessin, Delaware*

Before leaving India to study in the U.S., I went with Baba on field walk. He talked about the importance of company in human life (*satsaunga*). He advised me on how to carry myself in a distant country. He emphasized that I should stay in touch with margiis.

One day, while we were waiting for General Darshan, a person (Mr. X) who spoke a different language started to make snide comments about Bengali-speaking people in front of me. He knew I spoke Bengali. I was very hurt by his comments and he enjoyed hurting my feelings. When General Darshan started, Baba said he would like to give a demonstration on past lives. He chose Mr. X for the demonstration. He put his stick into the *trikuti* of Mr. X and asked him what he was seeing. Mr. X said, “Baba, I see myself a Bengali living in the city of Dacca (capital of Bangladesh).”

Baba asked him what was his name at that time. He replied, “Ram Chandra Roy.” Then Baba asked him to see my past life. I was a south Indian person speaking Kannad language. Baba asked him to see my past life further back. He replied seeing a European person living in Johannesburg in South Africa speaking English.

Baba concluded the General Darshan saying (paraphrased): “All languages, all races, all countries belong to *Parama Purusa*. We should treat them with equal respect. We take different bodies, speak different languages and live in different countries in our eternal journey to *Parama Purusa*. Having a disrespectful attitude to anyone, any language, any country is to have disrespectful attitude to *Parama Purusa*.”

Mr. X apologized to me afterward.

***

By His grace I experienced sounds at different stages of meditation: the sound of crickets, then the sound of a flute – at that point I lost my concentration. For a moment I got distracted with the thought that I hadn’t heard from my father for a long time and wondering if he was all right. He was very sick at that time. Baba rebuked me afterwards for losing my concentration. He told me my father would be all right – why can’t we

surrender everything to *Parama Purusa* and enjoy His bliss? This memory still brings tears to my eyes – He knows all our thoughts, even if it appears for a split second.

I came to the U.S. in 1980 to attend graduate school. Someone was supposed to pick me up at the JFK airport. My flight for Austin, Texas was on the following day. On arriving at JFK, I found that the person was not there to pick me up. This was my first time in this country, I didn’t know anybody, didn’t have phone number or name of any margii. I didn’t have enough money to take a shuttle or spend the night in a hotel. I just had about $22 in addition to my first semester’s tuition and one month’s rent.

While I was thinking what to do, I saw a man approaching me. He was the elder brother of a student who I met while boarding the JFK-bound plane at Amsterdam. I just had a brief chat with him. He was going to attend Texas A&M and he spoke Bengali. That was the extent of our conversation. I never saw him in that flight again. Strangely enough, his elder brother told him, “Let’s just check if there are any other Indian students and if someone is at the airport we can escort them.” He knew someone in New York and arranged for me to spend the night there. My problem was solved. I never heard from that student again.

**Tvarita**
*Millville, New Jersey*

I had been initiated into Ananda Marga and had not yet gone to see Baba. My friend and spiritual sister, Sadhana, was planning to make the trip in the summer of 1982 and was urging me to travel with her. It is important to note that my Sanskrit name at the time was Tarita. Sadhana did not think the name suited me – but now I am getting ahead of the story.

I felt much confusion and mental clash over making the decision to go to India. One morning while working in my flower garden the thought came to me that I had to go to India in order to “speed up” my spiritual progress. So the decision was made.

While in Patna at Baba’s house, Sadhana approached one senior acarya and asked him to change my name. It wasn’t right for me, she said. I was feeling rather clashed because I wanted Baba to personally give me a new name. The *acarya* told us to wait, which we did. He came back in a short time with a small slip of paper that had my new name written on it along with its meaning. I opened the paper and read it, still feeling clashed that Baba had not personally given my name to me. I put the paper away and didn’t look at it again until I returned to my home in the States. When I read it the second time my new name and its significance hit me in full. My new name was Tvarita – one who is very fast. I cried and cried the instant I realized that all-knowing Baba had personalized my name from my very thoughts. Baba Nam Kevalam.

### Alok

*Iowa City, Iowa*

I was initiated by Dada Ananjana in Austin, Texas, in 1982. Soon after that, I moved to Southeast Asia to work with Ananda Marga, so that throughout the mid- and late '80s, I had many opportunities to see Baba in India.

I didn't feel anything overwhelming the first time I saw Him, but something drew me to make the hour-long journey to His house from the AM *jagrti* in Tiljala every day. Perhaps it was a kind of inchoate longing, as if I felt something special could happen, if only I gave it enough time.

In the afternoons, He was driven to a nearby park where He went for a walk around the lake. When He returned, it was as if an electric pulse jabbed through the crowd. "He's coming, He's coming!" Everyone scrambled to get into a good position to see Him. The gates to the driveway swung open, and the car turned in. Baba would step slowly from the car. He was clean-shaven, and wore old-fashioned black-framed eyeglasses. His ears were large and elongated, framing a smooth brown face.

Everyone rushed to stand near Him, and someone would begin to sing one of His recent *Prabhat Samgiit* compositions. Baba would tilt his head and listen, then nod and smile.

"Do you like this song?" He would ask playfully. "Should we keep this one?"

One day, the crowd outside His house was as thick as usual. My chances of getting close to Him were slim. So I stood in the street outside the gate, which actually afforded me a better look, as His chair was just on the other side. An Indian *acarya* began to warble a devotional song, and the crowd followed in call and response. Everyone was singing earnestly, but I found myself just drifting as I sized Baba up.

The song went on. Baba nodded and smiled. Then, for seemingly no reason, He turned His head and glanced at me through the gate, out of the corner of His eyes. And it was as if a beam of light shot out from those eyes, piercing me.

With this one glance, my heart expanded, and my mind soared. It was not that I noticed a certain expression on His face, and that made me feel happy. It was something

deeper – an actual transformation of consciousness through a glance. I didn't understand what was happening. But I didn't really need to.

The look lasted for only a moment. Then things went on as before, except that I was changed. I couldn't speak, and after I left His house I stumbled about like a madman, drunk on a wine of great love. I didn't want to talk to anyone, but only to hold on to this sweetness for as long as possible, to hoard it, store it, to flow away into a new realm on the waves generated by His loving gaze.

Soon, Baba embarked on a tour of India, conducting a series of DMCs, and I followed Him. Thousands of margiis attended these three-day gatherings in a state of giddy expectation.

He made a stop in Tatanagar, a city famous for steel. A crowd gathered in the huge pinnacled tent, which was not unlike a circus big top. There were musical and dramatic presentations, and everyone looked forward to the highlight of the DMC, when Baba blessed the crowd with a bestowal of spiritual energy through His *mudra*.

As He sat on a raised stage with several local margiis around him, I found that I couldn't take my eyes off Him. He turned a very tender gaze upon the crowd as He spoke, and I felt flooded with feelings of fondness for this man.

Baba gave a discourse on the importance of discipline in one's daily practices. He had the most unusual voice. He drawled out his vowels, as if to emphasize the importance of what He was saying. At the end of His talk He seemed to shift gears, offering up a blessing of sorts. "Le-e-et everyone be-e-e happy," He said slowly. "Le-e-e-t everyone see the bright side of e-e-everything."

As He spoke, the crowd's expectancy leapt like barometric pressure. People leaned forward, and everyone was as if perched on the edge of some kind of revelation.

"Le-e-et everyone be free from ailments of all kinds. Let no-o-o one be forced to undergo-o-o any difficulties due to pressure of circumstance."

Then, slowly, His hands glided into the position of the *varabhaya mudra*. Baba held His right hand palm open next to the chest. His left hand was lowered palm down near the waist. The pose was not only metaphorical. An electric shock coursed through the crowd. People cried out, shuddered, fell over backwards. On the stage the local AM

leader was sobbing.

I felt cool electricity pulsing through the air, a subtle, tingling, blissful sensation that left me feeling refreshed and inspired. Someone mentioned afterwards that he felt as if time had stood still at the moment of the *mudra*, that Baba was intimating to us the timeless nature of the state of enlightenment, giving us a little taste of that state, as incentive to deepen our spiritual practices.

As the shock waves of the *mudra* subsided, Baba rose and began to walk slowly towards the exit. The local leader, with eyes wide and a trembling step, held out his hands to make sure Baba did not trip, and slowly backed down the stairs in this protective, overwhelmed state.

As they walked, a strange thing happened. It seemed to me that Baba was shimmering – that His body was now composed of thousands of tiny points of light, not one solid substance. His figure shone, vibrated, and the air around Him seemed to dance. I rubbed my eyes, but the phenomenon persisted. Swirling energy filled the air, as if the scene was bobbing up and down on ocean waves. Whether it was a result of my own heightened perceptions, or Baba was actually projecting this, I cannot know. But it touched me.

I went to Japan to work as an LFT. I was putting together a small weekly Prout newspaper in Tokyo. And so I had the opportunity to go to India for LFT reporting. A number of LFTs from around the globe were gathered to talk about their work with Baba. One dada poked me in the back, prodding me to stand in front.
