Like a squirming school kid called upon to give an answer in his worst subject, I approached Baba nervously, but with excitement. He asked my name and where I was working.

“My name is Aditya (my name at the time). I’m working in Japan, Baba.” Some of the *acaryas* ere laughed, since most LFTs worked in their home countries, and I did not seem to be Japanese.

Baba had been stressing the need to become fully knowledgeable about whatever place we were working in so that we could better serve the people. Flora, fauna, waterways, demographics, economics, agricultural and industrial potentialities – He wanted us to be well versed in all these things. Of course, He was interested in

everything. On another occasion, perhaps because He knew I loved literature, He asked a question about the significance of Harriet Beecher Stowe’s *Uncle Tom’s Cabin* to which I was able to stammer some answer. He also encouraged me to read French literature. Now He began speaking about Japan, mentioning that tidal power would make a lot of sense for the island nation. He then asked me something about Japan’s geography.

I didn’t know the answer, so I mumbled.

“What was that?” Baba asked.

“Baba, I don’t know,” I said.

“You don’t know?” Baba repeated.

I sensed a sudden change in the atmosphere of the room.

“He does not know?” Baba’s face assumed a stern look. As if electrical energy had built up in the room around Him, His anger flashed out.

“You should know this!” Baba said.

I jumped with the force of His *shakti*. Everyone in the room also seemed to sway back. This dispensation of energy lasted for only a second, but Baba’s anger was palpable. “What have I done?” I thought, chastened yet strangely fascinated. Something was going on beyond His response to my lack of answer. Not everyone was fortunate enough to see this side of Baba.

Then, as quickly as it came, the mood shifted, and Baba assumed an expression I will never forget. He gazed into my eyes and I was overwhelmed by a sweet sense of His affection for me. I could have stood there transfixed for hours in the glow of this smile. The flip-flop of energy in Baba’s demeanor and in the room was such that people were suffused with a feeling of great release. Everyone around me was laughing, pulled in by the charm of Baba’s cosmic joke. I had a sense that Baba was showing me two distinct faces, of strict disciplinarian and loving father. Also that anger was a tool, which could be utilized to instruct. I had always suppressed strong emotions. Was He trying to teach me their utility?

During Personal Contact, I sat and watched Him gazing off into space, and it seemed to me as if He were reading a page out of the book of my life.

“Hmm,” He said. Then, as if something particularly interesting appeared before his inner vision, “Something in your past.”

I knew that He was referring to an incident in my recent, misspent youth. It was as if there was no need for speech, and He had only to think, and I would know what He meant. I looked up at Him, much affected. A tremendous, tender, sadness lay in His eyes.

“You have so much potential, why are you wasting it?” The sadness grew until it filled the room.

“Baba, I made a mistake.”

He paused for a moment. “No, no, it was not a mistake. It was commissional.” He continued to gaze at me. We sat there.

His mood seemed to shift. Inwardly I felt a sense of my own inadequacy, but from Him I felt no blame, only gentle correction. Baba’s message to me: Clean up your act.

Then, turning the focus away from my shortcomings, to what I interpreted as a way for me to move beyond those shortcomings, He said, gravely, “Do something for humanity.” He looked into my eyes, and although I did not experience what I had that day outside his gate, I felt a great love from Him.

I left His room, and don’t remember going down the stairs, but a friend later said there was a great light in my face. I went down to the darshan hall and sat for sadhana. Later that afternoon, an *acarya* came up to me and conveyed a message that Baba had given about me. “Baba said, ‘He will do some work,’” the *acarya* told me.

God has forced me to learn how to swim the rough waves of this life by throwing me into deep water several times. When I was young I suffered from an inferiority complex that curtailed my ability to express myself. Baba’s teachings about this, that such complexes – whether inferiority or superiority – are simply distortions of ego, and can be overcome through ideation, helped me a lot.

As I began doing sadhana more seriously, the arena of struggle in my life seemed to shift from psychic to physical. Fifteen years ago my body was slapped with an acute illness which turned chronic. Living with a debilitating illness has been no joke, but *Prabhat Samgiita* and sadhana, the explanatory idea of *samskara*, and the support of

friends have helped me to deal with it. The illness has forced me to become more accepting; to surrender.

I also had a huge clash with my parents, who felt that Ananda Marga was an unhealthy organization, and tried to persuade me to leave. As a result of this, for a while I developed some grave doubts about this path, but Baba never released His grip on me. The struggle was ultimately a useful experience, since it forced me to look with clearer eyes at my involvement in AM, and to understand and accept some of the organization’s problems, instead of being in denial about them. For example, I began to feel that the emphasis on fearlessness and strength in our organization might sometimes come at the expense of compassion and humility. In any case, as I said, He brought me back to His shelter, for which I am grateful.

One final story. I was working for Ananda Marga in South Korea, and we had a *jagrti* in a city called Daegu. After several years there, the landlord asked us to move out, since she wanted to use the house herself. There was nothing we could do but comply, and so one brother and I roamed the streets in search of a ‘for rent’ sign. We walked all day and found no place suitable.

The next day, however, sister Parashakti called to say she had found a new *jagrti*. We hopped the bus over to a large, second floor loft for rent. Although it was large enough for yoga classes and group meditation, there was something about it I didn’t like. It faced a noisy street, for one thing, and somehow it just didn’t feel right. I told Parashakti that I really wasn’t sure that this was the right place for us. She became a bit upset, and we rode the bus back to the *jagrti* in silence.

When we got there, the landlord was waiting for us. She began talking quickly in Korean, most of which I couldn’t understand, but I knew something was up by the way Parashakti’s eyebrows arched higher and her eyes opened wider as the landlady went on.

“What is it?” I asked.

“Well,” she said. “She says she had a dream last night, in which a man came to her and told her that it was important that we stayed here, in this house. So she’s decided to take only one room, and leave the rest to us.” Just then, the landlady entered our DC hall, where a large photo of Baba rested on the table. She pointed and began speaking

again, in an animated fashion. Parashakti explained, “She says that’s the man who appeared in her dream last night and asks who he is!”

Tell her He’s someone very special…

### Kalyani

*San Diego, California*

I was initiated by Didi Ananda Anuradha. I’d been wanting a guru for a very long time. I’d heard that if you want the guru badly enough, He will come. And I thought, “Who could want the guru more than me? I want the guru so badly, and it’s not coming.” I didn’t realize then, but now I do, that maybe my ego was a little too much. Anyway, at the proper time, Baba came to me.

I went to a dinner where an *acarya* was speaking. I went not really expecting anything, because I think I’d kind of given up on finding a guru. I sat down with the margiis and did some sadhana, and then I saw Baba’s picture.

I went up closer to His picture, and I felt a pounding in my heart *cakra*, and the vibration was really strong. Then I opened up one of His books, and the book was pounding me, and vibrating me really strongly, too. And I began to read, and thought, “Oh. Everything that I’ve been struggling to put together, the philosophy is right here.”

It was really comprehensive. And I felt moved. When I went home that night, I started singing Baba Nam Kevalam, which was going through my mind strongly. I felt the one I’d waited for so long had finally come. I was crying and crying. In some ways I was so happy, and in another, I felt, why did I have to wait so long, and suffer so much? I had such a big ego. That I’m still working on. Still, I knew absolutely that this was the one I’d waited for many lifetimes, and whatever suffering I had to go through was worth it, to have Baba and Ananda Marga. I felt so completely blessed. I felt so sure about this path, and it never stopped.

The first year I went to India, I was getting into a lot of meetings with Baba. One particular day I’d been trying to sit for LFT testing so I could have Personal Contact, which I actually didn’t get. But this one day I wasn’t able to get into a meeting – Baba was having a meeting with some brothers. And I was sitting on Baba’s porch, doing sadhana with some other sisters.

Then the meeting ended, and someone said that sometimes after meetings the door is open and you can go in. So I rushed up the stairs, the door was open, and one

person said, “Oh, you just missed Him.” I was crushed. The vibration was so strong in that room. I walked just a couple more steps, and Baba was standing right there.

I was so happy; He was so close. But He was just leaving, walking out. And so I ideated. He was preparing to leave. I called to him, kind of quietly, with so much love, “Baba.”

At that instant Baba stopped and turned around and walked back towards me. And He stood right in front of me, and looked at me. And I felt like I wanted to drop to my knees and bow, but at the same time I was feeling embarrassed to do that, and also remembering to always keep your eyes on the guru. So I didn’t bow. But my whole being wanted to. I was looking at Baba. Was He looking at me? Could He have heard me? Did He know that was even me that said that? We were in a room full of people. He stood there for some time, and then He moved on. That was really special to me.

### Sudhiir (2)

Another visit I can remember was the September when the first *Prabhat Samgiit* was written. Baba was traveling; He was in Deoghar, in North Bihar. I took a train there. There were many margiis from Jamalpur there, and I got to know these margiis. It was like visiting Jamalpur, because they were all there. Baba was kind of in His Krsna mood; He wasn’t acting like the head of a worldwide organization. All of His early devotees were there, and it was such a family situation, such a mood.

He used to come out and walk back and forth. The first morning He came out, He asked the dadas to sing this song He had written just that morning. His Personal Assistant, General Secretary and several dadas sang this song. I was standing a couple feet away while He was correcting them. “Bandhu He Niye Calo.” It was the first *Prabhat Samgiit*. After a couple of days, as far as foreign margiis, there were only myself and three others. We were being trained by some of the acaryas to sing one of the *Prabhat Samgiits* to Baba, to be the first non-Delhi sector margiis to sing to Baba. We memorized the fourth or fifth song. But one brother left, and then it was just three Americans. That night something happened, and we didn’t get the opportunity to sing the song. The next morning, the two other Americans had to catch a train, and that left me [laughs].

That night Baba walked up and down the driveway. He called for a chair, and sat there, and all the Jamalpur margiis sat in a semicircle. The margiis started to sing *bhajans*, and He stopped them, and said, “I only want to hear *Prabhat Samgiit*.” Then He asked for the other-sector margiis to sing. I was the only one. I was so nervous, and I started to sing. It was a difficult song, but I managed to get out the first few lines, then the dadas joined in. I think they were terrified I was going to make some terrible mistake. We all together finished the song, and Baba said, “Very good.” It was wonderful to have the opportunity to sing to Baba.

I was the only non-Indian margii there. It was like being in a land forgotten. I learned later that Deoghar was one of the places Shiva lived. There were Shiva temples there, which I visited. They were doing a form of kiirtan to Shiva. I couldn’t help

thinking that Baba was revisiting it from His past life as Shiva. He spent so much time there.

And I needed to go back.

I said to Baba, “Baba, I’m leaving.”

Baba said, “So soon? Well, come again and again.”

I was dumbstruck. The way He said so soon, I couldn’t possibly leave, I couldn’t do it. They said He was going to Ananda Nagar. So I said, “The heck with my reservations, the heck with everything, I’m staying.”

We went to Ananda Nagar, a long dusty drive through the rice paddies and dusty countryside to Bengal from Bihar. All the margiis from around that locality were there. Again it was just Baba walking up and down His driveway, and us singing *Prabhat Samgiit* to Him. Occasionally He would sit, and everyone sat at His feet, and we were singing in the evening. It was unforgettable; there was just enough light to see Him, and not much else. It was more by the radiance of His light we were seeing, than anything else. Occasionally Baba would stop us and correct a word, or the way it was to be sung, and sometimes He would sing it the way it was to be sung.

I think in 1981 Baba asked for artists to come and decorate His house in Kolkata. There were only two other groups from around the world, a couple of Italians, and a German brother. So we were looking around His house, trying to pick out our favorite places to do. I thought the walls of the *dharmacakra* hall would be good, they wouldn’t be bothered by the weather.

Some days Baba would come walking around through the garden, inspecting the plants. This day He came around the back side, and we were all scrunched together to try to see Him. Then we started hearing shouts from the other side, they were shouting my name, as well as the Italian brother’s, to come and talk with Baba. We got to the porch, Baba was there, and He explained He was going to show us each where we would do our murals.

My spot was on the porch in front of the museum. I couldn’t help feeling a little disappointed because it wasn’t the spot I had picked. But I smiled, “Thank you, Baba.” Then we walked through the doors into the DC hall.

And Baba turned to me and said, “It would not be appropriate to decorate these walls.”

“Yes, Baba.”

And then I went to work. I had brought acrylic paints from the U.S., and I had permission to go up on the porch. I had the complete run of the place, and sometimes I took advantage of that. Sometimes Baba would come around, and I would go up to be the first to greet Him, before He saw the rest of the margiis. And I got several little namaskars from that. It took almost three weeks to finish. I did the ceiling and walls, and during that time Baba was going out into the garden some time and sitting. I was there all day long, and was getting fed with the leftovers from Baba’s meals. I’d be painting, or pretending to paint, because I’d be standing there in front of my mural, but my head would be turned watching Baba walk around in the garden.

I finished the painting, and at the same time I got very sick with bronchitis. The last couple weeks it was difficult to stand up and hold the brush and paint. But I wasn’t going to miss that opportunity. Also, all my friends got to pose for me, to hang out in the forbidden territory, and I painted them into the mural.

It was two days before my ticket to go home. In those days, Baba was showing groups of people through the garden and talking about the plants. Like everyone else I’d been trying to get on the garden party list, unsuccessfully. I was getting more desperate, and thought, “Well, Baba are you going to look at this thing?” So I took determination that I was going to stop Baba and ask Him about the mural, and see if I could get into a conversation with Him about it.

On the last day, I made sure I was right next to Baba’s car, with 30 margiis surrounding me. Well, Baba walked around, up on the porch, and I heard my name being shouted, and I was smashed in next to His car. I’d had the wrong strategy. So I had to fight my way out to the porch, and Baba was already looking at the mural.

And He said, “Very good, very good.” And He’s looking around, and I didn’t know what to say; I couldn’t think of anything to ask Baba about it.

Then He turned to me and said, “I think you will do more of this in the future?”

I said, “Yes, Baba.”

And at the time I didn’t realize He was talking literally. This was the first and only mural I had ever done. I used all the wrong materials; everything peeled off, because the acrylic did not adhere to the oil paint. It was a total disaster in terms of technical skill. But it didn’t matter, it was obvious to me that the whole experience was an excuse for me to be close to Baba. At the time I didn’t really think of myself as an artist. I didn’t have very much confidence that I could be a professional painter. He said that, and I didn’t really take it seriously.

About twelve years later I did a mural, and have since done a number of them. And they have been a way for me to live out my ideals in terms of bringing art to people. Baba talks about the several kinds of art: that which deals with the spiritual, and the kind that deals with the everyday life of ordinary people. And this seems to me to be what I do best, to do art about the ordinary life of the people, and to put art out there in front of people who would normally never go to a place where art was. And to work with populations of children, and students, to be part of the community. It’s been an excellent thing for me, and Baba saw it coming, of course.

One time I went to visit Baba and I had been there for two or three weeks and it was raining; it had been raining and raining and raining. And the whole city was flooded, completely flooded. Tiljala was underwater.

At the end of that week, I was the only Westerner left; otherwise it was all dadas and didis. I was with one dada, and he said, “Come on, everybody is going to give their *pranams* and namaskar to Baba and give garland to Baba.”

So he took me. We went all over the place trying to find a stall that was open – everything was closed, everything was flooded – and finally somehow we found a place that had them and he bought one for each of us. So then we were standing in line – there was this long line of *acaryas* giving garlands to Baba, and I was at the end of the line, and only Westerner and non-*acarya*.

After everybody had given their garlands but me, there was this mountain of flowers up to Baba’s eyes. Baba started taking them off one by one by one, and the VSS guard was shouting something at me saying, “No more, no more, this is too much, enough already.” And Shamitananda, who was standing beside me, started arguing on my

behalf; the VSS guard and Shamitananda were arguing. And the garlands were coming off of Baba and slowly you could see more and more of Baba’s face reappearing.

I was standing there with my garland not knowing what to do. So I just stood there. And Baba kind of had this twinkle in His eye. The guard was trying to get my garland away from me. So finally Shamitananda – I guess he won the argument with the guard – gave me back my garland, turned me around to Baba and mine was the last garland lifted up to Baba, and Baba said, “Oh, for me?” So I was able to give my garland to Baba and give my namaskar to him.

### Arun

*New York City*

Let me thank Dhyaneshananda whose book greatly inspired me. As he stated in his introduction, these accounts are also sacred to me. They serve to remind me of life’s purpose. I have attempted to sacrifice embellishment for precision in these stories. Direct quotes of Baba were only used when I knew the exact words, otherwise indirect methods were used. I also want to thank brother Giridhara who helped me to appreciate the historical significance of everything Baba said and did, and who helped me to realize that even an ordinary margii’s contact with Baba was extraordinary.

There was a time I would go out at night in secret to search for my Master. These walks would take me to the loneliest alleys and dimly lit areas of the city where I hoped my Master might appear to initiate me in private. I was naive, and did not know that Baba had already made arrangements. An old friend had visited me weeks before with a female companion. She had left the phone number of Ananda Marga with my mother. When my night walks were finally exhausted, I called that number and attended *dharmacakra*.

I became quickly enamored of Ananda Marga philosophy and was soon initiated. Initiation took place during *dharmacakra*, and between the accent of the acarya and the loud kiirtan, I only understood one word, the *nama mantra*. I made a strong resolution to practice it regularly. I glanced at Baba’s photo on leaving the room and thought, “Such a sublime philosophy! But this man is certainly not my Guru.”

The next day after initiation I returned home by bicycle from work fully exhausted. I tried to rest without sleeping, knowing that I should meditate. Finally I sat up without washing or removing my shoes to repeat the *mantra*. As soon as the process began I felt tremendous energy rising in my spine and soon lost all physical consciousness. Buried memories of my life, infancy and previous live’s meditations flooded my mind. A voice said, “You have come home.” I did not recognize the voice. This experience was brief but forever changed me.

Baba first revealed Himself to me in a dream. In a recurring nightmare throughout my life, I would be lost in the city behind the wheel of a runaway car, too young to drive. This time the car managed to come to a gentle stop in a familiar neighborhood of my childhood. I got out and sat under a tree with a sigh of relief. In a distant tree there was a sparkling light reflecting off of someone’s eyes. In a flash, this person appeared in the tree above me. As I looked up, he fell down onto me with a jolly laugh. I was surprised to recognize him... “Baba?”

“Observe,” He said. “Have you noticed that you are not wet?” There was a deluge of rain, yet we remained dry.

“Yes,” I replied.

He then took me to a sloping lawn where He directed me to sit at the bottom while He sat at a higher spot. He waved His right hand as if dealing cards and Bengali *shlokas* began to emerge from His palm, forming themselves into three stanzas, each with four lines. He made me repeat each syllable with tedious precision. He told me that I must learn this. These lessons continued all night, and on other occasions also.

Once after receiving second lesson I was crossing a busy intersection. A car came speeding towards me and I stepped back. The car suddenly wheeled around and stopped, blocking traffic in all four directions. An angry large man came running after me shouting profanity and vowing to tear me to pieces. He caught me by the shirt and pulled back his fist to punch me. I only thought “second lesson” and immediately heard the *mantra* as if it were reverberating everywhere. I looked into the man’s eyes. They were wide with fear. He panicked, ran full speed back to his car and drove away. Another margii witnessed the event from across the street. He came to me and asked for an explanation. “I think Baba just saved me,” was my reply.

I learned from one dada that Baba loved *bhajans* and would sometimes listen personally to the offering. I resolved to compose a *bhajan* for Baba in hopes of playing it for Him. When Baba landed in Jamaica, I was unable to go due to unusual complications in my life. I was heart-broken, and sat alone crying for days. Later I learned that Baba was reluctant to make His first appearance, but that finally He walked out onto the stage when a sister began singing the *bhajan* I had composed. This fact was confirmed by

audio recordings of the event. My burden was lifted. He did choose to accept my offering after all.

I composed another *bhajan* to be played for Baba at the first Global Conference of Ananda Marga. He did not attend but I was asked to play this song during a slide show of Baba photos. During the *bhajan*, many margiis began weeping. A brother next to me fell over. Wailing and other noises came from the gathering. I noticed some of the senior *acaryas* were crying. Soon I also was overcome and could no longer continue to sing. I had to pause. When I regained some composure I completed the *bhajan*. Afterwards many margiis asked me to teach them that song, but I would always say, “It was not the song, it was Baba.”

Finally I composed a third song to be sung for Baba and decided to take it to India personally, with my guitar. I was ashamed at having been a margii for 11 years without meeting Baba. I was seated on the plane next to another sister who had also been a margii for 11 years without meeting Him. “How He cares for our feelings,” I thought. When we arrived and Baba emerged from His house at Ananda Nagar, I was overcome to see that the Baba of my dreams was also in real human form.

During my first visit to India I entered a request to sing a *bhajan* for Baba. I was told, “Since one month Baba will only hear *Prabhat Samgiit*.” I was crestfallen. One dada saw my plight and encouraged me to learn *Prabhat Samgiit*. Later this dada became my good friend and Sectorial Secretary. After learning some songs, I reissued my request to sing for Him. I was told, “Baba will personally *hear Prabhat Samgiit* from you tomorrow. Come to the house by one p.m. and know ten songs.” I arrived early and waited with anticipation by Baba’s bedroom door. During this wait there was some commotion outside, as a group of European margiis had made their way past the gate guards expecting to receive darshan. Security promptly removed them and ordered all Western margiis from the compound. I tried to explain my circumstance, but was removed.

After years of this and other disappointing failures, I was still thinking about how I could sing for Him. I brought my guitar to His garden at an hour when He was walking and few others were there. I was told by a dada that only harmonium should be used for

*Prabhat Samgiita*, and the song was stopped. The next day I tried again, seeing that the person who had stopped me was no longer present.

Baba was walking in the garden when He stopped and said, “Your song is excellent. This *Prabhat Samgiita* can be played on local instruments such as the guitar and violin. Please continue.”

The next day was *Dharma Maha Cakra* (DMC). After much insistence, I was finally readmitted to Baba’s quarters. I waited outside His room, but the time had grown short and He was already doing sadhana in preparation for DMC. I was told to leave by 10 p.m. or I would fail to reach the *pandal* in time and would be left outside. I could not leave the spot. I did sadhana and *pranam* at His door. At last Baba came out for DMC and somehow I was whisked into a security car just behind Baba’s car. I watched Baba enter the *pandal* and, although the last to enter, I was then placed just in front of the dais, such is His Grace.

I returned to India with an electronic keyboard after learning 100 *Prabhat Samgiit* songs. While Baba was on field walk, the margiis were curious to sing *Prabhat Samgiit* with this accompaniment. But when Baba returned, I was shoved into the back of the garage for fear that it would not be acceptable. Due to the unexpected suddenness of Baba’s arrival, no new song could be started, so I continued to lead the song I had been singing from that spot.

After the car entered the gate and stopped, a senior didi by the car stretched her arm in my direction with the palm down and made a gesture that in my culture means, “Be Quiet!” I quickly stopped playing. She then turned her palm up and made another gesture that I took to mean, “Start playing again.” Once more she turned her palm down and I stopped playing. Finally she shouted, “Hurry up, Baba is calling for you!”

The crowd parted and I stumbled through expecting that I had made a major transgression. Baba rolled down His window, smiled sweetly and gave Namaskar. “Your pronunciation is very nice. Do you like this song?”

“Yes, it is lovely, Baba.”

He then told me that it was a *ghazal*. He patiently spelled *ghazal* for me and described its Arabian origins. He named the *ghazal* “Hava Ghazal,” or wind *ghazal*, and proceeded to sing various other types of *ghazal* melodies, noting their beats and

measures. I understood little of what He said, but He treated me like we were musical companions and I felt completely at ease and natural in His presence.

After that the dadas always arranged for me to be in front of Baba. I also learned that Didi’s first gesture had meant, “Come quickly.”

# CHAPTER SIX: DANCING IN DIVINE ECSTACY

> “Keep all your desires inclined towards Him. Always keep yourselves merged in His thought. Go deep into the mood of that infinite Love.”

During the latter half of the 80s, Baba’s speed in teaching and training *acaryas* and margiis seemed to accelerate, if such a thing was possible. He granted more Personal Contacts. The organization saw tremendous growth, and margiis traveling to India from New York Sector joined large gatherings of margiis from around the world at DMCs. Baba introduced the scientific concept of *microvita*, subtle entities linking matter and consciousness, and also taught “*microvita sadhana*” to a few senior margiis. He predicted the shifting of the Earth’s poles, and a coming Ice Age. He offered new perspectives on Prout and the world’s economic situation. He continued to compose *Prabhat Samgiit*, and to accept plants from devotees as part of a plant preservation program. Baba also began urging disciples to acquire Master Units around the world.

### Pavitra

*Los Angeles, California*

I was born in Ecuador and came to the U.S. in 1976. I have always been interested in yoga and spirituality, because my dad introduced me to those subjects through books by Vivekananda. I became very interested, and met someone who told me even more, and I asked her if she could direct me to a place to learn. She had no idea. I looked in the yellow pages under yoga and found Ananda Marga in Los Altos Hills. That was in 1979.

I called and asked about yoga lessons. I was more interested in the physical part. The woman who answered the phone said, “You can come, but you have to come for six weeks of training which includes meditation.” I was immediately turned off, because I had done other things. She later called and invited me to *dharmacakra*. And I always resisted.

Finally she said, “If you won’t come to us, I’m going to come to you.” She came to visit me, and started talking to me about yoga. I said, “I know a lot about yoga,” and showed her all the books I had read. She smiled and said, “You know, it’s better if you stop reading and start meditating.”

I was deeply offended; she offended my intellect. But because of her ways – she was a very wonderful person – I was intrigued. Finally I went to *dharmacakra* on a Sunday. I felt really strange, and I thought to myself, “What am I doing here?” But something happened and I continued to go to DC, until someone told me there was a teacher there, and asked if would like to be initiated. I was initiated in April, 1979, by Didi Ananda Anuradha.

I started with my meditation and got totally confused. I had a lot of preconceived ideas about what I was to do. There was one thing constantly in my mind. People kept talking about Baba, and we were meditating in front of His picture. I wanted to see this Baba. It became an obsession. Finally I convinced one of the margiis to go to India with me. Baba had just started composing *Prabhat Samgiit*.

The first time in India is such an adventure. We stayed at a hotel near Baba’s house. Then we went to see Baba. We were the only two Westerners there. It was very

intimate and beautiful. That first night Baba came from His room. We were waiting for Him; my friend was across from me – she kept saying I should come there where she was for a better view. I couldn’t move. I wanted to move but I couldn’t; it was strange. So I saw Him, and I was very taken by the fact that He was so short. I didn’t have any feelings then for Him, as it was all very new.

We stayed for two and a half weeks, going to Baba’s house twice a day, and all the while He spoke in Bengali. And I was really upset. I thought to myself, “I came all the way here, and spent all this money. I feel very uncomfortable, it’s so hot.” I had been thinking, “I wish I could understand what He’s saying,” but I resigned myself.

The last night He said, “And tonight I want to share with you that I have composed a song in English.”

And He sang “Tiny Green Island.” I was in such a state of mind, I thought, “Wow, I’m understanding Bengali.” Anyway, that was really nice, that He had spoken to me. Then He came back and we sat at His feet. I was so close to Him that I could have stretched out my hand and touched His feet. It was just a fantastic experience. The night was warm, and it was a very beautiful atmosphere. People were singing *Prabhat Samgiit*. I had sort of a feeling that this was not the first time I’d had this experience.

The following day we were leaving and we went to Baba’s house to say goodbye. I was still kind of upset in spite of everything. My friend said, “We have to buy a garland in the market.”

I said, “I’m not going to do it.”

She said, “You have to, this is the custom.”

And I said no, so she said, “OK, get some flowers.”

So I bought some beautiful red roses in a banana leaf. We went to Baba’s house where He was walking.

Jagadmitra said, “Baba, we’re leaving. We love you and we love your songs.” And He turned around. He didn’t answer her, but He turned to me and said, “Did you like the songs?”

“Yes, Baba, I did.”

Then He explained to me the kind of music He had used to compose these songs, music from all over the world. I cannot explain in words how I felt. The only thing I can

say is that at that very moment I felt that the world had vanished and the only two people in this space were Baba and I. I gave Him the flowers in His hands, and I just looked at His hands, His beautiful hands.

After that I came back to the U. S. and my life was really turned into complete havoc. All kinds of things happened to me. But the connection was so strong, I never lost that feeling of deep devotion.

I went to India in January of every year. I used to start saving my money to go to India in December. Every time it was with such a feeling that I was going to go see Baba. At the moment I saw Him, something overtook me – they say that is the *kundalini* rising. I don’t know if that’s true, but I had this feeling and I couldn’t stop crying. It was something fantastic. There has not been anybody before or after that made me feel that way. I didn’t cry out of sadness; it was something I couldn’t control.

My experiences with my guru have been very personal. They have reflected my own state of mind, or was it the other way around? By that I mean that Baba came into my life and really opened up my potential, my possibilities of being human. I was very limited before I met Baba because of cultural and other circumstances. After I saw Him in 1982, I had terrible experiences, very painful experiences. Yet, I always tried to understand why. If I had this fantastic guru, why was I going through such misery?

And it took many years for me to understand that what was happening was really the flourishing of my own being. That’s how I understand the relationship with this energy; that’s how I see Baba. I mean we have the photo of this person, but my take on this whole thing is that it’s not a person, it’s a universal energy. And yet He, it, has graced me with His love.

I have always been an intellectual. It seemed that Baba talked to me through the books I read. I was always desperate to find answers. And it seemed I was directed. Well, at first I thought it was my own doing. But now I understand that everything that I did, I was really guided. And so that understanding which came many years after, created in me – I don’t want to sound mystic – but it created a total, total devotion to this energy. I don’t understand it by any means, but this is my experience.

Baba came to me through dreams. One time I was clashed about something. Probably because He knew that I’m a person always in my mind, He came to me and said, “Use your mind.” And I have.

Even though I had that dream, I was still limited. I was very fearful. The feeling came back to me with such strength that I felt I had changed completely. Now I feel very strong, that I have no fear. When I have to take a decision, which was difficult for me in the past, I have the sense of going into my heart, and saying I have this connection with this energy and it’s a very loving one, so what should I do in this situation? I always get a very good accurate answer. So I attribute this to something beyond me.

I came to the U.S. and spoke no English. It was so difficult. In the years I’ve been here I have changed so much. I attribute my growth to my guru. I’ve explored other organizations, the Tibetan Buddhists, the Self-Realization – this is after Baba passed, the Brahma Kumaris – and I never found anywhere the type of vibration that I feel in Ananda Marga. Not to say that AM is paradise, but it’s the feeling that I’ve experienced personally.

I met a dada from India, and he told a story about his being in Sweden. He was in a very difficult situation, and someone was going to kill him. The only thing he remembered was his second lesson, and the man calmed down and nothing happened. That stayed in my mind. In 1984, I was in a situation in which I thought there was no way out. And I remembered that story. I kept repeating my *guru mantra*. And I always do anyway. I think each of us has something favorite of Baba’s teaching. *Guru mantra* is that for me, I’m always saying it. And in that situation I was saved. That’s not the only time. There have been several occasions – I have to attribute it to something protecting me. I was told once by a psychic that he was able to see an energy at my side that always protected me. My surrendering to this energy is really complete. Not to say I haven’t had doubts. Because of the kind of person I am, I’ve had lots of doubts.

When I went to Ananda Nagar, I was experiencing a lot of physical hardship. We got there late so all the rooms were taken. We were given a tent with no roof. It was very cold, and I was suffering tremendously. And in that state I felt a wave of unconditional love. It’s difficult to explain it in words unless you have experienced what I’m talking about. It was like a cloud and I felt that unconditional love.

### Kirit (2)

In 1979, Baba came to Europe, and I saw Him in Germany. There I had my first personal contact with Baba. The first thing Baba asked me was, “You came to India, why did you not ask for PC there in India? Weren’t you there?”

“Yes, Baba I was there.” And I told Him that PA Dada had told me that His health was an issue. After that I had a very nice PC. There was lot of discussion about my life, Baba told so many things, that He had given me new life on that day, and asked many promises, which I gave Him. He asked me to be an ideal person, and to live according to the ideals and teachings. He gave me a lot of blessings, for a long time. Everyone asked why it took so long. Many specific instructions He gave, which I cannot disclose. Many things He told me have come true. Some things are still to come.

Thereafter I went again to India, the January, 1980 DMC in Patna. Baba was no longer in a wheelchair, and He was walking without a stick. His health was coming back. He had black hair, not gray hair. He was turning younger. He was going on field walks, and I had several chances to go on field walk. He talked about geography, the courses of different rivers, so many things, where the river Yamuna used to go when Lord Krishna was there, the geography of hundreds of years before. And how the vibration of the space has changed accordingly. He used to talk about grammar, and the comparative study of languages.

One time I was in the car with Him, and Baba looked at me and said, “Remember, don’t waste a moment of time. You see how I work, I don’t waste a single moment. This life is a short period. Don’t think about material resources. When you do spiritual and ideological things, those resources will be arranged automatically. Move forward with full faith. Other resources will be managed.”

One time we were walking in the streets of Patna. Baba was in a very happy mood. It was like talking with a personal friend. For a moment you forget the fact that He is Baba, guru, the cherished goal of life, you forget everything, because He creates such a lively environment around Him. Everything is so light, you forget all tensions. Baba just hit me on the back, like a friend. As if to say, everything is fine.

After that I went to see Baba for winter and summer DMCs. I spent a lot of time doing sadhana in India, and tried to focus more on sadhana. Many things happened during that time in my personal and spiritual life. I could see a major progress in my sadhana. Every DMC was like a struggle for survival, with a lot of obstacles, yet Baba used to take us through those obstacles. And you could tell that spiritual progress had happened. I could see myself progressing by His grace.

During that time I developed some special relationship in my mind with Baba. This is also described in *Namami Krsna Sundaram*, as supra-aesthetic science. Baba talks about a personal relationship with *Parama Purusa*, the different stages. I could see my mental state developing. Gradually you begin to comprehend His attraction, and all you have read in philosophical books becomes real. I'm talking specifically about attraction, *mohan vijnana*, supra-aesthetic science, and Krsna, the supreme attracting entity. You cannot sustain separation from *Parama Purusa*. You cannot tell how or why this mind is feeling this, how it developed this attraction. It is as if a piece of steel all of a sudden has become a magnet. You cannot tell the difference by looking at it.

It became very hard for me to leave Baba, to come back to the U.S. It was an enormous struggle to leave Baba behind. I was like a child who has to go to school on the first day and wants to stay home with his mother. I used to feel very sad and almost depressed. Two or three days before coming back to the U.S., I would feel so much pain, like I wanted to give up everything and just stay with Baba. I remember Dada Jagadiishvarananda-ji asking me, "Why do you look so depressed?"

I said, "No, Dada, this is how I feel, I don't want to go back, but I have to go back. I cannot tell what it is about Him that is holding me here. When Krsna was there, the *gopis* could say it was the sound of the flute that was holding them there. But Baba was not playing the flute. What is it that was holding me?"

I was like a madman. He started laughing. Like this I used to spend some difficult days.

One time the DMC was in Kolkata, and it was coming to an end. I had to go back to the U.S., and I didn't want to. The Tiljala *jagrti* was complete, but there was no electricity there. This was soon after those dadas and didis were murdered [by Communist partisans]. Taxi drivers were scared and didn't want to drive there at night.

Baba also used to return from field walk very late, at 11 o’clock. We would wait for Him, spend some time with Him, and then go back to Tiljala. They were using the generator for electricity in the evening until 11 o’clock in Tiljala. It was my last day, and the following day I had to fly. Again I felt very depressed. My body would not accept any food. I had Baba’s darshan that night. Somehow we managed to come to Tiljala.

I went to my room, put up the mosquito net, and was doing my night sadhana, when the generator shut off. I could not do much sadhana because I was thinking, “Tomorrow I have to leave.” I became very angry at Baba.

I told Baba, “This is not a just thing; you are doing an injustice. I know you are *Parama Purusa*, I know you give spiritual talks saying you are always with me. OK, it’s fine, you are with me, but I don’t feel I am with you, and this is the difference. I am an insignificant speck of dust in the macrocosm. That you are always with me is consolation for you, not for me. You are my Lord, I am your devotee, this is the relationship. Not that you are macrocosm, I am microcosm. In this relationship why is it I must suffer the agony of my leaving tomorrow, but you will not suffer because you are always with me? This is an injustice. I am upset with you, I don’t want to do your sadhana.”

So, abruptly I stopped the sadhana. With heavy heart, I went to sleep. In the morning I did my morning sadhana, and thought I would go to Baba’s house one more time, then go to airport. As soon as I came in to Baba’s house, PA Dada called me.

“Come here. What happened last night?”

“Nothing, Dada.”

“No, something happened. Last night, at eleven o’clock, Baba was reading all the newsletters from different parts of the world, and all of a sudden He stopped, rang the bell, and called me. He said, ‘How is Kirit? What does Kirit say?’ I told Him, ‘He is fine, he is enjoying DMC.’ Baba laughed and said, ‘No, he is complaining against me. He wants to say something to me.’ ‘No, Baba, he has not said anything.’ ”

Then Baba said to Dada, “Do you know Kirit is leaving for U.S.A. tomorrow?” He laughed, and said, “He has some complaint against me.”

Then I remembered my conversation with Baba. Keshavananda asked me, “What is your complaint?”

“I don’t have any complaint.”

I was waiting, and Baba came out, and walked right up to me, and smiled at me, very nicely and very gently, and said, “You can tell me now. I am willing to listen to your complaint.”

I felt so overcome by emotion, and I said, “No, Baba, I have no complaint.”

Baba said, “Sometimes our mind loses devotion, and we think inappropriately.” But Baba smiled and said, “Don’t worry, you have to go back, but you will be back very soon.” So I also smiled. But little did I know what He meant.

The whole thing is so strange. I am by profession an engineer and we only get two weeks vacation a year, that’s all. And we have such tight schedules, and difficult work. Instead, two or three times a year I was spending so much time in India. It was impossible to think that someone in my profession got that much time. But something always happened. Either I was moved to another area and nobody knew that I hadn’t taken vacation, or I always used to take unpaid time from work, and nobody knew I was taking off. I always got changed from department to department and my bosses never knew I’d been taking vacation. Miraculously I was able to attend all the DMCs in the 1980s. When I look back it seems impossible but Baba always managed the time so I was able to do it. Baba prearranged everything.

One time I was called from India by Baba directly, personally. “Call him,” Baba said. This was the time He was giving *microvita* sadhana. I had just come back from India, but again Baba created such circumstances, that everything fell into place. The funny thing is, after giving His blessing, He smiled and said, “So Kirit, do you think you came or that you were brought here?”

“I was brought here, Baba.” He laughed. So before He tells us to do certain work, He has already completed the work on the psycho-spiritual plane. And it’s just a matter of tightening up, nuts and bolts; someone simply has to become an instrument. But the work has already been accomplished.

Like this, in His *liila*, I learned one thing. In the ’80s, Baba’s speed of work was unimaginable. He would erect a palace in a desert in no time, and remove a desert from a palace also in no time. It was nothing short of a miracle what He led us through. One thing was for sure, that when He gave some work to do, the work was already

accomplished by some force, and you were only filling in the gaps. When Baba tells you to do something, you do it right away. That’s the best time to do the work, with the least amount of energy. Go with full force and do it. Otherwise, as time goes by, the work becomes difficult, and eventually impossible. Of course, if you are not able to accomplish it, it’s His *liila*, something has already been done. His *liila* does what it has to do. But for you, who have the chance to fulfill His work and thereby progress through your *samskara*, you have the choice to take that opportunity to do it or not do it.

Many such experiences I had, and all taught me about His fundamental teachings – sincerity, humility, courage, hard work, love for humanity, sacrifice, service, and devotion and dedication. He personified all these qualities, and taught us about these things.

One time before Tiljala was built, Baba used to come to Jodhpur Park for reporting purposes. There was a limited amount of bathroom space, and no place to stay. You were there seven or ten days and you had to stay in the vicinity to be near Baba, but in Jodhpur Park there was no space. One of the local margiis asked me to stay with him, but unfortunately he lived so far away that when I would come to Jodhpur Park, Baba had already finished His morning darshan. I used to miss it. That went on for two or three days, and I realized that Baba was very unhappy with me. I was still a new margii and just learning about the ways of the spiritual world. I’m still learning, but at that time I did not understand the propriety of my behavior. I was thinking, what happened, what did I do? Maybe nothing, I should just try harder to see Baba. The harder I tried, the more difficult it was to see Him. There were so many people pushing and shoving to see Him, and I could not keep up with it.

I decided to wait downstairs by Baba’s car, and when He came out, then I would be able to see Him closely. I waited, and Baba started coming downstairs. Baba’s driver tried to start Baba’s car, but it would not turn over. It cranked and cranked and did not turn over. The car wouldn’t start. He was scared he would be taken to task for not keeping the car in shape. But guess what? Baba didn’t even go to His car, He climbed into the VSS jeep and left. Then the car started. I was helpless and couldn’t get Baba’s darshan. My blood was frozen, and I became motionless. There could be no greater

evidence that something was wrong. It was His thought, His idea, not mine. None of my tricks would work. Finally I thought about the problem. And from that day on, I took the vow, that when I go to India to see Baba, I will not go anywhere, not see anything, I will always be near Baba, and spend my time in sadhana. This was the vow I took. I broke that vow one time, but I asked Baba’s permission to go. There was one family that had given three children for wholetimers. They had some crisis. So I traveled to meet with them, and that was the only time I was away from Baba. So Baba teaches so many things, sometimes in a harsh way that we will never forget.

One time the plant program was going on. I used to bring hundreds and hundreds of plants. Baba loved orchids, and there was an orchid farm in San Francisco. I used to take a lot of orchids. One time – winter DMC – I thought I would not take any plants. Since Baba would be in Ananda Nagar where it was cold, I would bring a nice soft warm blanket. I learned that the best wool comes from Ireland. I found one blanket in the Neiman-Marcus store. They said it was the best, very lightweight and warm. I purchased it and went to India. Normally Baba is very happy to see the devotees coming. All the dadas have to give good news to Baba, and when I used to visit, it was considered good news. But that time, when Baba got the news, Baba didn’t say anything. Not only that, when Baba came down, I was standing to see Him, and He looked at me and didn’t say anything. He bent His head and went like this [shakes head back and forth]. And I knew what He was saying.

It was one of the most painful days in my life. You feel like your heart has broken into a hundred pieces, and you would prefer death over life. I had the desire to completely vanish into the earth. I didn’t know why Baba was angry, but it was obvious He was disapproving. I saw a couple of dadas, Dada Shamitananda and others, outside the gate. Baba had sent them out because they had not made their quota for the plants. I realized, “Oh, they also are being punished, and I am being punished. What do I do? OK, why not go to some nursery in Kolkata and bring some plants? But no, it was not just about simply replacing the plant, but about a mistake I made. By doing that I would not be rectifying my mistake. What was the process that caused my mistake? Why did I make

this mistake? *Parama Purusa* created every leaf on every plant. Who am I to bring Him what plant? What was it that went on here?”

So I thought and thought. Yes, it was related to plants. Why did I not bring plants, and brought this blanket? It was a subtle expression of ego operating. A lack of surrender. When we do something for Him *we* think is the best thing, we are being guided by our thought process. As opposed to our thought process being what He wants, what He expects, and acting accordingly. So that was my mistake, I did not allow my mind to be guided by what He wanted. It was a subtle expression of ego. This I learned on that day. That’s the reason I did not go to a nursery in Kolkata and pick up a few plants. *Parama Purusa* cannot accept any ego. I had to go through some difficulties, strive to correct myself. So I asked where the best plants were? I was told Nepal. I thought, “OK, I will go to the mountains there, I will climb those mountains and find the plants myself. I will not buy plants, paying my way out; I will pay with my labor.”

I flew to Kathmandu. I went to the training center, and the dada there said he would accompany me. It was so cold there, and I did not have a blanket. Next morning, Dada and I started climbing the mountain, and we collected a big bag full of plants. Meanwhile there were some difficulties on the way, with my visa back to India. Somehow the Nepal consulate on a holiday miraculously was open and I got approved. I came back and deposited the plants with Dada Asiimananda-ji. I distributed bags to the dadas outside to fulfill their quota. I submitted my plants also. Next morning Baba was smiling so much, and He was so happy to see me again. What we should think is what He wants, what He would like, and conduct ourselves accordingly.

### Rekha
*Oregon*

I have resisted sharing my stories over the years, feeling somehow that mine were insignificant compared with the more directly powerful tales of senior margiis and *acaryas*. Over the years, however, like all our deep learnings, my experiences with Baba have deepened and, to me, become more available and thus significant. Truly any words concerning our Lord can spark beauty and significance without warning!

I was an “instant” margii after attending Arjuna’s class in Pasadena in September, 1983. Everything about Ananda Marga’s practices felt “right,” despite the fact I wasn’t even aware of being on a spiritual search. Baba’s grace is truly amazing in the way it surrounds and enfolds. I was about to turn 30 and the first thing I wanted to do after beginning my sadhana practice was to study physics, thus satisfying strong internal questions I had. Devotion to Guru eluded me, as I was a Western intellectual – yet I felt strong yearnings in my heart for… something. My initial reason for taking the meditation class had been to solve health problems, and within two weeks my health not only improved, but I felt my body transform – almost as if I was beginning another puberty.

One night during dinner following *dharmacakra*, a didi mentioned she was leaving for India soon. I remarked politely how wonderful this was, wishing her a great trip. She looked deeply towards me and said, “When will you be going?”

I felt a jolt of recognition, and great surprise. In July when Arjuna described his plans to go in August, I quickly swung into action. Miraculously, passport and visa applications all came together smoothly. There was no question but that I should go directly to Baba. This is how it came that I spent three and a half weeks at His lotus feet in August, 1984.

My daily schedule was pretty regular: waking in the sisters’ *jagrti* at Tiljala, traveling with two or three other foreign margii sisters to Lake Gardens, listening to Baba’s darshans (sometimes twice a day), singing lots of *Prabhat Samgiit* (often to Baba), returning to Tiljala in the evening. I often played my flute as others sang *Prabhat Samgiit* to Baba. He looked sweetly toward me several times as I played. Since He was

composing six or seven new ones each day, there was much excitement when someone came out of Baba’s house with new melodies and lyrics.

Being such a new margii, I spent a lot of time talking with and observing other margiis and *acaryas*. It was August and “off season,” there were no more than 20 or so foreign visitors. I explored the idea of guru, watching Baba, hoping for a “sign.” One Sunday, during a darshan packed with people on an extremely hot day, I relaxed into a hazy dream state. Baba, who was speaking Bengali, began speaking English to me in my mind. I couldn’t quite follow His points, but I was tremendously impressed.

On August 15, two thousand sisters prepared at Tiljala for a Proutist demonstration march in Calcutta. They were making ten demands for women’s rights. We foreigners were told to stay at the *jagrti* and not participate. Baba was so impressed with the women’s efforts that He gave a special darshan the next day. His subject was “The History of Women.” I recall that He gave the talk at least partly in English. Afterward, without warning, He gave the *varabhaya mudra* and many people in that small gathering room fell on the floor. I felt a sudden cramp in my stomach and had to race out of the room to vomit outside. What a shock! I was ill for the next three days.

Toward the end of my visit Baba composed *Prabhat Samgiit* number 3000. He celebrated this by ordering *rasagullas* for all of us in the garden. Up until that point I hadn’t really enjoyed Indian sweets, finding them, well, just too sweet! But here was a sweet dessert sent to me by Baba, so of course I savored every bite. And to my complete surprise, I felt myself slowly transform into a flower! Every detail was clear, but especially the taste of the incredible nectar! What a lovely gift.

Over the years, with retelling and resavoring my experiences in India, I have come to cherish and delight in that special time with Baba. I never returned. However, I did finally “get” the concept and joy of guru, after five or six years of margii life. After Baba’s passing His presence became quite strong during my *dhyana* practices. Twice I have had experiences, seemingly out of the blue, with fragrance. One day, during an ordinary workday at my job, I smelled lilacs the entire day. It wasn’t the right season, only Baba’s grace. Another time it was sandalwood. I feel Baba sends reminders, and holds me always.

### Dhyanesh (2)

I went to Hong Kong Sector and worked there for a year as a Local Full-Timer (LFT), as Seva Dharma Mission (SDM) Sectorial Office Secretary in Kyoto. I was studying the Japanese meditation gardens, thinking about my future career as a landscape architect. And looking for some good sister to marry.

After one year in Hong Kong Sector I went to *Dharma Maha Cakra* in Ranchi in 1984. The second I saw Yamuna from a distance, I felt strong love for her. Not her beauty, just the way she stood. I felt something. But we were talking, and she was hiding, so shy, and I was talking only with her father, and my mind would not listen to that first intuition.

And I was thinking, “This is insane. I live on the other side of the planet, and they live in some remote village in the countryside.” So I said, “After DMC I’m going to do some traveling, and maybe I’ll come visit you.” But in my mind, foolishly, I was thinking I would not come back.

It was a wonderful DMC, and afterwards I went to visit Bhagalpur to design a Master Unit and a mango orchard. I also designed a Baba’s quarters there. When it was completed, Baba stayed there, and I felt very honored.

I was staying with one family *acarya*, Dada Pashupati, who reviewed my lessons for many years. Yamuna’s father happened to come at the same time I was there. So I took it as a sign that I would go back with them to visit Yamuna. I felt so much love in the family – really I fell in love with the family – more love than I’d ever felt in my own country, but I was still...

Anyway, I cornered Yamuna. I was so cautious and reticent about commitment, like so many American boys are. I said, “If I come back from Hong Kong Sector, do you want to marry me?”

And she did not answer. In her mind she was saying, “This is my father’s decision.” But finally, she looked up and said, “Yes.” And my heart was so happy. This was another sign. Actually she thought I asked her to marry, but still I had some escape clause there [laughs].

So I went back to see Dada Pashupati, and asked him, “Dada, you’re my *acarya*, should I marry this girl?” And he had the habit of always going into deep sadhana when

he reviewed my lessons. And this time, he came out of his sadhana and he said, “Yes, you should take a *tantric* stance in your life. You should invite struggle in your life. You should be a shining example to society!”

And I felt the *tantric* feeling so much, and I said, “Yes, I will do it!”

I sent a telegram, “Please meet me in Kolkata and we’ll marry, if it’s to your liking, after I get back from Hong Kong Sector.” And we met and got married in an Ananda Marga school in Kolkata. There was a flood. I was so happy, even with all the clash, the flood, and the *acaryas* who were going to marry us, saying, “You’re an LFT, you can’t marry.”

Lots of clash, but during the marriage I felt so much of Baba’s bliss. Then I had to come back, I had one week left on my plane ticket. We decided we were not going to be together until Baba blessed our marriage. We got married on June 24th, and my ticket was for July 2nd. Every day in Lake Gardens, we would petition the Personal Assistant and the other dadas to please say something to Baba about blessing our marriage before I had to leave. On Saturday, the P.A., Dada Ramananda, got angry with me.

“Ohh, why do you keep asking this question? Baba never blesses marriages outside of DMC. Just forget it, go on!”

Oh, we were so dejected then. We felt pretty bad. That night when Baba got back from field walk – this is a beautiful Baba story – we all sat around Him, and they started playing this beautiful *Prabhat Samgiit*. And Yamuna was feeling very feverish, sick, and in the middle of the song, she stood up, and tried to get out of the crowd, and she passed out. And I was looking at her, and then looked at Baba, and Baba looked at me, and with His mind, said to me, “I am doing something.”

Then the music stopped, and Baba said, “And how does Dhyanesh like this song?”

“It’s beautiful, Baba.”

“So it is Dhyanesh’s decided opinion that this song should be included in *Prabhat Samgiit*?”

“Yes, Baba.”

The second He called my name, Yamuna sat up, and heard that. Then we left. The next day was darshan. We were all waiting, crowded in there. And He called me and

Yamuna up, and took our hands and He blessed our wedding. Not in DMC. It was very special. And He looked at Yamuna almost the whole time. “Be like Shiva and Parvati. Be an example to society.”

Later He said He was very pleased with our marriage. Because she comes from a very strict caste, a very sentient caste. They observe *ekadashi* [fasting], and sentient diet. They never marry outside their caste. Not only did she marry outside her caste, she married some boy from America. So it took a lot of courage.

Then I had to go back to America. I had been an LFT for three years. I had to get a job, a house, all these things. After three months I received a telegram that she was pregnant – we had been together one night – and I came back to India to get her. During that time she had been ostracized by some of her extended family, and the village. They were cruel to her, they shunned her. “Oh, you married an American, all Americans get divorced, he only wants to use you.” Mean things they would say to her.

We had a difficult time getting her passport, because she was from a known margii family. There was a Member of Parliament who was a member of Ananda Marga who helped arrange the passport. I brought her back to the U.S., where it was so difficult for her. You know, we have such a lonely society here – air-conditioned house, air-conditioned car, the streets are empty. And in her village, everybody knows everybody, and the streets are always full of people. She cried so much, it was so hard for her. She never wanted me to see that. But we believe Baba took some *samskara* from Yamuna that evening, so that she could adapt, adjust, to her new life. I could not adjust to India, I always get sick, and also it would have been difficult for employment. I’m the weaker of the two.

And when our first child was born, we always felt she came from Baba. She was born very alert, and she did not cry. They were measuring her, and she reached and grabbed the tape. She was born very conscious. That was very special.

After some years I had the opportunity for Personal Contact.

It was in Lake Gardens, and I had to wait a long time, many days. I fasted, and waited, and every day it was not my time, not my time. And I saw other Western margiis

going directly in and coming out. This was part of my purification process, I’m sure. Finally it was my time.

Most of it was very personal. He told me many things about my past lives. And He made me promise many things, to serve society. Then He called me closer, and I was hugging Baba, and I was crying. I’m normally a very rational, intellectual person. But by His Grace, He balanced my mind so nicely with devotion.

Baba said to me, “Dhyanesh, you should do one more thing.”

Our faces were close together. “Yes, Baba?”

“You should laugh.”

And the two of us started laughing together. I mean really laughing. It was so blissful, and everything fell away from inside me, as if nothing mattered any more. All the fears, the clash, it was all His play, all His drama, and everything was happening according to His will. There was a moment when our two laughters became one. This was the bliss. And to this day, in my sadhana, I feel these stages of sentiments as the stories relate. You must have a strong, but surrendered mind. It seems paradoxical. A strong determined mind to stay focused, but a surrendered mind, that surrenders over and over with each *mantra*. This is the beginning stage of sadhana. In the next stage, I feel a loving proximity with Him. And then, if it is the time He wants for you, you feel a union, and go into Him, and this is the best joy.

### Suprabhata

*Longmont, Colorado*

I was initiated in Carbondale, Illinois, by Dada Jiitendra. I’ve been a margii for 28 years (2001).

I went to India in 1988. It was the second time I had gone. One dada came to my house in Iowa where I was living. He was raising money for Ananda Nagar. I didn’t really have any money; I had just finished paying all my bills, and my bank balance was zero.

And I thought, “Well, something will happen and I’ll have enough money to buy food until I get paid.” Then Dada came and said, “We really need money for Ananda Nagar.” I really wanted to do something so I gave him a check for $100.

After he left, I got a check in the mail for $100 for an income tax refund. The Internal Revenue Service said I had paid too much money on my tax return two years ago. That’s never heard of, you know. A couple days later I got $100 from a company which was a reimbursement for medical expenses. And then a couple of days later I got a check from my mother, who never sent me money. She sent $100.

“This is for you, Steve, for being a nice boy,” she wrote [laughs]. So then I had $300 in the bank, enough for whatever I needed.

I went to the summer retreat a month later at Ananda Kanan, and they were giving a big talk about how Ananda Nagar needed money. So I told this story to everyone, thinking that might help the situation. At the end of the story, I said, “And then they paid me $100 to come here to tell this story!” And everyone laughed. But that night they got pledges for $50,000 for Ananda Nagar.

Later in the retreat, they were trying to find someone to go to India for reporting. They were doing a rotation, one person for each region. It was my region’s turn. Dada Devashraddhananda was pushing me to go, because I was a *Bhukti Pradhan*. I didn’t think I could do it – I didn’t have the money, there wasn’t enough time to give notice at my job, all these things. But I decided that if I could get the money, I would try to go.

So when I went back to Iowa, I asked permission from my boss to go, and he said, “No problem.” I went to the bank and tried to borrow money – I didn’t have any assets,

but I had a good record at the bank. I asked to borrow $2000, and they agreed. My first daughter was living with me, she was 10 or 11, and I needed someone to watch her while I was gone. I asked a friend, and they said sure. Then I needed to get my name changed because the previous time I had been to India I had been blacklisted. I had only a few days to do that, but had to fly to Denver, so I did and got my name changed. At this point there were two days left.

In Denver, I went to the sisters’ *jagrti*. One didi there was scheduled to take all the money for Ananda Nagar to India. She couldn’t go, and needed someone to take it. I agreed, and she gave me a little pouch with $25,000 in cash, which I wore around my neck.

I flew to New York and applied for my visa and passport. It was the last day. The person at the Indian embassy said, “I see that you just changed your name two days ago. In order to get a visa, you need two weeks where you advertised that you changed your name.”

I said, “Well, I would, but I have a ticket to go tomorrow, and I don’t have time.”

They said, “This is against the policy and I’m sure we can’t.”

I replied, “Well, at least ask your supervisor.”

And she went to ask. Up to this point, I hadn’t been feeling strongly that I needed to go to India. I was going because Dada asked me to. But now I started thinking, “Baba, I really want to come.” My attitude changed. She came back to the counter and said, “Well, we never do this, but for some reason the supervisor says you can have your visa.”

In Kolkata, people were waiting for reporting. It was 110 degrees, and we stood outside the door in Tiljala. Day after day we waited. After four or five days, Baba called me in for reporting with several others. I was trying to think of what I could say in reporting, I hadn’t really been doing anything. Baba only asked three people to stand up and say their name, and He would explain what their name meant. He never called me, but it was a great experience watching Him, and hearing Him talk.

A few days later was the ten-year anniversary of His coming out of jail. He gave a talk, and at the end, He let everyone do *guru puja* and then gave His *varabhaya mudra*. A lot of screams and people falling over. It was a beautiful experience.

When I got back to Iowa, the first day at work I checked my mailbox and there was a check in there for $2001! The company had made more profits than they expected, so they divided the profits proportionally among the employees. It was the first time they had done that, and they haven’t done it since. I had been worried about how I would pay back the $2000 to the bank. All my debts were paid, and I had a beautiful experience.

### Keshava

*Los Angeles, California*

I was initiated in 1985, by Dada Ratneshvarananda. When I first got initiated, I very much wanted to see Baba. Each Christmas I tried to go, and for two years, I got really clashed out because I always had some big problem happen where I wasn’t able to go. I’m an actor and I had this pilot for a comedic series I’d had a terrible experience on, and then I booked this film, “Memphis Belles,” a big film, the biggest film in my career to date. And I couldn’t do both because I had this contract to do this television show. And I remember praying to Baba that if I could get out of the one and get to London for the film, I’d be so much closer to India, that I’d just go to India and pay my namaskar, my respects, and that I wasn’t going to come to get anything. I realized that’s what kept me from making it the other two times, that I had wanted to get something.

And at that moment, in my meditation, I felt a wind blow through the house, like the winds of change, and I knew the series was not going to happen, and that I was going to go do this movie. And I did do the movie, which was an incredible experience, and I went to India. I didn’t have Personal Contact, because I didn’t have all the requirements they had then, in 1988.

But I was very fortunate to get to see Baba give darshan. I remember being in the darshan hall, and hearing His voice, He was having a conversation with someone, in a reporting meeting, like, “Ahhh? Ahhhh.” This went on for a while, it sounded like someone was going to be scolded, and then that He was happy with the progress.

Then He came. As an actor I watch human beings; I was watching Him carefully. He was moving so slowly, and subtly. He seemed like He was old, kind of, but I watched Him closely, and for some reason sometimes He would move faster than the eye could see almost, and then move slowly again. I was in the presence of such a subtle human being, *Parama Purusa* possibly.

I see energy around people; I’ve been around a lot of celebrities who have huge presences, and you can see it around them. My experience of Baba was like none I’ve ever seen before; it was coming off of Him in waves, across the whole room. He only spoke Bengali, and I didn’t understand a single thing He said, but He was so charming,

and so funny, everyone was laughing, just delighted with Him at all times. So even though I didn’t understand a word, it was a completely blissful experience. After He left, we meditated, and as soon as I closed my eyes, immediately I was in white light, through my third eye *cakra*. I was completely full of white light, which I guess is what Baba was emanating to us the whole time. And I understood why people just wanted to be in His presence all the time; you were just so full of bliss when you were around Him.

Shortly after He passed away, my fiancé Jayashrii made me a *pratik*. I heard that Baba was supposed to bless the *pratiks*. And He came to me in a dream, and took the *pratik* in His hands, and He blessed it. I woke up in tears, the dream was so… it was more than a dream. He took care of it, His last business with me was to make sure I was taken care of. It was such bliss.

### Gaunga

*Toronto, Canada*

I was living in Kolkata for several months following the New Year’s *Dharma Maha Cakra* of 1988. Away from the responsibilities and hectic pace of Western life, in the atmosphere of sadhana, service and fraternity, I used those times as an opportunity to throw myself wholeheartedly into sadhana and cutting the bondages of the selfish and the mundane. In those days I was doing long meditations, deeply and sincerely, and was strictly following all my practices. Every day, I tried to think if there were any of His practices or suggestions that I had overlooked.

Each day I went in a taxi with a group of sisters to Baba’s house in Lake Gardens. Each night we came back together to the sisters’ quarters in Tiljala. One day, as our taxi was passing through a narrow market street, I noticed some loofahs hanging up in one of the many small shops. I saw some more in another shop and it seemed that most of the shops had them. Suddenly it came to me that Baba had once said that we should use the loofah when we take our bath because it removes many toxins from the skin that come out in the perspiration. I said to myself that I must buy one, but as it was not practical to stop the taxi at that moment, I would get one when I had the chance. Many days passed like this, and I had still not bought the loofah.

One evening there was to be a *Prabhat Samgiit* program at the large hall in the brothers’ compound in Tiljala. I had arrived on time, but the program was not yet ready to begin. Many were rushing to and fro with a lot of people milling about, inside and out. I was standing in the colonnade outside the entrance doors, enjoying the cool air, when I noticed an Indian man smiling at me. Often, strange men would approach me; usually they would ask if I could arrange a job for them in Canada. As he approached, I noticed that he had something in his hand. He stood very close, and began peeling this thing, which I could see was a loofah. He took my hand and started shaking the seeds into it. He said, “Take these back to your country and plant them.”

I had a habit of collecting seeds. I just stood there, incredulous, looking at the seeds in my hand. He then placed the loofah in my hand and stepped back. He smiled and

said, “Use this when you take your bath,” and with a twinkle in his eye, added “and you will have beautiful skin.”

I noticed that his own cheeks had acne scars. Within seconds, he turned and stepped into the hall. Suddenly the realization hit me. What stranger would give me such a gift? I stepped into the hall, but he was nowhere in sight. I had become familiar with the Indian margiis who regularly attended Baba’s programs, but I had never seen that man before, nor have I seen him since. He was very average, very ordinary in appearance.

Several years ago, Dada Sujit Kumar, a family *acarya* who was close to Baba since the early days, described how Baba had once pointed out a man doing namaskar from the back of a crowded train platform at a stop, as Dada accompanied Baba by train to some place for DMC. Dada was intrigued as Baba identified him as one of the “other” margiis, who were rarely seen, but who were highly evolved spiritually, and in whom all the occult powers were fully developed. Dada complained that he and the margiis close to Him were working so hard for His mission, and that these margiis should also be helping them to do this important work. Baba explained that He gives them their duties also, and that they are intuitionally tuned to hear His directions.

Through the medium of so many forms, He is fulfilling His initiation promise of taking care for the devotee’s progress. No one takes so much care as He does.
