It has often been said that in our spiritual life, He will coax us on, like a mother encourages her baby to walk to her. To those who take even a small step toward Him, He will take ten steps toward them and will scoop them up in His loving arms.

### Kamalanayan

*Portland, Oregon*

In late December 1987, shortly after arriving at Ananda Nagar for the first time, I was invited to visit the director of one of the schools there; he had some questions relevant to my profession. His office was upstairs in Baba's House, adjacent to the large room where Baba would hold meetings such as reporting sessions with workers and margiis. We were having a very pleasant visit, half-business in response to his technical questions, and half-tourism because of my curiosity about his life at Ananda Nagar, the projects, etc.

Suddenly, a call echoed through the large room outside Dada's office: Baba was arriving in that room to take His indoor walk, and was not to be disturbed nor confronted by anyone. All the doors opening into the big room were to be closed. Dada jumped up and shut the only door to his room. Now we were closed in his tiny office, and Baba was in the next room. By his casual manner I estimated that this was a normal situation for Dada, and I looked silently at him for some hint as to how to act: was it OK to talk, to move, to think normal thoughts? I closed my eyes for a moment. Meanwhile, I heard Dada get up and go to the door again.

When I opened my eyes, he had the door ajar about two inches and was peeking out, crouched a little. He gestured vigorously, summoning me to his side, then stepped away as I arrived and put me in his place. As I brought my eyes into viewing position, I saw the orange robes of an *acarya* only a few feet from me, then Baba was there, moving left to right across that little opening. Had I been more crazed with devotion I might have thrown open the door and grasped my Master's feet with one leap, He was so close. But I stood there, the passage of time seeming to slow down as I saw first His right shoulder, then His cheek, His ear, hair, the back of His head go past. He was walking along slowly and steadily, a cane in one hand, His other hand resting on the arm of the *avadhuta* who walked protectively at the side of his Guru.

Everything important a few minutes earlier was forgotten: Dada's questions, my travels, my hopes. This glimpse of the guru I had thought about for seventeen years seemed to fill my awareness, leaving nothing else to think, to see, to feel. This seemed

enough. I had the feeling, not easy to defend logically, that this was why Dada had called me to his office. I looked again at Baba’s back as He advanced carefully around the circular room. How grateful I was for this opportunity, so simple, so personal it seemed. I felt that Baba knew I was looking. Maybe in some way he had arranged this for me. Externally I had no special status among the ten thousand margiis who surrounded His house that day; I had earned no privilege. Internally I was one of his very own, near and dear. I was thinking like a child, like a devotee.

Now, many years have passed. The images seen through that barely-opened door are among my treasures. I keep them safe in my memory. They do not tarnish. Is this event significant? Imagine a two-inch fragment of mirror directly reflecting the sun at noon on a clear summer day. Is the light intense? Can the memory of a moment’s gaze at Baba illuminate the mind of one who seeks to know Him? Can it touch the heart of one who loves Him?

He knows.

### Dr. Ram (3)

This story is about my experience going to Kolkata for *microvita* sadhana. I did not know what it was really, but I was told I should go. There were several margiis from the States in Lake Gardens. I was there only for a few days and had to come back to Delaware. About a month later, there was a call from Kolkata asking me to come immediately, because Baba wanted to give special sadhana. My wife was pregnant with our third child, and expecting the baby any time, and she was quite upset that I was going to leave. It took a long time to persuade her that everything would be OK, that Baba would take care of it. So finally she gave me permission to leave, and I was going to go for a quick trip.

I was told I would see Baba the next day at 8:30 in the morning. I was the first person to see Baba that morning. *Microvita* sadhana, or sometimes called *shakti sampat sadhana*, was given to a few people, who had to meet certain criteria: within a certain age group, in good physical health, and doing all lessons. I understood that people were supposed to apply for it. I didn't know that and hadn't applied. Apparently Baba picked my name and asked General Secretary (GS) about it. GS later held my hand and said, "Baba loves you very much, you don't love Baba enough" [laughs]. I was trying to love Baba, but His love is overwhelming.

I was alone with Baba for two hours. Part of that time was missing, an hour or so. Then Baba told me that I received *anandam*, which I understand as a form of *samadhi*. That day in the hotel room, I was not able to do anything, only constantly say, "Baba Baba" nonstop. Some *acarya* told me that Baba commented on it, that Ram is saying Baba nonstop and could not hold back. There was that effect from the special sadhana. I don't know why I did that, it was only His grace. So I came back to the States, and within a week my wife delivered our third child. There was no problem. Baba was taking care of everyone.

Let me tell about my experience with Baba the last time I saw Him, in Dec. 1989. We had planned a trip to India to see our parents, so the whole family went to India via Bombay. We had a short vacation, but we were fortunate that on the first day in Kolkata

Baba was giving a darshan. This was the continuing Sunday program when Baba talked about *Shabda Cayanika*, the letters. I think He was talking about the letter ‘ka.’ The darshan hall was packed with 200 people. And Baba appeared very healthy and in good spirits. He was smiling and very humorous, making everyone laugh very much. And He talked without any fatigue for two and a half hours. The next day we took a flight to South India – we had to change planes twice and reached home in evening. When I arrived I was told that GS called from Kolkata, and there were some calls from Bangalore by the regional *acaryas*. I called Bangalore and Dada told me that Baba had been admitted to the nursing home in Kolkata. A nursing home in India is like a hospital, with intensive care. GS wanted me to come back to Kolkata right away. Dr. Ramesh was attending Baba, and functioned as a liaison between the nursing home doctors and the dadas. They wanted me to relieve Dr. Ramesh.

I took my wife and children to her home in a nearby village. The local airline was on strike, so my only chance was to take a night bus. It was 10 to 12 hours to Bangalore. Fortunately I got a plane ticket to Kolkata. I didn’t know what my parents thought because I was seeing them after four years and suddenly I had to leave. But they did not object to my leaving. Dada Santosananda-ji met me and told me not to worry about anything, that it was simply Baba’s *liila*. He was on his way to New Delhi. I’m saying this because all along, throughout the years, there was conflicting opinion on how to care for Baba. And some would say we should not pressure Baba to follow any points about diet, medical care and so forth, that He was all-powerful. Years ago I had written letters to PA, because I had seen Baba’s electrocardiogram, and there were some problems with it. And several instructions concerning diet were really not paid attention to, because of the same feeling, perhaps.

Anyway, I was at the nursing home morning to evening, for four or five days. And Dr. Wolden was the cardiologist who was taking care of Baba. Baba was admitted for high blood pressure and out of control diabetes, and a heart problem. And Baba was not taking rest even in the hospital, probably because He wanted to meet with people and all that. He then was heavily sedated, so that He was resting. I’m a cardiologist also, and I talked to Dr. Wolden on a daily basis. One day an echocardiogram was done. I had access to Baba’s records, and what I saw from all those, even though Baba had heart problems

and other physical problems, if given the proper attention and care, He could have lived another twenty years or so. That is our experience with some patients here in the U.S. who have cardiac problems. We did try to bring Baba to the U.S. for some treatment, but of course Baba refused. And I heard that Baba would not follow instructions at least some of the time, and would get angry when people insisted. On the fourth day, things were reasonably under control. I noted certain follow-up tests for Baba through PA and I understood Baba did not want to go through all of those things. So PA told me I could go back to South India because Baba would be leaving the hospital soon.

And one old margii, a businessman, he was like a millionaire, he knew a lot of people, went with me to the airline office and talked with several people but there was no way I could get a ticket. It was Christmastime, tickets were booked until mid-January, and I didn't have much leave. By train or bus it would take me three days to reach. So I was kind of disappointed. Then this brother Rate-ji took me to all the sweet shops in Kolkata. Next day however I was sick, with fever and headache, but I knew I had to leave somehow. Miraculously, my ticket arrived, someone brought a ticket for me. It was Baba's Grace only, because there were 150 people on the wait list, and they even said there was absolutely no way.

So then I wanted to take Baba's permission to leave, but Baba was sleeping, sedated. I didn't want to disturb Baba, but saw a little opening. Somehow the room door opened fully, and PA was inside, and it was as if I fell inside the room. I quietly did my *sastaung pranam* next to the cot. PA said something, and Baba was fully alert. He chanted something and I understood this was a blessing, and Dada Keshavananda told me later, you had something like PC that day. So I told Baba I was leaving, and Baba gave permission. I came out and had no fever, headache, nothing, all fresh, ready to go. Baba was supposed to be the patient lying in bed, but you didn't know who was doctoring whom.

### Mahajyoti

*Los Angeles, California*

I became a margii in 1970 in the Philippines. My acarya is Didi Ananda Mitra. My husband Phanindra encouraged me, but I didn't really want to be a margii. It was so hard to be vegetarian. He continued going to the *jagrti* that time. Many people were attending there. One day I became curious, and said, "I'll go and see, and if I like it I'll take initiation."

Phanindra said, "You cannot go unless you get initiated." And I said, "No way."

Anyway I went, and got inspired, and went to Didi Mitra's *jagrti*. She gave me first lesson. The experience I had was very beautiful; I felt elevated off the floor. What is this? I felt like I was floating. I told Phanindra about it, and he could not explain why. It was a very good feeling, but never happened again.

In 1989 we decided to visit Baba in India; that was my first time. But unfortunately He was in the hospital. It was frustrating for me. We waited a week to see if He would come out, but He did not. There was instruction that everyone was to go to Ananda Nagar for *Dharma Maha Sammelan*, because He might go there. Our flight back to the U.S. was on Jan. 3rd. We arrived back to Tiljala on Jan. 3rd in the morning, and found out that Baba had come out on New Year's Day. The only time I could see Him was that day, because our flight was in the afternoon, but they said He was not coming out. I wanted to see Him so much.

Then they announced that Baba was coming at 10 that morning, but they said no kiirtan, everybody should be quiet. He needed to walk around in a quiet and peaceful environment. So everyone stood at the fence. I had my five-year-old daughter Liila with me. I put her on top of the fence, but they said no one should be sitting on the fence. But she had made friends with the VSS guards, so they let her stay. And I also was able to stay there, close, to see Baba.

When Baba came out of the house, He did a very long namaskar. That namaskar was very inspiring. Every time I have a hard time in my meditation I just think of that namaskar, and the nice feeling, and I get back in the flow again.

Anyway, He came out of the house with his son Kinshu, and walked around. We were told not to make any noise at all. But since Liila was a handful and talkative at that age, she just continued talking. I said, “Liila, we were told not to talk, just think of Baba.” But she would not. But the minute Baba came right in front of us, she stopped talking, and did namaskar to Baba. Again when Baba went in the house, He gave a very long namaskar on all sides, and went in. Then I asked Liila, “Why did you stop talking when Baba was just in front of us?”

She said, “I just thought to myself, I love you Baba, that’s what I was thinking inside me.”

After He passed away, I had a nice vivid dream. I saw Baba in front of the *puja* table, cleaning the *puja* table, dusting it, taking off the cloth and shaking it out. Liila and Phanindra were talking to Him. That time I was very sick. I had a severe chronic cough. In the dream, Baba asked me, “Mahajyoti, how do you feel, are you still coughing?”

“No, Baba, I am feeling better.”

And He continued cleaning. And Liila had this lungi given to Phanindra by an *acarya*. She jumped on Baba’s lap, and said, “Look, my molar is coming out,” and Baba held her like a baby. Baba started to feel the molar coming out. Then He said to Phanindra, “Do you still want to change Liila’s name?” because we had thought about changing her name.

“No, it’s OK.”

I said, “But Baba, she’s very slow in learning in preschool.”

And Baba made a gesture like, don’t worry, she’ll catch up.

After Baba finished cleaning, there was a pot of roses, and one branch was broken, and Phanindra was going to remove it. Baba said, “No, this is how you do it.” Baba planted the branch, and as soon as He stuck it in the foil, like magic, red roses came up. I whispered to Phanindra, “Are you going to bring Baba to your office, and give him a denture?” He didn’t say anything. After that, Baba stood up, we did namaskar to Him, He went into a dark room, and then I woke up. I had thought it was real. This dream was very nice for me, and I cannot forget it.

### Phanindra

*Los Angeles, California*

I found Ananda Marga, or rather AM found me about 1972 or ’73. I feel it was not an accident. At the time I was searching for something, but could not find it. My place was very close to the AM *jagrti* in Paco, about ten minutes ride away. I always passed that place. I met one *acarya* there. He was a very good man, Dada Adveshananda. I learned *nama mantra* from him.

I didn’t really understand what AM was all about. In my first encounter with him, he told me to bring all the people around my area, then come back and he would give me another lesson. I didn’t have money for my fare; I was in total hardship. I thought, “How am I going to bring all the people around me?” We lived in a hard-pressed area. So I was not able to do it. But I still came to Paco.

One time there was a retreat and I really wanted to go. I was very excited to go, but I had no experience of what it was. There was one *acarya* (at DC) who asked who will go to the retreat. I raised my hand. He looked at me and said, “Do you meditate twice a day, for at least 30 minutes?”

I wasn’t doing that, but I had this buddy with me, and he hit me in the back with his elbow, “Just say yes.” I said yes. But this *acarya* looked me in the eye, and I felt he had X-ray vision, and he was just looking internally at me. And I couldn’t say anything, but just stayed quiet and dumbfounded.

“No, no,” he said, “you are not meditating for 30 minutes a day, twice a day.” I was shocked, and thought I should not go to the retreat. I went home, and I was crying in the jeepney. I felt so bad. I had really wanted to go. That was my first clash with AM. I did not go back. But my seeking was still strong, that longing for something. I started looking around for other practices. There was one place where some old folks were doing some practice. It was kind of a Biblical yogic practice. I did it very sincerely. I was in college at that time.

When I finished my studies, I practiced, giving it more time than my studies. I had this longing within me. And I had some experience I could not understand or explain, like being out of the world. Every morning when I woke up, I saw this person before me,

wearing white. Every morning I saw this person, and the experience was becoming stronger and stronger. One day I had a desire to go back to AM, even though the clash was still in my mind. And I attended classes for beginners at the *jagrti*. In the evening I would see a person in orange standing by the stairs, looking at me. Why is he always looking at me, I thought. The third night we were asked who would like to learn meditation. I was directed to come to that *acarya*. He initiated me. I had no idea that I had already been initiated by Dada Adveshanada.

Next morning, I woke up and saw the same person, but this time there was a face to the person – before there had been no face – and it was the face of my guru, of Baba. And all the difficulties I had in meditating, one by one they disappeared.

Years later I went to India. I had read many spiritual stories. I knew that Baba as guru could know and see everything, but sometimes the mind has doubt. In one darshan I was at the back of the hall. I knew the story of the man who was asking for a boon from the Lord, that he not die. He would remember God during the day and night, but unfortunately not at evening.

I had this desire to have something from Him. I said, “Please let me not forget you at daytime, nighttime, or dusk or dawn. Let me not forget you.” He would look at me and nod. Then again I repeated my request, that “I should remember you.” And again He looked at me, and nodded again. Then He was talking to General Secretary, and my mind was focused on my desire, and He would look at me and nod again.

After some time, He looked at me with a very serious face, as if to say, “Don’t you understand, I know what you’re doing, and I have granted what you want.” From that time on, I never doubted Him. I always remember Baba now, whether I am thinking, or happy, or just woke up or asleep, or taking a bath. He always comes in my mind. He kept His promise.

We had to wait for Baba to come out, and we knew the hour He would come. One afternoon, it was not time for Baba to come. But I was getting a strong desire to go to Lake Gardens. Nobody was there. Generally people would be in line there. I saw Baba

walking, and all the dadas were smiling at me, as if He was telling me, “This is the time you can see me.” I was not supposed to go at that hour, but I had felt a strong pull.

One morning, Baba was passing around His house, telling stories to the *acaryas* walking with Him, and all the margiis were standing there. I developed the desire for Baba to talk to me. In my mind I had a picture of Him holding my hand and talking to me. But every time, Baba would stand in front of me, and talk to the person on one side of me, then the person on the other side of me, but not to me. I was looking at Him, and I kept internally asking, “Please talk to me.” But anyway I was enjoying His presence. It must have gone on for a long, long time. Every time I would say “Please, Baba talk to me, hold my hand.” And He would come back and talk to the other brother and hold his hand. And He was playing with me like this. And finally I let Him go, but He did not hold my hand.

Then there was this *acarya* standing by the fence, and he said to me, “So, Phanindra, this morning Baba was for you alone.”

I said, “No, He was talking to the other brother.”

He said, “No, He was for you alone.”

It was a nice experience. He fulfilled my desire, and He was playing with me.

We moved to America, and were barely surviving. People coming here get a kind of social shock, especially Asians. We did too. We were hard-pressed; my wife used to work in the morning, and I worked in the evening. And we took care of the baby in turn. My sleeping hours were disrupted, and I barely got enough sleep. By the time I was about to go to sleep, it was time to go to work. This went on for four years. But in those four years, He was always prepared to help us. It was a blissful time.

I had a shift all day, and in the night. We used to live in another part of the city; it took several transfers to go to my work. I took the last bus of the evening. If I missed it, then I had to walk. I reviewed charts for doctors at that time, so I had to be there. Most of the time I was very sleepy. So I slept in the bus stop. I said to Baba, “Wake me up for the bus.” Before the bus came, automatically I would wake up. Always. One of those nights, I was really very sleepy, and I did not wake up. And someone came and shook me,

“Wake up, are you waiting for the bus?” So I should say that for many years He never forgot. He is always there; His hands are really very great and very long.

One time I was not yet practicing dentistry; I didn’t have my license. My wife had oral surgery. They gave her codeine, but she had an adverse reaction. We were eating with friends, and she passed out on the table. I didn’t know what to do. In my confusion, I closed my eyes, and saw Baba in divine gesture; in my mind I saw some *mantras*. When she was unconscious, and we didn’t know to call 911 or not, I closed my eyes, saw Baba in *varabhaya mudra*, and these *mantras* came. I put my hands on her *sahasrara cakra*, hoping the *mantra* would come through. And after that she opened her eyes. After that I also forgot the *mantra*. He just gave me enough chance so that she would recover.

His hands are everywhere. One time we were driving to our apartment, on a small side street. There was a big car and for some reason it was running past us, and I moved over. It was so fast I could not take any precaution. It almost hit us, and at that moment I was just looking at the car and in my mind I saw Baba in *varabhaya mudra*, and at that moment, the car went by, did a 90 degree turn, and went into an open driveway without getting into any accident. And I felt the Lord saved us from an accident. So much help He gives us.

I was wondering whether to go to India or not. To go or not to go. One of those times, I was sitting there and crying I was thinking of Him. My eyes closed and I felt the door open, and someone standing there before me. I turned around and looked, and I saw Baba waving His hands at me. After that I felt that He was not really playing, that this was His real body.

There was a time I went to India. I had to go home before DMC, so I did not get the chance to see Baba and enjoy His *mudra*. I had to return and so deep inside I started developing some resentment that I couldn’t see His *mudra*. I wanted to see how peaceful it was. Of course we know it from His picture, but I wanted to see it personally. One day when I was still working in the hospital, and I had no sleep, I woke up very late. I ran to my bus, and I missed it. I was so worried about how to get to the hospital. I did not know how to drive at that time. I said to my wife, “Please help me, accompany me.” My

brother-in-law had an old used car. The key was there. I got in the car, and somehow practiced. I said to Baba, “Please help me, watch out for me.” I was driving very, very carefully and in my mind was always Baba. By His grace I was able to get to my job, without a driver’s license or anything.

That night, during my break I was very tired. I put Baba’s picture in front of me, and looked at Him, and laid my head down to rest. Before I slept, I felt all my hairs standing on end, felt a different energy like someone was looking at me. You know that feeling when you are young, and someone wants to fight with you, and your hairs stand on end, you are all excited. I had the same feeling, but this was different as if someone was looking at me. I raised my head, and the first thing I saw was Baba in front of me. I saw Baba from the picture coming out, and He was giving me the *varabhaya mudra*. His hands were moving, and He gave the *mudra*. That night was so blissful for me. And after that, I never dreamed or desired that I missed *varabhaya mudra* in DMC. It just satisfied my desire.

I remember when my wife was pregnant, and we were going to the hospital. They were monitoring the baby. We held hands, and thought of Baba, in front of the doctor. The doctor was surprised, but we were just smiling. The baby was not due to come out; it was being monitored.

Then the alarm sounded. The doctor came and checked and said they had to operate. But we were not prepared for it. The doctor said, “You must have the operation right now.”

We said, “Please just give us a few minutes.”

So we held hands and were ideating on Baba, and saw Him in *varabhaya mudra*. And when that happened, the alarm stopped. And the doctor came and said, “It’s OK. Maybe we should bring her to the labor room.” After some time, the baby was born. We sang Baba Nam Kevalam to the baby. The doctor said, “I don’t know what you did there, and we cannot explain it, but it was a very big risk that you took.” We just smiled, because in our heart we knew that He was with us.

After all these years I still have the habit of going to work a little late. After I passed my board exam, I more or less knew how long it would take me to go to work. On my first day, I had just enough time for the travel. But the traffic was not moving. This was my first day, I was going to be late and I thought they would fire me. I was crying inside. I said, “Oh Baba, with all your powers, still what can you do with this traffic? I will miss my job.” I leaned on the steering wheel. Then someone honked from behind, and I looked up and the traffic was moving, faster and faster. When I got off the highway, all the traffic lights were green. And the only red that happened was the red just before my job. I came in, and the doctor said to me, “Oh, you are so precise, on time.” I felt, never doubt the power of guru, He can do anything. I was thinking it was almost impossible to make it, but by His grace I did.

*Purodha Pramukha* [the head of the organization after Baba left His body] came here. Margiis were walking with him, just like on field walk. He was asking how we were, and he asked me, “How’s your second lesson?” I said, “Fine, Dada.” I felt it was just casual. After the retreat I went home, then to work. I had a patient, and I was not doing anything yet, but she started vomiting, and started holding her throat. I didn’t know what to do, and she also did not know. I thought of Baba, and saw Him in *mudra*, and as soon as I said my second lesson. All of a sudden she just recovered. It was like a miracle. I had thought she was going to die. Then I went in my room, and cried to Baba, and remembered PP Dada asking, “How is your second lesson?”

### Girija

*San Diego, California*

I was initiated in 1979 by Didi Ananda Rama, so I’ve been a margii a very long time. When I first moved to San Diego there were no other margiis here, and there was a time when I didn’t really know if I was a margii or not, because there was no one else here, and I didn’t follow the practices so strictly.

One day I went to this metaphysical place where they do aura readings. There were two people in training there. The teacher came into the room, and she looked at me, and asked, “Do you have a guru?” I was surprised, because I hadn’t really thought about it for a while.

“Yes, I guess I do.”

She said to the two people, “When you’re reading her aura, her guru is right there, so be aware of that.”

I was surprised about it. They started doing the reading. The man started talking, “I see you are very spiritual. Deeply spiritual. Wow. You are so spiritual!”

And the other woman jabbed him with her elbow, and said, “That’s her guru!”

Then I thought, “OK, Baba, even though I don’t know if you’re my guru, you still know that you’re my guru, and you’re still with me.” That’s what He was telling me. That no matter what I think, He’s still with me all the time, even if I don’t know that He is.

I had another experience like that. I think Baba just needed to keep telling me this. We did this card reading – I don’t really do this very often, but every time, they talk about Baba. So I went, and the person said, “You know, you’ve had a very powerful spiritual guide, since your childhood.”

And suddenly, I remembered, that since I was five years old, I had thought I had a guardian angel. And it clicked with me, Oh, that was Baba. Even then. He was with me my whole lifetime, waiting for me to find Ananda Marga.

When I went to Tiljala, I was in GV (Girls’ Volunteers). I was in the guard when Baba walked into the darshan hall. I had never met Him before. I always felt so close to Him without seeing Him. And when I actually saw His body, it was like, who is that? It

took me a little while to see the person I was internally so attached to, and the physical body was kind of hard for me to see.

But when He walked by me, He turned, and looked straight into my eyes, and then walked on. I didn’t really think very much about it. Three years later, I was doing some meditation, and all of a sudden, I saw Baba, that image of Him walking by me and looking directly in my eyes. It just pierced my very being.

And I could feel that that was what He was doing, looking directly into my soul, and knowing who I was. It took me a long time to recognize what He was doing, but finally I understood. I may be slow about knowing what He wants me to know, but even though it takes me a long time, Baba always waits for me.

Gagan Monterrey, Mexico

I’ve been in Ananda Marga for more than 20 years. I was very interested in meditation, and began reading different books by different authors. I wanted to practice, but I didn’t know how to do it. Even in the books it said it was not good to do it without the guidance of a guru. All of a sudden, Ananda Marga came into my life. I met Dada Citsvarupananda, who came to stay with me for a few weeks. And I became completely involved with all the activities, the practices, social service, reading Baba’s books, hearing about His philosophy, everything. The Ananda Marga way of life was now in my house. It was a great opportunity, having Dada stay with me, and I began to practice regularly. Since then I have done meditation every day, twice a day.

Later, Dada Shankarananda said it was very important to have Personal Contact with Baba, and he lent me money to buy a plane ticket to India. So I went in October, 1987. It was an incredible experience: meeting margiis from all over the world, seeing Baba every day, and getting Personal Contact with Him. The more time passed the more I realized it was the ultimate privilege for me to meet Baba and to be in Ananda Marga. All the margiis in India around Baba’s house were always talking about Baba and His philosophy. There wasn’t any other topic of conversation. It was a complete involvement in His mission. After I came back, I began to realize more and more the personality of Baba. At the time of Personal Contact, I was not fully aware of His greatness. Now, the more time passes, the more I realize His all-encompassing personality.

At one time I was tempted to leave because I thought Ananda Marga was not growing. We did not have new margiis, we were just a few constant in our practices, and I felt something was wrong with Ananda Marga here in Monterrey. So I was tempted to leave for another yoga group. I have been in several groups, but only for a few days, because they did not fulfill my expectations. At that time I had a dream, and heard Baba’s voice, and He was telling me that I have come to Ananda Marga to stay. In the morning, I thought about it, and realized He was right. It was stupid for me to think I could leave Ananda Marga. It was my life. So many times I have had guidance from Baba. I ask for His guidance, and He is always with me. I have the greatest faith in Baba.

### Arun

*Denver, Colorado*

I was initiated March 27, 1971. In 1980, *Dharma Samiksa* had just ended. I was wanting to go see Baba. Dada Yatiishvarananda had returned to the U.S. and he said, “Don’t go, you won’t see Baba.”

But I wanted to go, and it was time. I got to New York, and the airline strike was going on, and my luggage did not come with me to India. That was my first taste of leaving to go into Baba’s presence, that we don’t need our physical possessions. I flew into Kolkata. When I went into downtown Kolkata, I had no idea how to get to any of the Ananda Marga quarters. So I thought if I sat down, something will happen. And lo and behold, within a very short time, someone came over and said, “You want to go see Baba?”

I said, “Yes, I do.” I was taken by rickshaw to His quarters.

At that time His body was weak, and so we were only able to see Him at the time of field walk. There were many Italian margiis there and their kiirtans were very sweet.

The next time was in 1986. I went with Mahadeva and I was able to get Personal Contact with Baba. We played the game that Baba likes to play, which was waiting. We had to make sure we memorized the 16 points. It took two or three days. We were told, “Wait,” then, “No, Baba’s not seeing anyone now.” Finally, I got to go in.

I remember going into His room. I don’t remember what I saw except for Baba sitting there on the bed. He told me I had been a bad boy, and that I had to serve humanity. He asked my name. I had some questions, but my mind was completely gone in front of Baba. I didn’t remember anything. He said, “You are the son of a great father.”

He touched me on the head, said some *mantras*, and then it was over. I came back to the U. S. and my whole life was in an uproar for about two years.

Baba on the spiritual level has been more profound, even though the experience of being with Him for PC was the most special thing in my life.

There have been a couple of profound meditation experiences. I came back from a summer retreat. It was the first year I had organized the Ananda Mela and I just had a feeling that something was going to happen. I came back to Denver on the 4^th of July. I

had been having these dreams where I’d be on an island. There would be a tetherball pole. I’d be holding on to the string and swinging around, and I had fear of letting go. There was this vast ocean all around.

So I sat for sadhana and had the feeling of my ‘I’ withdrawing to a point, where there was just space. Thoughts were like little boxes floating by. All the fear I had had, it related to having earlier been in the training center in Los Altos, where someone had said you should be called Viira, which meant fearless, and I had never felt that way, until now in this meditation. I felt there was no need to fear anyone, that we have a responsibility to serve on this planet, and we cannot fear.

I remember sitting down, closing my eyes, and swinging on that rope on that pole. Baba was with me, and we were jumping in and jumping out of the ocean. It was incredible. That feeling of not having any fear was very profound.

### Gayatrii (3)

In 1984 I was in Kolkata, in the beautiful abode of Baba. The name of His residential house is Madhu Malanch, meaning ‘The Abode of Sweet Charm.’ And so it was. Daily, the devotees waited and waited, hour after hour, for Baba to come downstairs and out onto the driveway, to distribute His infinite love and affection by a mere glance, or a sweet charming question to a dumbstruck disciple. People came from all over the world to visit Madhu Malanch. His attraction was so great. What He gave to the world over the span of just a few decades was so vast. It will take centuries for the earth’s inhabitants to realize the vastness of His gifts to our planet.

But this was not the main reason people came from all over the world. People came simply to experience His love, to feel His love. His love was the Infinite Divine love of the Infinite Entity, the Supreme Entity, the Cosmic Nucleus – the Supreme Stance. His love and affection were so great, just a drop put one into ecstasy, into higher levels of existence, into intense great rapture. People came because they could comprehend that Baba was indeed their best friend, their dearest darling. Nay, He was their Only Friend, their true Beloved.

All others come and go, move on in this world. But Baba is always there. Through whatever joys we have, He will be there, silently witnessing. And through our unbearable sufferings, our horrible pains and agonies, He becomes the most intimate companion, never leaving us alone, but keeping us so very close to Him. It is for this that we keep running to Him. Once we have experienced His care and His love, we cannot leave it. When we cannot leave His love, it means, we have simply fallen head over heels in love with the Lord! We have become mad with loving Him. It is called devotion.

In 1986, some of us were allowed to accompany Baba on a tour of His garden. There were perhaps twelve of us. We collected on the driveway and then Baba came and humbly thanked us for coming to see His garden. His words were the epitome of humility, the epitome of courtesy and politeness, of gentlemanliness, of Supreme Charm and Supreme Benevolence. In His every move, every gesture, every word, and every tone and accent when speaking any word, He was teaching us. He was showing us the Supreme example of how to be in this world. On a practical level, it only made Him

infinitely and unbearably irresistible. Then everyone began to move down the path. By some strange miracle – by His miracle – Baba and I were the last two to proceed on the path through the garden. By some unbelievable miracle – His miracle – Baba and I moved, side by side, down the path. It was a distance of maybe 50-60 feet, and then the path took a 90-degree left turn.

During that time, we walked side by side, almost touching each other. We were silent. But the vibration was overpowering! Was I walking? My mind was just spellbound by His close proximity. I do not know why, without asking, He gave me His close proximity so many times. I wanted to walk with Him forever and ever, side by side, in Divine Peace, in Supreme Shelter. But, the path took a left turn and Baba suddenly was ahead. I felt so much pain that He had moved a little ahead. And so much stunned disbelief that He had walked with me so far, alone, oblivious to all! In a short while we were upstairs in His room, sitting in a circle in front of Him. He asked some, “How did you like it? How did you like my garden?”

After a few minutes He pointed in my direction and asked, “And how did you like it?”

I quickly turned my head to the right, thinking He was talking to someone behind me. And then I heard, “No, you, you!” He was speaking to me! O Lord! Without a second’s wait, I simply said, “It is wonderful, Baba!” And then Baba smiled. He smiled with a closed mouth. But His smile was the biggest smile in the world! Infinite joy was being expressed through His smile!

How many times I was waiting amongst hundreds of devotees in the driveway of Baba’s house, but standing so far back that there was no hope of getting close to Him. And I would not push my way through. But again and again it happened that the rain came. With only a few drops of rain, the vast number of devotees would scurry into the garage seeking protection from the rain. The rain became my best friend and ally! The rain allowed me to move just so close to the very spot where, say, Baba would step out of His car, where He would begin His walk along the path, where I could even speak with Him if I wanted! Tiny, tiny miracles to arrange close proximity to my Beloved – all adding up to His supreme grace, His supreme love for His small child.

Another time I came to Kolkata, riddled with complexes, feeling so unworthy, so undeserving, feeling I should not even be a part of His mission, feeling He should make me an outcast in the farthest reaches of this earth. I stood in the driveway the first day, waiting for My Lord to come, but full of fear and complexes, thinking I could hardly dare to look on Him, or look at His glorious face. But He came and walked right up to where I stood, and stopped. He did namaskar to me and to me alone! And He smiled – how He smiled! – all teeth showing, smiling from ear to ear! It was such a magnificent, glamorous smile – it was full of His love, His affection! I was stunned. I thought, can this be happening? Why is He smiling at me? Why not others? Others around me are far more important; they are the VIP's of this world. I am nothing. I am nobody.

And in fact, I make hundreds or thousands of mistakes every minute of every day. Why did He smile at me? Baba turned and again walked down the path to His door. And then, again He came to the driveway and walked right up and stopped AGAIN right in front of me! AGAIN He did long namaskar, and AGAIN Baba was smiling – such a vast smile! This time, in a tiny little voice, I said, “Namaskar, Baba!” I was still too stunned, still convinced that I did not deserve His love. Look at my Baba! To the most undeserving and unworthy, He heaped and continues to heap His love!

Daily I used to play guitar and sing for Baba. I used to sing His *Prabhat Samgiit*, His tender songs. Once it happened that a brother said to me, “What’s this? You’re singing the same songs again and again!” I felt so hurt, and resolved not to sing again. I went to sleep that night firmly resolved not to even bring my guitar the next morning. Just before waking up, I had a short dream. In that dream, Baba was sitting in front of me. He said simply, “You will bring the guitar. And you will sing.” It was crystal clear, this dream. So when I woke, I felt I should take the guitar. I took the guitar, but with much hesitation and trepidation also, worrying that again that brother might be tired of my songs. After coming to Baba’s quarters, many sisters and brothers were there and were singing one song after another, as Baba sat in His chair on the driveway. I felt so hesitant. Finally when there was a small break between songs, I started to play and sing the song *Elo Anek Yuge Se Ajana Pathik*.

But I sang very quietly and, as they did not hear me, the sisters/didis started to sing another song and I was completely drowned out. Not only that, I was gradually pushed by all the sisters right into the garage, still feebly singing and playing, and completely out of sight of Baba. I felt weak due to the heat. I didn’t know whether to continue or to stop. I started to feel some mental irritation with Baba, thinking that, if this was going to be the state of things, then why did He appear in a dream and give me specific instructions to sing for Him! Feeling full of mental complex, I was almost crying. Suddenly, dadas and didis near me started saying, “Sing louder! Move forward!” And they made a path for me to move. They told me, ‘Baba is asking, “Who is singing ‘*Elo Anek Yuge Ajana Pathik*?’ It means ‘The Eternal, Unknown Traveler Came Today!’”’ Without any effort, I moved forward and then stood just close to Him, maybe four feet distant, and finished singing my song. How gracious He was!

On the last day, when departing, I was putting a thick garland of flowers over Baba’s head and around His neck. (Often on such occasions, BABA would become shy and would ask sweetly, “Are you sure these are for me?”) This time, another devotee said to Baba, ‘This reminds me of a song, but I cannot remember it.” Then Baba Himself started to softly sing, “*Ajana pathik tamogo kanik Tomay parobo mala.*” It means, “Oh Lord! Please wait and accept my garland!”

One day a very simple quiet lady came to Madhu Malanch. She was around fifty, and told me she had taught yoga for most of her life, but for one year had been in Ananda Marga. Now she had come to see Baba. She had come all the way from the little island of Cyprus, all alone, and was now in Lake Gardens! I used to sit with her on the little bench while we waited for Baba. I used to tell her also where to stand when Baba would come. In spite of this, she still had not got the chance to do namaskar to Baba. One time, Baba came down the path and got into His car and was sitting in the back seat. This time, I pushed this sweet sister in front of me so that she could quickly get right in front of the car window. In this process, I then could not see Baba. Perhaps just two feet separated me from Him, but as He sat back in the seat, and I had put her in front by His window and by Him, I could not see Him.

In this way, she was able to give Him a big personal namaskar. He also responded by giving her a big namaskar, just for her! Seeing her thus, I felt so excited. Then, as the car slowly moved out of the driveway, I began to smell the most beautiful scent or fragrance – of the most divine flowers or nectar – just enveloping me. The fragrance was so divine that it seemed almost unbearable. It took some moments for me to speak. Then I began asking others around me if they also smelled that most divine heavenly fragrance? But the others said that they did not smell anything. Then I realized what Baba had done. Baba knows everything. He knew that even though I myself was longing to see His sweetest smiling face, I stayed just a little bit behind and out of sight, to give my sweet Cyprus sister that opportunity. What did Baba do? He showered me with His own divine nectar!

January 1990. Due to His sweetest grace, and due to the sweet kindness of Baba’s personal assistant, I was inside the gate of Tiljala, His new residence on the outskirts of Kolkata. I was kneeling outside His door and peeking through a tiny crack in the door. BABA was not well. Hence, He was not coming outside for walks through His garden. Baba walked slowly from His bedroom to His living room, and there He lay down on His sofa. Only one brother was with Him, His guard of honor. This was the ONLY WAY one could see Baba, peering through a small crack in His door! What was Baba doing? He was telling jokes to His guard. And then? Baba was laughing! He was laughing so much! He would tell the joke, then the guard would laugh, and then Baba would Himself laugh so much, at His own joke!

Sometimes He laughed so hard, He would roll over onto His back, on the sofa, still laughing and laughing! It was unbelievable! Day after day, for nearly a week, I was peering into that crack, and watching Baba talking and then laughing, telling joke after joke, story after story, and laughing and laughing! It gave me so much realization to see the Lord in this way. I realized, first, that the funniest person in the entire universe is God – my Baba – that all humor emanates from Him. And second, I realized that the Lord wants all of us, all human beings to be laughing! He wants to see us all laughing! He does not want us to be sad or depressed. He wants to see us smiling and laughing! But, who could imagine the Lord of the Universe, laughing and laughing?! This is a beautiful

secret. Very few people know that the Lord, our Baba, is the funniest person in the world!

Baba’s health was somewhat better. He had resumed His daily walks through the garden in the mornings. Evenings He used to walk just outside His room. Baba was so beautiful. After His walk was over, a few of us were allowed to wish Him Namaskar, and gaze in bliss at His smiling face – a face whose smile was so vast that if we began to comprehend and feel it, we were in tears. On the last day, just prior to departure, I had so much desire to kneel and touch His feet – nay, to kiss His feet. But, how to dare to fulfill this desire! It was a strong and deep desire I had had for many months. How to do this? I felt it was too bold. Hence, I decided not to even attempt it.

But on that last day, when it was time to garland Him and tell Him goodbye, by His miracle, I stood directly in front of Him. Suddenly, in a flash, I felt myself going down on my knees. In fact, I felt myself pushed down by an external unseen force. Then I was holding His feet in both my hands. Then I was kissing His feet! Baba knows everything. He knows each and every desire. He must fulfill each and every desire before we leave this world. It happened so fast. But it happened. I knelt in front of Him, I touched His tender feet, and then I kissed them. This event would become the greatest treasure of my life. Henceforth, wherever I moved and lived, I would carry this event, this happening, with me in my heart. I touched His feet. And I kissed His feet. It was an event unsurpassed by any other event. Oh, Baba!

### Dharmapala

*Asheville, North Carolina*

By December of 1980, I knew I was overdue to see Baba. I had been initiated in 1973, while Baba was in jail and now He’d been out for over three years. I had graduated from college earlier in the year and finally, I knew it was time. Over the years, I had developed my own special relationship with Baba, through my spiritual practices and Baba’s books. It was, of course, an internal relationship. I wondered if Shrii Shrii Anandamurti and my internal Baba could truly be one and the same.

I set my sights on the January *Dharma Maha Cakra* to be held at Ananda Nagar. I traveled with a few other local margiis, who were also making their first trip to India.

We arrived in Kolkata on December 26th, 1980. Soon after landing at the airport, we realized that in ridding ourselves of any information about Ananda Marga that would make Indian Customs suspicious, we had no directions or phone numbers to Ananda Marga *jagrtis* or houses. We booked a room near Sutters Place, figuring we would connect with some margiis easily enough. But alas, it took us three days before we finally ran into an *acarya*. We then found out that Baba had already traveled to Ananda Nagar so we quickly headed there ourselves. We arrived in time for the DMC festival.

I remember that incredible experience of seeing Baba for the first time as He was quickly whisked by me, surrounded by His VSS guards and wholetimer workers, on His way in to give darshan. It was just a glance but I felt such great anticipation and joy. Immediately after darshan Baba was touring the Master Unit projects by car. A few brothers and myself decided we would run to each project and try to “catch” a namaskar from Baba, which we finally did by His third stop.

Later we headed down to the river for a bath. Earlier it had been so crowded but now it seemed deserted. It’s strange to have this memory of that bath being so exquisite and refreshing. Relaxing in the cool water, the sun was shining brightly against the rocks and reflecting off the water. A light breeze was blowing

sweetly on my face and there was a joyous feeling in my heart. I didn’t know it at the time, but Baba was saying, ”Hello, my boy, welcome home.”

After the DMC program ended, I, along with so many other margiis, followed Baba back to Kolkata. After no time at all, Baba returned to His daily routine schedule. On my first evening of observing this routine, there were 40 or 50 Westerners and even more Indian margiis hanging around outside Baba’s Lake Garden house, waiting to see Him leave for his evening field walk.

When Baba came out through His usual path from His garden, all the margiis converged towards Him. It was quite crowded and I was pushed backwards and wasn’t able to get close to Him. Afterwards some margiis were giving beads and garlands to a dada for Baba to bless. The thought entered my mind, “How foolish are these people? What could you possibly give to Baba?”

A few days later, I arrived early at Baba’s house, not wanting to miss Him as He left for His morning field walk, and was happily surprised to see that there were only six or seven margiis present. Baba would first do His garden walk which would end at His driveway; from there He would proceed to His car. Of course, if you could put yourself on the driveway between the garden and car then Baba would have to walk right by you. This was the goal of most the margiis during this time and that morning I had the best spot, at the beginning of the driveway.

As Baba came out of the garden I bade Him namaskar, which He immediately returned and then He stopped before me.

He was smiling so sweetly at me and then His eyes went from the top of my head down to my toes and back to my eyes. Even though I had never experienced it before, I was acutely aware that He was looking at me like a loving father looks at his son after a long separation. It was a look of total acceptance and love. Needless to say, I was stunned.

Baba departed quickly, leaving me standing there and I remembered the thought from a few days before, about what you could possibly give to Guru, and I remembered that it’s your mind that you give to the Lord and the garland represents that mind. My heart jumped with joy at that moment and I immediately

headed to town in search of the most beautiful garland that had ever been given to Baba.

That evening, the large crowd had returned and I wasn't in such a good spot. But I had my garland which I had so carefully picked out and I was hopeful for a chance to give it to Baba. After what seemed a long wait, Baba again made His way though the crowd towards His car, and again the crowd pushed towards Him and I was pushed back, behind the crowd and I realized I had missed my chance, or so I thought.

I closed my eyes and mentally said, “Baba, this is my mind, and I give it to you,” and I held out the flowers. At that moment, Baba stopped dead in His tracks and turned around and walked back towards me. The crowd now opened up like a wedge to let Him through.

He took hold of the garland and said, “And which is sweeter? Are these flowers sweeter or is this boy sweeter?”

He answered His own question touching me on my *anahata* (heart) *cakra*, saying, “This boy is sweeter.”

He smiled at me again and quickly disappeared through the crowd. I felt an intense, tingly vibration in my chest that began permeating my body and again felt stunned by the experience. Some of the margiis approached me and were congratulating me and sharing the experience but I was practically oblivious to them. Baba had just shown me that He and my internal Baba were One and if you have a pure, devotional thought, He responds.

I had now been in India over three weeks, staying in Kolkata, in January of 1981. Since returning from DMC at Ananda Nagar on the 1st, I had watched the large contingency of margiis slowly dwindle. I took advantage of this by getting as much contact with Baba as possible. I was blessed with some great experiences with Baba, (some direct and some indirect).

But I was greatly disappointed when I discovered I wasn't allowed to go on garden walks or field walks with Baba due to the rule that you had to have PC (personal contact) first. Of course, this was one of my primary goals of going to India: to get PC

with Baba. For seven years I had listened to the stories of so many of the margiis about their PC and how important it was to get it. I wanted it and I had to somehow get it.

I wasn’t alone. A few other margiis that I was hanging around with also shared this goal. But there was bad news. We had been requesting different important-looking dadas to have PC, but we would get different reports. At first we heard that Baba was only giving PC to LFTs (local full time volunteers) who had committed to *acarya* training. Then we would be speaking to a group of Indian margiis and one would mention that he had received PC that very morning. We went back and complained and one dada said “The senior *acaryas* have decided that Baba should not give PC at this time due to His being ill with piles.

This I found interesting (and a bit disturbing). I felt bad for Baba but I thought, “What’s a few more PCs for *Taraka Brahma*?” And who was in control here? The Central dadas? (This had also been a nagging question discussed among certain margiis in my unit in Palo Alto. Was Baba in charge of things or were the Central dadas somehow manipulating Baba?) Finally, another American brother and myself saw Baba’s personal secretary *Acarya* Ramananda *Avadhuta*, standing by himself, so we approached him. I asked, “Dadaji, we have been waiting here for three weeks to get PC; now we are told that Baba is sick and the dadas are not letting Baba give PC. Are we just wasting our time? Should we return to our homes and get on with our lives?” Dada thought for a moment and said “Mmmm, that’s a good question… I will ask Baba.” I accepted this answer, but for some reason I didn’t really take it so seriously.

As I said earlier, I had been trying to get as much contact with Baba as I could. This I was primarily doing by being at Lake Gardens when Baba left and returned for His field walks about mid-morning and again in the evening after supper. One afternoon my friend invited me to go downtown with a small group of margiis to have supper. I declined, stating I wanted to make sure I was at the gate (to Lake Gardens) for Baba’s field walk. He assured me that we would be back in time so I accompanied him. We had a great dinner but I realized it was running late. I tried to rush back by taxi but when I arrived at the gate, it was clear that Baba had already returned. While standing at the gate, I took an oath that for my remaining time in India, I would be at Baba’s house twice a day to see Him.

A few days later I was standing in Baba’s driveway waiting for Baba to emerge from His garden walk. I had gotten a great spot as there were only a few margiis present that morning (and I was the only Westerner). Baba stopped in front of me, and my hands were up in the ‘namaskar’ position.

Baba said, “Ramananda, what do they say?” Usually, this is where Ramananda would say, “Baba, they say ‘Namaskar.’” But instead he said, “Baba, they say they have been waiting here for many weeks to have your PC and they are wondering why no PC?”

Of course I was shocked. It now seemed so disrespectful and pathetic to make any such request from Baba, and my smile froze on my face. But Baba appeared entertained by it.

Baba again looked at me and said, “The answer to that question is “Why?””

He chuckled to Himself and then climbed into the back of His car with His cadre for the day. After they had left, one *acarya* said to me, “Now you will get PC” and he and a few others instructed me to hurry to Jodhpur Park (Central office) where Baba would be heading for reporting after His field walk. This was seven or eight blocks away so I decided to run all the way.

When I arrived, Baba was already inside the reporting room with the wholetimers. A large group of mostly Western margiis were in the DC hall outside the reporting room, singing *bhajans*. Ramanandaji was sitting outside the door waiting to assist Baba at the end of reporting. I approached him and requested PC. He shook his head in agreement, took out a small notebook and pencil and began asking me my personal data (name, unit, *acarya*, etc.). Finally he said, “Now, you are *bhukti pradhan* aren’t you?” I told him I was not, that I was *upa-bhukti pramuk* (below *bhukti pradhan*).

Dada said, “Oh, too bad. Baba is only giving PC to LFTs and *bhukti pradhans*. Are you sure you are not a *bhukti pradhan*?”

I hesitated only a moment. “Yes, I am *bhukti pradhan*.” At that very moment there was a loud roar from the reporting room and a dada opened the door and frantically signaled for Dada to come. Dada jumped up but Baba was already coming through the door followed by his guards and all the workers and then all the margiis followed downstairs and outside. I was left standing there eerily alone and wondering, “Did my little white lie cause that?” A dada later told me that Baba had jumped up so quickly to

leave that he (very uncustomarily) left His sandals behind and had to be brought back for them. What a morning.

That evening, I was alone outside the gate when Baba returned from His evening field walk. He looked up and pointed at me and said to the dada that accompanied him, “Look, he’s getting PC twice a day.” And it was so true. I am remembering that warm, sweet feeling that covered my body that night as I hung on that gate, watching Baba slowly make his way up the stairs and disappear into His home.

I returned to my home shortly after this. Baba began giving PC again right after I left (including to my friend). I was to make three more trips to India over the next five years before I finally got it. And it was worth the wait. But that is another story.

### Ista

*Albuquerque, New Mexico*

I was born in the Philippines and that is where I became a margii. I learned about Ananda Marga in December of 1980, but I didn’t have a chance to see a dada. I was fourteen years old. I learned of it from this hippie-like community in our town from which some became margiis.

I had a longing since I was a small child for something better than what common culture offered me: drinking, smoking, listening to music. I experimented, but it didn’t really make sense to me. I grew up Catholic and at one time tried to be an altar boy. My parents were agnostic; they didn’t really go to church. But I always had a longing. I just felt Western religion wasn’t for me.

In April 1981, my friend said, “I’m going to show you something interesting.” It was Yoga night, and I got initiated by Dada Kalatiitananda. Immediately after, I experienced a very strong response. I had this feeling like I was seeing bubbles. This intoxicating feeling stayed with me for several days doing sadhana. My first experience in Ananda Marga was so deep that I used the memory of this to help me pass through difficult times. I can remember listening to people singing *bhajans*, and the songs went so deep into my heart. It was very strong.

My first experience with Baba was when I was told I had to do *paincajanya*. I started doing it every morning when one day I saw the face of Baba floating on the wall. It was His picture from the *varabhaya* photo without the *mudra*. The interesting thing was I had never seen that picture before.

Shortly afterwards I turned fifteen and my mother died. Although it was a tragic experience, I think it was what made me serious with the practices. I had to take care of my brothers. Often, teenage life is fun without responsibility. But this made me become serious. I finished school but always felt that Baba wanted me to do something different with my life. So I left my scholarship and went to wholetimer training to become a dada. In the training center, I think the most powerful experience I had was when I received sixth lesson. I felt this laughing sensation for days. It was a blissful experience, and when I looked at people I felt laughter and joy. This was a very significant experience, and

many times in life I have wished to feel this state of mind again. At that time I was really convinced I had seen Guru personally, and it was a very mystical and important part of my life.

When I went to India I felt a sorrow in my life and I couldn't understand why. I guess it was one of those things that Baba gives you to prepare yourself. My theory from speaking with everyone who went to see Baba is that He makes you experience some chaos, sorrow, or distress in your life because it makes you more open to see Him. With everything smooth and no problems, you will not receive Him. Always He makes the circumstance that you have to be almost frantic and searching.

The first time I saw Baba was after several years of doing sadhana as an acarya. It was like I was a very dry flower for weeks. Very dry – and then seeing Him I was drenched. I looked into His eyes. There was something remarkable about His eyes. One of His eyes was like in a dreaming state, and the other eye was very piercing. I have never seen a person like this in my life. I know that Baba had trouble with His eyes from the poison He was given in jail. But to see Baba's eyes like that in person was very interesting. It was like He was in two worlds. One eye very aware of everything happening and present, and the other very distant or lost. It reminds me of the stories of Shiva. One of His personalities is very contented, and His one eye reminded me of that. Baba was going to His car for fieldwalk. At that time everybody would try so hard to see Him, but I didn't feel the need to. I just stayed there like a flower drenched in rain.

At this time Baba opened personal contact to everyone. Before, it was hard, but I think Baba wanted to see all of His missionary workers. Baba wanted to give them all P.C. I was at Lake Gardens. It was a cold morning around seven o'clock. Although there was a long line, they threw me right in. Even though Baba did organizational work all over, this house felt like it was His house, His home. Later, I found out it was the only house registered to Him, to P. R. Sarkar. Anyway, it was beautiful. I was waiting there and I saw a Dr. Seuss book. It was a nice feeling to see that Baba had a Dr. Seuss book. As a child, his books had always given me tremendous joy, and it felt nice to see them right before I went to see Baba.

I went in. I remember clearly that day. He was wearing a yellow *khurta*, and He smelled so fresh and naturally pure. I felt a little conscious about myself, whether I was presentable. But I also felt my own self was affected by His fragrance. Whenever I recall that moment even before Baba said anything to me, I think about how the environment changed. When we do sadhana we are supposed to feel alone in the universe. At that moment I felt this feeling of aloneness. I can see now why the scriptures always say Guru is everything. It is my opinion that without this personal relationship with the Guru, one will never achieve the goal of realization. I felt this teaching in the meeting, and maybe now I can share it with others.

Baba asked me, “Did you come alone from the States?”

And I said, “Yes, I came alone.”

Baba said, “Really, you came alone and you weren’t afraid?”

I had ridden on many planes in my lifetime and so I said, “No, no, I wasn’t afraid.” And Baba asked this question about five times. Then, I realized what He was really asking. I realized He was trying to teach me that one is never alone in life.

I caught myself and I said, “No, Baba, I didn’t come alone, I came with you.”

Baba was very happy, and He smiled. And then He became very serious and said in a very deep voice, “Yes, you are never alone.”

He recited some *mantra* and said, “Yes, you are not alone, you can never be alone.” This struck me very deeply inside and really made my heart jump. Then He closed His eyes and really made me feel very peaceful. This was another experience of mine. I felt Baba could control my heart rate. After He closed His eyes, He made me feel very peaceful. And with all of my experiences since then, I have never felt that level of intimacy again in my life.

### Jyotisha (2)

This trip was coming to an end all too quickly (although I was tired from the excitement and third world environment). I wanted to stay but was supposed to deliver a baby in a home birth back in Oregon in a few weeks. Also I was almost completely out of money. So I finally decided it would be irresponsible to stay and that I should leave with the group that day on the scheduled flight. I sat that morning and set aside the funds I would need: a rickshaw to Baba’s house, a taxi to the airport, food for the day. Cutting the estimations to the bone, I found that I had one rupee left over (I actually had to borrow money later from friends for an airport tax that I had not anticipated). I went out and with that rupee bought a simple garland of marigold flowers to offer as my parting gift to Baba.

There was a moderate crowd of margiis at Baba’s house that day. In recent days Baba had sometimes walked around His house inspecting the many plants and giving instructions for their care before getting into His car. The significance of this is that one could only guess which side of the car to wait on in order to be nearby to Baba when He walked by. If you guessed wrong He would be on the other side of both a crowd and His car. I chose the same spot that I had used the first night I met Baba. No one could get in front of me and I would be able to offer my garland easily. I waited and waited. Suddenly the guards snapped to attention. Baba was coming! Everyone crowded around. Baba came out the side door and immediately turned to walk the long way around the house away from me. I was crestfallen.

As Baba passed the rear corner of His house where He was no longer visible, the crowd shifted over to the far side of His car and everyone jockeyed for a good position. I was left alone standing unmoved in the other corner. I just couldn’t believe that Baba would not come to receive my offering. After a few minutes, Baba reappeared around the far corner of the house into view. I could see Him across the car and the crowd walking slowly, stopping before plants and discussing their care with the dadaji responsible for their welfare. He simply must come to take my flowers, I felt, yet He showed no sign that He would alter His route.

The door was open on the far side of the car. He was greeting people as He walked forward to enter the car. I was standing alone many feet behind the people on this side of the car. Vishvadeva and Divyamrta at the back of the crowd were waving me forward, “Jyotisha!” they whispered urgently. “Come on, you can give your flowers through the window.”

But I stood still. I didn’t want to just shove my flowers through the window. Baba was now approaching the car, never looking in my direction. My friends forgot me as they stood in salutation. Baba put one foot inside the car and started to stoop down. Suddenly I realized He wasn’t going to come for my flowers, that I must run over and put them through the car window if I could! At that moment I gave up and a wave of emotion swept through me. I just barely started to move in that direction when, at the same instant that I gave up, Baba stopped. I stopped, not having moved more than a fraction of an inch. He was part way into the car and looking upwards away from me. Baba stood upright. Everyone looked perplexed as if thinking, “What is going on?”

Baba, His back to me with a car and people between us, called out to Sudhiira, who was supposed to paint some murals on the walls in Baba’s garden. Slowly Baba started to walk back around the house the way He had come talking about the mural project. As He disappeared around the far corner of the house the crowd jostled me as they returned to my side of the car and tried to get a good position. But no one could move me from my spot nor could they stand in front of me. Baba came back into view as He rounded the rear corner of the house. He continued to walk and talk, pointing at the wall, presumably discussing the murals that should be painted. Then He nodded His head at Sudhiira and the workers indicating that the discussion was over.

He turned. He looked directly at me. He walked straight up to me and stopped. He cradled my hands in His, the marigolds flowing over the top and covering all. He drew Himself closer, His face just inches from mine and gazed into my eyes.

“Marigolds,” He said slowly. “And who would these be for?”

I was overcome. In a choked whisper I replied, “They are for you, Baba.”

Taking the marigolds between His hands, His palms together, the marigolds cascading over His fingers, He touched His thumbs between His eyes in spiritual greeting and said, “Thank you” with such feeling as if I had given him something wonderful. Yet

it was nothing more than a commonplace little garland of flowers purchased from a barefoot street vendor at the cost of a mere one rupee.

He turned, gave the garland to His personal assistant (as was the system), and continued walking to the car. Just as He passed me, He stopped. He turned and looked back at me. With a knowing smile He reached out and patted me affectionately on the cheek, then turned away again and proceeded to His car.

I heard from Dada Shantibodhananda (VSS CS in 1980) who’d been living in Baba’s house, that one day Baba said to him, “Look at your thumb,” and he saw a moving picture on his thumbnail, a motion picture, like a little TV screen. He said it frightened him. And Baba said, “Don’t be afraid, this isn’t magic, this is science.”

As I understood it, Baba said, every event that happens in this universe is recorded in the ether. And it’s possible to catch those vibrations from the past, and project them onto a transparent screen and view them. And that Baba was using that technology, so to speak, to project an image from the past. On his thumbnail. Baba said that in the far future scientists would learn this technique and be able to view historical events from the past.

My second trip to India, Fall ’86, I was deported on arrival from the Bombay airport. So I went again the next opportunity which was June ’87. Baba was very distant at that time. It was the only time I could go, the summer, and Dada Caetanananda was asking for good news for Baba. We had recently had an interesting experience in our unit in Oregon, where the landlady would not renew the lease for our school in Portland. She wanted an exorbitant amount of money to purchase it. She said she was going to sell it, and wouldn’t change her mind. Finally, my wife got involved and talked to her, and she said OK, and changed her mind.

Dada told the story to Baba, and then told me that Baba had said, “Oh, very good, very good” and that it was positive catalytic *microvita* that brought the change in this lady’s mind. Dada also said that Baba asked if the woman was of a certain racial type who, according to Baba, are characteristically very stubborn. I didn’t know if she was or was not.

In May of 1990, I had the good fortune to spend a month with Mahadevi and our four-year-old son in Tiljala followed by the DMC in Ananda Nagar. This was really the last big DMC. Back in Tiljala, before the DMC, Baba would walk in His garden everyday and we’d go to darshan every week. Caetanya, our son, was extremely popular. Everyone knew him, and no one knew us except as his parents. He knew GS, he knew PA, everybody, they were all carrying him around. He liked to do *tandava*. So they got him this little bitty skull, really just a face, and a knife, and a little *tandava* outfit. I had a *tandava* outfit, and we were going to do *tandava* before darshan. So since Caetanya was going in, Mahadevi said, “I’m his mother, I’m going to take care of him,” and we all got to sit right next to where Baba sits.

Then Dada Caetanananda came in and said, “What’s that boy doing here? Children can’t do *tandava* at darshan! Get him out!” It was completely packed, you couldn’t get anyone in or out. Baba was coming. Dada was so upset that he hid his face behind his clipboard. Then Dada Priyakrsnananda came in to call *tandava*. And Baba sat there and beamed at Caetanya at the whole time, just smiled, and really enjoyed, and afterwards He said, “Very good.”

So everything was OK. The next week the whole family again got to sit near Baba and we danced again, and Baba said, “Excellent.” The third week, Baba was very angry. He was yelling at all of the dadas, “You haven’t started a single school. You’re no good. You’re all scoundrels! Get out, you scoundrels!!”

The dadas went out; they were all looking in windows and peering around doors. It was tense, very uncomfortable. We got up to do *tandava*. Caetanya was a little out of rhythm. I jumped, and my knee came down and hit his skull, and it rolled across the floor in front of Baba, all the way over by GS and PA. Baba was sitting there with a very stern look. I whispered to Caetanya, “It’s OK, just keep going.”

But Priyakrishnananda stopped calling the *tandava*. We didn’t know what to do; we were all in the squatting position with our knives and skulls outstretched. It was tense. GS picked up the skull, looked at it, looked at Baba, then he rolled the skull across the floor in front of Baba. Caetanya picked it up, they started calling *tandava* again and we finished. Then Baba enthusiastically called out, “Well done!” And then Baba’s mood completely changed. Suddenly He was happy and He let everybody come back in.

Mahadevi and I were in Tiljala before the Jan ’88 DMC (at Ananda Nagar). We went to the didis’ quarters to pay our respects to the head didi (Ananda Bharati) whom everyone called Auntie. I remember she said to us that the secret to success in spiritual life is to follow the instructions of the guru to the letter, to do everything He says and to do it purposefully. That is something I have always tried to remember.

### Parvati

*Los Angeles, California*

I was initiated around 1966 or ’67. This was after I had had a very rare experience. Long ago I was a dancer, and at a studio in the suburbs of Manila. During our ballet break, we heard the most incredible chanting outside our studio gate. I rushed out to peep through the gate. The timing was just incredible. I saw lots of Filipinos and people in orange robes, screaming, “Baba, Baba.” I was so curious. I just kept on looking. And there He was, in a white *khurta*, very little, a holy man, I thought. He looked very holy, very strong. And everyone was going after Him, screaming, and He was just smiling and shaking His head.

And I said, “Hey, that’s really nice.” But all of a sudden my uncle ran out of the house and said, “Stop watching those pagans.”

I said to him, “Please, stop it, don’t say anything,” I felt like he needed to be hushed. There was a presence there, very rare.

A year later, a friend of mine said, “Would you like to check out this place, with monks in orange, you go there for meditation?”

I thought, “Meditation, what’s that?” Anyway, I said OK. We went, and sure enough there were monks sitting in meditation. I learned later they were Dadas Sumitananda and Adveshananda. They had their eyes closed, and all of a sudden they would jerk up in their meditation. And I said, “What the hell are they doing?” Some people would scream “Baba,” and then continue with their meditation. It was strange. I left, though it did not really get out of my mind.

Another year later I went to the mountains. A friend told me, “I heard there was a dude,” – we went for this hip talk – “let’s check him out.” And there was a monk in orange. I remembered him; it was Dada Adveshananda. I started talking with one Filipino about this path, the Ananda Marga path. He was saying how we have to practice this. I’m the kind of person, if I commit myself to something, I really practice it, to see what happens. So I was practicing twice a day, and I immediately changed my diet from non-veg to vegetarian. It was really something else.

But I was curious about this man in white I had seen, because His was the picture of the guru. “I’ve seen you before, and I need to see you. I need to know you more, to know how to know you more.” This was in my head.
