One day I made a wish. “I want to know what it is to realize.” I kept saying, “Baba, I want to realize.” I thought I was the kind of person who couldn’t do good meditation. I would close my eyes and open them and not know where I was, and I thought, this doesn’t make any sense. I was looking for sense, you know. So this is how I met Baba in a realization way. It came like a dream.

I was sitting, and out of nowhere things started coming in my mind. In the vision I was sitting at a table with a few friends, and I was saying, “I have never been happier sitting with a group of people.” This is really fun, my idea of fun, sitting with my friends and talking about holy things. We were having fun. All of a sudden one man stood up and said, “Oh, but I’ve got to go.” The man stood up, and I saw very clearly it was Baba, there at that table. It was really strong. I saw Him in every way no one could have ever felt. And I can’t explain it, and I will never be able to explain it.

I jumped out of where I was seated, and went to His picture and I said, “I’m surrendering to you, now I know what it is to realize.” Yes, Baba can do that for you, if you wish for it hard enough.

This path is very rare indeed. It’s like you’re thrown into a laboratory, where you’re experimenting with your *mantra*, your practice, and you’re exposed to all kinds of experiences. Before that I had a life that was mostly dream space, you know, but meditation is like consciously going through some kind of struggle. That memory of Baba saying, “I have to go,” was the thing that kept me going, and gave me courage to overcome every obstacle I had.

Of course He knew that I was a person who wanted all kinds of experiences, and He gave them to me. From living in the mountains, to working in film and coming to different places; He brought me to this town. I asked one dada, “Why is He bringing me to Hollywood? It’s a town that has a very bad reputation, all kinds of things here.”

That dada told me, “No, you will be exposed to a different kind of life where you will understand how to act.” And sure enough, I came and met people in the acting world,

and ended up being an actress myself, and finally realized the truth of the experience of a yogi, in terms of acting, consciously.

Now I’m an older woman, and I understand what consciousness is about, and for that I only have Baba to thank. That little head that moved, that smile that is unforgettable, but always there, to catch you, whose hand is always there to hold you, when you’re coming through some kind of a loop, you know. He never, never fails with His grace. He made me realize, Baba, my beloved guru, made me realize, that only through His grace can we go through life.

I will talk about how Baba inspired me. I was in Hollywood, in Hollywood movies and everything, but there was something missing. One day we had filming downtown and I saw all these people living in the streets. When Baba left His physical body, it was one of the strongest, most difficult experiences I ever had. I kept saying, “What to do, what to do, what will I do now?” And again He came into my mind. “Oh, He’s still here,” I said. “I have to make clear what He wants me to do for society.” And one day I was in the center of L.A. and I saw these people and out of nowhere I said, “Yeah, I’m going to feed people, I don’t know where to start, but…”

This was twelve years ago. I talked to Phanindra and Mahajyoti. I said, “I don’t even know where to begin.” But I said, “OK, I’ll just cook something vegetarian.” We were feeding people at night, and it was scary. I had to make believe that Baba was there, so that I would be able to go through the fear of going there and facing those homeless people, and not separating them from me. And sure enough, He performed, Baba performed for us in the form of those homeless people. He made us laugh, He extended His hand. Instead of seeing poor people, I’d see Baba joking. It started to be so much fun, all of us doing this, Jayashrii and everyone, we were not thinking actually that we were helping the poor, but only that we were being with Baba and having fun.

So if you want to do something on this path, you really have to connect it with Godhead. Baba is not away from us, He is within us. Nowhere else. You can forget how you wanted to do it, because He is the one doing it for you.

I remember Dada Adveshananda in the Philippines. He had an accident, and it was really a bad accident. He was in the hospital. None of the injections worked, so they had to operate on him without painkillers. His body would not take them. Long before that he said, “I will see Baba in any shape I can imagine, and I imagine he comes like a butterfly.” In the room after the operation, he was in pain, and he kept on moaning and groaning, and all of a sudden, I would see this butterfly, this big butterfly inside the room, and he would just pass out, be calm.

That was very strange. And I really believe that Baba visited him at that time. You don’t need philosophy too much, you need love. You need to know how to love, and then Baba is there. You can pull Him with your heart. Your mind will follow, if you feel strongly for your guru, because the guru is the one who leads you from darkness to light.

Dada had two accidents in a row, it was incredible. He had an accident, and told the WTs to go get help, and while they were gone, he was hit again by a bus. He was so human, and at the same time, a very high saint. Not only a holy one, but someone who was aware of everything happening around us. He said, “That’s what we’re here for, to be aware.” We spend so much time being unconscious. This is a path of consciousness, not unconsciousness. He told me not to worry about the world. Just do service. If you run out of service you can pour water on a plant, or give food to a cat. Or say hello to a bug. It’s the easiest thing to do. Service. If you don’t do it and realize who you’re serving, you’re wasting your time. You have to realize who it is. Poverty is just a word that has been coined. Nobody is really poor. Everything is *samskara*. Everything in this created universe is working out reaction.

### Giridhara (3)

I went to Jamaica to see Baba but left Cirasmita and our little Madhurii at home as we believed that Baba would finally come to the States or somewhere close. I had made myself a card that said Official Photographer – AM Public Relations Department and with that badge, I could go on all the field walks and take pictures of Baba. Chandra Shekhara, not to be outdone, volunteered to carry my camera bag so that he could join us on the field walks. Nothing like a clever margii.

It was very crazy as there were no more accommodations at the main hotel, so several of us rented hotel rooms in Kingston. We had to travel each day and late at night back to the hotel. I slept on the floor as there were not enough rooms for all of us. I ran after Baba many times to try to get Personal Contact. Finally I got close enough in line and got in to see Baba.

I did *sastaung pranam*. I was very happy to be with Baba. He spoke to me for a long time. He told me to do something great for the society and He told me of the suffering of so many people and the planet. I could feel that He really felt the pain of these people and the planet. I really understood His compassion and where He was coming from when He wanted us to help the people. He told me to do intellectual service.

I could barely walk when I left the room. I had to sit down in an outer room and I was lost in sadhana for some time.

The rest of the trip was very crazy but extremely blissful. I flew back to the states with a brother from Texas, who was very clashed out. He had a terrible time with the problems with accommodations, getting food, running after Baba, the overcrowding, etc. That was all he could think about. I realized then that I had been in the eye of the storm the whole time, feeling nothing but bliss while the chaos was all around me, clashing out others.

The last time I saw Baba was when I was leaving India in 1988. I had come for *bhukti pradhan* reporting and had spent much time with Kinshuk, Dada Pranavananda, and others showing them the computers. I was also working with Dada Vishvarupananda on publications. We put Baba’s books on disk with the capability to search them. This

was shown to Baba, who was very appreciative. Kinshuk spent a lot of time working on the new computer and at one point Baba asked him, if he “loved the computer or loved Baba?” After that Kinshuk left the computer alone for awhile and resumed working with it, when he felt more balance about it.

I was leaving and I was able to garland Baba. I followed Baba and eight or ten workers back towards His door on the side of the house. As Baba and GS had just reached the lower porch and looked back out to the path, GS saw me and said to Baba, “Baba, Giridhara is leaving.”

Baba turned to GS and said, “He has done some good work.”

Baba then turned to me and said, “So, is Giridhara a great intellectual or a great worker?”

I was almost speechless, but a nearby dada prompted me to say both.

I said, “By your Grace, Baba, both.”

Baba smiled and said, “That is a very graceful answer.”

He smiled and namaskared me and continued up the stairs. This brief conversation satisfied a desire that I had to share a regular conversation with Baba and especially to share puns.

I felt that Baba was telling me several things in this brief conversation. First, to go ahead doing what I was doing with putting His books on computer and helping the mission to use computers and to work towards translating His books into many languages. I felt like what I understood that He wanted me to do was correct and that it was accomplishing something. On a deeper level, I immediately realized that His comment was also to tell me that I was strong in intellectual and action areas, but devotion was lacking. I immediately felt I needed to do more kiirtan and develop more devotion.

Shortly after this Cirasmita and I took on Innersong, and over the next several years I spent time recording and making kiirtan available. I spent time with Jyosna and other great kiirtan musicians, and I found more depth in kiirtan. I found more depth in devotion. This was truly Baba’s grace for me and I feel that He personally put me on the right path for me, that last time that I saw Him alive.

# CHAPTER SEVEN:

## I WILL ALWAYS BE WITH YOU

> “If wise people take every object of the world as the expression of the one and single Cosmic Consciousness, then on seeing the changes in the name and form of any particular object, they will not be affected by pain or pleasure. Cosmic Consciousness to them would remain Cosmic Consciousness…”

On Oct. 21, 1990, the Ananda Marga community received a tremendous shock. Our beloved, our guru, our teacher, left His body, in what is known now as His *Mahaprayan*. Many traveled to Kolkata to pay their last respects, while others gathered locally to grieve and console one another. Up until His last moment on earth, Baba worked with incredible speed. It became clear He had known how much time He had, and that He had made sure the organization was ready to carry on His mission in the absence of His physical body. The loss was devastating. But on a spiritual level, He has demonstrated time and again that He continues to guide His devotees.

### Chandra Shekhara (2)

I did not see Baba again in physical form. In the late 1980’s I experienced a growing desire to see Baba and talk to Him personally about what I should do in my life. I knew that this type of access was pretty much impossible, but I still wanted to do it.

In 1990, people began speculating that Baba might be coming to New York Sector so we were getting excited. One morning – it was very early, maybe three a.m. or so – I was in the state between dream and wakefulness, not really awake but not really dreaming. I felt Baba’s presence very strongly. It was a physical presence – I couldn’t see or hear Him, but His presence was very strong and very real. He told me that I should work on creating a constitution for the world based on His principles.

Later that day I called Sectorial Office to ask if there was any news about Baba coming to New York Sector. The *acarya* who answered the phone told me that Baba had left His body earlier that day. It was a big shock and very difficult to comprehend. It wasn’t until many days later that I realized that the time that I felt Baba’s presence so strongly was about the same time of His *Mahaprayan*. Ever since, I’ve felt very deeply that Baba personally took care of me and answered my questions.

### Krsna (2)

When Baba left His physical body in October of 1990, I was in San Francisco and got the message from some sister in Los Angeles. At that time I did not have independent means of confirming this. I called the sectorial office and they didn’t know. I called some other margiis and they also didn’t know. But I did my sadhana just the same.

And in my sadhana, I was doing my *shuddhis*, and all of a sudden I saw or thought I saw some globe of light coming from the earth and speaking to me. And then I was speaking to this globe of light and said, “Is it true?”

And the globe of light said, “Yes, my son. It is true.”

“But why didn’t you say goodbye?” I asked this being I was speaking to.

“I am saying goodbye now,” it replied.

And then there was some interchange between us and that globe of light passed through the bottom of my *anahata cakra* and then proceeded onwards to another place of the universe. This small light is still there in the bottom of my *anahata cakra*. I still see it, and whenever I talk to it it’s still there, it still responds, it never left. It’s like Baba left his imprint there and has never left. It is always there.

### Dhyanesh (3)

I was doing *paincajanya*, and after I did my sadhana I fell asleep, and I had a very vivid dream of Baba. I was so happy to see Him. But immediately I started to cry; I was crying so much. He kept on stroking my head and saying, “Don’t worry, Dhyanesh, everything is going to be fine, I will always be with you.”

Then I woke up. And I couldn’t understand why I was crying so much. That afternoon we got a call from Kolkata that Baba had left His physical body. This was the saddest day of my life. But I feel He came to me in the dream to say goodbye to me, and so that I would not lose my mental balance. He is always with me. And I still feel Him so directly and closely. This was one of the great lessons, when I was in Denver and He was in India, and yet He came to me.

Some people who got to see Baba physically develop some ego that they saw Him. But Baba said, “Yes, there are good people in Ananda Marga, but better ones are coming, and then the very best are yet to come.”

The last few years He talked about *microvita* and the distance between unit mind and cosmic mind, and that there is no difference. That all is one.

This guru, this level of guru consciousness in the universe manifests itself to us through the form of Anandamurti. His nucleus may be in another planet at this time, but there is no distance. He’s that close, inside us., with so much compassion and love. If we make a little sincere effort towards Him, He makes so much for us, to help clean our minds, to teach us how to think and feel. He allows us to absorb into Him, to come closer. He is so loving, so compassionate.

### Arjuna (3)

In 1990, October, I got a call from Dadaji Devashraddhananda that he’d heard that Baba had left his physical body. He wasn’t sure, but if I could, I should go to India for Baba’s *Mahaprayan*. Somehow five of us here bought tickets and got on the plane and when we got to Delhi there were no plane flights to Kolkata, everything was sold out. We went into the main Indian Airlines office and went upstairs and gave this guy some money and somehow they got some VIP seats for us. We got into Kolkata about two in the morning. Everyone on the plane was crying. We did kiirtan all that night at Tiljala. The next day was *Mahaprayan*, a very, very sad day. Every space in Tiljala was filled up with people. The thing I remember most, after the fire was over, were just the birds coming, and a very peaceful feeling over the whole place.

I guess when I saw Baba the first time in the jail, and had this feeling of seeing this white light, this totally peaceful feeling, I hadn’t known what it was. I had never experienced it before. I’d never really studied Ananda Sutram before, just read parts, not really studied it in a deep way. But the past three or four months, I’ve been memorizing it. One *shloka* really sums up that experience of how I felt after seeing Baba. *Abhavarota ananda pratiya lambaniir vrititasya pramana*. The lingering bliss which follows that state of vacuity is proof of that state, the means of firm belief in that state. Now I know what that means, from that first experience with Baba. It was my introduction to a different world.

**Devinistha (3)**

We didn’t go back to India for two years. I did go back for *Mahaprayan*, after Baba died. We heard one Sunday afternoon. I came to *dharmacakra*; I had been in Boston for the day. All the way back I was singing kiirtan while driving, and I was getting so blissed out. I pulled in the driveway and my husband came out and said, “I have to tell you something. We got a call from India and Baba’s either in a coma or He’s died. We’re doing *akhanda kiirtan*.” And I was so confused, I said, “Wait a minute, I was just with Baba, I spent the last two hours with Him in the car; how could He be dead if I’d been feeling Him so much?” We did DC, and afterwards we talked. Punyatman agreed that he would take care of the children if the rest of us went to India. So he stayed behind with our children.

We got our visas and left that next day. I only took my oldest child with me, who was nine. In New York, everywhere we looked there were margiis, and all of us were pretending like we didn’t know each other, just to be safe, so they wouldn’t prevent us from getting visas. But we were able to get our visas and take the trip. Again on the plane, we agreed to all enter separately.

We got to Kolkata and went directly to Tiljala. There was a room there where they had Baba’s body on an ice cake, surrounded by flowers. I went in; they didn’t let us in for very long. I put my garland there, and I remember even before I got out they were removing my garland.

There was a garden outside and I sat and faced where Baba’s body was, and did sadhana. I had such an intense sadhana, that I didn’t really know how long I sat. I got up afterwards and I just felt so happy. I guess I had had a blissful experience. You know how you don’t know when you’ve had such an experience, you only know afterwards? And I couldn’t stop smiling. And everyone there was so sad and upset, and I couldn’t stop smiling. I felt embarrassed, but I was so happy to be there, and that all these other people were there. I felt blissful. I went through the burning of Baba’s body. I cried a lot, but it wasn’t a heavy sadness. In a way it was like all the sadnesses in my life were pouring out in the tears there; it was very therapeutic.

After the *Mahaprayan* we returned home to our children on October 31st. I had ridden with Giridhara and Cirasmita on the return trip. We decided we wanted to put together a book of all the people’s experiences. Many people had not been able to go to India, many had come to Ananda Kanan. Some people had been stuck at home, and had not had the chance to deal with these experiences. So we decided to put all these experiences in a book, and we didn’t have a name for it, but suddenly it occurred to me that we could call it “The Last Garland.”

The last garland was in the form of this book. We collected different stories, and also the stories about Baba’s leaving, and the last practices He had taught, such as *guru sakasha*. I stayed high, blissed out for a whole month until the night we completed the book. I was at Giridhara and Cirasmita’s home, at two in the morning, and we had collected every Baba picture in order to choose what we would put in the book. They were all lying on the floor, and waves of bliss were going over me. I was having trouble staying in my body looking at all these pictures. Finally after making some decisions, I went to sleep. I woke up, and that’s the point at which everything just went like that, and suddenly I was overwhelmed by sadness, and the bliss that I had felt was gone and I had to start dealing with the reality of His leaving.

### Jayamati (2)

When Baba left His physical body, I tried to be sad, but Baba wouldn’t let me. I remember crying before His picture, then He would fill me up with such bliss, that I could no longer be sad. I remember when I got the book in the mail remembering Baba, and they showed the picture of Him on the funeral pyre, then it seemed so real and I cried and cried and cried. For a whole year I kept saying that I wish Baba was still here in His physical form. I had a dream that I was looking at a Baba picture and there was an empty chair where Baba was supposed to be sitting. In another dream I was looking at a Baba photo and there was this light around Baba, and a dada told me that was Baba’s light body. I knew that Baba was in His light body. He had not gone anywhere.

I hope that someday that my husband, Amarendra, can be healed about his pain of not seeing Baba, to know that Baba still loves him, even if he did not go and see Him.

### Prakash (3)

The final time I saw Baba was in 1990. When Baba died there was this big shock. I felt scared and sad and didn't know what to do, but we only knew we had to go see Him. So we took Anandamayii, who was ten, and I remember seeing Him for the last time. What was most wonderful was the actual experience of *Mahaprayan*. They carried Baba's body out and put it on the funeral pyre, and lit the pyre, and quoted Baba saying, "You're all mini-Babas" and started singing kiirtan for a long time. The flames were coming up, and birds circling overhead. We were all breathing in the smoke coming from Baba's body; it was becoming part of us.

I was crying and feeling tremendous sadness and grief, and a tremendous love. We were letting go of our attachment, and breathing Him in. It was healing and inspiring, grieving and joyful; we were merging in Baba. Merging in Baba. I remember they were afraid someone would do something crazy, like jumping in the fire. They made a very strong statement, that nothing should happen. No one did anything crazy. What kept coming over and over again in my mind was, "If you want to merge with me, you have to see me in yourself and in everyone." It was incredibly loving: the basic ideology, first and second lesson.

I knew that all of us had this intense desire that Baba would do everything; He would become recognized, this savior. But He never taught that; that was our desire. It wasn't Baba's mission, which was about making everyone realize the Brahma in everyone. Brahma alone is the guru, no other, not the physical form. How could we ever know until Baba taught us through this incredible sacrifice? He seemed to say, "You can't understand me as long as you have this attachment." It's painful. But Baba said, "I came into this world crying and everyone laughed, but when I leave this world, I will laugh and people will be crying."

### Vishala (2)

I never got Personal Contact. I had this big drama about PC with Baba for the rest of my life. But everyone who got PC in Jamaica that I went with are no longer margiis. And the only people who did not are Arati and myself. And we’re still best friends and margiis. So that was an interesting drama. Jamaica was really the only contact I had with His physical body. I spent years after that trying to get to India, but always had these dramas and couldn’t go. But after Baba left, He’s given me many, many experiences in my sadhana. I’ve had many experiences of Him, and He’s kind of given me an experience of PC in samadhi. I feel like my PC with Baba started after He left His physical body. You can have so many experiences with Him, internally. He can touch you now very deeply, more deeply.

When Baba left His physical body, everyone was going to India. Again, a big drama. I was crying and crying, but I couldn’t get there. Instead I went to New York; they were doing something there. My daughter, who was seven, used to say, “Mommy, we have to go to India to see Baba.” I’d say, “We can’t go, I have a three-year-old and a seven-year-old.” Now she said, “See. Now I will never see Baba and His body is gone.”

Then we were in New York, and people were coming back from the ceremony, and the vibration was so high. I thought, “Oh Baba, I’ll never get to see you, and have PC with you.” And all of a sudden I felt like I was leaving my body, my body fell away from me, on the floor, and I was just with Baba. It was very beautiful. And Baba looked down and said, “See, little girl, before they had the advantage, but now you have the advantage.”

In my mind I thought I was the most unfortunate margii, because all my friends had seen Him so many times. But really I was the most fortunate. Because His relationship with me is inside. Now that He has left His body, I have that place He created inside of me. And since then I’ve had so many experiences with Him. I’m the most fortunate margii.

### Vishvadeva (2)

This story occurred after Baba left His physical body. I waited five years before going back to India. I thought maybe it would feel empty for me there. Finally I decided it was time to go back. I went with brother Divyamurti, and did some sightseeing, which I had never done before, wanting to spend maximum time with Baba. We went to Kolkata, to Lake Gardens, and then to Ananda Nagar. There I was being bothered tremendously by the mosquitoes. I became disturbed. I was supposed to be enjoying meditation in this beautiful place, but I was tired of being bitten, feeling that although it wasn’t a bad experience to come to India, Baba wasn’t there, and it hadn’t served much purpose in coming.

I went to the station to catch the train to Delhi. I was waiting and put on a Walkman, and listened to kiirtan. And all of a sudden I became ecstatic. Usually I’m a very reserved sort of person – I wasn’t jumping up and down in the waiting room, I restrained myself, but I just felt a strong feeling of Baba. And I said, “Baba, even though you’re not here in physical form anymore, please just remind me every once in a while that you’re still here, so I don’t get too foolish in my mind and think that you’re not.”

I got on the train and found one empty seat. I was talking to some young people, and they said one lady was in the seat next to me, although she had stepped out. Into the compartment came a didi, and she sat down next to me on the train. She said, “You know, I’m feeling very sick, and was thinking I wanted the comfort of having a margii on the train with me.”

And I said, “This is too much coincidence, the only empty seat on the train, and we’re sitting next to each other. I was thinking that I would like Baba to remind me that He’s still here, and I think He just has.”

# CHAPTER EIGHT:

## SEEK NOTHING BUT TO SERVE THE LORD

> “In the highest form of devotion, the devotee says, ‘Oh Lord, I love You. I want to love You. And why do I want to love You? Because I want that my love should give You pleasure… I love You not to get pleasure, but to give You pleasure.’ And by means of this type of devotion, the Yogi comes in closest contact with the Supreme Self and becomes one with Him.”

When Baba was physically present, many people reported receiving inspiration from Him through dreams and visions. This guidance continues to manifest itself through such indirect experiences today. A whole new generation is coming onto the scene, with many strong margii families and wonderful margii children, many of whom affirm that they feel His guidance in their lives. In the thirteen years since Baba’s physical passing, Ananda Margiis in New York Sector and the world over have worked with redoubled effort to realize His vision of a world grounded in justice and spirituality. They continue, of course, to grapple with *samskaras*, but also to immerse themselves in service. And, with the knowledge that He has already laid the groundwork, they work together to actualize Baba’s mission.

### Das Ragu

*Los Altos, California*

I was a pre-university student and had the opportunity to see Baba in Kerala in 1968. Inspired by Dada Citswarupananda, I became a VSS cadre. In VSS dress, I had a chance to get PC with Baba. I was hardly 16 years old. They said, “Hey, come on, come on,” and pushed me into the room.

Baba was sitting on a beautiful bed, and said, “Come, my little boy.”

I did *sastaung pranam*. He pinpointedly told me, “You are doing some naughty things.”

I said, “Yes, Baba.”

“You need punishment.”

“Yes, Baba.” I was reading AM literature, and I was a little egoistic. I said, “Yes, Baba, as you sow, so shall you reap. For every action there is equal and opposite reaction.” Baba brought his face close to mine and said, “You are teaching me?” Oh my God! I was so shocked, you know. And I just put myself in *padmasana*, and closed my eyes, and Baba came and blessed my eyes, probably to see the right thing in my life.

In 1978, just after my marriage, DMC was held. An early morning flight was scheduled to go to Hyderabad. We went to send off Baba. We were late, and the flight took off. Baba was in the Madras airport, seated alone, and it so happened that I and my sister-in-law, Shobha Shettigar, were sitting there. In the whole universe, we were only two souls singing to Baba. It was so nice that we had the privilege to be with Baba.

In Ananda Nagar, my family all went to see Baba at DMC. Everyone was assembled. Baba was walking up and down, and everyone was chanting Baba Nam Kevalam. I was holding my daughter. I told her, “Tell namaskar to Baba.” She was five or six years old. Amidst all the devotional things there, she said, “Baba, Namaskar!”

Instantaneously Baba turned back and gave namaskar to my daughter. It is one of the most gracious moments for me and for my daughter.

In 1988, I had the opportunity because of my revolutionary marriage to go across the oceans to America. I am from a poor village, and I would never have dreamt of going so far without the grace of guru. It is only the element of service that allowed me to come this way. In 1988 it was difficult to get guru, but somehow guru's grace is so gracious on me. We went from California to Kolkata. There were group sessions. Baba was explaining the term 'archipelago.' He wanted a margii from South India to answer his questions. I was the only south Indian margii. I sat near His feet, trying to touch His toes. He was saying, "No matter what, if the grace of guru is there, then one can always be near to Him anytime."

I planned to work as an LFT from 1990 for ten years. I had the opportunity to go with Dada Susmitananda. I was supposed to drive a car from Los Altos Hills, cross Mexico, Guatemala, and El Salvador, to raise money for a master unit in San Salvador. I was so excited. I brought the car, and crossed the border. In a small village, my car broke down. The part was small, a gasket, costing only $5. But because it had to be imported from New York, I stayed one month in Mexico. Anyway, it was one of the greatest experiences, because in these thirty days I was able to go to schools, colleges, even a prison, to teach yoga techniques, whatever little I knew. The beauty of it was that I did not know Spanish, and they did not know English. Somehow I was able to go through and give a lot of certificates to a lot of people. I had beautiful quarters in the police station. I would get rice and vegetables every day. I would cook and then go and do service. After that I got my car back and went on to San Salvador. I stayed for nearly one year and was teaching in the University of Technology. Believe it or not, *kaoshikii* dance was displayed on national television, because they videotaped it in the university.

There are so many elements in one's life to make oneself great. For example, Dr. Ravi Batra took one word of Baba's theory of the social cycle and made it known to the whole world. Similarly, I have some kind of love for the *kaoshikii* dance taught by Baba. Without *kaoshikii* and *tandava*, *Prabhat Samgiit* and kiirtan, Baba never spoke to any group. These are the four fundamental things. I made a song about *kaoshikii* and introduced it as a stretching exercise. It goes beautifully. If I start a class, it takes a

minimum two hours. The students will be laughing and laughing. And still I give the sense of what was given from guru.

### Jagadmitra

*Vancouver, Canada*

My acarya is Dada Priyakrsnananda, and I was initiated in the winter of 1989 in New York. My personal experience with Baba has been through the practices and through my dreams. My devotional life was very powerfully affected upon my initiation, and getting into Ananda Marga practices. And so I recognized by the intensity of my inner experience, how effective Ananda Marga practices really were. Over the years I've had a number of blissful experiences.

But more recently, in the last month, I had an experience of Baba in a dream, which was quite different. It was a longer dream than I'd ever had, a very intense experience with Baba. I recall in the first part of the dream I was in a house somewhere, maybe a *jagrti*. And Baba was in the house as well; there were two Babas. I saw a man sitting in meditation posture, up on a dais. And in front of him there was another man, who looked like an average man, but as I looked closer I realized he looked like Baba. How could there be two Babas? The man sitting up higher's head was larger, he looked larger, rounder, and the other looked like an average man. And so I imagined perhaps one was P. R. Sarkar and the other Shrii Shrii Anandamurti [laughs]. Baba had dual representation like that.

I did *sastaung pranam* before Baba, and lay down on the floor. What I observed was, He was angry, Baba was upset or angry. He walked past me and didn't really acknowledge me. Also I experienced that somehow my vibration was uncertain. Perhaps my devotion was not straightforward; my dream was showing me, perhaps Baba was showing me, that my devotion was wavering. It was very interesting.

In the next part of the dream, I found myself out on the ocean, on a large sailing ship, moving through the ocean at night. It was very stormy, the waves very choppy. Baba was being carried in this boat. He was sitting on some kind of wooden platform, and the boat was carrying Him across the water, but the boat fell apart. This wooden boat split, and broke into three pieces. Immediately Baba emerged from the boat and started moving by His own power across the ocean. Then He went to the front of the ship. He was grabbing these huge sails, and the boat started moving forward. The boat was no

longer carrying Him, but He was carrying the boat. He was the propelling force carrying the ship.

I interpreted this as in society or in Ananda Marga society, Baba is our treasure, we are the vessel carrying the treasure of Baba, but perhaps what it comes down to is He is in fact the force that moves us forward. Sometimes we can’t withstand the pressures. But it is in fact the power of Baba who is moving the vessel. That was the second part.

In the third part of the dream, I was in a courtyard. It seemed to be in Asia, in India perhaps, and perhaps in a different time – people were dressed in traditional Indian clothing, sitting on the ground in a courtyard. I had the sense these people were devotees. I was in this group, and observing this group. Then Shiva appeared. He was wearing a loincloth, had long hair, and was carrying a spear, not a trident, and I recognized him as Shiva.

I became aware of the contents of his mind. He was observing these people and saying to himself, “Who are these people, what kind of people are they?” And I had the sense that they were like the kind of people who might go to a nightclub, average people, colorful, not necessarily strict *sadhakas*. Shiva smiled at all these people, except for one boy, about 12, who kind of cringed away, and didn’t want Shiva to see him. He didn’t want Shiva to know he was there. Shiva recognized him, and became very angry. He took this boy, and I became this boy. Oh, no!

Shiva was leading the boy, he had a whip, and was taking him to a place of punishment. And they went to a field. It was dawn on this wide open field, with hills, perhaps what Ananda Nagar looks like. In the field was someone who looked like Krsna. He was standing in a one-legged pose, with foot tucked into his thigh, holding a device that looked like a big trumpet. It amplified his voice, and he was singing, vocalizing, not words, but musical sounds in a beautiful male voice, very resonant, and it was sunrise. It was a beautiful, colorful scenario, awesome and blissful.

So this boy was being led to punishment, and it was a beautifully intense scene. And I woke up. I never saw the punishment, I woke up, the child inside crying “Baba, Baba.” It was so beautiful, rich and overwhelming. In that part of the dream, the theme was about punishment, perhaps some kind of wickedness. Baba was upset, Shiva was

upset, the boy was willing to go for punishment and with that, there was a tremendous amount of love that he received. I felt this intense devotional bliss.

I had never had a dream that involved Baba being angry, He was always very blissful. Baba was angry this time! [laughs]. My devotional life has been filled with these kinds of experiences of direct realization, which are very subjective, but authentic to me, so I’ve been able to evaluate the instruction received from my *acarya* as very authentic. How else could I receive such intense experiences?

### Prashanta

*Denver, Colorado*

I was initiated by Ac. Jitendra Kumar in 1973. My two most important Baba experiences were pretty much internal. They happened around 1975.

All these experiences progressed out of the sleep state. In the first one, there was just white light, and I saw Baba’s face. My mind was completely still. Baba started very slowly smiling at me, the corners of His mouth just started slowly lifting up. The more He smiled, the more of this infinite ecstasy I felt, the experience of pure consciousness, like they talk about, the sugar dissolving in the water.

So by the time Baba was smiling all the way, I was gone. Some time later I started to become aware again, and I saw Baba, and I started to have some unit identity. Then Baba started smiling at me again, and I disappeared again. I had this experience two different times. It was so far beyond what I thought I could attain. This was something given to me to help me be motivated, to understand what my spiritual work was.

The repercussions are, when you go to that place, I don’t think you ever come all the way back again. There’s always some part of you still left there, some awareness. And, it’s pretty hard to be impressed with anything or be that attached to anything after that, it’s just so far beyond everything else.

The other story is about a series of visions I had. It was interesting, kind of opposite of the other experience where all I saw was Baba’s face. I saw this small gentleman in white clothes, but I couldn’t see the face. This individual had so much white light radiating from him that I couldn’t really make out the features. But the person was the size that Baba was, and was wearing the kind of clothes Baba did.

I found myself in this place, a physical place. I was seeing people, there was grass, a little road or path. But these experiences somehow changed my state of consciousness, so I understood more and more, so that I began to understand all of science.

I had the experience of knowing all these secrets I’d always wanted to know, and simultaneously, it wasn’t that I could understand anything I wanted to, it was that I understood it all at one time.

There was a state of consciousness where that knowledge actually exists. I’m extremely interested in science and math, it’s been a great love of mine, and this was having all questions completely answered. And then the knowledge would bleed away, and I would begin weeping, because all of these wonderful realizations were leaving me. I think that one of the repercussions has been that I’ve attempted a level of difficulty in my understanding of science, to merge back with that point of view, that state of consciousness. So I’ve been much more aggressive in my work.

### Ranjan

*Los Angeles, California*

Ananda Marga was introduced to me a long time ago, in 1970, when I was studying in college. But I became a margii and practiced meditation for the next six or seven years. I finished my college degree, and I really don’t know what happened, but suddenly I got out of practicing meditation and Ananda Marga.

When I migrated to the U.S. in 1990, one day I was reading in the library. I saw a book by Didi Ananda Mitra, and I had an awakening, remembering that I had been practicing yoga before.

In the middle of 1998, I received a call from Dada Sarvabodhananda, inviting me to Phanendra’s place. Then I realized I had to practice yoga again. I asked Dada to renew my lessons and I attended the weekly dharmacakra in Phanendra’s place. I had a lot of clashes and problems, because my family was not yet introduced to Ananda Marga. I had a hard time convincing my family about vegetarianism, to follow what I was doing. I had to really feel a lot of help from Baba. I said, “For me to be able to practice this, you have to help me.”

From then on, I did not stop practicing. I told myself that maybe some day they will realize that this is important. After two or three months, I asked them to attend *dharmacakra*. And they were convinced, so now they are regularly attending, and got initiated. I told Baba, that really He gave me the opportunity to bring all my family to the path.

I would like also to relate a story which happened just a month ago. I’ve never been absent at *dharmacakra* since that time. One week I was very sick beginning on Wednesday. I had a high fever; it seemed I had the flu, and I couldn’t work. I was taking medicine. By Friday I said to Baba, “I don’t want to miss a single *dharmacakra*.”

Saturday came, 7 o’clock, 8 o’clock, then 12 o’clock and I asked Baba, “Please, I don’t want to miss.”

Suddenly, I started to feel better. I took half-bath and told my family, “Let’s go,” and they were so surprised. I was able to attend the *dharmacakra*. I thought that was the end of that, that my sickness was gone. But then on Sunday, I got sick again [laughs]. I

told Baba, “Oh Baba, you only let me attend the DC, and now I have to suffer two more days.” Anyway, I realized He helped me to go to *dharmacakra*, and if you need help with some good deed, He will help you.

### Liila

*Taiwan*

I was initiated in 1981 and I first met Baba in India in 1986. I went there to attend DMC and also I got married there and got Baba’s blessing. When Baba gave blessing it was really like I was just in front of Him, and my self seemed to disappear. Only He was there. His eyes were so big. I visited India several times after that. The last year before Baba left His physical body, I was going to have a baby and I knew I would not be able to come for many years. So I went there for Baba’s birthday in May.

Always we read and got some information that Baba would stay until the year 2000 something. We never thought He would leave so early. That October when we got the news, I had so much pain inside. I tried to go to India that day, but because I had one more baby I could not go immediately. For many years I always felt much pain. Until three years ago I went to India, to the *Mahaprayan* commemoration. When Baba was physically there, the main purpose in going to India was to see Him, and wait for Him to come out. But this time He was not physically there. And I tried to compare the difference. When I went inside Baba’s room, there was such a strong vibration, as strong as when my eyes saw Him. In that moment, it completely fulfilled my desire. I knew that without the physical I still could feel Him everywhere. Later I went again to DMS. DMC and DMS were different, but even without His physical presence, really Baba was everywhere.

The important thing I feel is just to do His work. And it is very easy; you can feel He is there. I also never feel lonely, because He is always with me. This is the first time I came to the U. S. to attend a retreat, but there is the same big family feeling. It’s really Baba’s grace.

### Kirit (3)

On many occasions Baba gave me messages through dreams. And every one came true. I received information about my health, received certain medicines, and if I had physical problems, they were resolved. Many things He told about the energy crisis, about how one should conduct aspects of organizations, about training centers, and issues related to them. All these things He told me in dream.

One time Baba was angry because we did not have a medical unit in Kolkata. He said, “What is this, the headquarters are here in Kolkata and you don’t even have a medical unit. Scoundrels!”

All this stuff He used to throw at us, all His drama and *liila*. “I have to have a clinic by next Tuesday,” He said.

The *acaryas* promised. But how do you get a clinic in less than a week in Kolkata? You can’t even get a rental building. But they promised. Baba threatened to fast if they didn’t get it. And they called Dada Dharmadevananda. Dada called me, “We have only a few days to open a clinic.”

I said, “Well, OK, Baba wants us to do it, so He probably has done it already. Let us see what we can do. Let us try.”

I got the phone call on Wednesday and it had to be done by Tuesday. So Rathi-ji, a margii in Kolkata was running around like crazy to see if he could get some building. Real estate is so hard to get there, nothing is available. Finally he went to where they were constructing homes. He went to a businessman friend who owned a house, and said, “My guru wants this house, can you give it to us? Tell us what you want and we’ll pay you for it.”

He agreed, and the price was set. My target was to raise $22,000 in a week. That was a lot of money in those days. So we were all thinking, trying to get cash and some loans from the bank.

All of a sudden some margii called from New York, and said, “Dada, I had a dream.” He was given the message in a dream that some work was going on and he needed to talk to Dada to find out about it. He saw Baba in the dream, and saw the need for some project, and saw that some assistance was required. He called Dada, who said,

“Oh yes, we just received the news, they want to build a hospital.” That person contributed almost $10,000. So we put together some cash and got some additional loans, and we had the $22,000.

I decided to deliver it in person; at that time we could not send money through the bank. The earliest plane would not get there until Thursday, and so we asked if we could get some extension from Baba. Baba did not accept. He said, “No, don’t come to me. I gave you the date; complete the work by Tuesday. It’s not my concern when the plane arrives.”

PA Dada told me that Baba didn’t accept the delay; it must be done by Tuesday. So I was running around getting my visa, and we said we would come no matter what. I was traveling with Phanendra from L. A. I was hiding all this cash on my body. I got caught in the airport. The CBI asked, “How much money do you have?”

“I don’t have any.”

The man said, “I know you are carrying cash.” We were going to lose the money, and then what would happen? I turned pale. I don’t know what happened to that officer, but he let me go. “I’ll let you go but don’t insult my intelligence,” he said.

I went straight to the *jagrti* and handed the money over, “Please take this, I don’t want it in my possession any more!” Then I saw Baba. The last time, He had told me, “You will be back very soon.” And it had only been a month and a half. So when Baba gives some work, it is always predetermined.

Baba started giving *Prabhat Samgiita* on Sept 14, 1982. Ten days before that I had a dream. In it, Baba was sitting on a cot, all the margiis were there, and Baba was singing, composing a song, and teaching music. Next morning, I asked Dada Yatiishvarananda-ji, “Did Baba ever write any song?” “Oh no, Baba doesn’t sing, he never wrote any song.” “I don’t know but I had a dream that Baba was singing, teaching music.” He laughed at me. Within a week, Baba gave the first *Prabhat Samgiit*.

When Baba left His body, I got the news on a Sunday. On the Wednesday before that, I saw in a dream Baba walking through the desert. He was very hot, perspiring. He was unsettled. There was no umbrella to shade Him from the scorching heat. People were

walking around, looking very tired. I looked at them and said, “What are you doing? Where is the umbrella?” I was looking everywhere for one, running around in the desert. I became more nervous. Finally, I noticed Baba, and all His heat was gone. He was very cool and nice.

Next morning I woke up and thought, “What is the message of this dream?” Baba looked so red. And then, Baba left His body. I came to India. We were all so broken-hearted. I was on the second story of Tiljala. They put Baba’s body there for the final rites, and finally they started the cremation. Then I recalled my dream. “Oh, this is the same picture of heat, redness, that I have seen in my dream, and this was what my dream was.” So Baba gives indications.

This is my last dream, a very special dream which I can never forget. After Baba’s departure, it became very hard for me to adjust in my life. I became worse than a machine. I could not tolerate the separation, and life became impossible for me. However, Baba had told me some specific instructions on Sept. 8, 1987, and I remembered those and it helped me to go through this period. When He told me they didn’t make any sense. I thought, “Why did He tell me these things? They would never happen.” But it happened. Those instructions saved my life. I could not do sadhana for a long time, I could not concentrate on *mantra*. Many times I tried to do any lesson, I could not see any *cakras*, I could only see Baba. My eyes were constantly crying when I did sadhana. My body also gave up. I became very sick. My body collapsed because my mind collapsed. And to make things worse, Baba no longer came in my dreams. It became impossible to live, and impossible to die. It was the worst period of my life.

During this time I only had one question for Baba – “Baba, I was never worried about my spiritual progress, or what will happen to the organization, or who will guide us?” I had no questions like this. I was convinced there were no problems there. The only problem I had was this: How is it that Baba can break the bond of my love and go? How could He do this? Was this bond so weak that He can just break it and go? It didn’t matter to me that He was my guru, and that He chose to do this; those issues did not matter. As guru, whatever He did, I accepted. But what did come to me as a question was how it was possible that He could break the bond of love.

Finally after nine or ten months I had a dream. Baba was the most beautiful I had seen Him any dream or ever in person. I was sitting on a ledge of a building; He came close to me. In my dream I did not know that He had left the body. I was so happy to see Him. He said He had come to say something. “My work on this earth is complete.”

“Yes, Baba, no problem.”

“No problem?”

“Yes, Baba.” Whatever He decided was OK. I already assumed that He loves me so much that wherever He goes, I would go with Him.

“My work is finished and I have to go. And I have come to ask your permission to go.”

“Baba, what are you saying? How can you ask permission from me? You are everything. You cannot ask permission from me. Even for me to hear this word is not acceptable. That means that you are going and I am staying behind. No, I cannot hear this. It is not up to me.”

Then Baba explained, “It is important that I have your permission before I go.” How could that be? That got me thinking. I had some responsibility here.

“Do I have your permission?”

I became very sad, because I knew He would leave. Finally I folded my hands and said, “Of course, whatever your decision is, how can my thought process be different from yours? Of course my thoughts are in agreement with yours.” I did not use the word permission.

He smiled and said, “Yes, I know.” Again He talked with me for a while.

When I woke up next morning, my pillow was wet. I realized that *Parama Purusa* is such an expression of love. His littlest creation is of such importance to Him, that He comes back to insignificant existences and communicates through dream, and says that He cannot leave the earth without permission. Not only that, but He came to tell that He did not break the bond of love, which is what had been bothering me. Now I cannot think that He broke it, since He asked me.

To me all these dreams are much more vivid than real life experiences, because *kamamaya kosa* is not existing. In a subtle state of being, the higher *kosas* are present, and experiences are much more vivid.

My personal desire simply is to remain with Him and serve Him, truthfully. I don’t search the ultimate realization, I only want to serve Him. As Baba said, the devotee should seek nothing else but to serve the Lord. I want to serve Him all the lives to come. So that His existence, His form, remains comfortable and satisfied, His work gets completed, His desire gets completed.

**Vijaya Laksmi (2)**

In 1980 there was a tremendous earthquake in Guatemala. I was working in the mountains for AMURTEL in Chimaltenango. That was where the most damage and loss of life was as a result of that terrible earthquake. I was working with Dr. Ram and we were in charge of the field hospital that the Red Cross had left. Because we had the professional know-how, they put us in charge. I had many very beautiful experiences there. In fact, the head of nursing for the Red Cross got initiated as the result of Dada's talks. I remember getting up early to do kiirtan. All the volunteers that had come from so many countries including the nuns said to me, "It is so beautiful to get up in the morning and hear you chanting Baba Nam Kevalam. It is so inspiring."

When our work was done there, we went to Lake Atitlan. We thought we would take a holiday for a couple of days. It was a beautiful blue lake with volcanoes in the background. There I had a beautiful dream about Baba. I remember that I looked out in the morning at the beautiful lake and there was Baba coming up in this boat. He was dressed in blue, on this silver lake, rowing towards me. Then He called me to Him. I'm not a very good swimmer, but as soon as He called, I just dove in and swam to Him. I was actually kind of surprised that I had gone in when I could have drowned. I went up to Him, and He looked towards me and just signaled for me to hold onto the boat. I was looking up at His face. It was just so beautiful. He did a namaskar and then rowed away. It was so nice. It was such an example for me to do exactly what He wanted me to do, and the scenery was so fantastic, purples and blues. That was just one very nice dream.

I have a story of the first time I had a spiritual dream. It was right after I was initiated and I was new to Ananda Marga. In the dream, everybody had met Baba in the airport and they were singing Baba Nam Kevalam. I was so new that I felt I had to sit in the back just to observe. Then Baba got off the plane, and He walked all the way to the back. At that time I didn't have the name Vijaya Laksmi. I was called Vicky. And He said, "I really came to see you, Vicky."

And He looked right at me. He was so beautiful dressed in brown. I was so amazed and He looked so intently at me. At that time, I used to drink coffee, and He took

my hand and said, “C’mon, let’s go get a cup of coffee.” We went over to the corner, and we were talking. The talk was so moving that I don’t even remember what was said, but I remember the feeling was something deep and special for me like I had never felt before. Then He did namaskar, and He went out to His silver plane and flew away. That was so special for me. I had only been initiated for about a week or so. It also had humor as you can see because Baba would never go and drink coffee.

One of the many times I went to India was a special time. It was when I formed a very special bond with Baba. I had been in India for about two weeks and I wanted to go to Nepal just to look around. I was a little hesitant because I wanted to go to all of Baba’s darshans. I thought I would just go and come back. I got all ready to go with my flight and everything. It was getting late, and I guess Baba didn’t want me to go because a huge herd of water buffalo came onto the street. I was feeling desperate because I thought I was going to miss my chance to go. They never did get the water buffalo out of the street, and the man had to turn the taxi around and go back. But I was so glad I didn’t go and miss the darshan because there was such beautiful *Prabhat Samgiit*, with dancing and everything. I thought this was so much better than going to Nepal. Obviously, Baba didn’t want me to go that time, but later I went to Nepal on vacation.

I had been healthy all of my life. But as a result of my illness, and falling and breaking my hip, going through this tremendous *samskara* which Baba put me through in my old age, I believe Baba said, in this way you will burn a *samskara* and come close to me. And that is exactly what happened. There is a tremendous spiritual affinity that seems to grow and get deeper, and now I know Him more. When I was first diagnosed with cancer, I realized that this was how I have to surrender, it was pretty final, it was serious enough. Baba was calling me, and finally I said, “Now I am going to another planet.”

It seems that He is taking care of me in different ways. He is allowing this time for me to get closer to Him on a much deeper level than I had experienced before. Your life changes. You’re looking at things in a very different way, and it is almost as though you died and now you’re getting a chance to restructure everything. And you look at

everyone, every situation, in a much more spiritual way. Because of that I am exceedingly grateful. I feel it is a gift.

We become impatient. Ananda Marga has been around for a long time, and we are still struggling. But we do not take into account the many countries where Baba has developed strong ties with schools, and *acaryas* struggling all the time to make things happen, working with children’s homes. Certain things have happened in India with different *acaryas*, and we can lose perspective of who Baba is and what He is doing.

He is a dynamic force in this world right now. I would say the most dynamic. I think it is important that we go deep inside and realize that His mission is a special beautiful *tantric* mission; I don’t think we realize that. We may need to look deeper and through sadhana we can begin to understand. Baba left His body, but He is definitely in everyone’s *sahasrara cakra* guiding leading you into a more spiritual path, putting all kinds of *tantric* obstacles in front of you so that you can really know that you have to go through this battle, this internal and external battle.

And that is something I feel is unique to Ananda Marga and to Baba. This is a very special mission. I think that we need to look at it and understand that it has a special meaning to us individually. I know that I will not be alive and things are going to get pretty tough. We will need to prepare our young people and ourselves mentally. We are going to need strong hearts and minds and bodies to develop Baba’s mission.

**Nirmala**

This is the story of when I went to India for the first and only time to see Baba. It was the winter of 1986; I had been initiated in 1973. I was so excited and nervous that I left my passport in Denver. So it was quite a problem when I arrived in New York. I watched as all my friends, Guhan, Jayamati and Dada Shamitananda boarded the plane to India. I stayed with the didis for two days. They were wonderful to me; took me back to the airport and rearranged my flight. By His Grace I flew first class.

When I finally got to Kolkata and Tiljila, I was exhausted and exhilarated at the same time. I climbed upstairs to the room where all the margii sisters from Denver were staying. I could hear the kiirtan and smell the cook fires from the open window of the darkened room. There was an orange sheet waving in the breeze just outside the window. As I looked out I could see Baba’s residence and the Christmas twinkle lights all around the compound.

It was crowded in the cubicle and as I lay down on the concrete floor between two sisters, I fell into a restless sleep. I dreamed that Baba was at a dining room table with me and many other devotees. He was giving a discourse on the desserts on the table. There were jelly-filled tarts and tiny vanilla cakes with designs on top, there were cookies and coconut bars, orange- and lemon-scented breads and slices of some sort of Indian torte. He was describing all of these delicacies in great detail. And when He finished, He said to me in a melodious, sweet voice, but with a hint of mirth, “Nirmala, would you like one of these sweets? Which one would you like?”

I could feel the kindness and radiance of Baba’s being, but I could not answer Him! When I awoke, I felt so close to my guru, and in fact, I was only 200 yards from Him physically. This dream stayed in my mind for many days, and although I never spoke directly to Him or got very close to His physical body, He arranged for me to have a sweet visit with Him!

### Krsna (3)

Baba has come back to me in dreams, and at specific times He tells me something. One time, when we did not yet have a Master Unit in the Bay area, I had a dream that there was a gathering and the margiis and the dadas and the LFTs were reporting to Baba and all of a sudden Baba got very angry.

He said, “Who’s taking care of the Master Unit?! There’s nobody taking care of the Master Unit! Who’s taking care…?”

And He was furious! All of the dadas and myself were saying, “OK, calm down, Baba. We’ll take care of the Master Unit. Calm down.”

So we promised that we would take care of the Master Unit. Within a few days we got news that Vijaya Lakshmi had got a Master Unit. And since that time I have always tried to keep my promise to Baba that I gave in that dream, to take care of it.

The most recent dream I had of Baba was unusual, at least for me. I’ve had many dreams of this type, some of which I only remember when I’m doing sadhana, but in this last dream Baba appeared and we were in VSS uniforms, escorting him to a door. There were margiis there and they were saying that Baba has resigned, that He is no longer Baba. Because I was a VSS guard I had the opportunity to talk to Baba if I wished, and in that dream I did not even speak to him as Baba, I spoke to him in my own language. I said, “Papa – is this true?”

And He said, “Yeah.” And He pulled out a paper in his own handwriting declaring that He was no longer Baba.

I said, “But why?”

He told me, “YOU are Baba!”

Then the dream collapsed and I had a very strong feeling and awoke. My realization from that dream is that all of the margiis and *acaryas* and LFTs – it is now our ballgame. We do not have Baba as a physical person to tell us what to do anymore. It is now us that has to bring Baba’s mission to success. It is now up to each of us and we have to work together to make certain that His mission goes forward and is successful. We don’t have a physical person anymore to tell us, “OK, do this.” That person has imprinted Himself into each of us and He’s relying on each of us to fulfill His mission.

### Lokesh

*Albuquerque, New Mexico*

I was initiated in Ananda Marga in 1980. I had this dream about five years ago. In this dream, I was walking up a spiral staircase. I walked up a little while and came to a level area, a little platform. I stopped and rested for a little while and thought, “No, no, I need to go higher.” So I started walking up this spiral staircase again, reached another little platform, rested for a minute and said, “No, I should keep going higher.” I kept going up this staircase; I would reach another level, rest for a while and then keep going higher. Finally, when I got to the top there was a very, very nice meditation room – beautiful drapery with a nice level area for meditation.

I sat down and waited there thinking, “You know, Baba is around here somewhere.” I looked over to the room on the left and the door opened. Baba was sitting there kind of leaning back in a bathtub. You could see His head and shoulders, and He was looking over and He just had this big grin on His face almost as if he was playing a little joke or having fun. It was very nice going up this big spiral staircase and reaching a little meditation hall and then seeing Baba with this big smile. It was delightful and very refreshing.

### Krsna

*Los Angeles, California*

I was recently involved in a car accident. I was on my way to the Master Unit one day in December, 2000. The farm has a windy road, and for some reason I felt very heavy, and all of a sudden I fell asleep at the wheel. The truck hit a tree, but there was a big lake nearby, and I was lucky that I didn’t drive straight into that lake. There were people around who came to help me and called the paramedics. They called Dada, who came and took me to the hospital, and I got a few stitches. But the truck was totaled.

Just looking at the damage of the truck, I felt it was Baba’s grace that I came out of that accident with just a few cuts and bruises. It could have been much worse; I could have driven into the lake. But I hit a tree, and the truck was totaled. And I only had a few cuts and bruises. And I felt that Baba was really with me – He is with me every day, every time – and I think He saved me from that accident. Just looking at the damage of the truck it is hard to believe anyone came out of it alive. I think Baba is with us all the time.

### Karun

*Los Angeles, California*

My name is Karun, and I started meditating in February of the year 2000. I was initiated by Dada Rudreshvarananda on June 13^th of 2000. I was in prison at the time. I was doing the last year of a five-year sentence. Rajiv started a *dharmacakra* in the prison, and that was how I was introduced to Ananda Marga.

Dada began to make visits to the prison. My meditation was going well, and it was a nice experience to be in prison and practice yoga. Before I was initiated I was only doing Baba Nam Kevalam ideation. My meditation was going nice, and I was having some small, blissful feelings.

Then my meditation became more stale or stagnant and the blissful feeling began to leave somewhat, and I didn't understand why. I was only doing maybe 15 minutes of ideation at a time, and no kiirtan or half-bath; very basic things as I had just started.

One day at *dharmacakra* in the prison, I sat down for my meditation and there was Baba's face in my meditation, big, and He told me, "If you wish to have more blissful experiences and you wish to further your meditation, you must stop eating meat now."

The only feeling that I had then was complete love. The only way I can describe it is like a father giving love to you, but so much stronger, with no conditions to it. In a sense I felt like I had mud all over my clothes, and the father would look at me and say, "Oh, you're dirty," but he still loved me. At that time, the way the embrace was, no mud mattered, nothing mattered. It was just purely love, and the feeling that He loves me so much.

From that time I stopped eating meat, and my meditation got better. And slowly I started to implement the other practices, half-bath, kiirtan before meditation and all these different things. And my meditation grew and grew.

I recently got out of prison – May 22nd of 2001 – and I've been out a couple months now, and adjusting to the different life in society, which is very difficult for me, but the meditation is definitely helping me to make the adjustment. I don't know that I

would be adjusting so well without it. Recently I've been becoming very involved with Ananda Marga, and I'm very happy with the progress I'm making now.

### Kiirana

*Albuquerque, New Mexico*

I was initiated by Didi Ananda Shamita in 1995. As a new margii, I was enthusiastic to learn about Guru – where He came from, what He did, and all He passed on to us. I was coincidentally reading a book called *Who's Afraid of the Tantric Guru?* which turned out to be a pretty good primer for new margiis because it explains the life and travels of people who traveled with Baba. Those of us who never saw Baba physically of course are interested in everyone else's stories.

So, I was reading along in this book written in journal form, and the date May 29th sprang up. Baba was landing in Geneva, Switzerland. I just stopped right there and was riveted with that page because it occurred to me that I was living in Geneva then. Of course, at that time I didn't have a clue. I didn't know anything about Baba, Ananda Marga, or spirituality for that matter. I was beating myself up over this. I started to look back through my notes, tickets and all other kinds of things to determine where I was the day Guru came to Geneva. I became obsessed over this and what I thought of as my delinquency at not knowing about Baba. So, about a year later, I would still find myself in tears over this.

One day I was driving back from town with Dada Rajatakrishna, and he said very matter of factly, “If you want to know Him, if that is your desire, then that is what you will get.” He didn't say specifically how to do this, but I had an idea that it was probably through my sadhana. After several months of thinking about Guru and obsessing with knowing Guru, I had a dream. I'm not certain whether it was a real dream or a result of my intense desire. The Australian Aborigines say that we dream the creation into existence. If that is what happened, then so be it.

The dream unfolded in the Geneva airport. At this time I was long back in the United States, having come back in 1982. So in my dream Baba was coming to visit me in Switzerland. I was to meet Him at the Geneva airport. I was on my bicycle as I usually was in those days. It was a cold, foggy day, and there was no visibility. There were no incoming flights or outgoing flights, no cars. It didn't occur to me that Baba wouldn't come; after all, He was Guru. So, I continued and got to the airport and parked my bike

against a chain link fence. I waited and then heard the sound of an incoming flight. First, it was very faint in the distance, then it became stronger and stronger. I was hoping against hope that it was Baba. The plane landed and I saw it was a small Cessna type. Baba was the pilot, and He came down. I was very nervous.

He came over and said, “Namaskar, Kiirana.” I looked over my shoulder and since there wasn’t anyone else there, I assumed He was talking to me. So, I said, “Namaskar, Monsieur Sarkar.”

We were speaking in French, and I said, “Nice day today.”

Of course it wasn’t and was just a stupid nervous thing to say. But, Baba being very kind didn’t smile or question me. He simply agreed, saying, “Yes, it is a very nice day today.”

I looked down and noticed He was wearing sandals. I wanted to offer Him my socks, but I was too nervous. He said something in French, “Shall we have a walk?”

We began to walk through the airport. The fog was so thick it was like walking on a treadmill. He was talking about Switzerland, how He loved the mountains and how He was looking forward to staying for a while. He mentioned how He loved the flowers in the windows. Switzerland is famous for its flowers. He said that it was good that I was there and that once I was finished, I wouldn’t be interested in going back. I don’t remember much else of what He said. Finally, He asked me in French, “Is there something I can do for you?”

How do you answer such a question from Guru? The sky is the limit. I didn’t think about it very long. I said in French, “Yes, may I try on your glasses?”

He didn’t bat an eye and acted as if it were a very normal request. So, I put on Baba’s glasses. I realized it was the perfect question. It was the perfect thing to ask of Guru because behind His glasses was what I was looking for and what I would still be looking for if I hadn’t put on His glasses. It is still hard to explain in language. It was stillness – the formless, all-knowing, all-pervasive entity. Easy to read about, and even mildly possible to understand. But this was actually It. There was the motionless stillness that was holding up all the creation. The creation in this image was like fireflies blinking on and off. There was nothing that I could identify as form, just that blinking on and off.

But that wasn’t what was so noticeable. It was that stillness, that formless entity that was the Supreme entity that sustains us, that holds us. We were the fireflies in the creation. It was bliss. I was witnessing that bliss. I didn’t need to have His glasses on for long to feel this, but I left them on for as long as possible.

So, I asked him in French what He saw with his glasses. And He answered in French, “I see what you see.”

Thus, I assumed to myself that He knew what I saw. So, we continued our walk a little more. Finally He said, “I have to go now, Kiirana.” I said, “Yes, Monsieur Sarkar.”

He said, “Au revoir.” “Au revoir” doesn’t just mean goodbye. It literally means, “Until we meet again.” It is really a nice way to say goodbye, and I had never really thought about what it meant. Whenever I hear the word, I think of my meeting Baba. So, He went off towards His Cessna, waving. I said, “Au revoir, Monsieur Sarkar,” and He replied in French, “You can call me Baba, Kiirana.”

Since that time, it has always been “until we meet again,” meaning that any one of us can have Him anytime. Only the desire has to be there. My relationship really changed with Guru from that point on. If it wasn’t really a dream, it doesn’t matter. It was a turning point for me in my relationship to Guru, which became very personal for me from that point on. We had a lot of fun and played together. We had some really funny moments, as Baba can be funny. And I do call him Baba now. Certainly that turning point helped me escape a lot of clash – as clash is defined as a crisis of faith, wondering if we are doing the right thing, following the right path. Since then I haven’t had many doubts, and it has allowed me to live my life in a much calmer way and be more disciplined and confident about the future, which is obviously the present if we can go back to the airport anytime.

### Prakash 4

I feel that some of our strongest Baba stories are the ways in which our children come to us, because of connections, because Baba wanted them to be in our family. Several months after we adopted Anandamayii, we were visiting the birth mother. When she was in labor we spent five days with her. She told us, “I knew you were the right parents because Anandamayii was conceived on Devinishta’s birthday.” Later, [my wife] Devinishta was looking through journals and saw that she had written, “Today is my birthday; I asked Baba for a baby.” She asked Baba for guidance. But Baba had already arranged it.

Mahajoy was adopted two years later. Anandamayii’s mother was a friend of Mahajoy’s mother, and she wanted us to adopt her. The mother couldn’t make up her mind whether she wanted to accept us as parents or not. But all of this support came from family, friends and margiis all around the country. I would write letters about what was happening, and we got so much support; she was really part of Baba’s family. That was the most amazing time, when I really felt Baba’s direct hand. We went to court and the mother basically decided that she would not like us to adopt Mahajoy. Our lawyer went in to talk with her lawyer and the judge, and I sat and said to Baba, “Do whatever is best for Mahajoy. I don’t know.” The judge came out and met with the lawyers, and then said the mother had changed her mind. I felt my surrender made it possible. When I surrender to His love, miracles happen.

In 1985, we gave birth to Liina, which was another miracle. We had had an operation to reverse infertility. There was a 20 percent chance of success. We discovered at Ananda Kanan that Devinishta was pregnant. And all the margiis gathered around and did kiirtan. After the surgery, the surgeon had said there was one in a thousand chance of her getting pregnant. It was again a miracle of Baba. All of our children are gifts from Him. I experience His grace every day because of my family.

### Anandii

*Longmont, Colorado*

I was born in Argentina and initiated there in 1986 by Didi Ambuja. In December, 1992, I was pregnant with Jasmine. She wanted to be born when I was only six months pregnant. I was rushed to the hospital, and the nurses said it was too early.

So what happened was the doctor tried to stop all the contractions; they put an intravenous drip (IV) into me with magnesium sulfate. It relaxes all the muscles, which relaxes the uterus. It’s like torture, like you’re on fire. Finally they stopped the contractions. They showed me the care unit, with very tiny little babies, weighing one or two pounds. And I made a firm determination that Jasmine wasn’t going to be born for three more months.

During the time I was with the IV, Baba came to me. He was holding the hand of one of the nurses. He was all in white, with His stick. He was older, and needed some help walking. He came, and pointed with His stick to me, and He said, “You take good care of yourself. Don’t worry about Jasmine, I will take good care of her.” And that’s what He did. He took good care.

He was constantly guiding me about what to do. Many times I wanted to pray, “I want a girl that is strong and healthy,” because I saw the little babies in the care unit. But Baba said, “Don’t pray, I don’t want you to be a beggar, don’t do any prayers.” He did take good care of her.

We drove home and I had to stay in bed. Two weeks later I was in the hospital again. This time I told the doctor I preferred to walk on my knees from here to New York than to be put on the magnesium sulfate again. The baby at this point was only 900 grams, not even one kilo. We had thought that the baby was going to be a boy but they did an ultrasound, and it was a girl. I wasn’t ready with a girl’s name, and I wanted to name her. I wanted to stop my mind, but it was going very fast like a washing machine. And then suddenly, like a drop of water, the name “Jasmine” came. So Baba gave this name.

In January, there was a big storm, and it was the full moon. With the full moon, there is a tremendous pull on the uterus. And so I ended up again in the hospital. It was the 30th week. I said, “I don’t want Jasmine to be born yet.”
