The lungs and the immune system needed to be developed. I started my pregnancy with 110 pounds, and had gained four pounds, and I didn’t want to lose them. I was fasting for 48 hours. The next morning, the sun started rising, and all the contractions stopped. The doctors were ready to do the Caesarean section, and I didn’t want to go through that – the baby wasn’t ready. By Baba’s Grace, the nurse came over and asked, “Did the contractions stop?” “Yes!”

In February, at 35 weeks, the contractions were at every six minutes, and that was OK, but at every three minutes, I had to rush to the hospital. We thought, well, maybe Jasmine should be born. But still, there were parts of her lungs that needed more time, the lungs might collapse. Baba’s presence was always there. We had kiirtan going constantly, 24-hour kiirtan. And He stopped the contractions again. The nurses were thinking she would be born by the thirtieth week. “You did a wonderful job!” and I thought, “I did nothing, it was only by Baba’s Grace.”

Her due date was March 29th, and on March 11th the doctor said “No more.” I wanted her to be born naturally, but the doctor said no. So she was born by C-Section, and I was able to watch it, because I was looking at the doctor’s glasses. She was born very healthy, six pounds, and everything went very well.

### Anjali Natarajan

*Houston, Texas*

Jagannath’s baby naming ceremony was a memorable event. A devotional vibration encircled the *jagrti* as we charged the place with *Prabhat Samgiit*, kiirtan and sadhana. We had the honored presence of six *acaryas*: *Acaryas* Ananda Ragamaya, Ananda Sucandra, Giitainjali, Ishvarakrsnananda, Nityasatyananda, and Shankarananda. It was a day to welcome our little one into the spiritual community as well as a golden opportunity to introduce my parents to the sweetness of Ananda Marga culture; to show how we are all connected and how we care for others.

I was feeling hesitant that I had invited my mother and father to the ceremony. They both have had a hard time seeing the changes in me since I have become a margii. My mother is Jewish and my father is Catholic and their views on Eastern spiritual practices are not so positive. For instance, my mother is always questioning “guru.”

“Why does anyone need a spiritual master? You can find your own way to God.”

My father is not so verbose, but I felt that he might be uncomfortable just the same. I invited them out of great love and respect. Jagannath is their only grandson and the only grandchild living in the state, so I knew that for them it would be very important to take part in this ritual. They are also both retired and relish the time spent with their grandson. I worried about how they would fit in, and how the margiis would treat them. I realized that these were unnecessary worries and surrendered my mind to Baba. “Let it be in His hands.”

Dada Nityasatyanda began the ceremony leading *Prabhat Samgiit*. The celestial voices filled the room as I led my mother and father in. Giita sang a beautiful song that she had written – a window to the loving dance between a young child and his mother. I began to cry, feeling that it was a great gift that Baba had given us our little child. Didi Ananda Ragamaya began, explaining that the Baby Naming Ceremony was for the society to welcome the child and that we would take an oath to care for his well being.

My mother asked suspiciously, “Do the grandparents *also* have a part in this?”

“Oh, no,” I thought, “Please don’t embarrass me.”

“Of course they do,” beamed Didi, “They have a very important part in his life, and we are all very glad that you are here today!”

The other *acaryas* echoed parallel responses as we all laughed lovingly.

At that moment, I felt so proud to be a margii. I saw everyone embracing my parents, giving to them from the core of their hearts, and making sure that they felt a part of our spiritual family. Dada Ishvarakrsnananda and another brother moved my parents’ seats up close so that they would be a part of the circle. Didi Ananda Ragamaya moved the bath closer to my mother (who has difficulty walking), and got up to give her the cup so that she didn’t have to bend so far. Both Didi and Dada Nityasatyananda explained all of the procedures and the naming song to my parents directly. They went out of their way to make certain that Jagannath’s grandparents were included.

The air was full of love and joy during the ceremony. Everyone was laughing and sharing in the delight of seeing a new margii child into the community. I’d never felt so much love from a group of people before. From the day he was born, the *acaryas* and margiis have treated Jagannath as if he was more than their own little brother/child/grandchild and this day was just as special for them. Jagannath’s eyes were shining as we sang the *mantras* to him. He was wiggling around and expressing his pleasure as if he knew exactly that this day was for him. I had seen the same response in Trimurtii when he had his naming ceremony. It’s as if they realize that something wonderful is taking place.

I wondered, “How much do these babies understand…? Can they see and feel Baba as we do, or even more?”

Everything became profoundly clear as we began to bathe him – that this was a spiritual bath, a bath that had been blessed by Baba through the tender, loving hands of the margiis and my parents. I could feel His love and blessing as we washed our son’s body, and mentally gave thanks to Him for choosing us to be a part of his family.

At lunchtime, Dada Ishvarakrsnananda continued to demonstrate Baba’s unconditional love towards my parents, running back and forth asking them if they’d like anything to eat or drink. He was just like a beloved mother to them. My parents commented on how wonderful the ceremony had been, and how kind everyone was. They had enjoyed themselves tremendously.

In retrospect, I know that any other religious ceremony such as a Catholic baptism would not have been as joyful. That which sets the Baby Naming Ceremony apart from others is that everyone participates, all are equal. This demystifies our relationship to God because *we* are the expressions of His love, not some distant pastor who is put on a pedestal. Baba’s love is directly, deeply present and felt. As the years go by and my sadhana and devotion intensify, I can experience His vibrant force more and more. I wonder if my parents felt it too? On some level, I’m sure that they did.

At home that evening as we began to sing kiirtan, the sweet fragrance of sandalwood emanated from Jagannath’s body. I was perplexed… I had not smelled it on him earlier. The scent lingered as the kiirtan flowed on and I realized that this was Baba’s blessing.

# GLOSSARY

Acarya – one who teaches by example; a monk or nun of Ananda Marga engaged in teaching meditation and service

Ajina cakra – the so-called third eye cakra, located between the eyebrows; central point of the mind.

Akhanda kiirtan – the singing and dancing of kiirtan in three-, six-, twelve- or twenty-four-hour increments.

AMURT – Ananda Marga Universal Relief Team; an international relief and development organization.

Anandam – bliss

Ananda Mela – a festival sponsored by Ananda Marga.

Aparigraha – one of the ten points of morality; not overindulging in the possession of material goods; non-acquisitiveness.

Avadhuta/avadhutika – an acarya who has learned kapalika sadhana; a complete renunciate, wearing full orange dress.

Bhajans – devotional songs.

Bhakti – devotion for God.

Bhava a state of deep ideation in which the mind is completely concentrated.

Bhukti Pradhan – family person who is a local leader of Ananda Marga.

Brahma – Supreme Consciousness or God.

Cakra – psycho-spiritual controlling point in the body, linked on the physical level to the endocrine glands.

Darshan – “to see.” Being in the presence of a master. Also refers to the talks and lectures given by Baba.

Dharma – Spirituality; original nature or innate tendency; the path of righteousness.

Dharmacakra – “circle of dharma”; collective meditation.

Dharma samiksa – “analysis of one’s adherence to the path”; a program conducted by Baba in 1981 during which He gave direct guidance to many margiis.

Dharma pracar – the propagation of spiritual ideas among the public.

Dharma Maha Cakra (DMC) – a spiritual gathering of Ananda Marga, often attended by 20,000 people, in which Baba presented a discourse, and then bestowed a blessing through the varabhaya mudra.

Dharma Maha Samellan (DMS) – a gathering similar to DMC, without the presence of Baba.

Dhyana – contemplation; merging one’s mind into Cosmic Consciousness; the sixth lesson of AM meditation.

Diiksa – initiation into spiritual practice; learning meditation.

Ekadashi – eleventh day after the full moon and new moon; a fasting day.

ERAWS – Education, Relief and Welfare Section of Ananda Marga.

Gopi – a village devotee of Krsna.

Guru mantra – second lesson of Ananda Marga meditation; a process for seeing the divine in all things.

Guru puja – a mantra by which the devotee offers his or her ego attachments to the guru, sung after meditation.

Guru sakasha – “to be near the guru”; a practice which involves visualization of the guru.

Iishvara pranidhana – first lesson of Ananda Marga meditation on the Supreme.

Ista cakra – cakra upon which one concentrates during meditation.

Ista mantra – individual mantra which allows one to focus on one’s spiritual goal.

Jagrti – “place of spiritual development”; local Ananda Marga meditation center

Japa – repetition of the mantra

Kamamaya kosa – one of the five layers of mind; the conscious mind.

Kaoshikii – a dance exercise designed by Baba, which harmonizes many glands of the body.

Kapalik sadhana – meditation designed to overcome fear, done at night in the graveyard, most often by avadhutas and avadhutikas

Khurta – a long Indian shirt

Kiirtan – singing the Lord’s name, accompanied by a rhythmic dance.

Krpa – grace, as in God’s grace.

Laungota – men’s yogic underwear.

Liila – play, as in Divine Play.

Lungi – Indian garment that wraps around the lower half of the body.

Mahaprayan – Baba’s Great Departure; His leaving the physical body.

Mahasambhuti – a great incarnation.

Mohan vijnana – supra-aesthetic science; deals with the irresistible attraction human beings feel for God.

Mudra – meaningful hand gesture.

Nama mantra – simple mantra used in introductory form of meditation.

Nirvikalpa samadhi – absorption into pure Consciousness; total merger with the Supreme.

Padmasana – cross-legged sitting posture for meditation.

Paincajanya – spiritual practice done at five a.m., which includes Prabhat Samgiit, kiirtan and meditation.

Parama Purusa – Supreme Consciousness; God.

Prabhat Samgiit – a body of over 5000 mystical songs and poems composed by Baba.

Pranam – respectful greeting.

Pranayama – fourth lesson of AM meditation; control of the breath.

Prasad – food infused with spiritual vibrations.

Pratik – the symbol of Ananda Marga ideology; representing service and self-realization.

Prout – Progressive Utilization Theory; a socio-economic theory proposed by Baba.

Purodha Pramukha – the spiritual head of AM organization after Baba’s physical death.

RAWA – Renaissance Artists and Writers Association; a department of AM focusing on the arts.

RU – Renaissance Universal; a department of AM serving as a forum for intellectual discussion.

Rudra – Shiva’s mood of great anger.

Sadhana – meditation.

Sadvipras – spiritual moralists; revolutionaries working for social change

Sahasrara Cakra – the seventh or crown cakra, located at the crown of the head.

Samgacchadvam – mantra sung during dharmacakra signifying the unity of those present.

Samadhi – state of complete absorption; merging with Cosmic Consciousness.

Samaj – literally, “society” or “a group of people moving together.”

Samskara – karmic reaction lying in potential form in the mind, waiting to be expressed.

Sannyasi – renunciant, monk or nun.

Sastaung pranam – posture of respectful salutation to the guru; lying prone with arms and legs extended.

Satsaunga – good company; the fellowship of other spiritual people.

Sattvika – sentient principle; referring to one of the three gunas or qualities in the universe: tama, raja, and sattva; quality of purity and peace.

Shabda Cayanika – “A Collection of Words”; a series of lectures Baba gave on the linguistics and philology of the Bengali language.

Shloka – Sanskrit verse.

Shravanii Purnima – a day commemorating the first initiation Baba gave.

Shuddhis – process of withdrawing one’s identification with the material world during meditation.

Siddhis – occult powers.
Sixteen Points – a summary of the most important Ananda Marga practices.

Tandava – a vigorous dance for males that stimulates all the glands and develops courage.

Tantra – the oldest and foremost of spiritual traditions, originating in India in prehistoric times.

Taraka Brahma – that being that links the spiritual world with the material world.

Tattvika – one trained in AM philosophy.

Trikuti – ajina cakra.

Vanii – a spiritual message

Varabhaya mudra – “blessing of fearlessness”; a bestowal of energy through a particular hand gesture; Baba performed this mudra usually during DMCs.

WWD – Women’s Welfare Department; a department of Ananda Marga run by women and dealing with women’s development and issues.

Yama and Niyama – ten cardinal points of morality.

# I Am Yours

### Memories of a beloved teacher: stories from devotees of Shrii Shrii Anandamurti

It is said that the life of a great spiritual master can best be understood through his relationship with his disciples. "I Am Yours" chronicles the relationship between the great 20th century master, Shrii Shrii Anandamurti, and his devotees from North America and the Caribbean, spanning a period of more than three decades. Through the oral histories of more than 70 disciples, we get a unique look into that mysterious bond between teacher and disciple, a bond that begins and ends in the love which shines forth from the devotional heart of these beautiful stories.

*Devashish Donald Acosta*
*author of When the Time Comes*

This book is a most valuable addition to the record of the unparalled life of Baba, Shrii Shrii Anandamurtiji. Though He wrote and spoke virtually nothing about Himself, He had a vast, perhaps incomprehensible, effect on many people. And this will continue into the far future.

But this book shows that He may be comprehensible in the spiritual heart, to those who were and will be so touched by Him. It attempts—successfully I think—to help us look a bit behind the curtain of the most mysterious and most loving, compassionate and spiritually brilliant person we will ever know.

*Ac. Jyotirupananda*
*editor, New Renaissance, www.ru.org*
